# cline → Claude Shepherd — feature-mining report

> Source: `/Users/adam/Programming/check out/cline` (VS Code/CLI/SDK coding-agent platform: rules/skills/workflows, auto-approve, checkpoints, MCP, Kanban, multi-agent teams, scheduled agents).  
> Target: **Claude Shepherd** — local single-user macOS console that WATCHES/STEERS/SPAWNS Claude Code GUI sessions (no executor, no workspace editing, no server).  
> Method: 12 finders → dedup → adversarial FOR/AGAINST judge (cline mismatch: in-process executor + partial multi-user hub) → skeptic verify, cross-referencing queued L1–L6.  
> Counts: **177 raw → 90 candidates → 67 ADAPT, 22 DON'T-ADD, 0 pure-ADD** (89/90 verdicted; one judge dropped to an API overload).

**Headline:** cline (the closest analog to Claude Code itself) overwhelmingly CORROBORATES the backlog — 65 of 67 ADAPT reinforce L1–L6 (L1×28, L5×15, L4×10, L2×5, L6×5, L3×2). Genuinely new: **scheduled agents / routines** (a scheduling capability + a routine-board UI) and **OS-native system notifications**.

---

## ✅ ADAPT — 67 items, grouped by what they reinforce

### 🆕 GENUINELY NEW (scheduling / native notifications)  (2)

#### OS system notifications for approvals & completion (macOS/Windows/Linux)  `[H/S]`
- **For:** This is the purest local-notification match for Shepherd and it requires zero agent execution, tool running, or workspace editing — it only reads status transitions, which Shepherd already does every ~1s. The need is real and central: Shepherd's whole reason to exist is human-in-the-loop, and the operator is usually NOT staring at the panel when a session flips to "NEEDS YOU". The infrastructure is already 2/3 built: cc-core detects the status edge (claude-dashboard.lua:5386 `pv = prev[it.key]`, swapped at :5619), and FX.playSound()/FX.push() (claude-dashboard.lua:969-979) already fire out-of-app on the escalation/hung edge. The one channel Shepherd is missing is the native desktop banner — and in Hammerspoon that is a one-liner (`hs.notify.new{title, informativeText, ...}:send()`), with click-to-activate able to jump to the session window. Firing on the first approval-needed and done transitions (not just the stale/escalation nag) closes a genuine gap: today you get nagged only after an approval has sat for N minutes, never the instant it appears.
- **Against:** Much of the candidate's surface is already present or irrelevant. The cross-platform plumbing (osascript / PowerShell toast / notify-send) is dead weight: Shepherd is macOS-Hammerspoon-only, so only `hs.notify` applies and the Windows/Linux builders never ship. Out-of-app alerting on the attention edge ALREADY EXISTS via sound + ntfy push (escalation.sound/escalation.push, claude-dashboard.lua:5540-5544), plus an in-panel notification HISTORY with a 🔔 badge and Alerts tab. So the marginal addition is narrow: one more channel (a native banner) and two more trigger points (first-approval and done). An "auto-approved-command" notification risks becoming noise during autopilot, and naive per-tile firing every refresh would spam — it must be strictly rising-edge and once-per-episode, like the existing escalation flag. Risk of redundancy with the existing sound/push channels if not consolidated.
- **Ruling:** No cline mismatch: this watches status files and fires a local effect — exactly Shepherd's model, no executor/server/workspace involved. It is not a new system but an EXTENSION of the existing escalation-alert path, so verdict is ADAPT, not ADD. It doesn't map to any queued L1-L6 (those are agent profiles, policy bundles, templates, routing, observability, rule engine — none cover notifications), so reinforces is empty. The reshape onto Shepherd's primitives: (a) drop the cross-platform builders, use hs.notify only; (b) add a third channel alongside the existing playSound/push (an FX.notify wrapper) and gate it on the same per-tile edge tracker; (c) extend the trigger set from escalation/hung-only to also include the rising edge into `approval` and into `done`, each fired once per episode via a `notified` flag carried in newPrev exactly like the `escalated` flag at :5530/:5547/:5619; (d) make banner-on-approval / banner-on-done / banner-on-auto-approved independently toggleable config keys, OFF by default per Shepherd's automation policy. Every notification already lands in the ledger as a notificationEvent, so the in-panel history is unchanged. This is the strongest-fitting candidate seen — high confidence.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add `FX.notify(title, text, opts)` next to FX.playSound/FX.push (claude-dashboard.lua:969-979) wrapping `hs.notify.new(fn, {title=title, informativeText=text, withdrawAfter=0})` with a click callback that focuses the session window (reuse the existing jump-to-window dispatch). New config block `notifications.banner = { onApproval=false, onDone=false, onAutoApproved=false }` in cc-config.example.json, surfaced as checkboxes in the settings drawer beside the existing escalation sound/push toggles. Wire it into the refresh loop using the existing edge detector: at the per-tile block (around the escalation check at :5531), detect `pv and pv.status ~= "approval" and it.status == "approval"` (rising edge to approval) and the analogous edge to "done"; fire FX.notify once, guarded by a `notified` field carried in `newPrev[it.key]` (:5619) mirroring the `escalated` carry-forward pattern. Auto-approved notification hooks the existing gate auto-allow decision (the autoAllow/autopilot path) emitting an info-level banner. Pure edge/predicate logic (which transition fires which banner) goes in cc-core as a small `notifyDecision(pv, it, cfg)` helper, unit-tested in tests/core.test.lua; the hs.notify side effect stays in the FX table.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted but the ADAPT verdict holds. No cline mismatch exists: this watches transitions and fires a purely LOCAL effect — exactly Shepherd's model, with no agent loop, tool execution, workspace editing/snapshotting, or server/multi-user surface. So DONT_ADD does not apply. I pushed hardest on the redundancy angle (error category c), and it is the strongest objection but not fatal. Shepherd ALREADY HAS a hook-driven "fire a local effect on the approval-needed and done transitions" mechanism: focus.popOnComplete / focus.popOnApproval (cc-popup.sh, README:418-423). settings-hooks.json wires the EXACT three hooks the feature wants — Stop → cc-popup.sh ...complete (the done edge), and PermissionRequest + Notification → cc-popup.sh ...approval (the approval-needed edge) — each an independently-toggleable, off-by-default config key. Separately, escalation.sound + escalation.push (FX.playSound / FX.push → ntfy.sh, claude-dashboard.lua:969-979, fired at :5540-5544 / :5560-5562) already alert on the approval-stale and hung edges. But it is NOT fully redundant: focus.pop* RAISES/opens the editor window, it does not show a native OS banner carrying the abbreviated request text; the escalation push is a REMOTE phone push (ntfy.sh) gated on a staleness TIMER (waited > N min), not an immediate local desktop banner on the rising edge; and hs.notify (the native macOS banner) is used nowhere in the codebase (grep: zero hits). A native local desktop banner on the immediate edge is therefore a genuinely new LOCAL-console effect that improves the single-user watch/steer console — so it transfers, which is ADAPT, not ADD-as-new-system and not DONT_ADD. One correction to the judge's Shepherd mapping (does not change the verdict): the cleanest reshape is NOT the proposed polling-edge detector in claude-dashboard.lua's refresh loop (:5530-5547) with a new `notified` carry-forward flag — that reinvents edge detection the hooks already do for free and lags by up to ~1s. The right home is a THIRD effect in cc-popup.sh, which is ALREADY invoked on precisely these transitions with the complete/approval event arg and is ALREADY split-toggled; add an osascript `display notification` (macOS-only per Shepherd scope; drop the cross-platform builders) gated on new focus.bannerOnComplete / focus.bannerOnApproval keys beside the existing focus.popOn* keys, off by default. The auto-approved-command banner does belong in the gate (cc-approve.sh auto-allow paths at :157/:174-175/:186), independently toggled. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-popup.sh, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/settings-hooks.json, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-approve.sh, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (FX table :969-979; escalation edge :5530-5562), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-config.example.json (focus block, README:387). It maps to no queued L1-L6 (those are agent profiles / policy bundles / templates / routing / observability / rule engine), so reinforces is empty. Verdict: ADAPT holds, high confidence — implemented as a banner channel in the existing hook-driven cc-popup.sh path rather than a new refresh-loop edge detector.

#### Desktop Routine board (visual schedule management UI)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The operator NEED — "at 9am Mon-Fri, start a Claude Code session in this folder with this prompt/model/mode" — fits Shepherd's local model squarely, and is a genuine GAP not covered by any current primitive or queued L-item. Shepherd's automation is ENTIRELY level-triggered (per-refresh-tick predicates: shouldFeed/shouldAutoContinue/isHung/respawn); it has NO clock/cron primitive (grep confirms only doEvery refresh loops, runSequence beat-delays, daily-ledger filename math — no "spawn at a future wall-clock time"). Yet every ingredient a scheduled spawn needs already exists: spawnSpec(editor/folder/provider/permMode/task) at cc-core.lua:3061, spawn presets (cc-presets.json, M.presetPush ~3673), parameterized templates (L3), the local claude CLI on PATH (--agent/--mcp-config/--append-system-prompt/--add-dir), and Plan/Act mode switching. A scheduler is literally "fire an existing spawnSpec/preset on a cron tick" — high-leverage reuse. The TRANSFERABLE parts the candidate names are all pure, hs-free, and unit-testable in cc-core: buildCronPattern/parseCronPattern/expandCronDays (weekday+time<->cron, lines 266-336) drop in as pure functions with regression tests; the row layout (status dot, mode/cron badge, prompt/model/workspace, last-run/next-run, inline view/run-now/pause/delete) is the same tile/list idiom Shepherd already renders; the basic/advanced create modal mirrors the existing spawn-preset modal. nextRunAt computation from a cron pattern is pure math that belongs in cc-core. This directly serves the user's adjacency to saved-agent workflows (a routine = "spawn this saved agent/template on a schedule").
- **Against:** As shipped, the board is a control surface for the cline MISMATCH in its purest form: each row's payload is a RoutineExecution that the cline-hub SERVER runs headlessly — sessionId, status, errorMessage, maxIterations, timeoutSeconds, maxParallel, active-execution indicator, last-result. That is cline-as-executor AND cline-as-server (the hub), exactly what Shepherd structurally cannot host: no agent loop, no run-to-completion, no captured exit result, no maxIterations enforcement, no parallelism scheduler, no multi-user hub. The candidate itself concedes "the RPC-to-hub plumbing does not [transfer]" — which is most of the feature's actual value. Shepherd watches status files written by hooks; it cannot report a routine's "last result: failed at T" or "timed out" because it never ran the loop and has no execution record — at best it knows a tile it spawned later went error/done. maxParallel, timeoutSeconds, and maxIterations have no honest home (autopilot is a time-box on approvals, not an iteration cap). So a faithful copy is impossible, and a naive one would advertise execution semantics Shepherd can't deliver. There is also real cost/risk: a cron that auto-spawns unattended sessions cuts against Shepherd's human-in-the-loop reason-to-exist and its OFF-BY-DEFAULT-automation stance — it must be opt-in, bounded, and ledgered like respawn/continue.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD is wrong because the underlying capability — time-triggered spawns — fits the local single-user model perfectly and is a clean gap (Shepherd has zero clock-based automation; everything is state/level-triggered). ADD is wrong because a faithful copy depends on the hub server running and recording headless executions (RoutineExecution lifecycle, maxParallel, timeoutSeconds, maxIterations, last-result/errorMessage) — the textbook executor+server mismatch the candidate openly admits doesn't transfer. So ADAPT: keep the NEED and the genuinely portable pieces (the pure cron<->weekday/time builder, the row layout, the create modal), drop the execution-record columns, and reshape the rest onto Shepherd's spawn primitives. I left 'reinforces' empty because none of L1-L6 is clock-triggered (L4 is queue/route-triggered, L6 is event-callback-triggered, L3 is render-on-spawn) — a cron scheduler is an orthogonal new primitive, though it composes naturally with L3 templates (render-then-spawn) and L1 agent profiles (the saved thing being scheduled) as its payload, and its fire/skip/result lines fold into L6's automation ledger family.
- **How (Shepherd):** Build a "Scheduled Spawns" feature, not a hub-execution board. (1) cc-core (pure, tested): port buildCronPattern/parseCronPattern/expandCronDays verbatim as M.cronFromSchedule / M.scheduleFromCron / M.cronWeekdays; add M.nextRunAt(cronPattern, now) and M.dueSchedules(list, lastFiredMap, now) returning which routines to fire this tick — all deterministic on injected `now`, beside the existing level-triggered predicates. (2) Persist routines in a cc-schedules.json operator-data file (same pattern as cc-presets.json / templates): {name, cron, folder, editor, provider, model, permMode, prompt|templateRef, enabled, tags, lastFiredAt}. The payload is an existing spawnSpec or a saved preset/L3 template — NOT a new execution type; drop sessionId/maxParallel/maxIterations/timeoutSeconds/errorMessage (no executor to honor them). (3) claude-dashboard glue: the existing ~1s refresh loop already ticks — add a guarded pass that calls core.dueSchedules and, OFF BY DEFAULT + ledgered (mirror auto-respawn/auto-continue discipline), routes a due routine through the normal spawn fx (onSpawn / spawnSpec), then jumps the lastFiredAt marker via fx like routePending. (4) Panel: render routines as a list reusing the tile/badge idiom — status dot (enabled), cron + mode badges, prompt/model/folder, next-run (from core.nextRunAt), inline run-now (immediate spawn), pause/resume (toggle enabled), delete; an 'Add Routine' modal reusing the spawn-preset modal with the hour/minute/weekday picker -> live cron preview (the pure builder, hand-mirrored in JS like the other twins). "Last run / active" becomes a soft link to the live tile it spawned (status-file based), not an execution record. Effort sits in the timer-gating + off-by-default/ledger plumbing, since the cron math and UI are direct ports.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; the ADAPT ruling holds. I verified the codebase against all four common-error angles. (a) Executor: the portable pieces the judge keeps — the pure cron<->weekday/time builder, the row layout, the create modal — run no agent loop, execute no tools, and edit/snapshot no workspace. The execution-heavy parts of a cline routine (RoutineExecution lifecycle, maxParallel/timeoutSeconds/maxIterations/errorMessage, the headless run record) are exactly what the judge drops; what remains is "fire a spawn at a scheduled time," routed through Shepherd's EXISTING spawn primitive (M.spawnSpec at cc-core.lua:3061 / the onSpawn fx), not a new executor. (b) Server: the candidate itself states the RPC-to-hub plumbing does not transfer; the judge maps persistence to a local cc-schedules.json mirroring the existing cc-presets.json / cc-templates.json pattern (confirmed cc-core.lua:64-65). No server/users. (c) Redundant/queued — the hardest angle, checked thoroughly: I grep'd the whole repo for cron/clock/schedule/next-run/last-fired/routine and found ZERO clock-based trigger. Every Shepherd automation (auto-respawn cc-core.lua:2116, auto-continue 2200, autopilot, project routing, queue) is state/level/event-triggered. The one near-miss, 'auto-spawn on starvation' (todos.md:202), is queue-starvation-triggered (not wall-clock) and is itself deferred. L1-L6 confirm the judge's empty 'reinforces': L1 profiles, L2 policy bundles, L3 templates, L4 routing, L5 observability, L6 EVENT-callback rules (when-event->action, not a clock). A cron scheduler is a genuinely orthogonal, absent primitive that composes with L3 (render-then-spawn) and L1 (the saved payload). (d) Not SDK plumbing — a schedule board with run-now/pause/resume + cron-preview is an operator console surface. ADD is correctly rejected (faithful copy needs the hub recording headless executions); DONT_ADD is wrong (time-triggered spawns cleanly fit the local single-user console and reuse the off-by-default + ledgered discipline of auto-respawn/auto-continue via the existing ~1s refresh loop at claude-dashboard.lua:5786). Keep the need + portable pieces, drop the execution-record columns, reshape onto spawnSpec = textbook ADAPT.

### Reinforces L1 — Agent Profiles  (28)

#### Conditional / folder-scoped rule activation (YAML frontmatter globs)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The operator need is real and on-mission: "the right instructions/skills auto-attach when a session is spawned in a matching folder" is exactly the user's stated #1 interest (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory we hand work off to), with auto-scoping by folder removing the manual picking step. Shepherd already has every primitive this reshape needs: M.projectKey (cc-core.lua:57) is a stable launch-folder identity that labels and groups already resolve by; L1 agent profiles already carry a `folder` field plus skills[]/knowledge[]/seedPrompt that thread into spawnFlags / --append-system-prompt / --add-dir at spawn (spawnSpec cc-core.lua:3061); and L6 rules already scope by trigger:{scope: projectKey}. So "scope a rule/instruction file to a folder (or provider) so it auto-attaches at spawn" is a thin, pure cc-core addition: a folder-glob matcher (sibling to the existing fuzzyFilter) that, at New-Session time, picks which saved profile(s)/instruction files apply to the chosen directory and pre-fills the --add-dir / --append-system-prompt set. paths:[] = never auto-attach, invalid-glob fails open — both pure, testable, off by default. It widens an existing primitive rather than inventing a system.
- **Against:** cline's actual value is RUNTIME, in-loop behavior: the rule activates and deactivates as the agent's working set changes during the run, gating what reaches the LLM's context window each turn (frontend rules go dormant the moment the agent touches backend files; *.test.ts rules wake only while editing a test). That depends on (a) running the agent loop, (b) observing the live file set the agent reads/writes, and (c) mutating the system prompt / context mid-conversation — all three of which Shepherd structurally cannot do. Shepherd has no executor, never edits the workspace, and only ever touches a session ONCE at spawn (spawnSpec emits flags; after that Claude Code owns the loop). So the dynamic glob-matching-against-current-context engine is pure DONT_ADD territory. What remains after stripping the runtime half — "match the spawn cwd against a glob to pick which static instruction file to attach" — is a thinner thing than the headline, and native Claude Code already reads project CLAUDE.md / .clinerules-equivalents from the cwd, so even the spawn-time slice is partly redundant with what the editor does on its own.
- **Ruling:** Apply the cline mismatch: cline is an in-process executor and the conditional-rule feature's core worth is dynamic, per-turn context-window injection keyed to the live working set — running the agent and editing what it sees mid-loop. Shepherd has no agent loop and no context-window control; it touches a session only at spawn, so the runtime engine is DONT_ADD. But the underlying OPERATOR need — auto-attach the right saved instruction/skill bundle to a session based on WHERE it is spawned — is real, fits a spawn console, and maps directly onto primitives Shepherd already has queued. It is not ADD because you cannot build it close to as-is (the as-is is a runtime context engine); it is not DONT_ADD because the folder-scoping reshape lands cleanly on L1's folder-keyed profiles. Hence ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (folder->profile auto-attach) with an L6 connection (a scoped activation predicate, scope=projectKey). Effort S because it is a single pure matcher plus a pre-fill hook on the New-Session modal, riding L1's resolver and validator rather than introducing new storage or effects.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add to L1 (Agent Profiles): give a saved profile an optional folder-glob field (extend the existing exact-path `folder` to a glob list, e.g. folderGlobs:["~/work/frontend/**"], with [] meaning never-auto-attach and a bad glob failing open). Add a pure cc-core matcher core.profilesForFolder(profiles, chosenDir) (sibling to fuzzyFilter / the folder-scan path) that, when the New-Session modal's directory is chosen, suggests/pre-selects the matching profile(s) so their skills[]/knowledge[]/seedPrompt auto-fill the --add-dir / --append-system-prompt / --mcp-config set via the existing spawnFlags/spawnSpec path (cc-core.lua:3001/3061). The same glob field can key on provider too (auto-attach when the picked provider matches). Connect to L6: expose the matcher as a scope predicate (scope=projectKey/folder) so L6's rule registry can reuse it. Purely a spawn-time pre-fill, off by default, no runtime engine — honoring "Shepherd emits flags/files; native Claude Code owns execution."
- **Skeptic:** REFUTE fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. I tried each downgrade-to-DONT_ADD angle against the actual code and none lands. (1) Not the executor mismatch. The cline feature's LITERAL core — per-turn context-window injection keyed to the live working set as the agent reads/edits files mid-loop — is correctly carved off as DONT_ADD (Shepherd has no agent loop, no context window, touches a session only at spawn). But the reshape the judge proposes keys purely on chosenDir (the directory picked in the New-Session modal) at spawn time. That is a pure string-vs-glob match, never reads the working set, never injects mid-turn. It rides the existing spawn path I verified: spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061) / spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) emit native flags, and L1's documented wiring already threads skills/knowledge/MCP into --add-dir / --append-system-prompt / --mcp-config (todos.md:259-262, 284-286). So it needs no runtime engine, edits nothing, snapshots nothing. (2) Not redundant. Shepherd's preset already carries an EXACT-path `folder` field with presetForProject(state, folder) doing an exact-match last-used lookup (cc-core.lua:3644, 3720) — but there is NO glob/multi-pattern matcher and NO auto-pre-fill of a profile when a dir is chosen. The reshape GENERALIZES that exact-match primitive into folderGlobs[] (with [] = never auto-attach, bad glob failing open), which is a clean extension, not a duplicate of the tile-grid, presets, providers, gate, or live-model switch. (3) Not a server/multi-user surface and not a library/SDK detail — it is an operator-facing pre-fill on the New-Session modal. (4) Right altitude for ADAPT (not ADD, not DONT_ADD). Not ADD: you cannot build it close to as-is, since the as-is is a runtime context engine. Not DONT_ADD: the operator need (auto-attach the right saved instruction/skill bundle based on WHERE a session is spawned) genuinely improves a local spawn console and maps directly onto L1. Effort S: a single pure matcher core.profilesForFolder (sibling to fuzzyFilter cc-core.lua:3502 / presetForProject) plus one modal pre-fill hook, reusing L1's config-by-ref resolver + pre-flight validator, with an L6 connection (expose the matcher as a scope=projectKey activation predicate, cc-rules.json trigger.scope, todos.md:549-550). Off by default, spawn-time only — honors 'Shepherd emits flags/files; native Claude Code owns execution.' The judge's Shepherd mapping is accurate and I cannot refute it.

#### Multi-format / cross-tool rule ingestion (Cursor / Windsurf / AGENTS.md)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is pure read-only filesystem enumeration + UX surfacing + spawn-flag emission, which is squarely what Shepherd already does and what the cline mismatch explicitly carves OUT as transferable. There is no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing, no snapshotting, no server — Shepherd just scans a folder for rule files (.cursorrules, .cursor/rules/*.mdc, .windsurfrules, AGENTS.md, plus ~/.agents/AGENTS.md), parses frontmatter, and renders a card with toggles. That is mechanically identical to L1's already-specced read-mostly skills enumerator (FX.listSkills globbing ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md) and L1's knowledge[] -> --add-dir / small-ref --append-system-prompt threading. It directly serves the user's #1 stated interest ("pre-configured agents/skills/knowledge saved into a directory, carried over"): a team migrating from Cursor/Windsurf keeps its rule investment, and Shepherd can attach those files to a spawn via --add-dir / --append-system-prompt without reimplementing anything Claude Code owns. The "recognize an existing AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md in a target folder and offer to attach/seed at spawn" half is an especially clean fit for the existing New Session modal preflight + folder browser. AGENTS.md is a genuine cross-tool standard, so this is low-risk, durable surface area, not a vendor quirk.
- **Against:** A literal copy of cline's design overstates what Shepherd can honestly do. In cline the per-file "toggle" actually gates what the in-process executor loads into its own context each turn — Shepherd has no such loop, and CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md auto-load is owned by Claude Code itself, invisible to and uncontrollable by Shepherd on a live session. So a Shepherd "toggle" can only mean "include this file in the NEXT spawn's flags / picker scope," never "turn a rule on/off for a running session" — selling it as the latter would be a lie (the same caveat L1 already flags for skill toggles). There is also redundancy risk: native Claude Code already auto-loads CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md at the project root with no help from Shepherd, so for the common case the feature adds a panel that surfaces files Claude Code would have read anyway. The genuinely additive slice is narrow — cross-tool formats (.cursorrules/.mdc/.windsurfrules) that Claude Code does NOT auto-read, plus the explicit seed-at-spawn. Parsing the .mdc Cursor format (YAML frontmatter with globs/alwaysApply semantics) is also tool-specific plumbing whose nuances don't map to Claude Code's loading model, so faithfully "honoring" those semantics is partly impossible and shouldn't be promised.
- **Ruling:** The cline mismatch (no executor / no tool exec / no workspace edit / no server) does NOT bite here: the feature's whole value is detecting and surfacing rule files on the LOCAL filesystem and optionally threading them into a spawn — read-only enumeration + UX + flag emission, exactly Shepherd's lane and exactly the operator-config pattern the brief says CAN transfer. So it is not DONT_ADD. But it is not a clean ADD-as-is either, for two reasons that force a reshape: (1) the "per-file toggle" cannot mean live on/off — Claude Code owns native rule auto-load — so it must be reframed as picker/next-spawn scope, the identical honest framing L1 already uses for its skill toggles; and (2) it is not a standalone system — it is the same read-mostly enumerator + knowledge[]/--add-dir/--append-system-prompt machinery L1 already specs for skills and knowledge files, just widened to additional file formats and a Rules card. Hence ADAPT reinforcing L1: generalize L1's FX.listSkills/knowledge enumerator into a "rule/knowledge sources" reader that also recognizes cross-tool formats, attachable per agent-profile and offered at New-Session preflight. Scope the additive value to formats Claude Code does NOT already auto-read (cursor/windsurf) plus the seed-at-spawn flow, and present native CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md as "Claude Code loads this automatically" info rather than a Shepherd-controlled toggle. Effort is S because the enumerator/card/spawn-flag scaffolding is already designed under L1 — this is added file globs, a frontmatter parser branch, and one more card, all pure cc-core + one FX read.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's read-mostly enumerator. Generalize the planned FX.listSkills into FX.listRuleSources (pure cc-core parse, one FX file read) that globs, per target folder: AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, .cursorrules, .cursor/rules/*.mdc, .windsurfrules, plus ~/.agents/AGENTS.md and ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md; parse YAML/markdown frontmatter; return {path, format, name, description, scope(project|global), nativeAutoload(bool)}. Render a 'Rules / knowledge sources' card (sibling to L1's skills card) in the New Session modal + detail panel. Native files (CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md at root) show an honest 'Claude Code loads this automatically' badge and are NOT toggleable for live sessions. Cross-tool files (.cursorrules/.mdc/.windsurfrules) get a picker-scope toggle that, when on, adds the file to the spawn via L1's existing knowledge[] -> --add-dir / inline --append-system-prompt resolver (cc-core.lua spawnFlags ~3001 / spawnSpec ~3061), behind spawn.live (dry-run default). The 'attach/seed from existing AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md' becomes a New-Session preflight step: detect the file in the chosen folder (reuse the folder browser FX.listDirs) and offer 'attach as knowledge' / 'seed agent profile from it'. Reuse L1's safe-path hardening ([A-Za-z0-9._-], reject paths escaping root) and the schema-drift keep-valid/drop-bad+report pattern for malformed .mdc.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute ADAPT on every downgrade vector and could not. (1) No executor/workspace-edit bite: the feature is read-only filesystem enumeration of rule files (.cursorrules, .cursor/rules/*.mdc, .windsurfrules, AGENTS.md) + frontmatter parse + spawn-flag emission — exactly Shepherd's lane (it already reads/parses local status JSON; L1 already specs FX.listSkills as a read-mostly enumerator at cc-core.lua ~267). No agent loop, no tool exec, no file editing. (2) Not a server/multi-user surface — all local single-user reads. (3) Not redundant: Shepherd has no rule-source enumeration today, and I confirmed spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) currently emits only --permission-mode/--remote-control; the --add-dir/--append-system-prompt knowledge threading is planned under L1, not yet shipped. (4) Why it is ADAPT not ADD: it is not a clean standalone add — it IS L1's planned enumerator (generalize FX.listSkills → FX.listRuleSources), L1's knowledge[]→--add-dir/--append-system-prompt resolver, L1's safe-path hardening ([A-Za-z0-9._-], confirmed cc-core.lua:1950/2448/2796), and L1's keep-valid/drop-bad schema-drift pattern (presetList per-entry skip, cc-core.lua:3658), merely widened to more file formats + one Rules card + a seed-at-spawn preflight. (5) The per-file toggle must be reshaped: Claude Code natively auto-loads CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md, so native files get an honest info badge and only cross-tool files (cursor/windsurf, which CC does NOT auto-read) get a picker/next-spawn-scope toggle — the identical honest framing L1 already uses for skill toggles (cc-core.lua:320-322). That mandatory reshape + the fact it reinforces L1 rather than standing alone is precisely ADAPT, not ADD and not DONT_ADD. Effort S, all pure cc-core + one FX read. The ruling stands.

#### Skills: on-demand SKILL.md instruction packs with progressive loading  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the user's #1 stated priority almost verbatim — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." Skills live as plain directories at ~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md with YAML frontmatter (name/description), which is exactly the kind of read-mostly local-filesystem artifact Shepherd already enumerates (it reads ~/.claude/cc-status/*.json the same way). A skills CARD — a pure enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md parsing frontmatter into a scannable list of {name, description, /<name> command} — fits Shepherd's "watch the local filesystem, surface it, attach it at spawn" model perfectly. cline's name==dir validation and <=1024-char description matching are trivial pure-Lua checks. The skills then attach to a saved agent profile's skills[] and feed slash-command autocomplete in the nudge/spawn input. None of this requires running an agent.
- **Against:** The feature's headline mechanic — progressive/lazy loading (only the ~100-token name+description loads at startup; the full <5k body loads only when the request matches the description; the use_skill tool fires; bundled docs/scripts load lazily) — is purely an in-process agent-loop context-budget optimization. Shepherd runs no agent loop and owns no context window, so it has nothing to progressively load and no use_skill tool to dispatch; native Claude Code already does all skill triggering/loading inside the session. The "Enable Skills" experimental Settings toggle is likewise an executor concern. Taken literally, the differentiated value of THIS feature (the loading strategy, not the directory format) lands squarely in the cline mismatch — it presupposes the executor Shepherd structurally is not.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature: the directory/SKILL.md FORMAT and a card/picker/attach surface transfer; the progressive-loading RUNTIME does not. The transferable half is not just compatible with Shepherd — it is already fully specced inside L1 (Agent Profiles registry), which the user mined precisely to satisfy this stated priority. todos.md L1 already calls for: a pure enumerator over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md parsing frontmatter into a card view via a new FX.listSkills file read, keeping authoring as "drop a folder in ~/.claude/skills" with no DB (lines 234, 266-267); a dual-shape enumerator handling both flat skills/*.md and directory SKILL.md + references/, deriving a /<name> command and surfacing shape+description (307-310); per-profile skill toggle rows that add/remove from a profile's skills[] (320-322); slash-command autocomplete of skill names in the spawn/nudge input (311-313); a per-session "skills loaded" inspector that expands each row to its SKILL.md frontmatter, honestly labeled "what Shepherd attached at spawn" with an off-by-default skill_triggered ledger event (315-319); and skills attached at spawn via --append-system-prompt (256, 261). cline's specifics map cleanly: name==dir validation → a check in the L1 pure validator family (290-294); description<=1024 → field-length validation; the progressive-loading runtime → DROP, it belongs to native Claude Code which owns skill execution. So the verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L1, not ADD (no new system — it widens an existing queued primitive) and not DONT_ADD (the local directory + card + attach pattern is squarely in scope and explicitly wanted). This corroborates L1's skills enumerator from a fourth source (cline) after LiteLLM/crewAI/OpenHands; the main net-new nuance worth folding in is cline's explicit name==dir + description-length validation rules for the L1 validator and confirmation that the SKILL.md frontmatter key is description (matching the existing spec).
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's skills enumerator, not as a new feature. (1) FX.listSkills already specced (todos.md:266-267, 307-310) — confirm it parses the SKILL.md `description` frontmatter cline uses for matching and surfaces it on the card. (2) Add cline's two validation rules to L1's pure validator family (cc-core, sibling to presetList/templateList ~3590-3722): name MUST equal the containing directory name, and description MUST be <=1024 chars — flag mismatches in the card and refuse to attach an invalid skill, reusing the schema-drift "keep valid, drop bad, return dropped names" path (323-326). (3) Attach selected skills to a profile's skills[] via --append-system-prompt at spawn (256, 261); render the skills card with per-profile toggle rows (320-322) and feed /<name> into the slash-command autocomplete (311-313). (4) DROP the progressive-loading runtime, the use_skill tool, and the experimental "Enable Skills" toggle — native Claude Code owns skill triggering inside the session; Shepherd only enumerates the directory, validates, surfaces, and attaches flags. The per-session "skills loaded" inspector (315-319) stays the honest read-only view: "what Shepherd attached at spawn," not what the session loaded on demand.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not. Verified against the actual repo: L1 (todos.md:247-334) already specs the entire transferable half of cline's Skills feature. The frontmatter key cline matches on is `description` — confirmed at todos.md:235 ("Skills live at ~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md (frontmatter name/display_title/description)") and the FX.listSkills enumerator parsing it into a card is at 266-267/307-310; per-profile skill toggle rows into skills[] (320-322); slash-command autocomplete of skill names (311-313); the per-session "skills loaded" inspector with off-by-default skill_triggered ledger event (315-319); attach via --append-system-prompt at spawn (261). FX.listSkills/use_skill do NOT yet exist in code (grep empty), confirming L1 is queued not shipped — so this is a legitimate corroboration, not redundancy. Refutation angle (a) DONT_ADD as executor-only: fails. The progressive-loading RUNTIME (lazy <5k-token body load, use_skill trigger-matching, the experimental "Enable Skills" toggle) IS in-process executor behavior and the judge correctly DROPPED it — native Claude Code owns skill triggering inside the session; Shepherd runs no agent loop. But the SKILL.md directory format + enumerate/validate/surface/attach-as-flag is pure local filesystem read + spawn-flag emission, exactly Shepherd's model (no server, no file edits, no tool execution). So the feature is not WHOLLY executor-only; DONT_ADD overshoots. Refutation angle (b) net-new ADD: fails harder. The transferable half is not merely compatible — it is already fully specced inside L1, the registry the user mined to satisfy his #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents... same with skills and MCPs"). cline's only genuinely net-new contributions are two validation rules: name MUST equal the containing directory name, and description MUST be <=1024 chars. The judge folds both into L1's pure validator family (presetList/templateList at cc-core.lua ~3590-3722 — verified present, with the exact "validate + cap + replace-in-place, keep-valid/drop-bad" pattern at 3658, reused by the schema-drift path at todos.md:323-326). That is a micro-enrichment of an existing queued primitive, not a new system, so ADD is unwarranted. Net: ADAPT (high), reinforces L1, holds. Fold-in: (1) confirm FX.listSkills parses the `description` frontmatter and surfaces it on the card (already in spec); (2) add cline's name==dir and description<=1024 rules to the L1 validator, flag mismatches and refuse to attach invalid skills via the keep-valid/drop-bad path; (3) attach selected skills to a profile's skills[] via --append-system-prompt at spawn, with per-profile toggle rows + /<name> autocomplete; (4) DROP the progressive-loading runtime, use_skill tool, and experimental "Enable Skills" toggle. The per-session "skills loaded" inspector stays the honest read-only "what Shepherd attached at spawn." This is a fourth corroborating source (after LiteLLM/crewAI/OpenHands) for L1's skills enumerator.

#### Skill/plugin/config discovery across layered scoped directories with precedence  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Shepherd's L1 (Agent Profiles registry) is built directly on the user's #1 stated priority — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." L1 already specs a `FX.listSkills` enumerator and a config-by-reference resolver, but the queued spec assumes a SINGLE skills root (`~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`). cline's contribution is the realistic correction that skills/plugins/agents actually live across MULTIPLE layered scopes — project-local (`.claude/skills`, `.agents/skills`) AND global (`~/.claude/skills`) — and must be deduped-by-name with a deterministic precedence (project > global). For Shepherd this matters concretely: when the operator picks a folder in the New-Session modal and then attaches skills to a profile, the available skills depend on that project's local `.claude/skills` plus the global root. A single-root enumerator would silently miss project-scoped skills and mis-render which version wins. Folding cline's reverse-iterate/last-wins/dedup + a "source" badge (project vs global) into `FX.listSkills` makes the picker card honest about what native Claude Code will actually load at runtime. This is pure deterministic merge logic (zero `hs.*`) — exactly the cc-core shape Shepherd favors, and it mirrors the precedence resolver Shepherd already has (`resolveGateTools`, `providerById`).
- **Against:** Most of cline's discovery machinery is RUNTIME LOADING that Shepherd structurally must not own. Native Claude Code is the executor: when a session launches it auto-discovers and loads skills/plugins/agents from its own scoped directories with its own precedence rules — Shepherd runs no agent loop and loads nothing. So the "enabled-by-default on discovery, each with a toggle" half of the candidate is a live-agent capability switch Shepherd cannot truthfully provide (L1 already flags that a picker-scope toggle "can't disable native auto-load on a live session"). The bundled/system folder and the org/remote-push scope (remote > global > project) are squarely the SERVER/multi-user surface Shepherd's hard scope forbids — no network server, no org distribution, no remote precedence. And because Claude Code itself already resolves all six locations correctly at spawn, Shepherd duplicating the full precedence ladder risks drifting out of sync with the executor's real behavior, presenting a confident-but-wrong "this wins" badge. The genuinely useful slice — enumerating the project root + global root for an attach/display picker — is small; the rest is either redundant with the native executor or out of scope.
- **Ruling:** The fit question resolves cleanly to ADAPT-into-L1, not a parallel system. Shepherd already has no skills enumeration in code (grep confirms zero references to `.claude/skills`/`listSkills`), and L1 is the queued home for exactly this — its OpenHands-corroborated "Dual-shape skills enumerator" and "Config-by-reference resolver" are the insertion points. cline's distinct, transferable idea is NARROW: discovery should be multi-scope (at minimum project-local + global) with deterministic dedup-by-name precedence and a source badge, so the operator-facing picker reflects what the executor will actually load. That is deterministic merge logic — Shepherd's bread and butter — and it directly improves the watch/steer/spawn console by preventing a single-root picker from lying about project-scoped skills. But three pieces are DONT_ADD residue and must be dropped on the way in: (1) per-skill enable/disable TOGGLES that purport to gate live loading (native Claude Code owns runtime load; Shepherd's toggle is picker-scope-only positive-selection, as L1 already states); (2) the bundled/system folder and org/remote-push scope with remote>global precedence (server/multi-user distribution — violates the hard scope); (3) reimplementing the full six-location ladder when the executor already resolves it — keep it to the two roots Shepherd can verify on local disk. Net: the operator pattern (layered scoped config dirs with precedence + provenance badge) is real and maps onto Shepherd's primitives, but only as a refinement of L1's existing enumerator/resolver, not as new infrastructure. Confidence high because the mechanism is pure logic that mirrors resolvers Shepherd already ships and the queued L1 spec explicitly anticipates this enumerator.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's `FX.listSkills` enumerator + config-by-reference resolver. Concretely: (a) extend the enumerator to scan TWO roots — the spawn folder's project-local `.claude/skills` (and `.agents/skills`) plus global `~/.claude/skills` — already aligned with L1's dual-shape (flat `*.md` + directory `SKILL.md`) globbing; (b) add a pure `core.mergeSkillsByName(projectList, globalList)` that reverse-iterates with project>global last-wins dedup (mirror `resolveGateTools`:2537 / `providerById`:2632 precedence shape, zero `hs.*`, unit-tested); (c) stamp each surfaced skill with a `source` badge (project|global) on L1's skills card and the per-session "skills loaded" inspector so the operator sees which copy wins; (d) reuse L1's `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` safe-path hardening so a project-local skill name can't resolve outside its root. DROP: the live enable/disable toggle as a loading gate (keep only L1's picker-scope positive-selection toggle), the bundled/system folder, and the org/remote-push scope with remote-first precedence (out of hard scope). Same merge logic generalizes to the L1 plugin and `cc-mcp.json` pickers if project `.mcp.json` is in play.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; the ADAPT-reinforces-L1 ruling survives skeptical scrutiny. (1) Not executor plumbing: Shepherd spawns sessions into a specific folder via `--directory` (cc-core.lua:3080), so native Claude Code auto-loads that folder's project-local `.claude/skills`. A global-only picker therefore actively misrepresents what the spawned session will load. Scanning the spawn folder's `.claude/skills` plus global `~/.claude/skills` is honestly verifiable by Shepherd on local disk (it knows the folder; it can stat the path) — operator-facing truth, not running the agent or executing tools. The judge correctly DROPPED the per-skill enable/disable loading toggle, which was the only part needing runtime load control. (2) Not redundant with L1: L1's current enumerator (todos.md:266-267) scans ONE root only (`~/.claude/skills`); the OpenHands 'dual-shape' enrichment (307-310) is two FILE SHAPES (flat *.md vs SKILL.md dir) within that one root, NOT two scope LOCATIONS — so the multi-scope dimension is genuinely additive, not duplicative. (3) Not server/multi-user: the bundled/system folder and org/remote-push (remote>global precedence) ARE distribution surface, and the judge correctly DROPPED them; the surviving project>global last-wins is pure local logic. It does not collapse to ADD because it is a refinement of L1's already-queued FX.listSkills enumerator + config-by-ref resolver, not standalone infrastructure. The merge mechanism mirrors primitives Shepherd already ships: last-wins precedence (resolveGateTools cc-core.lua:2537), name-keyed lookup (providerById:2632), the seen[] dedup-by-name idiom (366/2519/2811), provenance tagging (870), and [A-Za-z0-9._-] safe-path hardening (queueKey:1953, tempImagePath:2451). Confirmed zero existing skills enumeration in code (grep: 0 hits in cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua) and FX.listSkills does not yet exist — purely queued in L1. Verdict: ADAPT, reinforces L1, with the three judge-noted drops.

#### Slash-command palette / autocomplete (built-ins + workflows + skills + MCP prompts)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A "/"-typeahead in the spawn/nudge input is a high-value, low-cost UX win that maps cleanly onto what Shepherd already does. The operator already sends steering verbs into sessions (live /model, /effort, permission-mode switch, clear, compact); today they're scattered across buttons/selects. A palette that pops as you type "/" makes those discoverable and keyboard-driven in one place. Crucially, Shepherd already ships every primitive needed: the #n-suggest keyboard-nav (onPathKey, claude-dashboard.lua:4083), the #tpl-menu render (~3462), and a pure fuzzy ranker (core.fuzzyFilter). Once L1 lands the ~/.claude/skills enumerator and cc-agents.json registry, the same menu can surface skill names and saved-agent names as spawn-flag selectors — directly serving the user's #1 stated priority (pre-configured agents/skills/MCP saved in a directory, handed off by name). It is genuinely additive to steer/spawn ergonomics, not executor plumbing.
- **Against:** Taken as-is, the feature's substance is cline-executor-specific and does not transfer. Cline's palette surfaces (a) built-in commands that RUN the agent loop, (b) "every enabled workflow by filename" — a workflow runner Shepherd does not have, and (c) MCP server PROMPTS as /mcp:server:prompt that the executor invokes against a live MCP connection. Shepherd runs no agent loop, executes no workflows, and holds no live MCP session, so it cannot own or validate any of that command vocabulary; inventing one would be a fake executor surface. "First match wins / built-ins take precedence / live validation as you type" all presuppose a real executor command registry Shepherd lacks. The genuinely transferable slice (typeahead over Shepherd-known steering verbs + locally-discovered skill/agent NAMES) is small and is already explicitly written into L1, so adopting the cline feature wholesale would either duplicate L1 or smuggle in executor concepts that violate scope.
- **Ruling:** This exact item is already queued: todos.md:311-314 lists "Slash-command autocomplete in the spawn/nudge input: reuse the existing #n-suggest keyboard-nav (onPathKey claude-dashboard.lua:4083) + #tpl-menu render (~3462) over a /-typeahead of skill names + Shepherd-known commands (/model, /effort, clear, compact) — never invent an executor-side command list" — an OpenHands-corroborated enrichment under L1. The candidate is a fourth independent sighting of the same pattern, which strengthens L1 but does not justify a parallel system. Per the rubric, when a cline feature matches a queued Lx the verdict is ADAPT with reinforces set. The cline MISMATCH bites the bulk of the feature: built-ins that run the loop, the workflow runner, and live /mcp:server:prompt are all executor/MCP-runtime surfaces Shepherd structurally cannot host (no agent loop, no tool execution, no live MCP). What survives the reshaping is exactly L1's note: a menu over (1) Shepherd's real steering verbs (/model, /effort, permission-mode, clear, compact) and (2) NAMES of locally-discovered skills/agents/MCP records that resolve into spawn flags via L1's config-by-ref resolver — never a live command registry, and "validation" means name-resolves-against-registry, not executor-side validity. Confidence is high because the match is named verbatim in todos.md and the UI machinery already exists.
- **How (Shepherd):** Build under L1 as the slash-typeahead it already specifies. Front-end: when the spawn/nudge input value starts with "/", route keystrokes through the existing onPathKey (claude-dashboard.lua:4083) and render results into #tpl-menu (~3462). Source list = (a) static Shepherd steering verbs already wired to actions: /model, /effort, permission-mode (Plan/Accept-Edits/Default), clear, compact; (b) skill names from L1's FX.listSkills enumerator over ~/.claude/skills (dual-shape: SKILL.md + flat *.md, deriving /<name>); (c) saved-agent names from cc-agents.json (agentList) and MCP records from cc-mcp.json. Ranking via the existing pure core.fuzzyFilter. "Validation as you type" = name-resolves-against-registry using L1's config-by-reference resolver (the generalized resolveGateTools/providerById), surfacing the same structured unknown-name errors L1's pre-flight validator produces — explicitly NOT executor command validity. Selecting a verb runs its existing handler; selecting a skill/agent/MCP threads it into spawnFlags (--append-system-prompt / --agent / --mcp-config) at spawn. DROP: workflow-file runner, built-ins-precedence semantics, and /mcp:server:prompt live-prompt invocation (no executor / no live MCP).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; ADAPT/reinforces-L1 stands. The feature is named VERBATIM in the queue: todos.md:311-314 "Slash-command autocomplete in the spawn/nudge input: reuse the existing #n-suggest keyboard-nav (onPathKey claude-dashboard.lua:4083) + #tpl-menu render (~3462) over a /-typeahead of skill names + Shepherd-known commands (/model, /effort, clear, compact) — never invent an executor-side command list." I verified every cited primitive exists: onPathKey at claude-dashboard.lua:4083, #tpl-menu at 3463, #n-suggest at 3249/2872, and pure core.fuzzyFilter at cc-core.lua:3502. The steering verbs are real and already paste-based: claude-dashboard.lua:1290 shows Shepherd sends /clear /compact /effort as TEXT into the session where the extension's OWN autocomplete resolves them — Shepherd executes nothing, so a Shepherd-side typeahead over its known verbs + locally-discovered names is structurally native. I could NOT downgrade to DONT_ADD: a genuinely useful single-user-console slice survives the cline reshaping (a menu over Shepherd's real steering verbs + NAMES of locally-discovered skills/agents/MCP records that thread into spawn flags — the L1 enumerators listSkills/agentList/mcpList and cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json are queued, confirmed NOT yet built). I could NOT downgrade to ADD: it is not a standalone parallel system, it is reinforcement of an already-queued L1 sub-item reusing existing UI machinery. The cline MISMATCH correctly bites the BULK — built-ins that run the agent loop, the workflow runner (none queued in todos.md), and live /mcp:server:prompt invocation are executor/MCP-runtime surfaces Shepherd cannot host; those are correctly DROP. 'Validation as you type' must mean name-resolves-against-registry via L1's config-by-ref resolver, NOT executor command validity. This is a fourth corroborating sighting of L1's slash-typeahead, strengthening the queue item without justifying a new system. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1.

#### Per-mode model binding (different model for Plan vs Act)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The need is real and lands squarely in Shepherd's wheelhouse, not in the executor. "Strong reasoner to plan, cheap/fast model to act" is exactly the kind of per-session economics a watch/steer/spawn console should encode — and Shepherd already owns every primitive it needs: live `/model` switching (claude-dashboard.lua onModelChange ~4246, cc-core.lua modelSlash ~232-237), live permission-mode switching (the Shift+Tab `modeCycleSteps` cycle, cc-core.lua:3019, with a Plan/Accept-Edits/Default dropdown), providers (model+thinking selection), and `patchedStatus` to keep stored mode in sync. The candidate maps almost verbatim onto the queued L1 Agent Profiles schema, which already carries both `permMode` and `model`/`provider` — the only change is promoting the single `model` field to a per-mode binding (e.g. `modeModels = {plan=…, default=…, acceptEdits=…}`). When "off," cline copies one mode's config to the other — trivially the single-`model` profile we already planned. So this is a small, schema-level extension of an item already queued and already justified by the user's #1 stated interest (saved agents in a directory), not a new system.
- **Against:** cline's auto-swap works because the in-process executor owns the API client and can hot-swap provider+baseURL+model mid-task the instant the mode flips. Shepherd structurally cannot do the load-bearing half of that: only `/model` WITHIN the current backend goes live; changing the provider/base_url (the strong-vs-cheap-vendor case the feature is really pitched on — e.g. a reasoning model from vendor A to plan, a fast model from vendor B to act) requires a fresh session, explicitly documented at cc-core.lua ~162-164 and README:293. So a faithful "auto-swap on mode change" only covers the model-within-one-backend axis; the cross-provider axis would silently not take effect live, which is worse than not offering it. There's also a coupling smell: it welds two independent live controls (mode switch + model switch) that the operator can already trigger separately in two clicks, for marginal benefit on a single-user console. And it adds per-mode persistence/state to a control surface whose value is manual, human-in-the-loop steering.
- **Ruling:** The OPERATOR PATTERN — "this saved agent uses model X when planning and model Y when acting" — transfers cleanly and reinforces an already-queued item; that earns an ADD-leaning verdict. But the cline MISMATCH bites the live-auto-swap mechanic: Shepherd can hot-swap model only within a backend, never the provider/base_url that the feature's headline economics depend on. That gap is exactly why this is ADAPT, not ADD: keep the per-mode-model binding as profile config (L1), and have the existing mode-switch glue ALSO emit the matching `/model` when (and only when) the target model shares the session's current backend; when it doesn't, surface it as a relabel/respawn hint rather than pretending it took. Redundancy check: Shepherd already has mode switching and live /model as separate controls, but it has NO per-mode model memory and no profile-level binding — so this is a genuine extension, not a re-add. It is decidedly not DONT_ADD (no agent loop, tool execution, workspace editing, or server is required — it's pure config plus reusing two existing live controls).
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1: extend the Agent Profile schema's single `model` into an optional per-mode map keyed by the existing MODE_CYCLE values (`{plan, default, acceptEdits, …}`), defaulting to the flat `model` when absent (the "off → copy config" case is just the absent-map case). Resolve it in cc-core via the L1 config-by-ref resolver (extends `providerById`:2632). At mode-switch time, after `modeCycleSteps` lands and `patchedStatus` records the new mode, look up the mode's bound model and, IF it resolves to the SAME backend as the session's current provider/base_url (use `providerByModel`:2659 to check), emit the existing `/model` slash via the onModelChange path; if it would require a different provider/base_url, do NOT silently fail — show the documented "needs a fresh session" hint (reuse the respawn/`respawnSpec` affordance) or skip with a one-line note. Spawn-from-agent picks the model bound to the profile's initial `permMode`. No new live mechanism, no executor — pure schema + resolver + reuse of the mode-switch and /model glue.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not. Every architectural anchor the judge cites checks out in the codebase, and none of the DONT_ADD triggers (executor, server, workspace edit/snapshot) apply. OPERATOR PATTERN IS REAL AND LOCAL. "This saved agent uses model X when planning, model Y when acting" is pure config plus reuse of two controls Shepherd already owns. It needs no agent loop, no tool execution, no file/workspace editing or snapshotting, no server/users. It's a single-user console steering an already-running Claude Code session. Fit question = yes. NOT REDUNDANT, GENUINELY EXTENDS L1. Shepherd has mode switching (MODE_CYCLE={default,acceptEdits,plan,bypassPermissions,auto}, cc-core.lua:3013; set-mode via Shift+Tab) and live /model (modelCommand cc-core.lua:234, onModelChange in claude-dashboard.lua:4248) — but as SEPARATE controls. Grep confirms NO per-mode model memory anywhere, and L1's profile schema (todos.md:256) carries a single flat `model`. So this is a new extension, not a re-add. SCHEMA MAPPING IS CLEAN. Extending the flat `model` into an optional map keyed by MODE_CYCLE values, defaulting to flat `model` when absent, is well-formed — and the feature's 'off → copy config to the other mode' really is just the absent-map case. Resolution rides L1's config-by-ref resolver (extends providerById:2632), and the existing set-mode glue (claude-dashboard.lua:2457, which already does the optimistic patchStatus after a mode change) is the exact hook to look up the bound model and emit /model. THE ADAPT (not ADD) DOWNGRADE IS THE CORRECT CALL — this is why it can't be refuted UP to ADD either. The feature's headline economics ('cheap/fast model to act') depend on a LIVE auto-swap on mode toggle, but Shepherd's /model is explicitly within-backend only: cc-core.lua:162-164 and 232-233 document that a base_url/provider change needs a fresh session, not /model. providerByModel:2659 is base_url-aware and exists precisely to detect this. So same-backend pairs (Opus-plan / Haiku-act, both Anthropic) hot-swap live = the common case works; cross-provider pairs must degrade to the documented respawn hint (respawnSpec:2688) rather than silently failing. That partial-fit is exactly what makes it ADAPT rather than ADD, and it's an honest, codebase-grounded constraint — not a reason to DONT_ADD. No cline mismatch bites the rest: no executor, no checkpoints, no multi-user. Verdict stands: ADAPT, reinforcing L1.

#### MCP server runtime ops: per-server timeout, restart, OAuth authorization  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The user's #1 stated priority is "pre-configured MCPs saved into a directory," and L1 already queues a `cc-mcp.json` registry of named server records `{id, label, transport, command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv}` that Shepherd emits as `--mcp-config` files at spawn. One slice of this candidate — a per-server request timeout (seconds) for slow servers — is pure declarative config that drops cleanly into that registry as a `timeoutSec` field and into the mcp-config-file builder, with a pure validator and a picker widget. It is exactly the kind of MCP management/config knob that transfers: no agent loop, no live connection, no secrets — just a number written into a config file the native CLI consumes. cline proves operators want to tune slow servers without hand-editing JSON, and Shepherd's New-Session modal is the right home for it. This nudges L1's MCP registry from "which servers" toward "how each server behaves," which is squarely a fleet-convenience config layer Shepherd can own.
- **Against:** Two of the three parts are textbook cline-executor/secrets/server violations Shepherd structurally cannot host. "Restart an unresponsive server connection" operates on a LIVE MCP connection held inside a running agent process — Shepherd runs no agent loop and holds no MCP connections; native Claude Code (the in-editor `/mcp` command) owns and restarts them. The browser OAuth flow is worse: a local callback listener IS a network server, and PKCE/dynamic-client-registration token acquisition + persistence with tracked statuses (configured/pending/error/last-authenticated) + clear/re-auth IS exactly the secrets storage Shepherd's context.md forbids ("NO network server, NO secrets storage; it resolves $ENV via the spawned shell"). The repo's own todos.md line 439 already explicitly rules: "DROP the webhook server, signature verification, OAuth ... the spawned session owns that." Native Claude Code runs the MCP OAuth handshake itself. Even the timeout, while real, isn't worth a standalone feature — it's a one-field addition that should ride L1, not stand alone. The candidate's center of gravity (live-connection restart + OAuth secrets/server) is precisely what makes it DONT_ADD territory.
- **Ruling:** The candidate bundles three parts of very different fit. (1) Per-server timeout = legitimate declarative MCP config; it fits Shepherd's "emit flags/files, let native CLI execute" model and reinforces the already-queued L1 `cc-mcp.json` registry. (2) Restart-a-live-connection = the cline-executor mismatch: Shepherd holds no MCP connections; native Claude Code's `/mcp` owns the restart, so there is nothing for Shepherd to build beyond optionally steering `/mcp` via keystroke (not a feature). (3) Browser OAuth with PKCE/DCR/callback listener/token persistence = a direct violation of Shepherd's two hardest limits (no network server, no secrets storage) and is already explicitly DROPPED in todos.md:439; native Claude Code performs the MCP OAuth handshake. Because only the small timeout slice transfers and it maps onto an existing queued item rather than a new system, the honest verdict is ADAPT(reinforces L1) — fold `timeoutSec` into the `cc-mcp.json` schema/validator/builder and explicitly drop restart and OAuth. Confidence is high: the scope conflict is stated verbatim in the repo's own context.md and todos.md.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold ONLY the per-server timeout into L1's already-queued `cc-mcp.json` registry: add an optional `timeoutSec` field to the record schema `{id, label, transport, command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv}` (todos.md ~263), include it in L1's pure validator (known-fields allowlist) and the mcp-config-file builder so it lands in the `--mcp-config` file at spawn (spawnFlags, cc-core.lua:3001), and expose it as a number input in the New-Session modal MCP picker. Explicitly DROP: (a) live-connection restart — steer it via an existing `/mcp` keystroke nudge if ever wanted, but build nothing (native Claude Code owns the connection); (b) the entire OAuth flow — no callback listener (would be a network server), no PKCE/DCR token acquisition or persistence, no configured/pending/error/last-authenticated status store (would be secrets storage). For server auth, reuse the existing NAME-only pattern: `authTokenEnv` storing the env-var name, expanded by the spawned login shell (loginShellWrap), never the value.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; ADAPT(reinforces L1) holds. The repo confirms the judge's three-part split verbatim. (1) RESTART a live MCP connection is the classic cline-executor mismatch — Shepherd holds zero MCP connections; spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) only emits flags into the --mcp-config file at spawn time, and native Claude Code's /mcp owns the live connection. Correctly DROP (steer via /mcp keystroke at most; build nothing). (2) The browser OAuth flow (PKCE/DCR/local callback listener/token persistence/configured-pending-error-last-authenticated status store) violates BOTH hard limits stated in context.md:13 ("no network, no multi-user, no secrets") — a callback listener IS a network server, the status store IS secrets storage — and is already explicitly DROPPED in todos.md:439 ("DROP the webhook server, signature verification, OAuth..."). Correctly DROP. So the candidate is two-thirds DONT_ADD-shaped, and I tried to drive the whole thing to DONT_ADD. But it cannot go all the way down because one genuine, non-redundant slice survives: per-server timeoutSec is a purely DECLARATIVE config field. It fits Shepherd's emit-flags-let-native-CLI-execute model exactly, lands in the --mcp-config file produced by L1's already-queued 'Pure validator + mcp-config-file builder' (todos.md:264-265), and is NOT already present — the queued cc-mcp.json record schema {id,label,transport,command,args,url,allowedTools,authTokenEnv} (todos.md:263) has no timeout field, so this is additive, not redundant with anything Shepherd already has or has queued. A real transferable slice that folds onto an existing queued item (rather than spawning a parallel system) is the textbook ADAPT-reinforces-L1 case. Mapping: add optional timeoutSec (number) to the cc-mcp.json record schema, gate it through L1's known-fields-allowlist validator and the mcp-config-file builder so it serializes into the --mcp-config file at spawn, and expose it as a number input in the New-Session modal MCP picker. Keep restart and OAuth explicitly dropped. The refutation does not succeed; ADAPT stands.

#### Use & display MCP tools/resources/prompts (call/read/list + rich response render)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The user's #1 stated priority is "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." An operator wiring an MCP server onto an agent profile genuinely benefits from SEEING what that server exposes — which tools, which resources, which prompts — before attaching it and spawning. cline's McpToolRow/McpResourceRow/McpPromptRow is exactly the inventory UX that makes "attach this MCP to this agent" legible instead of a blind name+command entry. L1 already plans an MCP server registry (cc-mcp.json with allowedTools) and an MCP inventory badge derived from ~/.claude.json + project .mcp.json; rendering the declared tool/resource/prompt rows on the agent-profile card or a detail-panel MCP tab is the natural display layer on top of that registry, and it's pure-render work (mostly panel JS + one FX file read) that fits Shepherd's read-off-the-filesystem model.
- **Against:** The headline capability — call a tool, read a resource, fetch a prompt — IS the cline mismatch in its purest form: it requires Shepherd to BE an MCP client, open a stdio/sse/http transport to the server, and run the tools/list-resources/list-prompts/list handshake plus actual call/read execution. That is an agent loop and tool execution, which Shepherd structurally does not host (the MCP client is Claude Code, not Shepherd). The candidate even concedes "Calling is agent execution." The rich-response display modes (image/link/markdown/plain previews of tool OUTPUT, LinkPreview/ImagePreview) are a byproduct of having executed the tool and have no source in Shepherd, which never holds tool output. And a LIVE tools/list display still needs a transport/handshake — i.e., turning Shepherd into a mini MCP client — which crosses the no-executor line. Only a STATIC, config-declared view (allowedTools from the registry, not a live capability fetch) is safe, which is a thinner thing than what the candidate ships.
- **Ruling:** The candidate cleanly bisects. The call/read/fetch half and the rich-output-render half are pure cline-executor surface (MCP client + tool output) that Shepherd cannot and should not host — DONT_ADD on their own. The candidate explicitly isolates the transferable piece: the read-only DISPLAY of which tools/resources/prompts a server exposes on a detail panel. That underlying NEED — let the operator inspect what an MCP server brings before attaching it to an agent — is real and ALREADY QUEUED under L1 (MCP server registry cc-mcp.json + MCP inventory badge, both pure derivations off local config files). So this is not a parallel system; it's the display layer of L1's registry, weighted favorably by the user's explicit "saved MCPs in a directory" priority. The crucial reshape: render the DECLARED config (allowedTools + enabled/disabled from cc-mcp.json / ~/.claude.json / project .mcp.json), NOT a live tools/list handshake — a live handshake would make Shepherd an MCP client and cross the no-executor line. Drop entirely: the call/read/getPrompt execution path and all rich-output display modes (LinkPreview/ImagePreview/markdown of tool results), since Shepherd never possesses tool output. Hence ADAPT, reinforces L1, high confidence.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's MCP server registry. On the New-session / agent-profile modal MCP picker and on a detail-panel MCP row (L5 detail-panel tabs), render per-server rows showing the DECLARED tools/resources/prompts the server is configured to expose — sourced from cc-mcp.json records (the planned {id,label,transport,command,args,url,allowedTools,authTokenEnv} schema) plus a pure derivation over ~/.claude.json + project .mcp.json (the L1 MCP inventory badge: configured servers + enabled/disabled). Reuse the read-mostly skills enumerator pattern (FX.listSkills → pure parse → card view) for an FX read of the MCP config files into pure cc-core rows. esc() every server/tool name at the render sink. Off by default, lazy-loaded on selection (never the 1s tick). Explicitly DROP: McpHub callTool/readResource/getPrompt, the live fetchToolsList/fetchResourcesList/fetchPromptsList handshake (no MCP client in Shepherd), and McpDisplayModeDropdown/McpResponseDisplay/LinkPreview/ImagePreview (Shepherd holds no tool output).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. The feature cleanly bisects and the judge handled both halves right. (1) Executor half — call tool / readResource / getPrompt + the rich-output render modes (LinkPreview/ImagePreview/markdown of tool results) — is pure cline MCP-client + tool-output surface; Shepherd runs no agent loop and never possesses tool output, so DONT_ADD, which the judge explicitly drops. (2) The transferable sliver (read-only DISPLAY of which tools/resources/prompts a server EXPOSES) is real AND locally sourceable WITHOUT becoming an MCP client: verified ~/.claude.json exists (49KB) with a top-level mcpServers plus 19 projects carrying per-project mcpServers configs, so the display renders DECLARED config via a pure file read — no live tools/list handshake (which would cross the no-executor line). (3) Not a parallel system: L1 in todos.md ALREADY declares the cc-mcp.json registry with the exact {id,label,transport,command,args,url,allowedTools,authTokenEnv} schema the judge cites (todos.md:263-265), the MCP inventory badge off ~/.claude.json + project .mcp.json (270-274), the modal MCP picker + the per-session skills/mcp inspector on the detail panel (315-317), and the read-mostly skills enumerator pattern it reuses (FX.listSkills → pure parse → card; not yet built — no listSkills in cc-core, confirming L1 is genuinely queued). (4) Cited reuse primitives all exist: providerById (cc-core.lua:2632), presetList/presetPush (3658/3673, the registry pattern), resolveGateTools (2537, the resolver), spawnFlags/spawnSpec (3001/3061), and esc() at the dashboard render sink. ADD is wrong (it's L1's display layer, not net-new); DONT_ADD is wrong (the read-only declared-config inventory is a legitimate local-console need that doesn't require the executor and matches the user's #1 'saved MCPs in a directory' priority). ADAPT reinforcing L1 is correct.

#### Plugin system: single-file/dir extensions registering tools/commands/rules/skills/providers/hooks  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The user's #1 stated priority is "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs" — and cline's plugin system is the strongest prior art for a directory of attachable extensions (tools/commands/rules/skills/providers/hooks bundled and applied per agent). The local `claude` CLI exists on this Mac (~/.local/bin/claude) and natively supports `--plugin-dir` and the `claude plugin install` subcommand, so Shepherd CAN make plugins first-class: let a saved agent profile reference a `plugins[]` list and emit the launch flags at spawn. That turns "drop a plugin folder, then attach it to the agent you spawn" into a real fleet-convenience workflow — exactly the saved-into-a-directory model the user wants. Plugins also unify several profile sub-capabilities (skills, providers, rules) under one referenceable bundle, which is genuinely useful UX.
- **Against:** The candidate's value as designed is the in-process plugin RUNTIME: an AgentPlugin's `setup(api, ctx)` calls `api.registerTool/registerCommand/registerHook`, capabilities gate the register surface, and contributions accumulate in a ContributionRegistry that is "exposed to the agent loop." The feature description states the whole purpose outright — "inject behavior INTO the running agent loop — executor territory; native runtimes own this via --plugin-dir." Shepherd has no agent loop, no tool executor, no message builders, no hook dispatch into a running session — so there is literally nothing for `register*`, ContributionRegistry, or AgentExtensionApi to plug into. Building cline's plugin loader/registry/capability-gating in Shepherd would be reimplementing executor machinery the native Claude Code runtime already owns and that Shepherd structurally cannot host. The plugin loader, sandbox, and provider/messageBuilder/hook contribution surfaces are all DONT_ADD.
- **Ruling:** This is the cline mismatch in its purest form: a plugin system whose explicit purpose is to register tools/commands/hooks/providers into a running agent loop via a ContributionRegistry. Shepherd runs no loop, executes no tools, dispatches no hooks — so the runtime half (plugin-loader, AgentExtensionApi, capability gating, contribution accumulation, the example weather-metrics/env-blocker/openrouter-provider plugins) is DONT_ADD; native Claude Code owns plugin execution via --plugin-dir / `claude plugin install`. But the OPERATOR need — a directory of saved extensions you attach to the agents you spawn — is real, matches the user's stated #1 priority, and is ALREADY queued as L1 (Agent Profiles registry), whose profile schema includes a `plugins[]` field applied via `claude plugin install` on spawn (todos.md:257, 268) and threaded through spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) as launch flags, gated by spawn.live, resolved by the config-by-ref resolver with safe-path hardening (todos.md:296, 302). So the verdict is ADAPT/L1: Shepherd does not build cline's plugin API; it references plugins by name in an agent profile and emits the native flag. I rule ADAPT rather than DONT_ADD because the launch-flag wiring is feasible and feature-positive, not because Shepherd should host the registry.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 Agent Profiles registry as the already-specified `plugins[]` field on a profile (cc-agents.json), not a new system. On "Spawn from agent": the config-by-ref resolver dereferences profile.plugins[] (single-sourced with provider/policyBundle/skills/mcpServers resolution, generalizing providerById:2632 / resolveGateTools:2537), pre-flight validation refuses spawn on bad cross-refs, safe-path hardening validates each plugin name against [A-Za-z0-9._-] before any FX write, then spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061)/spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) emit `--plugin-dir <dir>` and/or run `claude plugin install <name>` (gated by spawn.live, degrade gracefully if the CLI/subcommand is absent — todos.md:268). Optional off-by-default plugins-inventory derivation reading ~/.claude.json (sibling to the L1 MCP inventory badge). Explicitly DO NOT port: plugin-loader.ts, ContributionRegistry, AgentExtensionApi/register* methods, capability gating, messageBuilders/automationEvents/hooks dispatch, or the example provider/tool plugins — those need the agent loop Shepherd lacks.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — the ADAPT/reinforces-L1 ruling is correct. The FEATURE as described is the cline mismatch in its purest form: a plugin = a JS/TS module exporting an AgentPlugin whose setup(api,ctx) calls api.register* (capability-gated) to push tools/commands/rules/skills/providers/messageBuilders/automationEvents/hooks into a ContributionRegistry "exposed to the agent loop." Shepherd runs NO agent loop, executes no tools, dispatches no hooks — verified: grep for "plugin" across cc-core.lua/claude-dashboard.lua/cc-approve.sh returns zero hits, and the architecture edits nothing/runs nothing. So the runtime half (plugin-loader, AgentExtensionApi, register* methods, capability gating, ContributionRegistry accumulation, messageBuilders/automationEvents/hooks dispatch, the example provider/tool plugins) is correctly DONT_ADD — native Claude Code owns it via --plugin-dir / `claude plugin install` (CLI confirmed present and supporting these, todos.md:229-231). The only residue that transfers is the OPERATOR pattern: a directory of saved extensions referenced BY NAME and attached to spawned agents via the native flag. I tried to argue this is either (a) a standalone ADD or (b) DONT_ADD, and both fail.
