It is not a new system — it is ALREADY specified as the `plugins[]` field on the queued L1 Agent Profiles schema, with every wiring detail in place: profile schema includes plugins[] (todos.md:257 — verified verbatim); applied via `claude plugin install` on spawn, gated by spawn.live, degrade-gracefully if CLI absent (todos.md:268 — verified; spawn.live dry-run gate confirmed at cc-core.lua:3142); config-by-ref resolver dereferences plugins[] generalizing providerById/resolveGateTools (todos.md:296 — and both anchors are real: M.providerById at cc-core.lua:2632 looks up a record by name in a config list; M.resolveGateTools at :2537 is the precedence resolver); safe-path hardening validates plugin names against [A-Za-z0-9._-] (todos.md:302 — verified); threaded through spawnFlags/spawnSpec (verified: M.spawnFlags at cc-core.lua:3001 emits flat launch flags like --permission-mode/--remote-control, M.spawnSpec at :3061 is the structured spawn intent — the exact, feasible insertion point for --plugin-dir); build order lists "plugins" (todos.md:334). It can't be DONT_ADD because the launch-flag wiring is genuinely feasible and feature-positive, matching the user's #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents... saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs"). Verdict holds: ADAPT, reinforces L1 — Shepherd references plugins by name in an agent profile and emits the native flag; it does NOT host cline's plugin API/registry.

#### Plugin install/uninstall from many source types + auto-discovery  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The operator need is real and is the user's explicitly stated #1 priority ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs"). Plugins are part of that registry: an operator wants to attach plugins to a saved agent and have them applied at spawn, and wants installed plugins discoverable from a directory so they show up in a picker. The native `claude` CLI on this Mac (~/.local/bin/claude v2.1.175) exposes `claude plugin install` and `--plugin-dir`, so Shepherd CAN emit the install/flag without owning the runtime. The auto-discovery half (enumerate the local plugin dirs, merge/dedupe, surface a list) is pure filesystem reading — exactly what Shepherd already does for skills (~/.claude/skills) and MCP config (~/.claude.json), and it stays on Shepherd's side of the line.
- **Against:** The core of this candidate is an executor-adjacent installer: it downloads/clones/npm-installs arbitrary executable code from five source types (npm package, git repo+ref, GitHub blob/raw, arbitrary https URL, official slug, local dir) and STAGES it for the AGENT RUNTIME to load and run. Shepherd hosts no agent runtime — native Claude Code owns plugin loading and execution. Reimplementing parsePluginSource + installNpmPackage/installGitPackage/installRemoteFile would be Shepherd duplicating `claude plugin install`, which already exists. The precedence-merge resolver (project>global>bundled) is a runtime-loader concern: it only matters when something actually loads the merged set, which Shepherd never does. Pulling and executing code from arbitrary https URLs also brushes against Shepherd's no-secrets, thin-layer posture. The marketplace/discover/registry side is already explicitly on L1's DROP list.
- **Ruling:** Split the candidate on the cline mismatch. The VALUE that fits Shepherd — name plugins on a saved agent, apply them at spawn, discover installed plugins from a directory for a picker — is already absorbed by L1, which lists per-profile `plugins[]` applied via `claude plugin install` on spawn (gated by spawn.live, degrade gracefully if the CLI is absent) at todos.md:268-269, and a config-by-reference resolver + safe-path hardening for plugin names at 296/302-303. The PLUMBING that does NOT fit — a five-source-type fetcher/cloner/npm-installer that stages executable code, plus a runtime precedence-merge loader — is delegated to the native CLI (which performs the actual fetch/clone/install) or is a runtime concern Shepherd never executes. The marketplace/registry endpoints are already declined on L1's DROP list (275-279). So this is not a new system: it enriches L1 with (1) an honest scope note that Shepherd emits `claude plugin install <source>` and `--plugin-dir` rather than implementing the installer, and (2) a read-only plugin-dir enumerator (sibling to FX.listSkills) feeding the profile's plugin picker. Verdict ADAPT, reinforcing L1; not ADD because Shepherd must not own the fetch-and-stage installer, and not DONT_ADD because the management/discovery/attach UX genuinely maps onto its primitives and serves the stated priority.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L1's existing `plugins[]` profile field and its spawn wiring. (1) On "Spawn from agent", the config-by-reference resolver (todos.md:295-298) dereferences plugins[] and spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001 M.spawnFlags / 3061 spawnSpec) emits either `--plugin-dir <local path>` or a pre-spawn `claude plugin install <slug|local-path>` step (gated by spawn.live default dry-run; degrade if the subcommand is absent) — Shepherd delegates the actual npm/git/https fetch to the native CLI, never reimplements parsePluginSource. (2) Add a read-only `FX.listPlugins` enumerator (sibling to L1's FX.listSkills, todos.md:266-267) that globs the local plugin dirs (~/.claude plugin dirs / project .claude) and feeds a plugin picker in the New-Session modal — that is the auto-discovery half, reshaped as filesystem reading, not a runtime precedence-loader. (3) Reuse L1's safe-path hardening (todos.md:302-303): plugin name/path resolvers validate against [A-Za-z0-9._-] and reject anything resolving outside its root before any FX write. Keep on L1's DROP list: the npm/git/arbitrary-https fetch-and-stage installer, the official-marketplace/discover/registry workflow, and the project>global>bundled runtime merge resolver.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; the ADAPT-reinforces-L1 ruling is correct. I tried both downgrade paths and neither lands. DONT_ADD (pure executor/plumbing) fails: the feature has two separable halves. Half one — the five-source-type fetcher/cloner/npm-installer that "downloads/clones/npm-installs executable code and stages it for the agent runtime," plus the project>global>bundled runtime precedence-merge loader — IS pure cline-executor plumbing Shepherd must never own, and the judge correctly KEEPS it on L1's DROP list (todos.md:233-234 "does NOT reimplement MCP/skills/plugins"; the marketplace/registry endpoints already dropped at 275-279). But half two — name plugins on a saved agent, apply them at spawn, discover installed plugins from a directory for a picker — genuinely maps onto Shepherd primitives, so the whole feature is not DONT_ADD. Verified the mapping: L1 already has plugins[] as a profile field (todos.md:257), applies it via `claude plugin install` on spawn gated by spawn.live degrading if the CLI is absent (268), threads `--plugin-dir` through M.spawnFlags (262; cc-core.lua:3001/3061 confirmed real), dereferences plugins[] via the config-by-ref resolver (296), and hardens plugin name/path against [A-Za-z0-9._-] before any FX write (302-303). Shepherd delegates the actual npm/git/https fetch to the native `claude` CLI (present at ~/.local/bin/claude v2.1.175 per todos.md:230-234) and never reimplements parsePluginSource. A feature is judged on its operator pattern, not the sandbox plumbing it ships in — and the attach/discover pattern transfers. ADD (new system) fails: nothing here is a new system. Every mappable piece is an extension of L1 (the plugins[] field, spawn wiring, resolver, safe-path hardening all pre-exist in the L1 spec), and the only net-new piece is a read-only FX.listPlugins enumerator — a direct sibling of L1's planned FX.listSkills card (266-267), built on the existing FX.readDir/FX.readFile primitives (claude-dashboard.lua:269/277). That is reinforcement, not a parallel system. DONT_ADD-as-redundant is the strongest attack and still fails: L1 already covers per-profile attach + spawn application + resolver + hardening, so the candidate is mostly redundant — but two increments survive: (1) the auto-discovery half reshaped as a read-only plugin-dir enumerator feeding a picker in the New-Session modal (FX.listPlugins is genuinely NOT yet in todos.md; only skills/MCP enumerators are), and (2) an honest scope note that Shepherd emits `claude plugin install <source>`/`--plugin-dir` rather than implementing the installer. Thin but real, and it serves the stated priority ("pre-configured agents... same with skills and MCPs" — plugins share that shape). A surviving real increment keeps it at ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. Verdict: ADAPT, high, reinforces L1. Not ADD (Shepherd must not own the fetch-and-stage installer or the runtime merge loader), not DONT_ADD (the discover/attach UX maps onto existing primitives and adds a real read-only enumerator). #### @-mentions context injection (file / folder / url / problems / terminal / git)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A "@"-token typeahead over local context is one of the most useful operator affordances in cline, and the THIN version maps cleanly onto Shepherd's primitives. Shepherd already steers sessions by typing into the chat input (FX.pasteIntoWindow), already maintains a fast local folder index (folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin fd/find + core.fuzzyFilter, used by spawn's fuzzy folder search), and L1 (todos.md:311) already queues a slash-command autocomplete reusing the very same #n-suggest / onPathKey (claude-dashboard.lua:4083) + #tpl-menu render (~3462) over the spawn/nudge input. Adding an "@"-typeahead to that same input — completing against the existing folder index and (cheaply) the git roots Shepherd already discovers (FX.gitRoot) — lets the operator drop "@path" / "@git-changes" tokens into a nudge or queued task, and Claude Code's OWN native "@" resolver expands them in-session. Crucially, NO new fetch/diagnostics/browser machinery is needed: Shepherd emits the literal token, the running agent resolves it. This is exactly the candidate's stated "thin transfer," and L3 already plans the path-reference cousin (attachments[] -> --add-dir, "path reference, not byte injection," todos.md:420). It directly improves watch/steer/spawn ergonomics.
- **Against:** The feature AS SPECIFIED is an in-process executor capability, not an operator-console one. Its value is in EXPANSION: parseMentions/openMention read the workspace file/folder bytes, @problems reads the editor's live diagnostics, @terminal scrapes the latest terminal output, @git-changes computes the working diff, @url drives UrlContentFetcher (a headless browser) to fetch and convert pages to markdown — then injects all of that into the prompt the agent loop sends. Shepherd has none of these sources and structurally cannot host them: it runs no agent loop, reads no diagnostics, owns no terminal/editor buffer, makes no network calls (hard scope: no network client, no URL fetch), and cannot snapshot/edit a workspace. Diagnostics and "latest terminal output" don't even exist in Shepherd's status-file world. So building the real feature would mean re-implementing the cline executor's context layer — pure DONT_ADD plumbing. And the surviving thin slice is small enough that it's arguably just a few lines folded into the already-queued L1 autocomplete, not a standalone feature.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature by where the value lives. The RICH half (file/folder byte injection, @url via UrlContentFetcher/browser, @problems diagnostics, @terminal output, @git-changes/@commit diff expansion) is pure cline-executor territory — it depends on reading the workspace/diagnostics/terminal and making network fetches, all of which Shepherd structurally lacks (no agent loop, no workspace, no network client). That half is DONT_ADD. But the candidate itself names the transferable slice: a "@file" autocomplete that types the literal token into the session input for the agent to resolve. That fits Shepherd perfectly because (a) Claude Code natively expands "@" mentions in its own chat input, so Shepherd needs zero resolver — it just delivers the token via the existing pasteIntoWindow path, and (b) the autocomplete UI + data source already exist or are queued: the folder index (folderScanShellCommand/fuzzyFilter) is live, and L1 (todos.md:311) already specs a "/"-typeahead over the same #n-suggest/#tpl-menu input. An "@"-typeahead is the sibling of that "/"-typeahead, and it reinforces L3's path-reference attachments (todos.md:420). Hence ADAPT / reinforces L1, not a new system. Strictly limit scope to local-path (and optionally @git-changes as a literal token Claude Code resolves) completion; explicitly DROP @url/@problems/@terminal byte-expansion.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold an "@"-trigger into the L1 spawn/nudge/queue-input autocomplete: reuse onPathKey (claude-dashboard.lua:4083) + the #tpl-menu render (~3462) + the #n-suggest keyboard-nav already planned for "/". On "@", complete against the existing local folder index (folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin fd/find -> core.fuzzyFilter, the same cache spawn's fuzzy folder search uses) and the git roots Shepherd already discovers (FX.gitRoot); selecting an entry INSERTS the literal "@<relative-path>" token into the input (no auto-send, like templateInsert at claude-dashboard.lua:3480). Shepherd never fetches or expands — it types the token via FX.pasteIntoWindow and lets the running Claude Code session resolve it natively. A pure cc-core helper (e.g. core.mentionCandidates(prefix, index, gitRoots) beside fuzzyFilter) does the ranking; effects stay in fx. Optionally allow a small fixed set of literal Claude-Code-resolved tokens (e.g. @git-changes) as completions, labeled honestly as "typed for the session to resolve." Hard DROP: UrlContentFetcher/@url, @problems diagnostics, @terminal output, and any byte-injection of file/diff contents (no parseMentions/openMention port). This is the "@"-cousin of the L1 "/"-autocomplete (todos.md:311) and complements L3 attachments[] (todos.md:420).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT/reinforces-L1 ruling holds. I verified every load-bearing reference in the codebase and the judge's split is correct. THE EXECUTOR HALF IS REAL AND CORRECTLY DROPPED. The rich part of this feature — file/folder byte injection, @url via UrlContentFetcher/browser, @problems diagnostics, @terminal output, @git-changes/@commit diff expansion — is pure cline-executor territory: it requires reading the workspace, a network/browser client, and reading diagnostics/terminal, none of which Shepherd has (no agent loop, no workspace, no network client). The judge already amputated that half as DONT_ADD (no parseMentions/openMention/UrlContentFetcher port). The only thing left to judge is the thin slice. THE THIN SLICE IS GENUINELY TRANSFERABLE AND NEEDS NO EXECUTOR. The surviving slice is a '@'-typeahead that INSERTS a literal '@<relative-path>' token into the nudge input — Shepherd never resolves it; the already-running Claude Code session expands '@' mentions natively in its own chat input. Delivery is the existing literal-text path: the nudge textarea (claude-dashboard.lua:3480 templateInsert sets el.value verbatim) -> FX.pasteIntoWindow (claude-dashboard.lua:1277), the same path templates/improvePrompt already use. Zero resolution in Shepherd, so test (a) executor-dependence does not apply. NOT REDUNDANT, BUT A SIBLING OF A QUEUED ITEM (so ADAPT, not ADD, not DONT_ADD). I confirmed NO '@'-trigger exists anywhere (grep for mentionCandidates/onAtKey/@-trigger returned nothing). L1 (todos.md:311) already specs a '/'-typeahead in the same input reusing onPathKey (claude-dashboard.lua:4083) + #tpl-menu (~3462) + #n-suggest keyboard-nav (verified: arrow/Enter/Escape nav at 4083-4096, fuzzy folder search via folder-search -> folderScanShellCommand -> core.fuzzyFilter, live at claude-dashboard.lua:872/1733). An '@'-typeahead over the same local folder index + the git roots Shepherd already discovers (FX.gitRoot, claude-dashboard.lua:954) is the exact sibling of that '/'-typeahead — same UI primitive, different data source (paths/git-roots vs skills/commands). It also reinforces L3's path-reference attachments (todos.md:420-422, explicitly 'Path reference, not byte injection (Claude Code owns multimodal)'). That is the textbook definition of ADAPT/reinforces, not a parallel new system (so not ADD) and not redundant (so not DONT_ADD). NOT A SERVER/MULTI-USER SURFACE. Stays local single-user; even @workspace:name multi-root degrades to FX.gitRoot discovery. SCOPE DISCIPLINE IS THE ONLY VALID PUSH-BACK, AND THE RULING ALREADY ENFORCES IT. ~80% of the original feature (byte/diff/url/problems/terminal expansion) must be dropped; the candidate itself names the right slice and the judge hard-drops the rest. Strictly: complete only literal local-path tokens (optionally a small fixed set like @git-changes labeled honestly as 'typed for the session to resolve'); ranking in a pure cc-core helper (e.g. core.mentionCandidates beside fuzzyFilter), effects in fx, insert-only (no auto-send), off the existing autocomplete. ADAPT (high), reinforces L1, complements L3 — verdict holds.

#### Import / export schedules (JSON & YAML)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the purest possible cline-mismatch pass: it's plain file I/O over a config record — serialize a saved definition to a file, re-read it, tolerate camelCase/snake_case keys. No agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing, no server, no secrets. It is exactly the kind of operator-facing MANAGEMENT/CONFIG pattern the brief says CAN transfer. And it lands squarely on the user's stated #1 priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs," versioned in git and seeded across machines. Shepherd already persists three such records (cc-presets.json, cc-templates.json) with cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json queued in L1 — but it has NO export/import affordance, so today those files are hand-edited or stuck on one Mac. An export/import command turns the "saved-agents directory under version control" from aspiration into a workflow: export an agent profile / preset / template to a portable file, commit it, re-import on another machine. The key-alias tolerance (resolveImportedModelSelection) is real prior art for forgiving hand-edited / cross-tool config, and folds straight into L1's planned config-by-reference resolver + schema-drift fail-safe loader. Cheap to build on existing pure cc-core JSON CRUD + the existing FX file-write/Reveal side-effect path (the one L5's session-archive export already reuses).
- **Against:** Shepherd has no "schedules" primitive at all — it runs no in-process cron and no agent loop, so there is literally nothing called a "schedule" to export. Taken at face value (export a SCHEDULE), this is DONT_ADD: scheduling spawns is a thin, mostly-external concern and the object the feature serializes doesn't exist. The YAML half is an outright mismatch with Shepherd's invariants: there is zero YAML support in the codebase (pure Lua + hs.json), and todos.md L3 has already explicitly ruled it out ("no YAML parser, no Jinja2 — keep it JSON"). Pulling in a YAML library to satisfy "JSON & YAML" would add a dependency and a second serialization surface for no operator benefit on a single-user local console. There's also real footgun risk: a too-liberal import (silently accepting unknown keys, snake/camel both) can mask schema drift and let a stale or malformed profile spawn a session with the wrong flags — the import path must REFUSE on bad cross-refs, not coerce. And arguably L1 already implies persistence (the records are JSON files git can version directly), so a dedicated export/import command is partly redundant with "just commit cc-agents.json."
- **Ruling:** The cline-mismatch test passes cleanly — this is config file I/O, not agent execution — so it is not DONT_ADD. But the literal object ("schedule") doesn't map to a Shepherd primitive, so it cannot be ADD as-is either. The underlying NEED, though, is exactly what the user asked for and what L1 is built around: portable, versionable, shareable, cross-machine config records for saved agents (and presets/templates). Reshape the feature off "schedule" and onto Shepherd's actual config records → ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (secondarily L3, which already has the analogous ".prompt → JSON import" item). Drop the YAML half (no parser, JSON-only is an established invariant). Keep the camelCase/snake_case key-alias idea, but route it through L1's planned pre-flight validator/fail-safe loader so import REFUSES on unresolved cross-refs rather than silently coercing — turning a footgun into the existing schema-drift discipline. Effort is S: pure cc-core export-assembly + an FX file reader/writer reusing the cc-presets/cc-templates round-trip and the cc-exports/Reveal side-effect path that already exists.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add export/import to the operator-data registries, not "schedules." Pure cc-core: agentExport/presetExport/templateExport assemble a record (or whole registry) into a portable JSON document (sibling to presetList/templatePush ~cc-core.lua 3590-3722); import = decode → run the L1 pre-flight validator (known-fields allowlist + cross-ref resolve for policyBundle/mcpServers[]/skills[]) → REFUSE-on-error, else feed presetPush/templatePush/agentPush. Key-alias tolerance (camel/snake) lives in the import normalizer, single-sourced with L1's config-by-reference resolver and the schema-drift fail-safe loader (todos.md ~324). Effects via FX only: a new FX file reader (mirroring FX.readTemplates/readPresets at claude-dashboard.lua ~729-746) and the existing write/mkdir cc-exports/Reveal side-effect path (the L5 archive reuses it, ~todos.md 522-524). DROP YAML entirely (no parser; JSON-only is an L3-stated invariant). UI: "Export" / "Import" buttons on the agents/presets/templates manager so the saved-into-a-directory dir becomes git-shareable across machines — directly fulfilling the user's #1 stated priority.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails on every angle. (a) No in-process executor needed — this is plain file I/O over a config record (decode JSON → validate → write/read a file), exactly cline's own framing ("plain file I/O over a config record"). It runs no agent loop, executes no tools, edits/snapshots no workspace. The cline-mismatch test passes cleanly. (b) Not a server/multi-user surface — the explicit goal is a local directory put under version control and shared across machines, single-user. (c) Not redundant: Shepherd has NO export/import of its operator-config registries today. The only export path that exists is audit-export of the ledger (claude-dashboard.lua:2120-2131), which is event data, not config records. Presets/templates today only round-trip to their own fixed files (FX.readPresets/writePresets ~738-747, readTemplates/writeTemplates ~726-735) — no portable document, no cross-machine seeding, no key-alias tolerance. (d) Not a cline SDK plumbing detail — "git-shareable saved-into-a-directory config" is literally the user's #1 stated priority.\n\nBut it cannot hold as ADD verbatim, which is why ADAPT is correct, not ADD or DONT_ADD. The literal object "schedule" is not a Shepherd primitive: Shepherd has no schedules (spawns are manual or fed by the level-triggered project router; the cron/schedule skills in this environment are CLOUD-agent routines, unrelated to the local console). So "schedule export <id>"/"schedule import <path>" must be reshaped onto Shepherd's actual config records — presets/templates and the planned L1 agent profiles — to mean anything. That reshape is exactly an extension/reinforcement of L1 (saved-agents registry, the user's stated need), secondarily L3 which already carries the analogous ".prompt → JSON import" item (todos.md:405).\n\nThe judge's Shepherd mapping is fully grounded in real code/spec: export = a pure cc-core assembler sibling to presetList/templateList (cc-core.lua ~3590-3717); import = decode → L1 pre-flight validator (known-fields allowlist + cross-ref resolve for policyBundle/mcpServers[]/skills[], REFUSE-on-error per todos.md:290-298, 323-326) → feed presetPush/templatePush/agentPush; effects via FX only, reusing the readPresets/readTemplates reader pattern (claude-dashboard.lua ~726-747) and the audit-export mkdir+writeFile+alert side-effect path (~2125-2130). Dropping YAML matches the established JSON-only invariant explicitly stated for the L3 import item ("no YAML parser"). Routing camel/snake key tolerance through L1's config-by-ref resolver/fail-safe loader (refuse on unresolved cross-refs rather than silently coercing) turns a footgun into the existing schema-drift discipline. Effort S. The ruling stands: ADAPT, reinforcing L1.

#### Per-connector agent configuration (provider/model/system-prompt/mode/tools + welcome message)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Strip the connector clothing and what remains is precisely Shepherd's #1 stated priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory." The feature is a NAMED config bundle (provider + model + system-prompt + permission-mode + tool-enablement) applied to a session AT SPAWN — which is exactly what an Agent Profile (L1) is, and most of it is already wired. spawnFlags/spawnInner today emit `--permission-mode` (Plan/Act IS the mode knob), and the provider env carries ANTHROPIC_MODEL/ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL, so provider + model + mode at spawn already exist. The two genuinely-missing knobs map cleanly onto queued work: the system-prompt override is L1's `--append-system-prompt` (+ the `{role,goal,backstory}` persona block), and tool-enablement (Slack --enable-tools / Telegram --tools/forceDisableTools) is L2's named policy bundle attached to the profile (read-only / no-Bash / MCP-only starters). cline naming each connector's session config independently is strong third-party corroboration that "a session's config is a saveable, attachable, named unit" — the core L1/L2 thesis. This isn't a new system; it's a 1:1 enrichment of L1's profile schema with an explicit per-spawn tool-enable toggle, which costs almost nothing on top of work already planned.
- **Against:** The feature as literally named is "PER-CONNECTOR" config — its frame is the Slack/Telegram connector, which is the exact multi-user network-serving surface Shepherd's hard scope forbids (NO server, NO multi-user). The welcome message ("first-contact, sent once per new thread", welcomeSentAt) is purely a chat-connector artifact: it presumes inbound user threads arriving over a network that need greeting, a bidirectional messaging loop Shepherd has no concept of and structurally cannot host. A Shepherd-spawned session is driven by a seed prompt + a queue, not a conversational thread with first-contact semantics, so the welcome-message half has NO analog and should be dropped outright. There is also a redundancy risk: provider/model/permission-mode at spawn already exist, and system-prompt/tools are already specced under L1/L2 — adopting this "as a feature" rather than as a note risks proposing a parallel config path or implying a connector primitive that doesn't belong. The honest read is that nothing genuinely new arrives here that isn't already in the backlog.
- **Ruling:** Apply the cline mismatch: the value of "per-CONNECTOR" config depends on the connector — a network/multi-user serving surface Shepherd cannot host — so the connector framing and the welcome-message are DONT_ADD. But the rubric says judge the operator pattern, not the serving plumbing it ships in, and the candidate itself states the knobs "map to existing provider/model/mode/system-prompt/tool-gating features" and configure a session "exactly like a spawn modal / agent profile." That underlying need — a named, saved config bundle (provider+model+systemPrompt+mode+toolEnablement) applied at spawn — is a real, already-recognized need and is exactly L1 (Agent Profiles registry), with the tool-enablement piece being L2 (named policy bundles). Provider/model/permission-mode at spawn already ship; system-prompt and tool-gating are queued. So this is not ADD (the literal feature is connector-bound and partly impossible) and not DONT_ADD (the config-bundle need is real and on-roadmap) — it is ADAPT reinforcing L1. The concrete contribution is a corroboration plus one small enrichment: an explicit per-profile/per-spawn tool-ENABLE toggle (analogous to --enable-tools / forceDisableTools) layered onto L1's policyBundle/L2 bundle, while the connector layer and welcome message are dropped. Confidence high because the candidate text itself names the exact Shepherd primitives it maps to.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry), not a new feature. Profile schema {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt/{role,goal,backstory}, policyBundle, skills[], mcpServers[]} ALREADY captures every transferable knob: provider/model via providerEnv (ANTHROPIC_MODEL/BASE_URL), permMode → spawnFlags `--permission-mode` (Plan/Act, already shipped), system-prompt → L1's `--append-system-prompt` persona block, tools → L2 named policy bundle (the gate's autoAllow/autoDeny + gate.tools; cline's --enable-tools/forceDisableTools maps to a read-only/no-Bash/all-tools starter bundle attached to the profile). One small net-new enrichment worth noting on L1: an explicit per-profile tool-ENABLEMENT toggle (tools on/off) distinct from the per-tool gate, mirroring --enable-tools / --tools. DROP: the per-connector framing (no server/multi-user) and the first-contact welcome message / welcomeSentAt (no inbound chat-thread concept — sessions are seed-prompt + queue driven, not greeted threads). Net code: a one-line enrichment note on L1's policyBundle/tool field; the rest is already specced.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. I verified each transferable knob against the code: provider/model override = providerEnv injecting ANTHROPIC_MODEL/ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL, already shipped (cc-core.lua:2714-2724); Plan-vs-Act mode = `--permission-mode` (default/acceptEdits/plan/...) emitted at spawn by M.spawnFlags, already shipped (cc-core.lua:3001-3013); system-prompt override = L1's `--append-system-prompt` persona block (queued, todos.md:262/281); tools-enabled (--enable-tools / forceDisableTools) = L2 named policy bundles + per-profile default bundle (queued, todos.md:357-359/372). The "configures a session at spawn exactly like a spawn modal / agent profile" need IS L1's Agent Profiles registry `{name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt, policyBundle, ...}` (todos.md:256) — a real, single-user-local, already-recognized need. Cannot upgrade to ADD: the literal feature is connector-bound and the "first-contact welcome message once per new thread" is impossible — Shepherd has no server, no inbound chat-thread, no Slack/Telegram surface (grep found zero connector/thread/welcome concept anywhere). The per-CONNECTOR framing trips downgrade trigger (b) and is dropped, as is welcomeSentAt. Cannot downgrade to DONT_ADD: it is not the executor (trigger a — edits/runs nothing) and not redundant — system-prompt and tool-enablement are queued, not shipped. Net-new enrichment that makes this ADAPT rather than pure corroboration: an explicit per-profile/per-spawn tool-ENABLEMENT toggle (tools on/off, mirroring --enable-tools / --tools) distinct from L2's per-tool escalation gate, layered onto L1's policyBundle/tool field. The candidate's own text names the exact Shepherd primitives, so confidence is high.

- **For:** This is the most direct expression of the user's #1 stated priority — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs" — on the rules/context axis. The pad-mirror batch declined "durable conventions+notes/playbooks" as a CLAUDE.md duplicate (todos.md:151), but the judge correctly distinguishes building a standalone rules SUBSYSTEM (which would re-add that declined surface — explicitly DROPPED) from merely ATTACHING files via the existing knowledge[] path (allowed, and matching the OpenHands precedent). The cline-specific extras all map cleanly: two-scope global+workspace = L1 fleet-default-vs-per-profile; workspace-wins = a precedence rule on L1's config-by-reference resolver (todos.md:295-298), not a new system; paths flow through L1's safe-path resolver ([A-Za-z0-9._-], reject-outside-root, todos.md:302-303) and pre-flight validation so a missing rules dir refuses spawn. Code sites confirmed: spawnInner (cc-core.lua:2970) threads opts.flags; spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001). Net: once the non-hostable per-turn-injection half is dropped (correctly), a real LOCAL kernel remains — static, human/Claude-edited, attached at spawn — that lands squarely on a queued primitive (not full ADD: no executor to host per-task injection; not DONT_ADD: L1 is queued, not built, and the static-file attachment has genuine local value and triple-source backing). Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1, high confidence — consistent with the established ruling line. I cannot honestly downgrade to DONT_ADD.

#### Per-item enable/disable toggle (rules/skills/workflows/MCP/agents)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The underlying need — keep a configured item but turn it off without deleting it — is exactly the user's #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs saved into a directory") expressed as a lifecycle toggle. It maps directly onto L1, which already queues "Per-profile skill toggle rows" (todos.md:320-322: a skills card where each row toggles add/remove from the editing profile's skills[]), a read-mostly skills enumerator (FX.listSkills over ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md, dual-shape flat+SKILL.md), and per-profile mcpServers[]/plugins[] selection. cline's synchronizeRuleToggles() — toggle state auto-reconciling as files appear/disappear on disk — is a clean, executor-free UX pattern that fits Shepherd's read-mostly enumerator perfectly: it is pure derivation over the filesystem Shepherd already watches, with persisted operator-data toggles (a JSON sidecar exactly like cc-presets.json / the queued cc-agents.json / cc-mcp.json). The CRUD plumbing already exists (presetList/presetPush/presetRemove, cc-core.lua:3658-3717) to clone for per-item enabled state, and L1's schema-drift "keep valid, drop bad" loading (todos.md:323-326) already handles the appear/disappear sync gracefully. This makes saved agent profiles genuinely usable: "strict-testing skill off while prototyping; client rule on only for that client's work" becomes a profile/picker selection at spawn — no parallel system, pure cc-core, off-by-default, low marginal cost on top of L1.
- **Against:** Two of the feature's pillars hit the cline mismatch head-on. (1) The "durable variant writes disabled:true into the item's own YAML frontmatter" is Shepherd EDITING the user's ~/.claude config/source files — workspace/config-file editing Shepherd structurally avoids (it emits flags/files for its own operator-data, never rewrites the user's authored skill/rule sources). (2) The deep value of a toggle — that the running agent actually OMITS the disabled rule/skill on the next turn — depends on the in-process agent loop: native Claude Code owns which skills auto-activate and which rules load during a live session, and there is "no documented settings.json key" to flip that per-item (mirrors the Remote-Control caveat at todos.md:130). Shepherd watches/steers/spawns; it cannot disable a skill's native auto-load on a live session (L1 already concedes this at todos.md:321-322). So a toggle that promises "this item is now off" would be misleading unless scoped strictly to "what a Shepherd-spawned profile attaches at spawn." cline's toggles also span rules AND workflows AND global/local scopes (globalClineRulesToggles/localClineRulesToggles) — most of that surface (workflow files, project-local .clinerules execution) presumes the executor reads them, which Shepherd never does. Building the full cross-type, frontmatter-writing, live-disable toggle as-is would over-promise control Shepherd cannot deliver.
- **Ruling:** The need is real and already partially queued, so this is not DONT_ADD; but the as-shipped cline feature (frontmatter-writing durable variant + an implied "globally disable this skill/rule for the running agent") crosses into config-file editing and runtime agent control Shepherd lacks, so it is not ADD either. Reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives it is a positive-selection, picker-scope toggle: each enumerated skill/MCP/plugin/rule is a toggle row that includes/excludes it from a SAVED AGENT PROFILE's attach-at-spawn list (skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]), persisted in operator-data JSON (cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json) — not in the user's source frontmatter — and reconciled against disk via a synchronizeRuleToggles-style pure derivation (keep-valid/drop-bad already in L1). It only affects future spawns through spawnFlags (--mcp-config / --append-system-prompt / --agent / --plugin-dir), honestly labeled "what Shepherd attaches at spawn; can't disable native auto-load on a live session." That is an enrichment of L1, not a new system, so verdict ADAPT / reinforces L1. The clean transferable nugget beyond what L1 spells out: the auto-sync-on-appear/disappear semantics for the toggle list and a fleet-level "disabled but kept" state for whole agent profiles (an enabled flag on cc-agents.json records, hiding a profile from the picker without removing it) — both pure, both off-executor.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's skills/MCP card. (1) Add an `enabled` boolean to each attach-list entry on cc-agents.json profiles (skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]) and to whole profile records — pure cc-core CRUD cloned from presetPush/presetList/presetRemove (cc-core.lua:3658-3717), capped + replace-in-place + keep-valid/drop-bad. (2) FX.listSkills (dual-shape enumerator, todos.md:307-310) feeds the skills card; render each item as a toggle row (extends "Per-profile skill toggle rows", todos.md:320-322). (3) A synchronizeRuleToggles-style pure derivation reconciles persisted toggles against the current ~/.claude/skills + cc-mcp.json on each ~1s refresh — drop toggles whose file vanished, default-on for newly-appeared, emit by:"config-drift" ledger event. (4) Disabled items are simply omitted by the config-by-reference resolver (todos.md:295-298) before spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) emits --mcp-config/--append-system-prompt/--agent/--plugin-dir. Label strictly "attach-at-spawn / picker scope — cannot disable native auto-load on a live session." DROP: writing disabled:true into the user's YAML frontmatter; any claim of disabling an item for an already-running session; the global/local cline rule-execution scopes.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on every axis; ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds, with the executor/file-editing halves dropped. Verified in the repo: NOT DONT_ADD — the need is real (user's #1 stated priority: "pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs saved to a directory") and already queued in L1. The transferable kernel — a toggle row per skill/MCP/plugin that includes/excludes it from a SAVED AGENT PROFILE's attach-at-spawn list — is pure config CRUD reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, needing NO executor, NO tool execution, NO workspace editing/snapshotting, NO server. The exact CRUD blueprint exists: presetPush/presetList/presetRemove (cc-core.lua:3658-3717) with the cap + replace-in-place + keep-valid/drop-bad pattern the judge cited. "Per-profile skill toggle rows" is ALREADY an L1 enrichment (todos.md:320-322); the dual-shape FX.listSkills enumerator is queued (todos.md:307-310, 266-267); and the by:"config-drift" keep-valid/drop-bad ledger idiom the toggle-reconciler reuses is at todos.md:325. NOT ADD (no parallel new system) — this rides L1's spine, which is unbuilt: cc-agents.json and cc-mcp.json do NOT exist on disk, and spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) currently emits ONLY --permission-mode/--remote-control. All the --mcp-config/--append-system-prompt/--agent/--plugin-dir threading and the config-by-reference resolver (todos.md:295-298) are queued L1 work. So the toggle is an enrichment of L1, not a new capability. The two genuinely net-new nuggets the judge logs — auto-sync-on-appear/disappear toggle semantics and a profile-level enabled flag ("disabled but kept" hiding a profile from the picker) — are NOT yet specced (grep for auto-sync/appear/disappear/reconcile toggle found nothing in todos.md), confirming they extend L1 rather than re-adding it: that is exactly ADD->ADAPT. The cline-as-shipped halves are correctly DROPPED, and they are precisely the in-process-executor/file-editing error that makes the literal feature un-ADD-able: (1) writing disabled:true into the user's own SKILL.md/rule YAML frontmatter is config-file EDITING — but Shepherd "edits nothing," skills are read-mostly with authoring being "drop a folder in ~/.claude/skills — no DB" (todos.md:234,266-267); the toggle state belongs in operator JSON (cc-agents.json/cc-mcp.json), never the source frontmatter. (2) "globally disable this skill/rule for the running agent" is runtime control of a LIVE session — Shepherd runs no agent loop and cannot disable native auto-load mid-session; flags affect FUTURE spawns only, so the honest label "attach-at-spawn / picker scope; cannot disable native auto-load on a live session" is mandatory. Mapping (all confirmed against code): add an enabled boolean to each attach-list entry (skills[]/mcpServers[]/plugins[]) and to whole profile records on cc-agents.json via pure cc-core CRUD cloned from presetPush/List/Remove; FX.listSkills feeds the skills card rendered as toggle rows; a synchronizeRuleToggles-style pure derivation reconciles persisted toggles against ~/.claude/skills + cc-mcp.json each ~1s refresh (drop vanished, default-on for newly-appeared, by:"config-drift" event); disabled items are omitted by the config-by-ref resolver before spawnFlags emits flags. Pure cc-core, effects via fx, off the executor. ADAPT / reinforces L1 stands.

#### Per-MCP-server / per-MCP-tool auto-approve list  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The need is real and Shepherd is the right surface. MCP tools surface to Claude Code hooks as a literal `tool_name` of the form `mcp__<server>__<tool>` — exactly the field Shepherd's gate already keys on. So granular MCP auto-approve is not a foreign concept; it is the existing autoAllow/autoDeny/gate.tools machinery pointed at MCP tool names. Today an operator CAN auto-approve `mcp__github__get_pr` but only by hand-typing the raw double-underscore string into a flat policy list — undiscoverable and error-prone. cline's pattern adds the missing operator UX: a per-server grouping with a 'auto-approve all tools' roll-up and per-tool checkboxes, plus a discoverable inventory of which tools each server exposes. That UX maps directly onto the already-queued L1 `cc-mcp.json` MCP registry (which already carries a per-server `allowedTools` field) and L2 named bundles (which already specs an explicit *MCP-only* starter bundle). Auto-approve policy is squarely an operator/config/UX concern — the exact class the brief says transfers — and it advances the user's #1 stated interest (saved MCPs in a directory). It is pure watch/steer: deciding which already-running tool calls to wave through, no execution involved.
- **Against:** cline's specific implementation leans on capabilities Shepherd structurally lacks. cline hosts the MCP client connection, so it can enumerate a server's LIVE tool list to render an accurate per-tool checkbox grid; Shepherd does not run the MCP client (Claude Code does) and cannot introspect a connected server, so its tool inventory must come from static config (`~/.claude.json` / `.mcp.json` / the registry's declared `allowedTools`) and may be stale or incomplete — the same limitation that forced L1's MCP health feature to be narrowed to a static 'inventory badge'. cline also stores `autoApprove` as per-server state inside its own task/server runtime; Shepherd has no per-server runtime object, only the fleet policy lists + per-session gate override. And there is a real redundancy risk: building a parallel per-server autoApprove store would duplicate what autoAllow/autoDeny/gate.tools + the queued L2 bundles already do, violating the 'extend, don't fork' rule. The raw value (auto-approve a named MCP tool) ALREADY exists in the flat policy lists today; only the discovery/grouping UX is missing.
- **Ruling:** The crux is that the feature's core mechanism — auto-approving named tool calls — already lives in Shepherd's gate, which matches on `tool_name` exactly (cc-approve.sh:58) and supports `Tool(glob)` prefix matching (cc-approve.sh:55), and MCP tools arrive as plain `mcp__server__tool` strings in that same field. So this is not a new capability; it is a UX + grouping layer over existing primitives, which makes it ADAPT, not ADD, and certainly not DONT_ADD (it needs no executor, no tool execution, no workspace editing, no server). It is also not a parallel system: L1 already specs `cc-mcp.json` with named server records carrying `allowedTools`, and L2 already specs named bundles with autoAllow lists plus an MCP-only starter bundle. The honest constraint is that Shepherd derives the per-server tool inventory statically (no live MCP introspection), so the per-tool checkbox grid is best-effort over config rather than a guaranteed-complete live list — but that is acceptable and matches how L1's MCP inventory was already narrowed. Reinforces L1 as the primary anchor (the MCP registry + per-server roll-up + tool discovery), with the policy plumbing riding L2's resolver.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L1's `cc-mcp.json` server records: add a per-server `autoApprove: string[]` (tool basenames) plus an `autoApproveAll` roll-up boolean, mirroring cline's server-level toggle. The MCP picker in the New Session modal (L1) renders each registry server as a group with per-tool checkboxes sourced from the record's declared `allowedTools` (static inventory, honestly labeled 'declared, not live'). At resolve time, the config-by-reference resolver (L1, generalizing resolveGateTools:2537 / providerById:2632) expands the selected server's auto-approve set into concrete `mcp__<server>__<tool>` entries appended to the effective autoAllow list — feeding the SAME ordered cc-approve.sh pipeline (autoDeny -> autopilot -> autoAllow -> approveRepeats), honoring the cc-core.lua <-> cc-approve.sh KEEP-IN-SYNC contract. Package the result as an L2 named bundle (the queued *MCP-only* starter bundle becomes the worked example). No new runtime store, no live MCP connection: pure cc-core expansion + fx config write. autoDeny still runs first and wins, preserving safety-first semantics.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on every lever; ADAPT (reinforces L1, riding L2's resolver) holds. (a) No executor: auto-approving a named tool is exactly what Shepherd's gate already does — cc-approve.sh:69 reads `.tool_name`, pattern_match (lines 48-60) matches bare `Tool` or `Tool(glob)`, and the autoAllow branch (line 186) emits allow in the ordered autoDeny->autopilot->autoAllow->approveRepeats pipeline (lines 166-196). MCP tools arrive in that SAME `.tool_name` field as plain `mcp__server__tool` strings, so they need no new mechanism; no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace edit/snapshot. (b) No server/multi-user: it's a per-server `autoApprove: string[]` field in local operator JSON (cc-mcp.json) rendered in the local New-Session modal — cline's own version is a single-user checkbox grid, not cline-hub. (d) Not SDK plumbing: per-tool auto-approve is an operator-console UX in cline (the MCP settings panel). (c) The redundancy lever lands but only downgrades ADD->ADAPT, not to DONT_ADD: L1 already specs cc-mcp.json with named server records carrying `allowedTools` (todos.md:263-265) and L2 already specs named bundles + an MCP-only starter bundle (todos.md:357-358) and a resolver mirroring resolveGateTools (todos.md:346), and resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537)/providerById (cc-core.lua:2632) exist as cited. So it is NOT a new capability and NOT a parallel system — but the per-server `autoApprove`/`autoApproveAll` roll-up field and the per-tool checkbox grid that expands selections into concrete `mcp__<server>__<tool>` autoAllow entries are a genuine UX+grouping increment over those primitives (today's spec stores allowedTools for the picker/--mcp-config, not an auto-approve policy set). The one honest gap — no live MCP introspection, so the grid is 'declared, not live' from static allowedTools — the judge already conceded and it matches the existing MCP-inventory-badge best-effort-from-config stance (todos.md:270-274); it requires only config reads, no executor, and does not drop the feature below ADAPT. Net: improves a local single-user console, rides existing gate + L1 registry + L2 resolver, needs no executor/server. ADAPT (high) confirmed.

#### MCP server config file (single source of truth, multi-transport)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the operator-config half of MCP — declaring servers in one validated JSON file — NOT the agent-execution half (Shepherd never connects to an MCP server or calls its tools; native Claude Code does, via the flags Shepherd emits). So it clears the cline mismatch cleanly: it is pure config data plus a validator plus a config-file builder, exactly the kind of "saved into a directory" management surface the user put at the top of their priority list ("pre-configured agents... same with skills and MCPs"). It also fills a real gap: today Shepherd has providers/presets but no MCP layer at all, and L1's "spawn from a saved agent" already promises to thread mcpServers[] into spawnFlags as --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config — which is impossible without a registry of server definitions to reference. A discriminated-union-style validator (stdio vs SSE vs streamable-HTTP, each with its required fields) is precisely the "pure validator + mcp-config-file builder" L1 calls for, and it slots beside the existing providers and presets registries with near-zero new architecture. The ${ENV_VAR}/authTokenEnv pattern is a perfect match for Shepherd's no-secrets stance: store the env-var NAME, let the spawned login shell expand the value (loginShellWrap, cc-core.lua:2711/2754) — secrets never touch Shepherd's JSON. disabled and timeout are trivial pass-through fields; autoApprove maps onto the existing gate/autoAllow vocabulary.
- **Against:** Most of this is already specified verbatim in the queued L1 spec — cc-mcp.json with {id, label, transport(stdio|sse|http), command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv}, a pure validator, an mcp-config-file builder, requiredEnv, and a config-by-reference resolver — so accepting it as a standalone "new" feature risks spawning a parallel system instead of folding into L1, the exact failure mode the rubric warns against. Several specific cline fields are weak or out-of-scope for a watch/steer console: oauth implies Shepherd performing an OAuth flow / holding tokens, which collides with the hard no-secrets-storage limit (Shepherd can at most reference an authTokenEnv var, not run an OAuth dance); zod-schema legacy-shape migration is cline's own historical baggage that a greenfield cc-mcp.json simply won't have, so it should be dropped, not ported; and autoApprove is partly redundant with Shepherd's existing per-session gate/autoAllow globs. Native Claude Code already reads project .mcp.json and keeps its own MCP registry, so Shepherd must stay a thin flag/file emitter and not reimplement MCP connection logic, lifecycle, or health pings (L1 already narrows "health check" to a config-inventory badge for exactly this reason).
- **Ruling:** The candidate is the config/management pattern of MCP, not the execution of it — Shepherd emits a --mcp-config file for native Claude Code to consume and never connects to a server, so it passes the cline mismatch. It is not a new need: it is the cc-mcp.json MCP server registry already written into L1 (todos.md:263-265, 287-298), down to transport=stdio|sse|http, command/args/url, a pure validator, and a mcp-config-file builder. Hence ADAPT, reinforcing L1, rather than ADD. Honest scoping: keep the multi-transport discriminated-union validator, disabled/timeout, ${ENV}-by-reference (via authTokenEnv + loginShellWrap, never stored values), and the single-file source of truth; DROP oauth-flow/token handling (violates no-secrets) and zod legacy-migration (no legacy shapes exist); treat autoApprove as a thin alias into the existing gate/autoAllow vocabulary rather than a second policy engine.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's MCP server registry. Concretely: cc-mcp.json sits beside providers/cc-presets.json/cc-agents.json as operator-data JSON. Add pure cc-core CRUD mcpList/mcpPush/mcpRemove mirroring presetPush/presetList (cc-core.lua ~3590-3722), with a discriminated validator keyed on transport: stdio requires command(+args/env/cwd), sse/streamableHttp require url, all carry optional disabled/timeout/allowedTools/autoApprove and an authTokenEnv (name only). A pure builder turns the selected, non-disabled records into a temp mcp-config JSON file that spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) emits as --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config; ${ENV} resolution happens by passing the var NAME through and letting the spawned login shell expand it (loginShellWrap cc-core.lua:2711/2754), so no secret lands in JSON. requiredEnv per record feeds core.missingEnv (todos.md:287-289) for the New-Session preflight badge. Schema-drift handling reuses presetList's keep-valid/drop-bad-and-report pattern (todos.md:323-326). Drop oauth flows and zod legacy-migration.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. (1) Passes the cline mismatch: this is the CONFIG/declaration pattern of MCP, not its execution. The value is "declare all MCP servers in one validated JSON file"; Shepherd can emit exactly such a file as --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config for native Claude Code to consume (spawnFlags, cc-core.lua:3001; CLI confirmed present at ~/.local/bin/claude per todos.md:230-231) and NEVER connects to a server itself. No agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing, no server/users. So it is not DONT_ADD on the mismatch. (2) It is an extension of an already-queued item, not a new system — which is precisely ADAPT, not ADD: todos.md:263-265 already specifies the 'MCP server registry (cc-mcp.json)': named records {id,label,transport(stdio|sse|http),command,args,url,allowedTools,authTokenEnv}, a 'Pure validator + mcp-config-file builder', a modal picker, attachable to a profile. That IS the single-file, multi-transport, discriminated-union-by-transport, ${ENV}-by-name (authTokenEnv + loginShellWrap, cc-core.lua:2754), builder-to---mcp-config feature. The extra cline fields map cleanly: disabled/timeout → optional record fields; autoApprove → thin alias into the existing gate/autoAllow vocabulary (gate is a hold-for-approval list per context.md, not a second policy engine); requiredEnv → core.missingEnv preflight (todos.md:287-289); schema validation/drift → the keep-valid/drop-bad presetList pattern (cc-core.lua:3658, todos.md:323-326). CRUD would mirror presetPush/presetList (cc-core.lua:3658-3722). (3) The two genuinely cline-specific bits are correctly DROPPED by the judge: oauth token handling (violates Shepherd's no-secrets-storage limit — it stores only env-var NAMES) and zod legacy-migration (no legacy shapes exist in a greenfield registry). (4) The only refutation lever — 'so already-present it is redundant/DONT_ADD' — fails because L1 is QUEUED, not shipped; reinforcing/enriching an unbuilt roadmap item is the textbook ADAPT. Verdict: ADAPT (high), reinforces L1.

#### MCP server management (add/edit/list/enable-disable/delete; CLI wizard, webview form, remote-server flow)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is config-and-UX management, not agent execution, so it clears the cline mismatch cleanly: editing which MCP servers exist requires no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing, no server, no multi-user. It directly serves the user's #1 stated priority — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." It is an exact match for an already-queued backlog item: L1 already specifies an `cc-mcp.json` MCP server registry with named records `{id, label, transport(stdio|sse|http), command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv}`, a pure validator, an mcp-config-file builder, a modal picker, and attach-to-profile. cline's List/Add/Edit/Enable-Disable/Delete CRUD, its stdio (command/args/env) vs remote (URL + transport + auth header) split, its disabled-but-kept flag, and its non-interactive `cline config mcp --json` listing are concrete, well-proven UX for exactly that registry — they tell Shepherd what fields the form needs and that a disabled flag (keep config, exclude from the emitted `--mcp-config`) is worth having. Shepherd has the spawn-flag plumbing to make these records LIVE: the local `claude` CLI (~/.local/bin/claude, v2.1.175) exposes `--mcp-config`/`--strict-mcp-config` and `claude mcp add`, and `core.spawnFlags` already threads launch flags, so an edited registry actually changes what a spawned session loads. The remote-server static-auth-header flow maps onto the established `providerEnv`/`authTokenEnv` pattern (store the env-var NAME, expanded by the spawned login shell) — no new secret-storage surface.
- **Against:** Native Claude Code already owns MCP: it reads project `.mcp.json` and `~/.claude.json`, keeps its own MCP registry, and ships `claude mcp add` for interactive add — so a chunk of cline's wizard duplicates a path the operator already has. cline's wizard EDITS the live MCP config the running agent consumes; in Shepherd the value is thinner because Shepherd doesn't run the agent — it can only emit `--mcp-config` at spawn (best-effort/keystroke-only on VS Code/Cursor, reliable on Kitty/Terminal), and it cannot toggle a server on a session already running. The enable-disable flag is genuinely useful only at the moment of spawn; mid-session it does nothing, which risks an operator expecting a live effect. cline's remote-server flow (Streamable-HTTP/SSE endpoints, auth headers) edges toward network/secret surface that Shepherd's hard scope limits forbid storing — Shepherd must keep it to env-var-NAME references and never persist a token or header value. And a full standalone wizard/CRUD as a feature in its own right would be over-scoped: Shepherd's plan is deliberately a thin fleet-convenience layer that emits flags/files and does NOT reimplement MCP — so this should arrive as the `cc-mcp.json` slice of L1's profile picker, not as a parallel MCP-management subsystem.
- **Ruling:** The cline mismatch is about RUNNING the agent / executing tools / editing+snapshotting the workspace / serving users — none of which this feature requires. Managing the LIST of MCP servers is pure config CRUD over operator-data JSON, and Shepherd's architecture (pure cc-core CRUD mirroring presetPush/presetList, effects via fx, attach via spawnFlags + the present `claude --mcp-config` flag) can host it natively. It is not DONT_ADD: nothing here needs the in-process loop. It is not ADD-as-is: a standalone three-surface management tool (terminal wizard + `--json` listing + dedicated remote form) is broader than Shepherd's deliberately thin posture, and it directly overlaps an item already on the roadmap — so building it parallel to L1 would create the duplicate registry the plan explicitly warns against. Therefore ADAPT, reinforcing L1. The right shape: realize L1's `cc-mcp.json` registry (validator + mcp-config-file builder + modal picker attachable to an agent profile), borrowing cline's field set (stdio command/args/env vs remote URL+transport, a disabled flag) and its non-interactive listing idea, while honoring three Shepherd invariants the candidate doesn't enforce: (1) secrets are env-var NAMES only (authTokenEnv), never stored values, even for remote auth headers; (2) "disabled" / attach is a SPAWN-time effect, surfaced honestly (no implied mid-session toggle); (3) Shepherd does not reimplement MCP — it emits `--mcp-config` and defers execution to native Claude Code. Effort is M for the MCP slice on its own (it is one component of the L-sized L1).
- **How (Shepherd):** Build as the MCP portion of L1: a pure `core.mcpList/mcpPush/mcpRemove` CRUD over `cc-mcp.json` (operator-data JSON outside the Settings round-trip, like cc-presets.json/providers), records `{id, label, transport(stdio|sse|http), command, args, env-NAMEs, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv, disabled, requiredEnv[]}`; a pure validator (sibling to presetList ~3590-3722, fail-safe per-entry drop returning dropped names) + an mcp-config-file builder that emits the JSON consumed by `claude --mcp-config`/`--strict-mcp-config`, wired through `core.spawnFlags` (~3001) and `spawnSpec` (~3061), gated by `spawn.live`. UI: an MCP picker in the New-Session modal + an editor form reachable from ⚙/the spawn modal (borrow cline's stdio-vs-remote field split and disabled toggle); `disabled` simply excludes the server from the emitted config at spawn (no mid-session effect — label it spawn-time). Auth via the `providerEnv`/`authTokenEnv` env-NAME pattern (~2710), never a stored token/header value; `core.missingEnv` preflight for required env. Optional read-only MCP inventory derivation from `~/.claude.json` + project `.mcp.json` for the off-by-default inventory badge (the L1 "narrowed health check"). Reuse the safe-path/sanitize guards for server ids/paths.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT ruling holds. I verified the judge's three load-bearing claims against the code. (1) The feature is pure config CRUD that needs NONE of the cline-mismatch machinery: managing the LIST of MCP servers requires no agent loop, no tool execution, no file editing, no workspace snapshotting, and no server/multi-user surface — cline's "remote server" flow is just a config record holding an endpoint URL + transport, not Shepherd hosting anything. Shepherd already emits the right flag: spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) and spawnSpec (3061) build argv for native Claude Code, and todos.md confirms the local claude CLI supports --mcp-config/--strict-mcp-config, so Shepherd defers all MCP execution to native Claude Code. So DONT_ADD is wrong. (2) It is NOT add-as-is, because it directly overlaps an already-queued item. L1 in todos.md (lines 263-265) already specifies the 'MCP server registry (cc-mcp.json): named records {id, label, transport(stdio|sse|http), command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv}, modeled on the providers registry. Pure validator + mcp-config-file builder. MCP picker in the modal; attachable to a profile.' That is a near-verbatim match to the cline candidate (stdio-vs-remote split, disabled flag, non-interactive listing) — building cline's three standalone surfaces in parallel would create the duplicate registry the plan explicitly warns against ('a superset of the existing preset, not a parallel system'). So ADD-as-is is wrong. (3) Shepherd's architecture can host it natively and the right invariants exist: presetList/presetPush/presetRemove (cc-core.lua:3658-3705) are the exact pure-CRUD template; and the secrets-as-env-NAME-only invariant is real and reusable — providerEnv (cc-core.lua:2714-2733) stores authTokenEnv and emits ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN='$'+name, expanded by the login shell at spawn, never a stored value, matching context.md's 'no secrets' scope. The remote-auth-header case must follow that same env-NAME pattern, and 'disabled'/attach must be a spawn-time-only effect (Shepherd cannot toggle a running session's MCP config). This is the textbook ADAPT profile: a genuine transferable operator pattern (CRUD + UX + non-interactive listing + stdio/remote field split) that must be reshaped onto the existing L1 cc-mcp.json registry rather than copied wholesale. Cannot refute; ADAPT holds, reinforcing L1.

#### MCP Marketplace (searchable/sortable catalog with one-click install)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The candidate itself names the transferable kernel: "the searchable/sortable pickable-grid UX over a local/curated set." That maps cleanly onto Shepherd's stated #1 user priority — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." Shepherd already proves the local-catalog pattern works: cc-presets.json is a named/capped/validated CRUD store rendered as one-click chips in the New Session modal, providers is a parallel registry, and the spawn path (spawnFlags/spawnSpec, cc-core ~3001/3061) already threads launch flags. L1 explicitly plans a cc-mcp.json MCP-server registry plus a skills card, and the local `claude` CLI supports `--mcp-config`/`--strict-mcp-config`, so a browse-and-attach MCP catalog slots straight into the modal: pick servers, materialize them into the session's .mcp.json / --mcp-config at spawn. The search/filter/sort UX even has shipped primitives to reuse (core.filterTiles, core.fuzzyFilter, searchArgv). A curated, sortable card grid over the local cc-mcp.json catalog (and the read-mostly skills enumerator) is a real, glanceable improvement to a console whose whole job surrounds the spawn.
- **Against:** The feature AS SPECIFIED is overwhelmingly the cline mismatch in two distinct ways. (1) Network/server surface: it depends on cline's HOSTED marketplace API (axios POST /download, fetchMarketplace, getLatestMcpServers) and a sort-by-stars/download-count remote catalog — Shepherd has NO network server and makes no API calls; download-count/stars/newest only exist because a remote registry publishes them. (2) Agent-execution surface: "one-click install" is its core value and it is pure executor work — downloadMcp fetches a README, BUILDS an install-task prompt, and calls controller.initTask to RUN an agent loop that creates dirs, edits mcp settings, and demonstrates a tool. Shepherd runs no agent loop, executes no tools, and edits no workspace. So the literal feature — remote catalog + agent-driven auto-install — is DONT_ADD on scope. Without the remote registry there are no stars/downloads to sort by, and without the executor there is no "install"; what is left is a much thinner local picker, so the as-is value does not transfer.
- **Ruling:** This candidate was already mined and ruled in the LiteLLM report (docs/feature-mining/litellm.md), under "Public MCP registry / discovery + BYOM submission/approval workflow" and the adjacent MCP items, all of which were folded into L1 as ADAPT (high). The decision splits cleanly: DROP the multi-tenant/network publishing layer (hosted marketplace API, /discover, registry.json, stars/download-count sort, submission/approve/reject) — it violates NO-network/NO-multi-user — and DROP the cline executor "one-click install" (initTask running an agent loop that edits settings/dirs) since Shepherd hosts no agent loop and edits no workspace. The surviving kernel — a searchable/sortable card grid over a LOCAL curated catalog you browse and attach at spawn — is exactly L1's planned cc-mcp.json registry plus the skills card, and lands on the user's explicitly highest-weighted interest. So it is not a new system: it reinforces L1's MCP-registry slice and the picker UX. Not ADD, because the as-is feature is network+executor plumbing that must be reshaped onto Shepherd's preset/spawn/gate primitives; not DONT_ADD, because the reshaped local catalog genuinely improves the console and is absent today (verified zero MCP references in the repo). Verdict: ADAPT, reinforcing L1.
- **How (Shepherd):** Folds into L1 (Agent Profiles registry → MCP server registry slice + skills card). Concretely: (a) the catalog = the planned cc-mcp.json operator-data registry (records {id, label, transport, command, args, url, allowedTools, authTokenEnv} — env-var NAMES only, reusing the providerEnv $ENV rule, never secrets), plus the read-mostly ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md enumerator via FX.listSkills. (b) The searchable/sortable card grid = reuse the shipped search/filter primitives (core.filterTiles / core.fuzzyFilter, mirrored in panel JS) over the local catalog, sorting by LOCAL fields (name / recently-added / attach-count) — NOT remote stars/download-count. Surface it as a card view next to the existing preset chips in the New Session modal (claude-dashboard.lua). (c) "Install/attach" is reshaped: no agent loop — selecting a server materializes it into the session's MCP config at spawn (project .mcp.json or --mcp-config) via spawnFlags/spawnSpec + the FX file-write/spawn path; per-session "active servers" folds into the existing enable/disable + gate.tools (held-for-approval, NOT an allow-list — fix the inverted semantics at design time). Optional off-by-default MCP inventory badge from ~/.claude.json + project .mcp.json. DROP entirely: hosted marketplace API/axios, /download, /discover, registry.json, stars/download-count sort, submission/approve/reject, controller.initTask agent install.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT and could not; it holds, reinforcing L1. The cline MCP Marketplace has three load-bearing parts, two of which are exactly the disqualifying shapes the framing warns about. (1) Hosted marketplace API with search + sort-by-stars/download-count = NETWORK + multi-user publishing surface (violates NO-network/NO-multi-user). (2) "One-click install" = controller.initTask running the IN-PROCESS AGENT LOOP that edits mcp settings, creates dirs, and demonstrates a tool — pure executor + workspace editing, which Shepherd does NOT do (it runs no agent loop, edits no files). Both are correctly DROPPED. The only survivor is (3) the searchable/sortable pickable card-grid over a catalog you attach at spawn. DONT_ADD (executor-dependent) fails: the surviving kernel does NOT need the agent loop. Verified spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) and spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061) already build the claude launch line; attaching MCP servers is just emitting --mcp-config / writing project .mcp.json, which Claude Code itself consumes — no initTask, no tool execution, no Shepherd-side editing. DONT_ADD (redundant) fails: verified ZERO MCP references anywhere in the repo (cc-core.lua, claude-dashboard.lua, .sh hooks, JSON, README, context.md, todos.md). spawnFlags currently emits ONLY --permission-mode/--remote-control — no MCP/--agent/--add-dir wiring exists. The MCP attach path is genuinely unbuilt. cc-presets.json is a different object (folder/editor/permMode/provider; no MCP/skills attach). The card-grid UX reuses, not duplicates, existing primitives: M.filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322) and M.fuzzyFilter (cc-core.lua:3502) already exist for tile search — so the grid REINFORCES shipped primitives. ADD fails: the as-is feature is overwhelmingly network + executor plumbing; the surviving kernel must be reshaped onto Shepherd primitives (catalog -> local cc-mcp.json {id,label,transport,command,args,url,allowedTools,authTokenEnv}, env-var NAMES only via the providerEnv $ENV rule; install -> materialize at spawn; sort-by-stars -> sort by LOCAL fields name/recently-added/attach-count). Heavy reshape = ADAPT, not ADD. It is a third-source corroboration, not a new system: this is the cline analog of LiteLLM's already-mined "Public MCP registry / discovery + BYOM submission/approval" and "Claude Code Plugin Marketplace," both ruled ADAPT (high) and folded into L1 (todos.md:247-334; source items explicitly include "MCP server registry, ... Public MCP registry/discovery, ... Plugin marketplace"). It lands on the user's #1 stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCPs in a directory) and is absent today, so it genuinely improves the local single-user console. One correction the judge correctly makes and the earlier LiteLLM doc got wrong: gate.tools is an ESCALATION / hold-for-approval list, NOT an allow-list. Verified at cc-core.lua:2525 (DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS = "Bash Write Edit MultiEdit NotebookEdit") and resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537) — the listed tools are the ones HELD for approval; tools not in the list run freely. Per-session "active MCP servers" should fold into enable/disable + this hold-for-approval gate, not an inverted allow-list. Verdict: ADAPT (high), reinforces L1. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1, lines 247-334), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/docs/feature-mining/litellm.md (prior MCP/marketplace rulings), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (spawnFlags:3001, spawnSpec:3061, preset CRUD:3658-3710, resolveGateTools:2537, filterTiles:322, fuzzyFilter:3502).

#### API config profiles / named saved configurations (provider+model+credential+tuning, mode-scoped, persisted)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** A persisted, named, mode-scoped configuration bundle (provider + model + permission-mode + tuning) that survives restarts and mode switches, with partial/field-mask updates that don't clobber siblings, plus pre-flight readiness checks before a session launches, is squarely an operator/spawn-config pattern — exactly the part of cline that transfers to a watch/steer/spawn console. Shepherd ALREADY has the seed of this: cc-presets.json stores {name, folder, editor, permMode, provider} and presetMarkUsed/presetForProject persist per-project last-used recall (cc-core.lua ~3640-3722). The candidate is the cline twin of that, and the deltas it adds — persist MODEL too, validate required credentials/fields before spawn, and accept the same via CLI flags — are precisely what the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry already specs: profile = {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, ...}; a config-by-reference resolver; requiredEnv + core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) NAME-presence preflight; a pure pre-flight validator that REFUSES spawn with the full error list before emitting any flag. The native claude CLI (~/.local/bin/claude) takes --mcp-config/--agent/--add-dir/--append-system-prompt, and spawnFlags/spawnSpec already thread provider env + flags, so wiring --provider/--model into a spawn is feasible. This directly improves the spawn experience: pick a saved agent instead of re-choosing provider/model every session, with a guardrail that catches a missing AWS_REGION / GCP project before a dead session starts.
- **Against:** Two cline-isms don't transfer and must be stripped. First, "credential" in the bundle and the readiness checks that validate API keys / AWS creds / GCP project / Azure identity exist because cline IS the executor that makes the API call — it must hold and verify the secret. Shepherd has a HARD SCOPE LIMIT of NO secrets storage; it resolves $ENV via the spawned shell and never proxies or calls the API. So persisting a "credential" verbatim is a scope violation, and "validate the credential" can only honestly become "check the NAME of the required env var is present in the login shell" — never the value. Second, cline's field-mask updateApiConfigurationPartial is plumbing for a live in-process provider object the agent loop consumes mid-run; Shepherd has no such object — its "config" is emitted once as launch flags and the running session is driven by Claude Code itself. There is also real risk of building a PARALLEL config system: framed as "API config profiles" this could spawn a second registry beside cc-presets.json / L1 cc-agents.json, which the todos explicitly warn against ("a superset of the existing preset, not a parallel system").
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED — persist the full spawn configuration (now including model) as a reusable, named, mode-scoped bundle, update it without clobbering siblings, and refuse to spawn when a required field/credential is missing — is real and already half-built in Shepherd (cc-presets.json + per-project lastByProject recall) and fully specced in queued L1 (cc-agents.json profile with provider/model/permMode, the config-by-reference resolver, requiredEnv + core.missingEnv, and a pre-flight validator that refuses spawn before emitting flags). That makes this ADAPT/reinforces-L1, not a fresh ADD. It is NOT DONT_ADD: nothing here requires the agent loop, tool execution, workspace editing/snapshotting, or multi-user serving — it's pure spawn-config-and-validate, the strongest cline→Shepherd transfer surface. The single decisive reshape is the cline mismatch on secrets: cline validates credentials because it executes the call; Shepherd stores no secrets, so the readiness check becomes NAME-presence-of-required-env (already L1's missingEnv design), and the bundle stores a provider reference + requiredEnv list, never a key value. Field-mask partial update collapses onto presetPush's existing "replace-in-place, lastByProject rides through untouched" merge. Build it inside L1; do not create a parallel "API config" registry.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 Agent Profiles registry; do not create a parallel system. (1) Extend the existing preset to persist model: add `model` to the cc-presets.json/cc-agents.json record and to presetList/presetPush/presetMarkUsed/presetForProject (cc-core.lua 3658-3722) so per-project recall and saved agents carry provider+model+permMode together; lastByProject already gives mode-scoped survival across restarts. (2) Field-mask partial update = the existing presetPush replace-in-place semantics ("lastByProject rides through untouched") generalized to merge only the supplied subfields — no new mechanism. (3) Pre-flight readiness = L1's pure validator family + core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv): reshape cline's hasAws/Gcp/AzureCredentials into NAME-presence checks over the login-shell env (loginShellWrap), keyed off provider so an AWS-Bedrock provider's required names (AWS_REGION/creds) and a GCP provider's project var are declared in the provider/profile's requiredEnv[]; surface as a New-Session preflight warning + refuse-spawn-with-full-error-list (never read or store a value — honors NO secrets storage). (4) CLI flags = thread the resolved provider/model through spawnFlags/spawnSpec (cc-core.lua 3001/3061) to the native claude binary's env (ANTHROPIC_MODEL via providerEnv) + flags; Shepherd EMITS, never parses an inbound CLI. Reuse providerById (cc-core.lua 2632) and the L1 config-by-reference resolver as the single dereference point.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. Verified against source. WHY IT'S NOT DONT_ADD (the executor/server/library checklist all clear): The feature is pure spawn-config-and-validate. It needs NO agent loop, NO tool execution, NO workspace editing/snapshotting, NO multi-user serving. Shepherd already EMITS provider config: providerEnv (cc-core.lua:2714) sets ANTHROPIC_MODEL from profile.model (line 2724) and threads it via spawnSpec/spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3061/3001) into the native claude binary's env. Persisting that selection so it survives restarts is squarely an operator-console concern, not SDK plumbing. The one cline mismatch the judge flagged is real and already correctly reshaped: cline validates CREDENTIAL VALUES because it executes the API call; Shepherd stores no secrets (context.md:13 'no secrets'; README:275 'the key lives in your shell, never in cc-config.json'; cc-core.lua:2729 emits the token as a NAME reference '$AUTH_TOKEN_ENV', expanded by the login shell). So the readiness check MUST become NAME-presence-of-required-env, never a value read — which is EXACTLY what L1 already specs as core.missingEnv(profile, shellEnv) checking 'NAME-presence only (never values, via loginShellWrap)' (todos.md:287-289).
