And a name-presence preflight is genuinely useful locally: the loginShellWrap comment (cc-core.lua:2750-2752) documents that an unset named env var makes the spawned session 401 — so warning the operator before spawn has real value. The DONT_ADD angle ('Shepherd executes nothing, so credential validation is pointless') therefore fails: it's not credential validation, it's emit-time env-name readiness. WHY IT'S ADAPT NOT FRESH-ADD: The need is half-built and fully specced under L1. Shipped today: presetPush/presetList/presetMarkUsed/presetForProject (cc-core.lua:3658-3722) persist {name, folder, editor, permMode, provider} to cc-presets.json with replace-in-place merge where 'lastByProject rides through untouched' (line 3693) — that IS the field-mask partial-update mechanism, and lastByProject already gives per-project recall surviving restarts. The gaps (model in the record; the missingEnv validator; the config-by-ref resolver; spawn-refuse-with-full-error-list) are all verbatim in queued L1 (todos.md:256 profile schema includes provider+model+permMode; :287-289 requiredEnv + missingEnv; :290-294 pre-flight validator 'refuses spawn... before emitting any flag'; :295-298 config-by-reference resolver extending providerById:2632). CLI flags = Shepherd EMITS provider/model through providerEnv/spawnFlags, never parses an inbound CLI. MAPPING (fold into L1, do NOT create a parallel API-config registry): (1) add model to the preset/agent record + presetList/presetPush/presetMarkUsed/presetForProject. (2) field-mask = generalize presetPush replace-in-place to merge only supplied subfields. (3) preflight = L1 validator family + core.missingEnv over loginShellWrap env, keyed off provider's requiredEnv[] (AWS region/creds, GCP project, etc.) as NAME-presence; surface as New-Session warning + refuse-spawn. (4) CLI/flags via spawnFlags/spawnSpec → providerEnv (ANTHROPIC_MODEL). Single dereference point = providerById + the L1 config-by-ref resolver.

#### Model picker with fuzzy search + capability/pricing/context info + favorites + variant switch  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** Shepherd already exposes model selection in two places — the New-session/provider picker and the live `/model <id>` switch — but both treat a model as a bare id string with no help choosing one. cline's pattern improves exactly that operator decision: fuzzy search over a model list (Shepherd already has the ranking primitive in `core.fuzzyFilter`, today only wired to folders), favorites pinned to the top (matching cline's `favoritedModelIds`), and curated 'Recommended'/'Free' onboarding tabs — all pure-UX wins that map cleanly onto a single-user picker and reinforce L1's saved-agent flow (an agent profile carries `model`, so a better model picker is the natural front-end for it). The 1M/200K variant toggle is nearly free: Shepherd's `core.contextLimitFor` ALREADY encodes which Claude models are 1M vs 200K, and switching is one `/model <id>` send (the live switch already exists), so a one-click "swap to the 1M variant" button is a thin, high-value extension of a primitive already there — and it directly serves the context-fullness bar that already depends on getting the window size right.
- **Against:** The richest part of cline's card is executor/billing shaped and structurally outside Shepherd. The per-million-token input/output/cache pricing (including tiered) presumes an app that transacts against the API and accounts for cost — Shepherd makes ZERO API calls, stores no secrets, and prices nothing; a baked-in pricing table would be a stale-data maintenance liability that informs no decision Shepherd actually makes. The capability badges are mostly executor capabilities: 'browser-use' describes a tool cline RUNS and Shepherd cannot, and supports-images / prompt-cache describe behavior of the agent loop Shepherd doesn't host. cline's catalog (`model-facts.ts`, `catalog/types.ts`) is also tuned to OpenRouter's heterogeneous multi-provider universe; Shepherd's world is overwhelmingly native Claude Code models it already classifies. So copying the card 'close to as-is' imports a billing/capability data model for a watcher that neither bills nor executes.
- **Ruling:** Decompose the candidate: (1) fuzzy model search, (2) favorites, (3) Recommended/Free tabs, (4) info card with context-window + max-output + capability badges + pricing, (5) 1M/200K variant switch. Pieces 1-3 and 5 are pure picker UX that fit a local single-user console and have existing primitives to build on (`core.fuzzyFilter`, the provider picker, `core.contextLimitFor`, live `/model`). Piece 5's knowledge already lives in core. Piece 4 splits: context-window + max-output are useful and already partly known to Shepherd, but the pricing table and capability badges (esp. browser-use) are executor/billing concerns Shepherd structurally lacks (no API calls, no secrets, no tool execution). That mix — real underlying need (help the operator pick/switch a model) reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, with the executor-only parts dropped — is the textbook ADAPT, not ADD. It is not DONT_ADD because the core need (choosing/switching a model when spawning or steering) is squarely Shepherd's job and already half-built. It reinforces L1: the Agent Profiles registry is where a curated, favorited, searchable model selector belongs, since a saved agent carries a `model` field; do not stand up a parallel catalog system.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing provider/model picker (New-session modal + the L1 agent-profile editor) rather than build a new catalog. (a) Reuse `core.fuzzyFilter` to rank a model-id list (it's already the folder-search ranker). (b) Add `favoritedModelIds` to cc-config.json with a star toggle that pins favorites to the top of any model picker — mirrors cline's `toggleFavoriteModel`. (c) Recommended/Free as small static curated lists surfaced as tabs in the picker for onboarding. (d) A 'model info' line driven by what Shepherd already knows: `core.contextLimitFor` gives the window (1M vs 200K), plus a small static max-output map; SKIP the pricing table and the browser-use badge (executor/billing, not Shepherd's domain — at most note supports-1M as a tier hint). (e) The 200K<->1M variant switch as a one-click button next to the live `/model` control, using a small `{base200kModelId, base1mModelId}` map and sending the existing `/model <id>` slash command (then refreshing the context-fullness denominator). All new UI affects nothing by default; no network, no secrets.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — ADAPT holds. I tried to push this to DONT_ADD and could not, because the feature's surviving core maps onto verified existing Shepherd primitives and serves the local steer/spawn console directly. Verified in-repo: - core.fuzzyFilter (cc-core.lua:3502) is a generic tokenized-substring ranker over ANY string list — already reused for folder search; trivially applies to a model-id list. No executor needed. - core.contextLimitFor (cc-core.lua:1765–1778) already encodes the 1M-vs-200K knowledge (Opus-4/Sonnet-4 = 1,000,000; else 200K default), and core.nextContextTier/contextFractionFor consume it. The context-window line and the variant tier hint are already half-built in core. - core.modelCommand (cc-core.lua:234) builds `/model <id>`; the live switch is wired in the detail panel (the d-model select + onModelChange, claude-dashboard.lua:3091/4248/4321). So a one-click 200K↔1M button is just emitting an existing slash command with a different id, then refreshing the context denominator. - The current model dropdown is a PLAIN <select> populated only from configured providers' models (claude-dashboard.lua:4321–4331). It has NO fuzzy search, NO favorites, NO info card. So the picker UX is genuinely new value, not redundant with what exists. - grep is clean for pricing/price/cost-per-token/supports-image/browser-use/max-output across cc-core.lua and claude-dashboard.lua — none of the billing/capability data exists. Skeptic checklist outcome: (a) Executor? The kept pieces (fuzzy search, favorites, variant switch, context-window display) need NO agent loop, tool execution, or workspace editing. Only the pricing table + capability badges (esp. browser-use) are executor/billing/API concerns — and the judge ALREADY dropped them. So executor-dependence touches only the excised part. (b) Server/multi-user? No — favoritedModelIds in cc-config.json + small static curated lists are local single-user. (c) Redundant/queued? The /model switch exists, but fuzzy model search, model favorites, and the one-click variant toggle do not. It is adjacent to L1 (Agent Profiles carry a `model` field, todos.md ~256), which is exactly why this REINFORCES L1 rather than standing up a parallel catalog. (d) SDK plumbing? Only cline's pricing/capability catalog is SDK/billing plumbing — already dropped. Because the genuine surviving need (search/favorite/switch the model when spawning or steering a local Claude Code session) is squarely Shepherd's job and already partly implemented, this is the textbook ADD→ADAPT reshape, not DONT_ADD. Shepherd mapping: extend the existing provider/model picker (New-session modal + L1 agent-profile editor) — rank model ids with core.fuzzyFilter; add favoritedModelIds with a star toggle pinning favorites to the top; small static Recommended/Free tabs; a model-info line from core.contextLimitFor + a tiny static max-output map; a 200K↔1M one-click button next to live /model using a {base200kModelId, base1mModelId} map. SKIP the pricing table and the browser-use/capability badges (executor/billing, not Shepherd's domain). All new UI off by default; no network, no secrets.

#### Resume / new / clear / cancel task lifecycle controls  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The bundle's one genuinely novel, transferable piece is RESUME-FROM-HISTORY: reopening a past task's full conversation as the active session. Shepherd cannot rehydrate in-process (no conversation store, no agent loop), but it doesn't need to — the local `claude` CLI on this Mac supports `--resume [sessionId]`, `--continue`, and `--fork-session` (verified via `claude --help`). Shepherd already captures everything required: cc-status.sh records `session_id`, `cwd`, and `transcript_path` per tile, and `core.respawnSpec` (cc-core.lua:2688) already rebuilds a session's editor/project/permissionMode/provider. So "resume that history item" = `spawnSpec` + one `--resume <session_id>` flag in `spawnFlags` (cc-core.lua:3001) — a clean extension of the spawn/respawn primitive Shepherd already owns, and a natural fit for the New Session modal and L1's "spawn from a saved agent." This directly serves operator value: a session went idle/done/closed and you want to pick it back up where it left off without retyping context. The other three controls map onto things Shepherd HAS, confirming the lifecycle vocabulary fits: cancel-mid-stream = the existing `stop` action (KEY_STOP = Escape interrupt, cc-core.lua:136-137); clear/close = the existing `close` action (closeWindow + removeStatus) plus `/clear` ghost-prune; new-task-with-overrides = the New Session modal + providers + permission-mode picker + autopilot. The seed-text/per-task-override surface is exactly L1 Agent Profiles.
- **Against:** Three of the four controls are already shipped, so taking the bundle "as-is" is mostly re-adding what exists: stop (interrupt), close/clear (+ /clear prune), and new-task-with-overrides (modal + spawnFlags + providers + perm-mode + autopilot). Cline's resume is in-process rehydration of a conversation store cline OWNS as the executor — Shepherd structurally cannot do that; it can only RELAUNCH a `claude --resume` window, which is materially weaker (no live rehydration into the panel, depends on the CLI's own resume picker, and the resumed session mints a NEW session_id so tile/label continuity needs care). The "cancel reverts in-flight diff edits first" sub-feature is pure executor/workspace-snapshot territory (checkpoints of files cline edited) — DONT_ADD, Shepherd edits no files. The `--id`/`--data-dir`/auto-sandbox/CI-isolation piece is headless multi-instance/server plumbing for unattended runs — outside Shepherd's single-user GUI scope. So the net-new value is narrow: a single resume-launch flag.
- **Ruling:** Decompose the bundle and judge each part against Shepherd's primitives, not cline's plumbing. (1) New task + per-task overrides → ALREADY present (New Session modal, spawnSpec, providers, live /model, permission-mode picker, autopilot) and the saved-overrides version is queued as L1 — re-adding it is redundant. (2) Clear/close → ALREADY the `close` action + `/clear` ghost-prune. (3) Cancel mid-stream → ALREADY the `stop` action (Escape interrupt); the "revert in-flight diffs first" rider is executor/workspace-snapshot work Shepherd structurally cannot host (DONT_ADD that rider). (4) Resume-from-history → the only piece with real net-new value, and it CLEANLY adapts: the local claude CLI exposes --resume/--continue/--fork-session, the status hook already stores session_id+cwd+transcript_path, and respawnSpec already rebuilds a session — so this is a flag added to the existing spawn/respawn path, not a new subsystem. That makes the overall verdict ADAPT (the underlying NEED — pick a past task back up — is real and reshapes onto Shepherd's spawn primitive), reinforcing L1's spawn-from-agent surface. The --id/--data-dir/CI-sandbox isolation is out of scope (no headless/server surface). Net: build resume-launch, route it through the modal/respawn path, skip the diff-revert and CI-isolation parts.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a "Resume" action on idle/done/closed tiles (and a "Resume past session" entry in the New Session modal / lineage view). It reuses `core.respawnSpec` (cc-core.lua:2688) to rebuild editor+project+permMode+provider, and adds a resume flag to `core.spawnFlags` (cc-core.lua:3001): emit `--resume <session_id>` (session_id is already in the tile status from cc-status.sh; add `--fork-session` as an option so the original transcript stays intact). The launch goes through the existing `spawnSpec` (cc-core.lua:3061) editor-aware dispatch, gated by `spawn.live` dry-run default like every other spawn. Continuity: the resumed window mints a new session_id, so key it back to the same projectKey/cwd via the existing applyLabelsByCwd path so the label/group/respawn-budget carry over, and emit a `respawn`/lineage event (the lineage aggregator at cc-core.lua:1040 already understands respawn churn). On L1, a saved Agent Profile gains an optional "resume last session in this folder" toggle. EXCLUDE: the in-flight diff-revert (no workspace), --data-dir/auto-sandbox CI isolation (no headless/server surface), and any in-panel conversation rehydration (no conversation store).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) verdict holds. I verified the judge's decomposition against the code. (1) New-task+overrides and (2) clear/close and (3) cancel-mid-stream are genuinely already present (New Session modal/providers/live-model/perm-mode picker/autopilot; the `close` action + `/clear`; the `stop`/Escape interrupt), and the "revert in-flight diffs first" rider is executor/workspace-snapshot work Shepherd structurally cannot host (it edits/snapshots nothing) — correctly DONT_ADD. (4) Resume-from-history is the one piece with real net-new value and it cleanly reshapes onto Shepherd's spawn primitive, so it cannot be downgraded to DONT_ADD. Evidence found in-repo: - cc-status.sh already persists everything the resume needs: session_id (line 57, the keying field), cwd (58), permission_mode (91/211), transcript_path (203-205), and projectKey (251-261). So no new data subsystem is required. - The local CLI genuinely supports the flags: `~/.local/bin/claude --help` shows `-r, --resume [value]` (resume by session ID), `-c, --continue`, `--fork-session` (resume into a new session id), and `--session-id <uuid>`. So Shepherd emitting `--resume <session_id>` is identical in kind to how spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) already emits `--permission-mode`/`--remote-control` — native Claude Code owns the resume, Shepherd only emits the flag. - It is NOT redundant with existing respawn: respawnSpec (cc-core.lua:2688) rebuilds editor/cwd/permission_mode/provider but emits NO resume flag and mints a brand-new session, losing the conversation (auto-respawn doc at 2104-2132 confirms it merely relaunches). Adding the resume flag is therefore a real new capability on that path, not pre-existing. - The lineage aggregator the judge cites (lineageByProject, cc-core.lua:1061; LINEAGE_TYPES includes spawn/respawn/clear/continue) already keys by projectKey and would absorb a resumed session's new session_id under the same project, so label/group/budget continuity via applyLabelsByCwd (cc-core.lua:293) is plausible. Why it cannot hold as DONT_ADD and cannot rise to ADD: The feature's headline ("rehydrates that task's full conversation+context") IS the cline in-panel conversation store, which Shepherd lacks (only a ledger of events, no message store — confirmed) — that part is correctly EXCLUDED, along with the diff-revert (no workspace) and the --id/--data-dir/auto-sandbox CI isolation (no headless/server surface). What survives is a thin flag reshape onto an existing spawn/respawn path that reinforces L1's "spawn from a saved agent" surface (todos.md:247) rather than a new subsystem — that is the textbook definition of ADAPT, not ADD (no new surface) and not DONT_ADD (the residual is real, feasible, non-redundant, and in-scope). The judge's Shepherd mapping (resume action on idle/done/closed tiles + a resume flag in spawnFlags routed through spawnSpec, gated by the dry-run default) is accurate to the code.

#### Spawn a role-prompted teammate / sub-agent (team_spawn_teammate, spawn_agent)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the single closest external corroboration of the user's #1 stated priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory." cline's team_spawn_teammate(agentId, rolePrompt, model, maxIterations) is mechanically a saved-role + role-system-prompt + model + spawn — which is EXACTLY Shepherd's L1 "spawn from a saved agent" action: rolePrompt -> the {role,goal,backstory} block rendered into --append-system-prompt; model -> ANTHROPIC_MODEL via providerEnv; agentId -> a cc-agents.json profile id. The mechanical seams already exist and are unit-tested: spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061), spawnFlags (3001, already emits --append-system-prompt), providerEnv (2714, injects the model), and the preset CRUD (presetList/presetPush 3658+) that L1 widens into the profile registry. The "tag it as a teammate / parent_session" piece lands on L4's already-specced parent/delegation lineage stamp (todos.md L487-492). The local claude CLI supports --append-system-prompt/--agent/--model, so the spawn-flag wiring is feasible today. This is a high-value, low-novelty reinforcement, not a new system.
- **Against:** cline's team_spawn_teammate creates a "full delegated agent with its own tools/context" and spawn_agent runs a "focused background sub-agent" — i.e. an in-process agent loop that executes tools and reports a result back to the lead in the same process. That delegated-executor semantics (maxIterations bounding an internal loop, the teammate running headless and returning structured output) is precisely the cline mismatch: Shepherd runs no agent loop, has no background worker, and cannot give a teammate "its own tools/context" because Claude Code owns execution. The "lead orchestrates a swarm of subordinate executors and collects their outputs" team model is server/multi-agent-orchestrator plumbing Shepherd structurally can't host. If copied literally — a background sub-agent that the lead awaits — it is DONT_ADD.
- **Ruling:** Judge the operator pattern, not the executor plumbing. Strip cline's in-process delegation (maxIterations loop, awaited result, teammate-owns-its-tools) — which Shepherd cannot host — and what remains is "spawn a NEW Claude Code GUI session pre-seeded with a saved role/persona prompt + model + provider, and record it as a child of the spawning session." That is a direct, near-verbatim instance of L1's spawn-from-agent (seedPrompt split into {role,goal,backstory} -> --append-system-prompt; provider/model -> providerEnv; profile id -> cc-agents.json) plus L4's parent/delegation lineage stamp. The spawned session runs the agent loop itself; Shepherd only emits flags and tags lineage — which is exactly its role. It does NOT introduce a new primitive, so it must NOT spawn a parallel "Teams" system; it reinforces L1 (and the handoff angle reinforces L4). Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L1. The one thing to explicitly DROP: any awaited/headless background sub-agent that returns a result to the parent in-process (the spawn_agent variant) — the spawned "teammate" is a normal visible tile the human still watches and steers, not a hidden worker. This is the third+ independent source converging on L1, which raises confidence to high.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 "Spawn from agent" (todos.md:259-262): (1) cc-agents.json profile already carries seedPrompt split into {role,goal,backstory} (L1 crewAI enrichment, L281-283) -> render the role/goal/backstory persona block into --append-system-prompt via spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001); (2) profile model + provider -> ANTHROPIC_MODEL/ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL via providerEnv (2714) and the spawnSpec env list (3061); (3) the "teammate" tag = L4's parent/delegation lineage (todos.md:487-492) — stamp the spawn ledger event with parent_session/spawnedBy when one session's action spawns another, surfaced via existing session-lineage/groups. A "Spawn teammate from <profile> as child of this session" right-click action on a tile is the concrete UI affordance, dry-run by default and gated by spawn.live. Explicitly DROP: maxIterations/in-process loop, the awaited background sub-agent (spawn_agent), and any teammate-owns-its-own-tool-sandbox semantics — Claude Code owns execution; the teammate is a normal watched/steerable tile, not a hidden worker.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) ruling holds. I tried each skeptic path against the actual code and the roadmap, and none lands as a DONT_ADD. (a) Executor-needed? The cline feature DOES carry executor plumbing — the maxIterations loop, the awaited result, teammate-owns-its-own-tool-sandbox, and the spawn_agent headless background sub-agent that returns a result in-process. Shepherd runs no agent loop, edits nothing, executes nothing (context.md:13 "no network, no multi-user, no secrets"; it only reads ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json). But the judge already strips ALL of those and lists them as explicit DROPs. What remains is not executor work: emit a spawn with a role/persona prompt + model + provider env and stamp lineage. That is pure flag emission + a visible tile + a ledger stamp — Shepherd's actual role. (b) Server/multi-user? No. Lead-spawns-teammate is local single-user; no hub/tenants. Correctly NOT a parallel "Teams" system — the judge says so. (c) Redundant or already-shipped? This is the strongest angle and it points to ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. The residual is near-verbatim L1: seedPrompt split into {role,goal,backstory} -> --append-system-prompt (todos.md:281-283), provider/model -> providerEnv (todos.md:259-262), profile id -> cc-agents.json (todos.md:254). Crucially it is NOT redundant with shipped code: I grepped both cc-core.lua and claude-dashboard.lua and found ZERO occurrences of --append-system-prompt / seedPrompt / cc-agents / --add-dir / --agent / --mcp-config. The shipped spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001-3009) emits ONLY --permission-mode and --remote-control. So this reinforces a QUEUED item (L1), not a re-add of an existing one. The handoff angle reinforces L4's parent/delegation lineage (todos.md:487-492), which rides the existing ledger 'by' convention (cc-core.lua:675, 1169) and lineageByProject (cc-core.lua:1040-1098). (d) SDK plumbing mistaken for a feature? Only the spawn_agent variant (in-process awaited background worker) is plumbing — and the judge drops it. The team_spawn_teammate operator pattern (a lead spawns a visible, watched, steerable role-prompted session recorded as a child) is a real operator affordance. The infrastructure the judge cited is all real and present: presetPush/presetList/presetRemove registry CRUD (cc-core.lua:3658-3722) as the L1 prior art, templateList/templatePush (3590), providerEnv (2714, builds ANTHROPIC_MODEL/ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL), spawnSpec (3061), spawnFlags (3001), and the L4 Delegate/handoff path (todos.md:460-467). The local claude CLI supports the needed flags, so the wiring is feasible. Conclusion: every refutation route either attacks the executor parts the judge already excised or confirms the residual maps onto L1 (+L4). It introduces no new primitive. This is a third+ independent source converging on L1, which is precisely an ADD->ADAPT reinforcement, not a hold-as-ADD and not a DONT_ADD. Verdict stands: ADAPT, reinforces L1.

#### File-based agent/automation specs in a watched directory (markdown + YAML frontmatter, live-reload)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the cleanest, most literal match in cline to the user's stated #1 priority — "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." cline's pattern is: author a spec as a plain markdown file with YAML frontmatter (id, title, model, mode, tools, timeout, tags, extensions, systemPrompt) plus a markdown body as the prompt, drop it in a watched directory, and a debounced recursive filesystem watcher live-reconciles edits/adds/deletes into the running app — with invalid files PERSISTED as parse_status='invalid' instead of silently dropped. That maps almost field-for-field onto L1's agent-profile schema (name/folder/provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt/skills[]/mcpServers[]/systemPrompt) and L3's "definition SOURCE directory," and it converges with two enrichments already in the backlog: the OpenHands dual-shape skills enumerator (flat *.md vs SKILL.md frontmatter) and the schema-drift "keep the valid, drop only the bad, RETURN the dropped count" loader. Crucially, the live-reload substrate is NOT foreign to Shepherd: it already runs a debounced hs.pathwatcher over the status dir gated by the pure core.watcherShouldRefresh, and ledgerSnapshot already does fingerprint/mtime-signature cache invalidation (core.ledgerCacheStale) so it re-reads only on change. loadConfig already re-reads from disk on every call. So a generic "config-directory watcher emitting upsert/remove/error with content-fingerprint dedupe" over ~/.claude/agents is a generalization of machinery Shepherd ALREADY runs, not a new subsystem — and it upgrades L1/L3 from author-by-modal/read-on-tick to author-by-file with instant hot-reload into the panel, which is genuinely better operator UX for a directory of saved agents.
- **Against:** The HEADLINE of the feature — the one that gives the file format its name (*.cron.md, the `schedule` field, daily-code-review.cron.md) — is a scheduler that FIRES an agent run on a cron/event trigger. That is squarely the cline mismatch: it needs a firing engine plus an agent loop to run the reconciled spec, neither of which Shepherd has. Shepherd runs no agent loop, executes no tools, and — decisively here — has NO scheduling/cron/timer-driven-spawn concept anywhere in the code or the L1-L6 backlog (grep for schedul/cron/recurring/launchd returns nothing relevant). The `mode`/`tools`/`timeout`/`extensions` frontmatter fields and the "config-directory watcher as the substrate for hot-reloading skills/rules/workflows/plugins/hooks" exist to feed cline's in-process executor: hot-reloading a *plugin* or a *hook* only matters if your process is the thing that loads and runs them. Shepherd emits launch flags to a native `claude` it spawns; it does not host plugins/hooks/extensions in-process, so most of the unified-watcher's reason for existing doesn't transfer. Taken whole, the feature is an executor-config + scheduler-fires-agent system; only the authoring/format/live-reload slice is Shepherd-shaped.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature and weigh each half against the cline mismatch. (1) The cron/scheduled-firing half (the *.cron.md spec running an agent on a timer) is DONT_ADD on its own merits — it requires a firing engine and an agent loop Shepherd structurally lacks, and there is no scheduled-spawn primitive in the backlog to attach it to. (2) The file-based-spec + watched-directory live-reload half is a strong, direct fit: it is the literal shape of the user's #1 stated priority and it reinforces L1's cc-agents.json registry and L3's definition-source directory, with two of its distinctive details (invalid-but-retained loading; dual-shape frontmatter parsing) ALREADY captured as L1 enrichments. Because the dominant headline value depends on the executor/scheduler Shepherd can't host, the verdict cannot be ADD. Because the underlying NEED (author saved agents/skills as files in a directory, hot-reloaded into the running console) is real and reshapes cleanly onto Shepherd primitives that mostly already exist (pathwatcher + watcherShouldRefresh + fingerprint-cache + disk-fresh loadConfig + the L1/L3 registries), the verdict is ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (and L3). Confidence is high: the architecture facts are confirmed in-repo (loadConfig re-reads disk; hs.pathwatcher + core.watcherShouldRefresh at dashboard:5787/core:2423; core.ledgerCacheStale fingerprinting; no cron anywhere; L1/L3 already name a definition-source directory and dual-shape skills loading).
- **How (Shepherd):** Build the AUTHORING + LIVE-RELOAD slice; drop the scheduler/cron-firing and the in-process plugin/hook/extension hot-load. Concretely: (a) Add a configurable definition-source dir (e.g. ~/.claude/agents, already named in L3) where each saved agent/template/skill is a markdown file with YAML-ish frontmatter + a body-as-prompt — feeding L1's agentList/agentPush and L3's templates. Since cc-core stays pure and there's no YAML parser, parse via a small pure core.splitFrontmatter (key:value lines + body) — NOT full YAML — mirroring cline's splitFrontmatter, and emit a parsed record {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt/{role,goal,backstory}, skills[], mcpServers[], systemPrompt, parse_status}. (b) Generalize the existing debounced hs.pathwatcher (claude-dashboard.lua:5787) from STATUS_DIR to also watch the agents dir, gated by a generalized core.watcherShouldRefresh, so edits/adds/deletes hot-reconcile the in-memory registry on the next 1Hz-ish tick; reuse the core.ledgerCacheStale mtime/size-fingerprint pattern to skip re-parse when unchanged. (c) Adopt cline's invalid-but-retained posture by extending the OpenHands-corroborated "keep the valid, drop only the bad, RETURN dropped count+names" loader already specced under L1 — surface parse_status='invalid' rows with their parse error in the agents card + a one-time alert + by:'config-drift' ledger event, instead of silent drop. (d) The schema-drift / dual-shape concerns (flat *.md vs frontmatter file, fall back to file stem for name) are the L1 dual-shape skills enumerator — same code path. DROP: the `schedule` field and any timer-fired agent run (no scheduler in Shepherd); the unified-watcher-as-host-for-plugins/hooks/extensions (Shepherd emits flags, doesn't load these in-process); `mode`/`tools`/`timeout` as executor knobs (Shepherd's analogs are permMode switch + L2 gate bundles, not file-driven executor config).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT(high)→reinforces L1 verdict holds. The judge's split is correct on verified in-repo facts. (1) The cron/scheduled-firing half is genuinely un-hostable — Shepherd runs no agent loop and has no firing engine; the only "schedule" in cc-core.lua:433 is the keystroke-injection beat scheduler, and there is no doAt/cron/timer-fired-agent anywhere, nor any scheduled-spawn primitive in L1-L6 to attach it to. That half is correctly DROPPED, so the verdict cannot be ADD. (2) The file-based-spec + watched-directory live-reload half is a real, single-user, local need that maps cleanly onto existing primitives: L3 (todos.md:400-404) already names ~/.claude/agents as a configurable definition source feeding the spawn modal (the user's #1 priority), and L1 already captures cline's two distinctive details — the dual-shape frontmatter skills enumerator with file-stem fallback (todos.md:307-310) and the invalid-but-retained "keep the valid, drop only the bad, RETURN dropped count+names" loader with a by:'config-drift' ledger event (todos.md:323-326). The live-reload substrate exists in-repo: hs.pathwatcher.new(STATUS_DIR) gated by core.watcherShouldRefresh (claude-dashboard.lua:5787, cc-core.lua:2423), loadConfig re-reads disk fresh, and core.ledgerCacheStale (cc-core.lua:1362) gives the mtime/size fingerprint to skip unchanged re-parse. The tempting DONT_ADD refutation ("redundant with L1/L3") overshoots: L1/L3 today specify a one-shot scan at spawn-modal time, NOT a debounced watcher hot-reconciling edits/adds/deletes into the running registry — and the pathwatcher currently watches only STATUS_DIR. Generalizing it to the agents dir is a concrete, non-redundant reinforcement of L1/L3, the literal definition of ADAPT. The would-be ADD value depends on the executor/scheduler Shepherd can't host (forces below ADD); the underlying authoring + live-reload need is real, requires no agent loop / tool execution / workspace editing / multi-user serving, and reshapes onto primitives that mostly already exist (forces above DONT_ADD). ADAPT reinforcing L1 (and L3), with the scheduler/cron and the in-process plugin/hook/extension hot-loader dropped, is correct.

#### Per-run run controls (provider/model/key/system-prompt/thinking/compaction/retries/timeout + verbose stats)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** These are exactly the operator-facing per-launch knobs a spawn/steer console wants, and the cline flag catalog is a useful completeness checklist for Shepherd's spawn surface. The NEED is real and almost entirely already exists in Shepherd's primitives: provider/model -> provider profiles + live /model + spawnProviderKey; system prompt -> --append-system-prompt already wired through spawnInner/spawnFlags; thinking none|low|medium|high|xhigh -> the live /effort switch with the SAME level vocabulary (EFFORT_LEVELS = low/medium/high/xhigh) plus the New-modal effort picker; retries/timeout -> bounded auto-continue + the stuck-session watchdog (both already shipped, off by default); compaction -> existing clear/compact actions + auto-compact modeling; verbose tokens/elapsed -> sumUsage/formatTokens/usageInWindow + the per-tile context bar. So adopting it is mostly cataloguing existing controls into one coherent per-spawn surface and folding them into the L1 Agent Profiles record (provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt + a thinking field) so a saved agent launches with its knobs preset. The only genuinely-new sub-item, est cost, is a small price-table addition on top of token counts Shepherd already parses.
- **Against:** Taken literally this is a cline CLI-argument surface (commander flags + commanderToParsedArgs last-wins/invalid normalization + printRunStats), and that arg-parsing/run-config layer is bound to cline's in-process executor: the values configure the agent loop cline RUNS (the LLM call, retries on the agent's consecutive mistakes, the run timeout it enforces) and printRunStats reports a run cline EXECUTED. Shepherd runs no loop, so retries-as-consecutive-mistakes and a hard run timeout have no native meaning beyond the proxies already shipped (auto-continue/watchdog) and shouldn't be reimplemented as agent-control. -k key (a key override) directly violates Shepherd's hard NO-secrets-storage limit -- Shepherd only selects a provider profile naming $ENV vars resolved by the spawned shell, never stores a key. The commander parsing/validation layer doesn't transfer; the modal already validates its own inputs. And every transferable piece is already shipped or queued, so building this as a standalone per-run controls feature would be redundant and risk a parallel system competing with L1.
- **Ruling:** Apply the cline mismatch: this candidate's literal form is a per-invocation arg surface for cline's executor, and its run-config/validation/run-stats plumbing is tied to RUNNING the agent (retries on consecutive mistakes, run timeout, printRunStats) -- none of which Shepherd hosts. But the OPERATOR PATTERN underneath -- choose provider/model/system-prompt/thinking/compaction per launch and see token usage -- maps almost one-for-one onto controls Shepherd already has (provider profiles, live /model, --append-system-prompt, /effort with the identical low/medium/high/xhigh vocabulary, clear/compact, sumUsage). So it is not DONT_ADD (the value does not depend on the loop -- the proxies exist) and not ADD (building it as-is would duplicate shipped controls, reimplement the loop-bound retries/timeout, and -k would breach the no-secrets rule). The correct shape is ADAPT, reinforcing L1: fold these knobs into the saved Agent Profile record and the existing spawn/steer surfaces; drop -k (provider-profile env only); express retries/timeout strictly via the existing bounded auto-continue + watchdog, not as agent-loop control; add only est-cost as net-new.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 Agent Profiles: add per-profile/per-spawn fields {provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt(->--append-system-prompt), thinking(->/effort low|medium|high|xhigh via EFFORT_LEVELS/effortCommand)} so a saved agent launches preset; provider/model resolve via existing spawnProviderKey/providerById + live /model; system prompt via the spawnInner [prompt]/spawnFlags --append-system-prompt path. compaction policy -> a per-spawn enum routed to the existing clear/compact actions + auto-compact (usageBarLevel) modeling. retries/timeout -> reuse the SHIPPED bounded auto-continue + stuck-session watchdog (no new agent-loop control). verbose stats -> the existing per-tile context bar + sumUsage/formatTokens/usageInWindow, adding only an est-cost figure from a per-model price table (small L5-flavored detail-panel addition). DROP -k key: Shepherd stores no secrets; selecting a provider profile that names $ENV vars (providerEnv) is the only key path.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. I verified every proxy the judge cites against the source. The durable operator knobs map one-for-one onto SHIPPED Shepherd controls, so the value does NOT depend on the agent loop (rules out DONT_ADD): thinking low|medium|high|xhigh is the EXACT vocabulary of M.EFFORT_LEVELS (cc-core.lua:223) driven live by effortCommand; model by modelCommand/--model + spawnProviderKey/providerById; provider by the providers registry + providerEnv (cc-core.lua:2618-2735); system-prompt via spawnFlags' --append-system-prompt path (cc-core.lua:3001); compaction via the existing clear/compact + auto-compact (usageBarLevel) modeling; verbose stats via sumUsage/usageInWindow/formatTokens (cc-core.lua:1684-1727) feeding the existing context bar. These same fields {provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt, thinking} are ALREADY enumerated in the queued L1 Agent Profiles schema (todos.md:256), so it reinforces L1 rather than spawning a parallel system. But building it literally (ADD) is wrong on three counts: (1) the literal surface duplicates shipped live /model, /effort, --append-system-prompt and clear/compact; (2) --retries (max consecutive MISTAKES), the run --timeout, and printRunStats are bound to cline's in-process EXECUTOR running the agent loop — Shepherd runs no loop, so these must instead reuse the SHIPPED bounded autoContinue.maxAttempts/delaySeconds + the stuck-session watchdog, not be reimplemented as agent-loop control; (3) -k key override would breach the documented no-secrets scope limit (context.md:13) — provider profiles name $ENV vars only. I also confirmed NO cost/price logic exists anywhere in the codebase, so est-cost is the one genuinely net-new piece (a small per-model price-table detail-panel addition). Net: not DONT_ADD (value survives the mismatch via real proxies), not ADD (as-is duplicates controls, reimplements loop-bound retries/timeout, and -k breaks no-secrets) — ADAPT into L1, dropping -k and routing retries/timeout to the existing auto-continue+watchdog.

#### Configured/saved agents loaded from a directory (markdown + YAML frontmatter, layered, spawnable)  `[H/L]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** This is the single cleanest prior art for the user's explicitly stated #1 priority — "pre-configured 'agents' we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." The feature is an operator-config pattern, not agent execution: agents are declarative records (name, description, tools[], skills[], providerId/modelId, cwd, maxIterations, system-prompt body) stored on local disk and turned into spawnable presets. That maps one-to-one onto Shepherd's L1 Agent Profiles registry: profile fields (name/folder/provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt/skills[]/mcpServers[]) are a near-superset, and "becomes a callable subagent_<name> / list_agent_presets" is exactly Shepherd's "Spawn from a saved agent" action threading selections into spawnFlags (--agent/--agents/--append-system-prompt/--add-dir, all confirmed present on the local claude CLI). Three sub-design elements are immediately transferable and sharpen the existing spec: (1) the LAYERED directory with override-by-name (bundled < global < project) is a cleaner storage model than L1's current single cc-agents.json and directly realizes the L3 "definition SOURCE / saved into a directory" sub-item (~/.claude/agents scanned with the rg/fd accelerators); (2) "deduped + validated + load errors surfaced" maps precisely onto L1's already-specified pre-flight validator + the OpenHands-corroborated "schema-drift / fail-safe loading: keep the valid, drop only the bad, return dropped count+names, raise a one-time alert + config-drift ledger event"; (3) markdown + YAML-frontmatter as the on-disk format aligns with Shepherd's existing ~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md frontmatter enumerator. It needs no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing, no server — pure cc-core CRUD + FX file reads + spawn-flag emission, all of which Shepherd already does for presets/providers.
- **Against:** The candidate's framing carries two executor-side assumptions that DON'T transfer and must be stripped, which is why this is ADAPT not ADD. First, "become callable subagent_<name> tools" presumes an in-process tool registry where a running parent agent invokes a child agent as a tool — Shepherd runs no agent loop and exposes no tool surface, so there is no "callable tool"; the only honest Shepherd realization is "spawn a GUI session pre-seeded from this profile" (native Claude Code, not Shepherd, owns any real subagent/Task-tool dispatch). Second, fields like maxIterations and tools[] are agent-loop knobs Shepherd cannot enforce — maxIterations has no meaning when Shepherd never runs the loop (drop it, or keep only as an informational note), and tools[] must be carefully re-mapped: Shepherd's gate is a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL escalation list, NOT a capability allow-list, so a naive "grant these tools" import is backwards (a read-only agent is built via autoDeny of the mutating set + an L2 bundle, never by seeding gate.tools). There's also a redundancy risk: native Claude Code already reads .claude/agents and its own agent definitions, so Shepherd must stay a thin fleet-convenience layer (emit flags/files) and not reimplement the agent runtime or duplicate Claude Code's own loader. Finally it's not a new capability — it's the same need L1 already captures from LiteLLM/crewAI/OpenHands, so it adds confirmation and design detail rather than a parallel feature; treating it as ADD would risk building a second agent system.
- **Ruling:** The fit is unusually strong because the candidate is pure operator-config: a directory of declarative agent definitions that get loaded, validated, and turned into spawn presets — which is precisely what Shepherd's queued L1 (Agent Profiles registry + "Spawn from a saved agent") already proposes, and which the L1 source line quotes as the user's #1 stated interest verbatim. Nothing about the loading/validation/spawn-preset half depends on running the agent, executing tools, editing files, or a server, so the cline mismatch does NOT block it. Verdict is ADAPT (not ADD) for two reasons: (a) it reinforces an already-queued item rather than introducing new capability — per the rules, a match to L1-L6 is ADAPT with reinforces set; and (b) the candidate's execution-flavored framing (subagent_<name> callable tools, maxIterations, a tools[] allow-list) must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives — spawn-a-pre-seeded-session instead of call-a-tool, drop/inform-only maxIterations, and map "tools" through Shepherd's escalation-gate + L2 bundles (autoDeny), not as a grant list. The genuinely additive contribution over the current L1 text is the storage/loading design: layered bundled/global/project directories of YAML-frontmatter markdown with override-by-name and surfaced load errors — a cleaner, more concrete model than L1's single cc-agents.json, and it directly realizes the L3 "definition SOURCE" sub-item. I'd fold those refinements into L1 rather than spec a new feature. Effort is L: it's the registry + spawn-from-agent + skills enumerator + validator work L1 already estimates at L, with the directory-scan/layered-loader being a modest addition reusing the existing folderScan accelerators and the SKILL.md frontmatter-parse pattern.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1 (Agent Profiles registry). Concretely: (1) Storage — adopt the candidate's layered directory model as L1's definition SOURCE: scan ~/.claude/agents (+ optional project .claude/agents) for *.md with YAML frontmatter using the existing folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin rg/fd (grep/find fallback) and the SKILL.md frontmatter-parse pattern; later sources override earlier by name (bundled < global < project < operator cc-agents.json). Keeps L1's pure cc-core agentList/agentPush/agentRemove (mirrors presetPush/presetList ~3640-3722) as the editable layer. (2) Schema map — frontmatter name/description/providerId/modelId/skills[]/cwd → profile name/description/provider/model/skills[]/folder; system-prompt body → seedPrompt {role,goal,backstory} rendered into --append-system-prompt; tools[] → re-mapped via L2 autoDeny/bundle, NOT gate.tools (escalation list, not allow-list); maxIterations → DROP (no loop) or informational-only. (3) Spawn — "Spawn from agent" via the New Session modal → spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061), threading --agent/--agents/--mcp-config/--append-system-prompt/--add-dir/--plugin-dir through spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001); gated behind spawn.live (dry-run default). NOT a callable subagent_<name> tool — a pre-seeded GUI session. (4) Validation/loading — realize L1's pre-flight validator + the OpenHands "keep-valid/drop-bad, return dropped count+names, raise one-time alert + by:config-drift ledger event" (generalize presetList's per-entry skip ~3658); cross-ref skills[]/mcpServers[]/policyBundle must resolve before any flag emits. (5) Skills/MCP halves reuse L1's FX.listSkills enumerator (~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md) and cc-mcp.json registry as already specced.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails on both sides; ADAPT/reinforces-L1 stands. NOT DONT_ADD: The feature is pure operator-config — load declarative agent-definition files from a directory, validate them, surface load errors, turn them into spawn presets. None of that touches the cline mismatch (no agent loop, no tool execution, no file editing/snapshotting, no server). It is the loading/validation/spawn-preset half, which is exactly Shepherd's queued L1 (Agent Profiles registry + "Spawn from a saved agent") and the user's verbatim #1 stated priority ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory"). The execution-flavored framing the candidate ships (callable subagent_<name> tools, maxIterations, tools[] allow-list) is correctly reshaped by the judge onto Shepherd primitives: spawn-a-pre-seeded GUI session via spawnSpec/spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3061/3001) instead of a callable tool; drop maxIterations (no loop); map tools[] through the L2 autoDeny/bundle, NOT gate.tools — todos.md line 236-239 confirms gate.tools is a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL list, so a read-only agent is built via autoDeny, never an allow-list. So it can't drop to DONT_ADD. NOT ADD: The candidate introduces no new capability over what's already queued, so it can't be promoted. The judge's only stated additive contribution is the "layered directory of YAML-frontmatter markdown, scanned, override-by-name." But todos.md ALREADY specifies the directory source: L3 lines 400-404 (explicitly tagged "the local agent-definition directory that feeds L1") declares a configurable local dir (~/.claude/agents or user-set) scanned with the existing folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin rg/fd (grep/find fallback) producing records that feed the spawn modal + spawnFlags — verbatim what the judge's mapping says to "adopt." Frontmatter parsing is already in scope (L1 lines 266-267, 307-310, dual-shape over SKILL.md/*.md). Per-agent ~/.claude/agents/<name>/CONTEXT.md is already there (lines 304-305). Fail-safe keep-valid/drop-bad loading with a returned dropped count + one-time alert + by:config-drift ledger event is already specced (lines 323-326). The pre-flight cross-ref validator (skills[]/mcpServers[]/policyBundle must resolve before any flag emits) is already specced (lines 290-298). The precedence/override idiom is the established resolver pattern (line 346-347). Notably line 405 explicitly chooses "no YAML parser" for the import path, so the candidate's YAML-frontmatter premise conflicts with a stated L1/L3 design decision — further evidence it's a re-skin of an already-decided sub-item, not new ground. Net: the ONLY genuinely net-new sliver is multi-LAYER precedence (bundled < global < project < operator JSON) with override-by-name — a thin loader refinement, not a new feature or capability. That makes this a reinforcement of L1, which by the rules is ADAPT with reinforces set, not ADD. Effort is L (registry + spawn-from-agent + skills enumerator + validator that L1 already estimates at L; the layered-loader is a modest add reusing folderScan accelerators + the SKILL.md frontmatter-parse pattern). The judge's ruling — ADAPT (high), reinforces L1 — is correct; I could not refute it. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L1 lines 247-334; L3 definition-SOURCE lines 387-404; load-bearing facts lines 228-245), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (presetList/presetPush ~3658/3673, spawnSpec 3061, spawnFlags 3001, folderScanShellCommand 3474, resolveGateTools precedence ~2537).

#### Per-schedule agent configuration descriptor (model/mode/tools/system-prompt/extensions/notes)  `[H/L]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The descriptor is exactly a saved spawn profile emitted as flags — which is precisely what Shepherd's #1 queued item (L1 Agent Profiles registry) already is, and the user's stated #1 interest ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs"). Almost every field maps 1:1 onto primitives Shepherd has or has queued: provider+model -> L1 {provider,model} + live /model; mode (act/plan/yolo) -> permMode, and Plan/Act-style mode switching ALREADY EXISTS; systemPrompt override -> L1 seedPrompt/{role,goal,backstory} rendered into --append-system-prompt; extensions (skills/plugins/MCP) -> L1 skills[]/plugins[]/mcpServers[] emitted as --agent/--plugin-dir/--mcp-config; notesDirectory for durable cross-run memory -> L1's per-agent contextDir/memoryFile (~/.claude/agents/<name>/CONTEXT.md) -> --add-dir. The local claude CLI supports every one of those flags, so emitting them is feasible and squarely on the "Shepherd emits flags/files; native Claude Code owns execution" side of the line. The descriptor itself needs NO agent loop and NO tool execution — it is pure config + a spawn-flag builder (spawnFlags/spawnSpec already exist). The candidate's own note concedes this: "descriptor FIELDS = a saved spawn profile emitted as flags; tool-policy/extension resolution at runtime is owned by the native runtime." That is the transferable half.
- **Against:** The feature is framed as PER-SCHEDULE — it presupposes a cron/scheduler that fires the agent on a timer, and Shepherd has NO scheduling primitive at all (grep confirms zero cron/recurring/time-of-day spawn logic; runSequence is only a keystroke-stagger ladder). The descriptor's whole reason for existing in cline is to bundle the agent setup for an UNATTENDED, periodic, executor-driven fire — i.e. it leans on the in-process agent loop that Shepherd structurally lacks. Two of its fields, maxIterations and timeoutSeconds, are pure executor loop-control knobs with NO Claude Code launch-flag or native counterpart Shepherd can emit — Shepherd watches status files, it cannot cap an agent's iteration count or enforce a per-run timeout on a session it does not run. And the "tool allow-list" field is backwards relative to Shepherd's documented gate semantics: gate.tools is a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL / escalation list, not a capability allow-list (tools NOT in it run freely), so porting an allow-list verbatim would invert the safety model. Taken as a self-contained "per-schedule descriptor," it is mostly redundant with L1 and partly un-implementable.
- **Ruling:** Strip the "per-schedule" framing and this is the L1 Agent Profiles registry, full stop — a saved spawn profile (provider/model/permMode/systemPrompt/skills/plugins/MCP/contextDir) emitted as concrete claude CLI flags, which is both queued and the user's top stated interest. So the NEED is real and already captured: ADAPT, reinforcing L1 (with the tool-policy slice belonging to L2's named bundles, not an allow-list). It is NOT a parallel system and must not be built as one. What does NOT transfer: (1) the scheduler that fires it is a separate, currently-nonexistent capability — judge that candidate on its own, do not smuggle a cron in via the descriptor; (2) maxIterations/timeoutSeconds are executor knobs with no flag to emit (DROP — Shepherd can't enforce loop bounds on a session Claude Code drives); (3) "tool allow-list" must be reshaped into an L2 hold-for-approval bundle, not copied as a grant list (the gate-semantics invariant is explicit that allow-list phrasing is backwards). The honest verdict: the descriptor's value to Shepherd is entirely the L1/L2 saved-profile work already on the roadmap; adopting it adds nothing new except two un-emittable fields and a scheduling assumption Shepherd does not satisfy.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L1's profile schema and spawnFlags emitter rather than building a parallel "schedule descriptor": provider/model/permMode/seedPrompt already in the L1 record (cc-agents.json); extensions -> L1 skills[]/plugins[]/mcpServers[] resolved by the config-by-ref resolver into --agent/--plugin-dir/--mcp-config (spawnFlags cc-core.lua:3001, spawnSpec :3061); notesDirectory -> L1's per-agent contextDir/memoryFile -> --add-dir (todos.md:304). Tool policy rides L2 named bundles attached to the profile (NOT an allow-list — autoDeny the mutating set per the gate-semantics invariant). DROP maxIterations/timeoutSeconds (no Claude Code launch flag exists; Shepherd cannot bound a loop it does not run). The "per-schedule" wrapper is out of scope for this card — Shepherd has no scheduler primitive; that is a separate candidate to judge on its own (a timer that, when present, would simply call the existing FX.spawnSession with an L1 profile's resolved flags).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed — ADAPT (reinforces L1) holds. Stripping the "per-schedule" framing, the descriptor's PAYLOAD is a saved spawn profile emitted as launch flags, which is exactly the queued L1 Agent Profiles registry (cc-agents.json: {name, folder, provider, model, permMode, seedPrompt, skills[], mcpServers[], plugins[], policyBundle}, todos.md:247-334) feeding the existing spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) / spawnSpec (cc-core.lua:3061) emitter — and it matches the user's #1 stated interest verbatim ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs"). So it is neither new (rules out ADD) nor sinkable (rules out DONT_ADD). Field-by-field: provider/model/mode/systemPrompt/extensions(rules/skills/plugins) -> L1 record resolved by the config-by-ref resolver into --agent/--plugin-dir/--mcp-config/--append-system-prompt; notesDirectory -> L1's per-agent contextDir/memoryFile -> --add-dir (todos.md:304); all flags VERIFIED present in the real claude 2.1.175 CLI on this Mac. The four DONT_ADD trigger checks all come back negative for the descriptor core: (b) no server/multi-user; (c) the SAVED PROFILE REGISTRY is queued (L1), not shipped — only Plan/Act mode-switch and autopilot are shipped, so it's ADAPT-into-L1, not redundant; (d) "parser validates tools/extensions; runner translates into session start request" is the L1 resolver + spawnFlags emitter, an operator-console feature, not SDK plumbing. The only executor-smell (trigger a) is the two knobs maxIterations/timeoutSeconds — and the judge already DROPs them correctly: I verified the interactive launch path has NO --max-turns/--max-iterations and no session --timeout flag, and the lone --max-budget-usd works ONLY with --print (headless), which Shepherd never uses (it spawns GUI sessions). The "tool allow-list" must be reshaped into an L2 autoDeny bundle per the explicit gate-semantics invariant (todos.md:236-239: gate.tools is a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL/escalation list, NOT a capability grant list — a read-only agent is autoDeny of the mutating set, never an allow-list) — the judge maps this to L2, correct. Finally the "per-schedule/cron" wrapper assumes a scheduler primitive Shepherd does not have (no cron/scheduler in todos.md or context.md; only event/level-triggered dispatch) — the judge correctly excises it as a separate candidate rather than smuggling a cron in via the descriptor. Every load-bearing claim in the ruling and mapping verified against the source. Refutation to DONT_ADD or up to ADD is unsupported; ADAPT reinforcing L1 (tool slice on L2, drop the two executor knobs, scheduler is a separate card) stands.

#### Ephemeral git worktree per card/run (parallel isolation + symlinked node_modules)  `[M/M]`  · reinforces L1
- **For:** The prompt's own carve-out — "creating a detached worktree at SPAWN time and pointing a session's cwd/--add-dir at it (native runtime does the editing)" — lands squarely on a layer Shepherd already owns. `spawnInner` builds `cd <project> && claude <flags>` where `project` is just the cwd; swapping that cwd for a freshly-created `git worktree add --detach` path is a small pure-logic change plus one `fx` effect (shell out to git), exactly the spawn-flag/cwd wiring Shepherd already does (the local `claude` CLI even supports `--add-dir`). Crucially this is the real fix for a problem Shepherd already ships but cannot solve: `core.collisions` flags 2+ active sessions sharing a repo and the comment admits "Detection only -- we can't lock another process's writes." A worktree-per-spawn turns that amber warning into actual isolation, and it makes Shepherd's parallel project-routing (E, shipped) safe to fan out across the same repo without clobbering. A saved "isolate this agent in a worktree" toggle is a natural field on an L1 Agent Profile / spawn preset.
- **Against:** Most of the cline feature's value is executor plumbing Shepherd structurally cannot host. The symlinked-node_modules / reinstall-avoidance only pays off if something is running installs and builds inside the worktree — Shepherd runs no agent loop, edits no files, and runs no commands, so that half is dead weight. The "parallel cards run isolated" benefit is owned by the native Claude Code session, not Shepherd; Shepherd just spawns a window. And worktree LIFECYCLE is a genuine mismatch: cline's runtime knows when a run ends and can GC the worktree, but Shepherd only watches status files off the filesystem and never reliably owns the session's death (it can't cleanly know when to `git worktree remove`), so it risks orphaning worktrees + detached commits. Same-dir collisions are also rarer in Shepherd's single-user model than in cline's team/hub world, weakening the motivating pain.
- **Ruling:** cline's worktree feature is two-thirds executor plumbing (symlinked deps for the agent's builds, snapshot/checkpoint integration, the parallel-run loop) that Shepherd can't host. But the prompt deliberately isolates the one operator-facing slice — make a detached worktree at spawn and point the session's cwd/--add-dir at it — and that slice is a clean fit: it plugs into the existing spawnSpec/spawnInner cwd path, uses a CLI flag Shepherd already wires, and is the missing remedy for the collision warning Shepherd already detects but can only flag. That's textbook ADAPT (reshape onto the spawn primitive, don't copy the executor). It reinforces L1 (a saved per-agent "isolate in worktree" option) and operationalizes the shipped collision warning + project routing. Not ADD because the as-is feature is built around file-editing/build isolation Shepherd lacks; not DONT_ADD because the spawn-cwd slice is real, feasible, and uniquely useful. Confidence medium: the spawn wiring is easy, but worktree lifecycle/GC is awkward when Shepherd never authoritatively owns session death.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a spawn-time option to spawnSpec/spawnInner: when set, an `fx.gitWorktree(repo, key)` effect runs `git worktree add --detach ~/.claude/cc-worktrees/<projectKey>-<n>` (pure helper `core.worktreePathFor` + `core.worktreeAddCmd` building the quoted command; the dashboard shells it out via the deadlock-safe redirect pattern). spawnSpec then uses that path as `project` (the cd target) and optionally adds `--add-dir <mainRepo>` so the session can still read the source tree. Surface a checkbox in the New-session modal and a field on an L1 Agent Profile ("isolate in a worktree"). Label such tiles (e.g. a ⑂ badge) so the operator knows the cwd is a worktree, and add a right-click "remove worktree" once the session is done/idle (operator-driven GC, since Shepherd can't reliably auto-detect session death) plus a startup sweep that lists `git worktree list` orphans under cc-worktrees/. Skip the symlinked-node_modules half entirely — that only matters to an executor running builds. Everything off by default.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT and could not; it holds. The refutation traps don't apply to the operator-facing slice the prompt deliberately isolates. (a) Executor: the as-is feature is mostly executor plumbing (symlinked node_modules for the agent's builds, checkpoint/snapshot integration, the parallel-run loop) — but the operator slice is just "make a detached worktree at spawn, point the session's cd target at it," which needs NO agent loop, NO file editing, NO tool execution, NO snapshotting. spawnSpec/spawnInner (cc-core.lua:3061/2970) take `project` as the literal `cd <project>` target, so substituting a worktree path is a pure string swap. And Shepherd already shells out to git the same way the proposed fx.gitWorktree would: FX.gitRoot runs `hs.execute "git -C <q> rev-parse --show-toplevel"` (claude-dashboard.lua:961) and `git remote get-url origin` (line 1012). A `git worktree add --detach` effect is mechanically identical — feasible today. (b) No server/multi-user surface at all. (c) Not redundant: Shepherd has no workspace-isolation primitive; critically, the same-directory collision warning (core.collisions cc-core.lua:2334) is DETECTION-ONLY — the dashboard only sets a display flag (collFlags, line 5368) with no remedy, so a spawn-time worktree is the missing fix for a problem Shepherd already flags but can't solve. projectKey is transcript-path-derived with cwd fallback (cc-core.lua:57), so a detached worktree gives each session a distinct cwd/projects-encoding, keeping per-session queues/labels/respawn budgets isolated — it fits the model rather than breaking it. There is also prior art: openhands.md:439 already carved out a local `git worktree list`/`git branch` sliver as legitimate while ruling the broader provider-discovery feature DONT_ADD. (d) Not SDK plumbing — it's an operator console action. One correction to the judge's reasoning: --add-dir is NOT 'a CLI flag Shepherd already wires.' spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) emits only --permission-mode and --remote-control; --add-dir appears solely in todos.md (L1 design) and feature-mining docs as QUEUED, unbuilt work. This doesn't change the verdict because the load-bearing slice is the `project=` cwd substitution (genuinely shipped-primitive-compatible); --add-dir <mainRepo> is an optional read-the-source nicety whose wiring is feasible and is itself part of L1. Reinforces L1: a saved per-agent 'isolate in a worktree' option is a clean new profile field (no such field exists today — grep found no isolate/worktree/sandbox in L1's schema), and it operationalizes the shipped collision warning + projectKey routing. The GC awkwardness the judge flagged is real (Shepherd never authoritatively knows session death — it infers via stale/respawn machinery) but the judge already mitigated it with operator-driven 'remove worktree' + a startup orphan sweep. Drop the symlinked-node_modules half (executor-only). Not ADD because the as-is feature is built around file-editing/build isolation Shepherd lacks; not DONT_ADD because the spawn-cwd slice is real, feasible on shipped primitives, and uniquely useful as the missing remedy for a problem Shepherd already detects. ADAPT (reinforces L1) stands.

### Reinforces L5 — observability/detail  (15)

#### Approval-needed + long-running-command OS notifications (opt-in)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** This is the purest possible fit for a watch/steer console: an unattended operator needs to be pulled BACK to the machine when a session needs them, and an OS notification (macOS Notification Center) is the only surface that survives focus-loss. Shepherd today has ZERO OS notifications — it only fires `hs.alert.show` HUD overlays and an in-panel 🔔 badge/history (cc-core.lua M.NOTIFY_TYPES, claude-dashboard.lua setNotifyBadge), both visible only if you are already looking at the screen. Worse, the two exact triggers the candidate wants are ALREADY computed: `escalation` ("approval waiting too long", cc-core.lua:659) and the stuck-session watchdog `isHung` (cc-core.lua:2375). So the hard part — detecting "needs you now" — is done; the candidate just adds a delivery channel (hs.notify) and an opt-in toggle. It requires no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace edit, no server, so it passes the cline-mismatch test cleanly. The opt-in bell toggle maps onto the autopilot/auto-approve menu Shepherd already has, and the ~140-char privacy abbreviation is trivially honored.
- **Against:** Shepherd already surfaces both of these episodes — the 🔔 badge increments and the history overlay logs every escalation/hung event — so a skeptic can say the operator already has the signal and this is just a second rendering of an existing alert (redundant-with-what-Shepherd-has). The candidate's exact "auto-approved command running ~30s" trigger is also a slightly different concept than Shepherd's watchdog: Shepherd times TRANSCRIPT STALL (no transcript growth), not literal command duration, so a faithful copy would over- or under-fire. And cline's framing is partly tied to its own in-process auto-approve executor (it KNOWS a terminal command is running because it launched it); Shepherd only infers activity from status/transcript files, so the long-running-command half is an approximation, not the same signal.
- **Ruling:** The need is real and the fit is excellent — but it is not ADD because it is not a from-scratch build of a console feature; it is an extension of an existing subsystem reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives. Shepherd already detects the triggers (escalation + hung) and already classifies them as notification events, but only delivers them passively (badge/HUD). The candidate's genuine contribution is a PUSH delivery channel that survives focus-loss, which Shepherd lacks entirely. That is the textbook ADAPT shape: real underlying need, must be reshaped (fire hs.notify from the already-computed M.NOTIFY_TYPES events, behind an opt-in toggle, with the 140-char abbreviation). It strengthens the observability lane (L5) rather than spawning a parallel system. The long-running-command half maps onto the transcript-stall watchdog with a short threshold rather than a true per-command timer — an honest approximation, not a blocker. No cline-mismatch concern: zero agent loop / tool execution / workspace editing / server involved.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add an opt-in 'OS notifications' toggle (bell) to the autopilot/auto-approve menu, persisted in cc-config (e.g. notifications.os.enabled, default false — consistent with everything-off-by-default). In the ~1s refresh loop where escalation and isHung episodes are already raised into the ledger (claude-dashboard.lua around lines 5536/5558, the M.NOTIFY_TYPES events), fire hs.notify.new() once per NEW episode (dedupe by the same once-per-episode `alerted` flag the watchdog already uses, so it does not re-nag every second). Build the notification title/body via a pure cc-core helper (mirroring cline's createApprovalNotificationMessage) that abbreviates tool detail to ~140 chars and appends '(explicit approval required)' for escalations; keep it pure/testable like the rest of cc-core, with hs.notify in the fx table. Map 'long-running auto-approved command ~30s' onto the existing transcript-stall watchdog with a short configurable threshold (notifications.os.busySeconds). Clicking the notification can route to the existing jump-to-window / select-session action.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT holds. I tried two counters and both collapse on the code. REDUNDANCY counter (would force DONT_ADD): Shepherd ALREADY ships an opt-in push channel that fires on the exact two triggers. claude-dashboard.lua:5541-5544 fires FX.push on a NEW escalation (approval-wait) episode and :5561-5562 on a NEW hung episode, both gated on escalation.push (default false, README:417 'Both channels off by default'), both deduped once-per-episode via the same escalated/alerted flags, both carrying an abbreviated summary. FX.push (:973-979) POSTs to https://ntfy.sh/<topic> with a Title and high priority. So the triggers, the once-per-episode dedupe, the opt-in gate, and a push-notification channel all exist. This is the closest the feature comes to redundancy. BUT it is the REMOTE channel: ntfy.sh requires a configured pushTopic and lands on a phone. The candidate is a LOCAL macOS hs.notify banner — zero config, surfaces on the same machine when the panel loses focus, clickable to jump-to-window. grep confirms there is NO hs.notify anywhere in the repo, and the only existing local alert is a non-clickable audible chime (FX.playSound, Submarine/Ping). So a local visual+clickable channel is a genuine gap; not fully redundant. DONT_ADD refuted. FRESH-ADD counter (would push ADD): rejected because almost all the machinery pre-exists — M.NOTIFY_TYPES={escalation,hung,...} (cc-core.lua:786), the once-per-episode edge flags, abbreviated summary text, AND a sibling push channel. The judge's mapping is to fire hs.notify from those already-computed events behind a new opt-in toggle (notifications.os.enabled, consistent with everything-off-by-default), build the title/body via a pure cc-core helper, and map the '~30s long-running command' onto the existing transcript-stall watchdog (core.isHung) with a shorter configurable threshold — an honest approximation, since there is no per-command timer in a console that runs nothing. The 140-char abbreviation and the '(explicit approval required)' suffix are minor deltas. This is reshaping onto existing primitives, not a from-scratch console build. ADD refuted. cline-mismatch: clean pass. No agent loop, no tool execution, no file edits, no workspace snapshots, no server/users — it fires a local banner off status-file-derived events. It genuinely improves the local single-user console (pulls an unattended at-desk operator back). Verdict ADAPT (high), reinforcing the existing escalation-alert lane / L5 observability, stands. #### Persistent task/session history records (per-task metadata)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** A unified per-session record that survives session death — task text, model, cwd, token counts, $ cost, lineage — is exactly the kind of operator-facing artifact a watch/steer console wants. It powers a "History" view of past sessions (what ran where, how much it cost, which model), enables an at-a-glance Usage tab on the detail panel, and underpins a session export for archival/review. Crucially the value here is NOT cline's agent-resume loop; it is the management/observability metadata, which is precisely the layer that DOES transfer to Shepherd. Much of the raw data already flows through Shepherd (token counts via parseUsageLine/sumUsage, cwd/last_prompt/transcript_path in the status hook, lineage, the ledger), so consolidating it into a per-session record and surfacing it is low-cost and high-utility. The one net-new datum — total $ cost — is a small pure addition (a price table times the token counts Shepherd already sums) that operators genuinely want.
- **Against:** As designed, the candidate is an executor's own bookkeeping. The per-task directory (api_conversation_history, ui_messages, context_history, task_metadata) is the serialized state of cline's in-process agent loop — the message stream IT generated, plus IT's truncation/condense ranges — kept so cline can re-hydrate and KEEP GENERATING on resume. Shepherd runs no loop and owns no such stream: Claude Code itself produces and persists the transcript JSONL under ~/.claude/projects/, and "resume" is Claude Code's --resume, not something Shepherd can re-host. So copying cline's persistence layer (a global-state HistoryItem array plus mirrored conversation dirs) would duplicate data Shepherd already reads and re-implement a resume capability Shepherd structurally cannot provide. The metadata half is also largely redundant: Shepherd already derives per-session token usage zero-cost, already keys stable identity by projectKey, and already has the ledger + lineage + insights + notification history covering "what ran, when, how much."
- **Ruling:** Split the feature in two. (1) The per-task JSON directories + the resume mechanism are pure cline-as-executor: that conversation state exists because cline runs the loop and must re-hydrate it; Shepherd watches status files and steers a session Claude Code drives, so this half is DONT_ADD — it duplicates the transcript Claude Code already writes and a resume Shepherd can't host. (2) The HistoryItem metadata (task text, modelId, cwd, token in/out/cacheRead/cacheWrite, $ cost, on-disk size, timestamp, lineage) is real operator value AND mostly already present: parseUsageLine/sumUsage already produce the token + byModel breakdown zero-cost from the transcript; the status hook already captures cwd/last_prompt/transcript_path; projectKey is the stable per-session identity; the ledger + lineageByProject + fleetStats already record what ran and when. So this is not a new system — it reinforces queued L5 (detail-panel Usage tab; Export session archive = transcript .jsonl + meta.json) and the L4 task_done per-task duration/outcome counters. Net-new is only $ cost (price-table x summed tokens, a pure cc-core helper). Hence ADAPT onto Shepherd's existing transcript-derived usage + ledger + lineage primitives rather than persisting a parallel records array; verdict reinforces L5.
- **How (Shepherd):** Do NOT persist a parallel HistoryItem array or mirror conversation dirs; derive the record. Plug in as: (a) L5 detail-panel Usage tab — render core.sumUsage byModel + a new pure core.estimateCost(usage, priceTable) for the $ figure, plus modelId/cwd/last_prompt already on the status row; (b) L5 'Export session archive' — copy the Claude-Code transcript .jsonl + a meta.json {label, provider/model, projectKey, lineage, shift-report counters, summed tokens, $ cost}, reusing the audit-export FX path and ledger redaction posture; (c) a fleet 'History' list = reuse lineageByProject + filterLedger over past sessions (the ledger already records session lifecycle) rather than a new store. New pure helper: core.estimateCost in cc-core (price table x input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate), unit-tested. Drop the api_conversation_history/ui_messages/context_history/task_metadata dirs and the resume mechanism entirely — Claude Code owns the transcript and --resume.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT and could not — every claim the judge made is verified in the code, and the verdict lands exactly on the ADAPT line. REFUTATION ATTEMPT 1 (push to DONT_ADD as redundant): fails. The judge's split is correct and the metadata half is NOT fully redundant. core.sumUsage (cc-core.lua:1684) already yields the precise HistoryItem token fields — input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate + a per-model byModel breakdown — zero-cost from the transcript (parseUsageLine, cc-core.lua:1647). cc-status.sh captures cwd/last_prompt/transcript_path/projectKey (lines 58, 199, 203, 260). The append-only ledger + lineageByProject (cc-core.lua:1061) + fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954) already record what ran and when and SURVIVE session death (session_end is a ledger event, cc-core.lua:644), which is the feature's stated 'survives session death' purpose. BUT there is one genuinely net-new operator-facing primitive: $ cost. No price/cost logic exists anywhere in cc-core (only 'zero-cost'/'$ENV' false hits). A pure core.estimateCost(usage, priceTable) is real, useful, local-console value. So it is not pure redundancy — DONT_ADD is too strong. REFUTATION ATTEMPT 2 (push to DONT_ADD because the value secretly needs the executor): fails. The only executor-bound parts are the per-task JSON dirs (api_conversation_history/ui_messages/context_history/task_metadata) and resume — those exist solely because cline RUNS the loop and must re-hydrate conversation state; Claude Code owns the .jsonl transcript and --resume, and Shepherd edits/runs nothing. The judge already carves these off as DONT_ADD. The residual operator metadata (tokens, modelId, cwd, prompt, cost, timestamp, lineage) is derivable from the transcript + status row + ledger with NO loop, NO tool execution, NO workspace snapshot. The valuable half does not depend on the executor. REFUTATION ATTEMPT 3 (upgrade to ADD as a new system): fails. L5 ALREADY queues both target surfaces — 'Detail-panel tabs' explicitly includes a Usage tab over views 'Shepherd ALREADY produces' (todos.md:517-521), and 'Export session archive' already specifies copying the .jsonl + a meta.json {label, provider/model, lineage, shift-report counters} via the audit-export FX path (todos.md:522-525). L4 already queues task_done per-task duration/outcome counters (todos.md:473-475). A fleet History list reuses lineageByProject + filterLedger. So this is not net-new architecture; it reinforces existing queued items.
