CONCLUSION: The feature is two halves — a DONT_ADD executor half (conversation dirs + resume, which Claude Code owns) and a reinforces-L5 metadata half whose only net-new piece is a pure core.estimateCost helper rendered in the already-queued L5 Usage tab / export meta.json. That is the precise definition of ADAPT (reinforces L5): not redundant enough for DONT_ADD, not new enough for ADD. The do-NOT-persist-a-parallel-HistoryItem-array-or-conversation-dirs Shepherd mapping is exactly right. The ruling holds.

#### Bulk history management (multi-select delete, delete-all, size accounting)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** A local console that accumulates records over months needs housekeeping, and the OPERATOR pattern here — "select records, see a size/count total, modal-confirm a scoped delete or wipe-all, irreversibly remove them" — is exactly the management UX Shepherd is built around, not agent execution. Shepherd already proves the appetite: it ships an Audit overlay with filter-scoped purge, a "Purge ALL recorded events? This cannot be undone" blockAlert (audit-purge handler, claude-dashboard.lua:2133), and even byte-level retention GC (core.expiredLedgerFiles / core.ledgerCapVictims, cc-core.lua:595-638). The two genuinely missing deltas are small and high-value: (1) a storage READOUT for the local state Shepherd does own (ledger bytes, plus cc-queue/cc-labels/cc-groups/cc-presets/cc-recent/cc-templates JSON) so the operator can see what's piling up; (2) multi-row select-and-delete on the audit view, which today only purges by the active filter, not an arbitrary hand-picked set. Both are pure-core derivations + panel JS over data Shepherd already manages — no executor, no workspace, no server.
- **Against:** The cline feature's defining substance is workspace plumbing Shepherd structurally cannot host: getTotalTasksSize sums each task's on-disk DIRECTORY plus its CHECKPOINTS, and deleteTasksWithIds removes those task directories. Shepherd owns no task directory and snapshots no workspace — the conversation transcripts and any checkpoints belong to Claude Code, which Shepherd only WATCHES via ~/.claude/cc-status/*.json. There is no "history of tasks" store to bulk-delete; Shepherd's nearest equivalent records (the opt-in ledger) already have a complete, safer lifecycle: scoped/all purge, per-file retention, AND size-cap GC. So copying cline's HistoryView verbatim would either operate on nothing Shepherd controls or duplicate the purge/GC machinery it already has. The transcript-deletion half edges toward deleting Claude Code's own files, which is outside Shepherd's read-only-watcher posture.
- **Ruling:** Apply the cline mismatch: the feature's value splits cleanly. The size-accounting-over-task-directories + checkpoint sizing + delete-task-directory half depends on owning a managed workspace/checkpoint store — Shepherd has neither (it reads status files; Claude Code owns transcripts/checkpoints), so that half is DONT_ADD plumbing. But the OPERATOR pattern (multi-select + size/count total + modal-confirmed scoped-or-wipe-all delete of local records) is a management/UX pattern that maps onto records Shepherd genuinely owns, and Shepherd already implements most of it on the ledger. That makes this an ADAPT, not ADD (would duplicate existing purge/GC) and not DONT_ADD (the housekeeping NEED is real and partly unmet for non-ledger local files). The two concrete additions — a local-storage size/count readout in Settings, and hand-picked multi-row delete on the audit view — extend the existing audit/ledger and the queued L5 'Export session archive' item (which already owns the per-session transcript+meta side-effect path and the ~/.claude/cc-exports plumbing). Reinforces L5 rather than spawning a parallel 'history manager.' Effort is S: reuse splitLedgerEvents/purgeFilterIsScoped/the audit-purge blockAlert, add a pure core.localStorageReport(sizes) (FX feeds dir byte-sums) and a multi-select set into the existing serverFilter purge path.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into the Audit overlay + L5, no new system. (1) Storage readout: pure core.localStorageReport({ledgerBytes, queueBytes, labelsBytes, groupsBytes, presetBytes, recentBytes, templateBytes}) → {total, byCategory}; FX supplies byte sums via dir scans of LEDGER_DIR/QUEUE_DIR + the JSON state files (reuse the os.remove/writeFile FX layer in claude-dashboard.lua:464-746); render a compact strip in the ⚙ Settings ledger section near the existing retentionDays control (claude-dashboard.lua:3172). (2) Multi-row delete: add a checkbox-select set to the audit Rows view; pass the selected ids/ts into the existing serverFilter→audit-purge path so core.splitLedgerEvents matches the hand-picked set; the audit-purge handler's blockAlert (claude-dashboard.lua:2142) already gives the modal-confirm + 'cannot be undone' wording and core.purgeFilterIsScoped already distinguishes scoped vs wipe-all. (3) The transcript-archive analog (cline's per-task copy) is the already-queued L5 'Export session archive' — keep it there; do NOT delete Claude Code's own transcripts/checkpoints. DROP entirely: getTotalTasksSize over task directories, checkpoint sizing, deleteTasksWithIds removing task workspace dirs.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted on two grounds; both fail to dislodge ADAPT. (1) Executor-plumbing: cline's "history" is its list of past agent TASKS — each a workspace dir with checkpoints (file snapshots) and a copied task directory. That whole substrate (getTotalTasksSize over task dirs, checkpoint sizing, deleteTasksWithIds removing workspace dirs) is in-process-executor plumbing Shepherd doesn't own (Shepherd edits nothing, snapshots nothing, owns no workspace/checkpoint store). The judge ALREADY drops exactly those three, so this ground only refutes the half already discarded, not the residual. (2) Redundancy (the strongest attack): Shepherd already ships the operator pattern's core — audit-purge with scoped-vs-wipe-all + modal-confirm "cannot be undone" (claude-dashboard.lua:2133-2149 via core.purgeFilterIsScoped/splitLedgerEvents at cc-core.lua:567-584) AND genuine size accounting + size-driven deletion (ledger.maxTotalMB + core.ledgerCapVictims oldest-first byte eviction, FX.expireLedger claude-dashboard.lua:654-690) AND retentionDays GC. So "size accounting + delete local records + wipe-all" is largely MET for the ledger. But redundancy is NOT total: two residual gains survive on records Shepherd actually owns — (a) a surfaced storage size/count readout in Settings (the maxTotalMB CAP exists at line 3174 but there is no live "using X MB / N events" display), and (b) hand-picked MULTI-ROW delete on the audit Rows view (today's purge is filter-scoped by session/type/time, not arbitrary cherry-pick). Both edit nothing, run no agent, need no server, and improve the local single-user console. That blocks a downgrade to DONT_ADD. Equally it blocks a bump to ADD: a standalone "history manager" would duplicate the existing purge/cap/GC machinery — it must be folded into the Audit overlay. Net: ADAPT is correct. One quibble with the judge's anchor — the size-readout + multi-row-delete fit the Audit/ledger overlay more squarely than L5 (L5 legitimately owns only the per-session transcript "Export session archive" analog at todos.md:522), but that is an anchor-precision nit, not a verdict error. Ruling holds.

#### Export / inspect a task transcript (Markdown, HTML, copy, open conversation file)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** This is one of the cleanest cross-app fits in the whole mining exercise: it is pure read-only inspection/export over the exact files Shepherd already reads, with zero agent-execution dependency. Every sub-capability lands on an existing primitive. (a) Export to Markdown/HTML: Shepherd already tails each session's transcript JSONL (transcriptSnippet, ACTIVITY_BYTES tail reads, the rg-backed fleet search over *.jsonl), already renders Markdown (core.standupMarkdown) and already owns an export side-effect path (the audit overlay's redact/export/purge → mkdir cc-ledger, write, alert/Reveal). cline's generateConversationHTML is a deterministic pure renderer of an already-read transcript — a textbook cc-core function (zero hs.*) feeding one fx write. (b) Copy task text: Shepherd already ships Copy buttons (standup, queue copy in the { tasks=... } shape). (c) Open raw conversation JSON on disk (OpenDiskConversationHistoryButton): Shepherd already computes the canonical transcript_path / projectKey for every tile; "Reveal in Finder / open file" is a one-line fx effect, perfectly read-only. (d) Title editing already exists as the persistent relabel feature. Crucially, the backlog ALREADY contains the spine of this as L5 "Export session archive [H/S]" (copy the .jsonl + a meta.json, reuse the audit-export path, mkdir ~/.claude/cc-exports, optional zip, honor ledger redaction). The candidate extends that queued item with two concrete output formats (Markdown + standalone HTML) and the open-on-disk affordance, which are genuinely useful for a single-user operator who wants to hand a finished session's record to a teammate, file it, or read the raw turns. It also pairs naturally with the queued detail-panel tabs and shift-report counters. Effort is small because the assembly is pure and the side-effect path is built.
- **Against:** Parts of the candidate are redundant or off-mission. Shepherd is a live WATCH/STEER console, not an archive/reporting tool — the operator's job is approve/deny/nudge on RUNNING sessions, and a finished-task HTML report is a nice-to-have at the cold edge of that job. cline's CLI verbs (history list/get/update/delete/export) describe cline's own persisted-task store, which Shepherd does not own — Shepherd has no task DB; the source of truth is Claude Code's transcript JSONL on disk, so "list/get/delete a task" is not Shepherd's to manage (deleting Claude Code's history from Shepherd would be a foot-gun, not a feature). "Edit a session's title/prompt/metadata" is partly redundant (title = existing relabel) and partly meaningless for Shepherd (editing a completed session's stored prompt changes nothing about a watch console; pre-spawn prompt editing is L3 template territory, not this). The standalone-HTML generator is the largest net-new chunk and the least aligned: it is presentation polish, and the simpler "open the raw JSON / copy snippet / reuse standupMarkdown" affordances cover 90% of the operator value at a fraction of the work. So adopting the candidate verbatim risks importing a task-management CRUD surface (delete/update of someone else's data) that Shepherd should not host.
- **Ruling:** No cline mismatch at all here — nothing in the candidate runs an agent loop, executes tools, edits a workspace, snapshots files, or serves users. It is read-only inspection/export over local transcript files Shepherd already tails, which is squarely inside scope (reads session status/transcripts off the local FS; no network, no secrets). The need is real and already recognized: it directly reinforces the queued L5 "Export session archive" sub-item rather than introducing a new system, so the verdict is ADAPT, not ADD. The reshaping discipline: keep the export/inspect/copy/open-on-disk affordances (assemble in cc-core, emit via fx, reuse the cc-ledger redact/export path and standupMarkdown), but DROP cline's history list/delete/update CRUD over a task store — Shepherd doesn't own that store and must never delete Claude Code's transcripts. "Edit title" collapses into the existing relabel; "edit prompt/metadata" is out of scope. The standalone-HTML renderer is optional polish layered on top of the cheaper Markdown-copy + open-raw-JSON path. This is the operator inspection layer of L5, not a parallel feature.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L5 "Export session archive". (1) Per-tile + fleet "Export selected": pure core.exportTranscript(transcriptTail, meta) assembling Markdown (reuse the standupMarkdown idiom) and optionally a deterministic HTML render (port generateConversationHTML as a zero-hs.* cc-core function); write via the existing audit-export side-effect path (mkdir ~/.claude/cc-exports, write file, optional zip, alert/Reveal), honoring ledger redaction so prompt bodies aren't leaked. (2) Copy: add a Copy button reusing the existing copy plumbing (transcriptSnippet / full tail). (3) Open raw conversation JSON: a one-line fx "reveal/open" effect on the transcript_path Shepherd already computes per tile (cc-core.lua:58 projectKey/transcript_path). (4) Title editing = the EXISTING persistent relabel (cc-labels.json) — do not re-add. DROP: cline's history list/get/delete/update CRUD (Shepherd owns no task store and must not delete Claude Code transcripts) and any "edit a completed session's prompt/metadata". Surface the export/copy/open actions from the right-click menu and the detail panel (pairs with the queued detail-panel tabs).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; ADAPT holds (reinforces L5). I checked every standard trap and none apply. (a) No in-process-executor / snapshot dependency: the candidate is read-only inspection/export over transcript JSONL Shepherd ALREADY tails (FX.readTail on it.transcript_path, claude-dashboard.lua:5410), already computes per tile (core.projectKey/transcript_path, cc-core.lua:57-58), and already ripgrep-searches fleet-wide (cc-core.lua:810+). Exporting an already-written transcript is NOT cline's "checkpoint the workspace it edited" — Shepherd edits/snapshots nothing. (b) No server/multi-user surface; writes a local file under the user's home. (c) Not redundant and not a mere relabel: the Markdown/copy/open-raw-JSON facets are net-new operator inspection, while the parts that WOULD be redundant are correctly dropped — "edit title" collapses into the existing persistent relabel (core.setLabel cc-core.lua:307, relabel action claude-dashboard.lua:2367). (d) Not a cline SDK/library plumbing detail. Every reuse the mapping names is real in the tree: standupMarkdown assembly (cc-core.lua:1201), the audit-export side-effect path (mkdir exports + FX.writeFile + alert, claude-dashboard.lua:2120-2131, already forcing limit=0 as the full-data path), ledger redaction posture (cc-core.lua:565/610), and copy via hs.pasteboard.setContents + transcriptSnippet (claude-dashboard.lua:2011, cc-core.lua:1544). The DROPs are sound: cline's history list/get/delete/update CRUD is over a task store Shepherd does not own and must never delete, and edit-prompt/metadata is out of scope. It maps onto the already-QUEUED L5 "Export session archive" (todos.md:522-525) — same cc-exports/audit-export/pure-cc-core/redaction mapping — so it reinforces L5 rather than proposing a parallel system: ADAPT, not ADD. Only genuinely new bit is an "open raw JSON on disk" reveal effect (no existing reveal-in-Finder effect; only /usr/bin/open -na for spawn at claude-dashboard.lua:1423), but that is a trivial one-line fx on a path Shepherd already holds and does not change scope. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md.

#### Context-window usage meter + cost/token breakdown  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** An operator running a fleet of Claude Code sessions genuinely benefits from seeing context fullness and spend at a glance: which session is about to auto-compact (so the human can intervene/compact deliberately), and what a given task is costing. This is pure WATCH territory — exactly Shepherd's reason to exist — and it's computed with zero network/zero tokens by parsing the local transcript JSONL Shepherd already reads. The candidate's three pieces map cleanly: a context progress bar, a token breakdown hover card, and a Compact action. The one piece Shepherd lacks — a per-task running dollar cost — is a real operator value-add and is cheap: a static per-model price map ($/Mtok in vs out vs cache-read vs cache-write) applied to the buckets cc-core already aggregates in sumUsage/computeUsage. No agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace edits, no server are required.
- **Against:** This is almost entirely already built, so importing it as a 'feature' is redundant and risks duplicating well-tested code. Shepherd has the per-tile context bar (with NN% overlay, model-aware effective limit, auto-compact-reserve calibration against a live 1M session, and a 7-band color ramp mirrored core↔JS with a build-failing test pin), the detail-panel Usage card (session total real/gross, output/input split, per-model breakdown), AND a Compact button in three places — plus official plan-window usage via the OAuth endpoint, which the cline feature doesn't even have. The dollar-cost piece is the one thing cline computes that Shepherd doesn't, and cost-per-token tables drift with model releases, so a hardcoded price map carries maintenance burden and can quietly go wrong; some operators may not want a possibly-stale dollar figure presented as authoritative.
- **Ruling:** Verified against the code, not the summary. Shepherd already ships ~90% of this feature: cc-core.lua has parseUsageLine (captures tokensIn/out + cache_read + cache_creation), contextFraction/contextFractionFor (model-aware limit + autoCompactFraction reserve), sumUsage with per-model byModel, contextBand (7-band ramp). claude-dashboard.lua has ctxBarHtml (per-tile bar with NN% + hover title), renderDetailUsage (the breakdown card), and a Compact button at b-compact / tile menu / bulk. So the candidate's ContextWindow.tsx, ContextWindowSummary.tsx, and CompactTaskButton.tsx all already have direct equivalents. This is therefore not ADD (would re-build shipped code) and not DONT_ADD (it passes the cline mismatch cleanly — it's pure local-transcript WATCH, no executor/server). It's ADAPT: the genuine deltas are small additive enhancements to L5's richer-observability surface — (1) per-task running dollar cost from a static price map over buckets already aggregated, and (2) surfacing the cache-read/cache-write split in the detail card (currently parsed but the card shows only output/input + per-model total). Effort is S because the data pipeline, the bar, and the card already exist; this is a price table plus two extra rows, with the only care being to mark cost as an estimate and keep the price map hand-editable in config like the existing context.* knobs.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L5 (richer session observability). (1) Add a static per-model price map (input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate $/Mtok) in cc-core, hand-editable via cc-config (mirror the existing context.* config pattern); a pure costFor(byModel, prices) -> usd helper, unit-tested in core.test.lua. (2) In claude-dashboard.lua renderDetailUsage, add a 'cost (est.)' row and a 'cache read / write' row to the existing #d-usage card — the buckets are already in ps.byModel/ps.cacheRead/ps.cacheCreate. Optionally a small $ figure in the official-window footer. Reuse the existing ctxBarHtml bar and b-compact button unchanged (the 'surface Compact near the limit' behavior already exists). No new fetch, no token use; cost computed from the data computeUsage already produces. Label cost as an estimate so a stale price map can't read as authoritative.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; every load-bearing claim verified in code. Shepherd already ships ~90% of this feature, so it is not a full ADD, and it passes the cline mismatch cleanly (pure local-transcript WATCH), so it is not DONT_ADD — leaving ADAPT/L5 correct. Verified: (1) cc-core.lua:1647-1662 parseUsageLine captures input/output/cache_read/cache_creation; (2) contextFraction (1672) + contextFractionFor (1806, model window * autoCompactFraction reserve) + contextBand (1817); (3) sumUsage (1684-1704) with per-model byModel carrying cacheRead/cacheCreate; (4) claude-dashboard.lua ctxBarHtml (4428-4433, per-tile bar with NN% + hover title) and renderDetailUsage (4914-4924, the breakdown card); (5) the Compact button at b-compact (line 3070), tile menu (2302-2305), and bulk. The two genuine deltas the judge named are real and absent: a grep for cost/price/usd/dollar/pricing finds ONLY 'zero API cost' comments — there is no price map — and renderDetailUsage renders only Session total, output/input, and per-model total (no cache read/write split row, no $ cost). The data for both deltas is already pushed to the webview: perSession[key] (claude-dashboard.lua:373-375) carries cacheRead, cacheCreate, and byModel, so cost is a pure static-price map over already-aggregated buckets and the cache split is two extra rows. The cline mismatch is clean: FX.computeUsage (331-388) reads local transcript JSONL incrementally (ZERO API cost, no network, no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing/snapshotting, no server) — pure WATCH, which is what Shepherd is. The context.* config knob pattern the mapping reuses exists exactly as described (cc-config.example.json:143-145, hand-editable with _comment, model-aware contextLimit at line 104). Effort is S: a price table plus two card rows plus a pure costFor helper unit-tested in core.test.lua; only care is labeling cost an estimate. Holds as ADAPT (high) reinforcing L5.

#### Focus Chain (per-task live TODO/progress checklist)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** The explicitly-named copyable part is exactly Shepherd-shaped: file-watch a markdown checklist, parse done/total, surface a read-only mini-checklist + progress on a tile. That is pure observation — no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing. Shepherd already owns every primitive needed: it tails each session's transcript.jsonl (transcriptError, cc-core.lua:1588, with a ^%s*{ JSON guard) and already runs a pathwatcher with event-batching (shouldRefreshForChange, cc-core.lua:2419), so swapping chokidar for hs.pathwatcher is a one-line analog. Claude Code's native TodoWrite tool already emits exactly this checklist into the transcript Shepherd reads, so the "model maintains a live checklist" half exists for free without Shepherd reminding anyone. A done/total progress count on a tile is genuinely useful glanceable signal for a watch console — it answers "how far along is this session" at the grid level, which Shepherd currently can't show.
- **Against:** Half of cline's Focus Chain is structurally a DONT_ADD: "the model updates it" and "the model is periodically reminded to update it" require driving the in-process agent loop — injecting reminder turns into the running agent and having it write the file. Shepherd runs no agent loop and would have to type steer-prompts to nag, which is noisy and out of scope (only the L5 post-run/self-summary path even types into a session, and that is off-by-default and operator-triggered). cline's value here is tightly coupled to its executor authoring the file mid-task; Shepherd can only READ whatever Claude Code's own TodoWrite already produced. Cline also persists a dedicated per-task focus-chain markdown file as part of its task-workspace management, which Shepherd has no workspace to own. So adopting it whole would mean re-implementing executor behavior Shepherd cannot host; only the read-only surface survives the mismatch.
- **Ruling:** The read-only surface (file-watch + done/total parse → mini-checklist + progress on a tile) passes the fit test cleanly and is the part the candidate itself flags as copyable. But it is not a new system — it is a near-verbatim restatement of the already-queued L5 item "Surface the agent's plan/TODO on a tile" (todos.md:504), which proposes a pure core.planFromTranscript(text) reading the latest TodoWrite/ExitPlanMode entries (same JSON-guard pattern as transcriptError) plus an optional <cwd>/PLAN.md FX file fallback, loaded on selection/status-change rather than the 1s tick. So per the rubric this is ADAPT reinforcing L5, not ADD. The cline framing adds two concrete refinements worth folding into L5: (1) parse done/total counts to render a "3/7" progress chip on the tile, not just the plan text in the detail panel; and (2) the PLAN.md fallback should be file-watched via the existing pathwatcher (cc-core.lua:2419 batching) so external edits sync, rather than a one-shot read on selection. The reminder/auto-update half is dropped as the cline executor mismatch.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L5's "Surface the agent's plan/TODO on a tile." Concretely: extend the planned pure core.planFromTranscript(text) to also return {done,total} by counting checked/total TodoWrite items (and, for the optional <cwd>/PLAN.md fallback, counting markdown - [ ]/- [x] lines via a sibling pure parser core.checklistCounts(md)). Render the count as a small "3/7 📋" progress chip on the tile (cheap, glanceable) and the full checklist in the detail panel (Activity/Timeline tab), loaded on selection/status-change, never the 1s tick. For the PLAN.md path, register the file with the existing hs.pathwatcher and gate refresh through shouldRefreshForChange (cc-core.lua:2419) so external edits sync. esc() at the render sink. Settings toggle, default OFF. Explicitly DROP cline's "remind the model to update" / model-writes-the-file behavior — that needs the agent loop Shepherd doesn't run; Shepherd only reads what native TodoWrite/PLAN.md already produced.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt FAILED — the ADAPT-reinforces-L5 ruling holds, verified against the code. I tried the three standard downgrade angles and none lands: (1) "Secretly needs the in-process executor" — REFUTED by direct evidence. A real native Claude Code session on this box (~/.claude/projects/.../9b1e9460...jsonl) contains 18 TodoWrite entries; the latest payload is exactly 7 todos, 6 completed = a literal "6/7" already sitting on disk. Native TodoWrite (Claude Code's own tool) writes this with NO cline executor, NO model-reminder loop, NO file-writing by the watcher. The dashboard ALREADY tails this transcript (FX.readTail at claude-dashboard.lua:283/5410) and already feeds it to core.transcriptSnippet/core.transcriptError. So the copyable half (file-watch/parse → mini-checklist + done/total chip, read-only) is a pure parse over bytes Shepherd already holds. The judge correctly DROPPED only cline's "remind the model / model-writes-the-file" half as the executor mismatch. (2) "Redundant with what Shepherd already ships" — the path that could push to DONT_ADD — fails: grep shows NO plan/TODO surfacing exists. transcriptError (cc-core.lua:1588, with the exact ^%s*{ JSON guard at 1594) is the cited sibling pattern, but planFromTranscript / TodoWrite / ExitPlanMode parsing are entirely unbuilt. Not redundant. (3) "Already queued" — this is the judge's actual basis and it's airtight: todos.md:504-507 is the L5 item "Surface the agent's plan/TODO on a tile — pure core.planFromTranscript(text) ... same ^%s*{ JSON guard as transcriptError ... latest ExitPlanMode/TodoWrite ... optional <cwd>/PLAN.md fallback." The cline feature is a near-verbatim restatement, so per the rubric it ADAPTs/reinforces L5 rather than being a new ADD-system. Two judge refinements check out as real reinforcements (not noise), which is why it's a legitimate ADAPT and not DONT_ADD: (a) the done/total chip is backed by actual on-disk data I verified (6/7); (b) the PLAN.md fallback should be file-watched through the EXISTING pathwatcher (hs.pathwatcher at claude-dashboard.lua:5787) and gated through the existing refresh-gate — note the function is actually named core.watcherShouldRefresh (cc-core.lua:2423), not "shouldRefreshForChange" as the judge wrote; minor naming slip, the mechanism the judge points to is exactly there. Load on selection/status-change, never the 1s tick; esc() at the render sink; Settings toggle default OFF. Conclusion: ADAPT (high), reinforces L5, is correct. I could not refute it down to DONT_ADD (the surface is genuinely useful, executor-free, and not yet built) nor up to ADD (L5 already covers it).

#### Board-level progress rollup (counts, completion %, ready/blocked, member/run tallies)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** This is a pure deterministic projection over already-parsed runtime state — exactly the kind of logic cc-core.lua is built for (zero hs.*, unit-testable). It needs no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing, no server — it just folds over the session list Shepherd already parses every ~1s. The data it wants ALL exists in Shepherd today: per-session status (idle/working/approval/done/error/stale), queue depth per project (core.queueDepth), routed/free members (the routing dispatcher already computes free sessions and starvation), and the SD_LABELS taxonomy already maps Shepherd statuses to board language ("done"->"ready", "approval"->"NEEDS YOU"). Yet there is a real, concrete gap: the header bar today shows a static "Claude sessions" string and the menubar a bare "sheep" icon — there is NO live at-a-glance rollup of "N working / N need you / N ready / N error / N queued" over current state. The existing fleetStats/fleetStandup are HISTORICAL aggregations over the opt-in ledger (events that already happened); they do not project the live snapshot. A small buildFleetSummary(items, queues, members) feeding a header pill row plus a menubar count (e.g. "sheep 3" when 3 need you) would materially improve the watch-the-fleet job — operators currently scan tiles or open the insights overlay to learn the same thing. cline's board rollup is strong, directly-mappable prior art for the operator-facing shape, and it cleanly reuses Shepherd's primitives.
- **Against:** Shepherd's tile grid IS already the board/overview, and it already RANK-sorts approvals/errors to the front, color-codes status, exposes group filter chips, an unseen-notification badge, fleet INSIGHTS, a shift report, and sparklines. So much of the "overview" need is met that a counts rollup risks being redundant chrome — the human can see 3 red tiles. The candidate's vocabulary is also a partial mismatch: cline's "tasks" with completionPct/readyTaskIds/blockedTaskIds assume a TASK BOARD with a fixed denominator of work items and a notion of "blocked" as a dependency state. Shepherd's primitives are SESSIONS, not tasks; "completion %" has no honest denominator (sessions aren't a finite backlog you burn down — context.md explicitly rejects a "what shipped" outcome metric because Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth), and "ready/blocked" would have to be redefined as done-vs-approval-waiting, which is just the status counts re-skinned. Importing cline's task-rollup framing wholesale would invite a fake progress bar. The member/run tallies (active runs, queued runs, members by status) are a TEAM-SERVER concept; Shepherd's analog (queue depth, free members, starvation) is per-project and already computed for the dispatcher, so re-aggregating it fleet-wide is low marginal value.
- **Ruling:** No cline-mismatch trip: this is pure projection over runtime state Shepherd already parses — no executor, no tools, no workspace, no server. So DONT_ADD is wrong. But ADD as-is is also wrong: cline's TASK-board framing (completionPct, ready/blocked task IDs, run/member tallies) assumes a finite task backlog and a team-server runtime that Shepherd structurally lacks — and context.md explicitly forbids a fake completion/outcome metric (no git/CI ground truth). The honest, valuable core is a live SESSION-status rollup, which Shepherd has the data for but does NOT currently surface as an at-a-glance header/menubar count (the header is a static string; insights/shift are historical, ledger-based, opt-in — a different thing). So the NEED is real and must be reshaped onto Shepherd's session primitives: counts per live status + queued-work depth + free-member count, NOT completion% over phantom tasks. That extends L5 (richer session observability & detail) — a fleet-level read alongside its per-tile surfacing — so verdict is ADAPT, reinforces L5. Effort is S: a pure fold in cc-core.lua over the parsed item list + existing queueDepth/free-member helpers, a small header pill row in the webview, and a menubar setTitle badge — all reusing existing parsed state and the SD_LABELS taxonomy.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a pure M.buildFleetSummary(items, queues, members) to cc-core.lua returning { byStatus = {idle,working,approval,done,error,stale}, total, needsYou = approval+error count, queued = sum of core.queueDepth over armed projects, freeMembers, starvedProjects }. Map cline's ready/blocked HONESTLY onto existing statuses (ready = done; needs-you = approval+error) and DROP completionPct (no finite backlog / no outcome ground truth — context.md forbids it). Surface it two ways: (1) a compact pill row in the #bar header of claude-dashboard.lua next to "Claude sessions" (reuse the existing status color map + SD_LABELS), and (2) a menubar count via M.menubar:setTitle (sheep + "3" when 3 sessions need you, plain icon otherwise) so the fleet state is legible even when the panel is hidden. Reuse the dispatcher's existing free-session / queueStarved computation rather than re-deriving. Unit-test the fold like fleetStats. Files: cc-core.lua (new fn + tests under tests/), claude-dashboard.lua (#bar pill render + setTitle badge wiring in the ~1s refresh). Extends L5.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on all three downgrade paths; the ADAPT/reinforces-L5 ruling is correct. (1) Cline-mismatch DONT_ADD is wrong: the honest core is a PURE deterministic fold over state Shepherd already parses — no executor, no tool exec, no workspace edit, no server. Verified building blocks: core.parseStatusList yields the live item list (statuses idle/working/approval/done/error + a stale flag); M.SD_LABELS (cc-core.lua:33) already maps done="ready" and approval="NEEDS YOU" (the judge's honest ready/blocked remap); M.queueDepth/M.queueStarved/M.routePick (cc-core.lua:1856/2075/2080) already compute queue depth and free-member availability. (2) Redundancy DONT_ADD is wrong — this is the closest call but it loses on evidence: M.fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954) folds HISTORICAL LEDGER EVENTS (prompts/tool_requests/decisions/blockedSeconds), is opt-in, and lives behind the 📊 insights overlay — it is NOT a live status-count rollup. M.nextAttention (cc-core.lua:1474) returns a SINGLE neediest session, not counts. The #bar header (claude-dashboard.lua:2986-2987) is a STATIC 'Claude sessions' string with no aggregate; the menubar setTitle is called exactly ONCE with static '🐑' (line 5139), never updated per refresh; the bulk-bar nAp/nSt/nLv counts (lines 3687-89) are scoped to the bulk-action confirm flow only. So an at-a-glance live status count is genuinely absent. (3) It is not a parallel system — it maps cleanly onto L5 (richer session observability/detail). ADD-as-is is also wrong: cline's completionPct over a finite task backlog and team-server run/member tallies have no ground truth in Shepherd (no git/CI, no finite backlog), and context.md forbids a fake completion/outcome metric — those fields must be DROPPED and reshaped onto live session statuses. Dropping completionPct + remapping ready→done, blocked→approval+error is exactly the reshaping that makes this ADAPT rather than ADD. Effort S (a pure fold in cc-core + a header pill + a dynamic menubar setTitle badge in the existing ~1s refresh), all reusing parsed state. Verdict holds: ADAPT (high), reinforces L5.

#### Live session overview dashboard / hub (board of running sessions with filter chips)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** A live board of every session with status/model/title/tokens/cost is EXACTLY what an operator console is for, and the strongest transferable nugget — the evidence even pre-isolates it — is the TYPED faceted filter chips (status:/provider:/model:/source:). Today Shepherd's `core.filterTiles` (cc-core.lua:322) only does free-text substring AND over label/name/cwd/projectKey/status/group; it cannot say "show me only provider:gateway model:opus working sessions." Shepherd already carries every field these chips need per tile (status, provider/providerId, live model from cc-status.sh, source/editor, group), so a `key:value` facet parser layered on filterTiles + chip buttons derived from the distinct present values (mirroring groupNames -> header chips) is a small, high-value upgrade to scanning a large fleet. The per-tile cost line is a thin pure addition on top of the token tracking that already exists. This is a clean fleet-management/UX win, not agent execution.
- **Against:** Most of the candidate is the cline-HUB, which is a multi-user network SERVER (ROOM_SECRET, LAN/tunnel, connected hub clients) — precisely the surface Shepherd's hard scope forbids (no network server, no multi-user, no secrets). And the "board of running sessions" concept is already Shepherd's reason to exist: the tile grid IS the board/overview, with `filterTiles` search, `applyGroups`/`groupNames` filter chips, click-to-detail, rename (relabel), spawn modal, and per-session timeline/stream-equivalent via the audit overlay. "Start a new session from a prompt" = the New-session modal + seedPrompt; "view history / stream output" = detail panel + sessionTimeline; "Restart Hub" is a server-lifecycle action with no analog (Shepherd reloads via make deploy / Hammerspoon). So the dashboard-as-a-whole is redundant-or-forbidden; only a sliver (typed chips, cost line) is net-new, which argues against treating this as a feature to "add" at all rather than a minor filter tweak.
- **Ruling:** Split the candidate. The hub-qua-hub (browser server, ROOM_SECRET, LAN/tunnel, multi connected clients, Restart Hub, web "start session from prompt") is the canonical cline server/multi-user mismatch and is DONT_ADD on its own. The board concept is already shipped — the tile grid plus filterTiles, applyGroups/groupNames chips, click-to-detail, relabel/delete, New-session modal, and the audit/timeline (history/stream stand-in) cover open/view/rename/delete/start. What is genuinely NEW and fits a local watch/steer/spawn console is the TYPED faceted filter (status:/provider:/model:/source: chips that filter on structured fields, not free-text) plus the per-tile token/cost line — and that is exactly the "search/filter facet" and richer-tile-detail theme already queued under L5 (error-reason taxonomy explicitly lists a "search/filter facet"; auto-title/plan-on-tile enrich the tile). So the honest call is ADAPT reinforcing L5: extend filterTiles into a faceted parser with chip rails, not build a parallel dashboard. Keep it off-the-shelf-small and pure; drop everything server/multi-user. Confidence high because both the redundancy (board exists) and the mismatch (server) are unambiguous, and the salvage is precisely pre-named in the evidence.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend `core.filterTiles` (cc-core.lua:322) into a faceted matcher: parse `key:value` tokens (status/provider/model/source/group) against the structured tile fields each token already carries (it.status, it.providerId/provider, the live model captured by cc-status.sh, it.editor as `source`, it.group) while bare tokens stay free-text substring AND — keep it pure + unit-tested. Build the chip rail like the existing group chips: a `core.filterFacets(list)` sibling to `groupNames` returns the distinct present values per facet; render clickable chips in the panel JS (the `filterTiles` twin) that compose into the query string. Add a per-tile token/cost line as a pure formatter over the existing token-usage tracking (provider rate table -> cost; off by default if cost is shown). Fold the whole thing into L5 (search/filter facet + richer tile detail). DROP: the network server, ROOM_SECRET/LAN/tunnel, multi-user connected-clients, Restart Hub, and any server-mediated "start session from prompt"/stream-output endpoint — open/view/rename/delete/spawn already exist via the tile detail panel, relabel, New-session modal, and sessionTimeline/audit overlay.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT-reinforces-L5 ruling is correct. The candidate splits cleanly and the judge split it correctly: (1) the hub-qua-hub (browser server, ROOM_SECRET, LAN/tunnel, multi connected clients, Restart Hub, web "start session from prompt", stream-output endpoint) is the canonical cline SERVER/multi-user mismatch and is correctly DROPPED — context.md confirms the hard scope "no network, no multi-user, no secrets" (context.md:13) and grep finds no httpserver/socket/ROOM_SECRET anywhere; open/view/rename/delete/spawn already exist via the tile grid, relabel, New-session modal, and sessionTimeline/audit. (2) The salvaged pieces are NEITHER server- nor executor-dependent and are NOT redundant. The two refutation paths both fail: a) "Secretly needs the executor/server" — FALSE. The salvage is pure local UX over fields tiles already carry. filterTiles (cc-core.lua:322) reads it.status/it.cwd/it.group/it.label off the LOCAL status files; the missing facet fields are all already present: it.editor (cc-core.lua:125, the "source"), provider/providerId (cc-core.lua:2706), the live model captured by cc-status.sh (cc-status.sh:213 writes model into the status JSON), and it.group via applyGroups/groupNames. Token usage is already tracked (cc-core.lua:1611, "ZERO-COST: parsed from transcript JSONL"). A faceted matcher + chip rail + token/cost formatter run with no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace edit/snapshot, no server. Cost-in-dollars is the only net-new datum, and it is a pure formatter over existing tokens × a provider rate table — exactly the off-the-shelf-small pure addition the brief allows. b) "Fully redundant / already shipped" — FALSE for the salvage. The board itself IS shipped (tile grid, groupNames chips, click-detail, relabel/delete, New-session modal, audit/timeline) so the dashboard-qua-board is redundant — but the existing filterTiles is FREE-TEXT ONLY (token-AND substring over a fixed hay, cc-core.lua:328-331) and the only structured chips are the single-facet GROUP chips (#groupchips, claude-dashboard.lua:2681 / 3632). A TYPED multi-facet status:/provider:/model:/source: filter that matches on structured fields does not exist. And it is precisely pre-named in the backlog: L5's error-reason taxonomy item explicitly lists "a search/filter facet" (todos.md:514), and the detail-panel/Usage-tab + richer-tile theme is L5 (todos.md:499, 517-520). So a parallel dashboard is wrong; extending filterTiles into a faceted parser with a filterFacets sibling to groupNames, plus a pure per-tile token/cost line, folded into L5, is the honest call. Confidence high: the mismatch (no server is possible in this architecture — verified by grep), the redundancy of the board, and the genuine novelty + pre-naming of the salvage in L5 are all unambiguous in the code/backlog. ADAPT (reinforces L5) stands; I cannot refute it.

#### Cross-session team/group persistence + interrupted-run recovery  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** The candidate self-identifies its transferable half precisely: "the persisted-team-on-disk pattern + 'this session was interrupted' surfacing transfer; the run re-executor does not." The surfacing need is real and currently under-served. Shepherd already persists most state to disk (cc-queue/ projectKey-keyed, cc-labels.json, cc-groups.json, cc-presets.json, cc-templates.json, the append-only ledger, settings, panel geometry) and reloads it after a Hammerspoon reload or Mac restart — so the on-disk-persistence pattern is native here, not foreign. What Shepherd does NOT do today is explicitly mark "this session's turn was cut off when Shepherd/Hammerspoon last went down" as a first-class tile reason. It infers wedged-ness only obliquely: the magenta Error state (transcriptError) for API freezes, the hung watchdog (isHung/trackProgress), nextAttention ranking, and auto-respawn of dead sessions. A small "interrupted since last boot" reason — derived purely from comparing the prior persisted snapshot timestamp against status files on startup — would slot cleanly into L5's error-reason taxonomy / tile-reason work and make the restart gap visible. The queue/routing re-dispatch sliver (a task that was mid-delivery when the dispatcher restarted should be re-armed, not silently dropped) corroborates L4's routing-on-restart robustness. Crucially this is a WATCH/STEER surfacing, not an executor action: Shepherd flags it; the human (or the existing Continue/respawn paths) resumes it.
- **Against:** The headline construct is a cline TEAM: members + mailbox + mission log + runs + outcomes persisted to ~/.cline/data/teams/<name>/ so `cline --team-name X` resumes a multi-agent group. That is the cline-hub/teams SERVER and multi-agent-executor surface — Shepherd has no team object, no mailbox, no mission log, no runs/outcomes ledger of agent executions, no server, and is explicitly single-user. The load-bearing mechanism — markInProgressRunsInterrupted, queued-run re-dispatch, buildRecoveredRunMessage, hydrateState, the run re-executor — is run-lifecycle machinery for an in-process loop that READS/WRITES files, CALLS the LLM, and EXECUTES tools. Shepherd runs no agent loop and cannot re-execute a run; Claude Code itself owns that. Re-dispatching "queued runs" and feeding a "recovered run an auto-recovery prompt" is the executor doing work, which is the core cline mismatch. Building a per-team SQLite store (Sqlite->File fallback) for members/mailbox/runs would be a parallel persistence subsystem duplicating Shepherd's existing per-projectKey file stores and ledger, for entities (teams, runs, outcomes) Shepherd doesn't model. Taken as-is this is DONT_ADD; only the thin surfacing residue survives.
- **Ruling:** Apply the cline mismatch: the candidate is a multi-agent-team SERVER state store plus a run RE-EXECUTOR — both squarely in Shepherd's structural no-go zone (no server, no multi-user, no agent loop, no tool execution). The candidate even concedes the re-executor doesn't transfer. So the verbatim feature is DONT_ADD. But the residual NEED it points at is genuine and small: after a Hammerspoon reload or Mac restart, explicitly surface sessions whose turn was cut off mid-flight, rather than leaving that gap to be inferred from Error/hung/respawn heuristics. Shepherd already has every primitive to do this cheaply — it persists prior snapshots and reads status files on each refresh, so a pure cc-core comparison of "prior persisted updated-ts vs current status on first post-boot tick" yields an 'interrupted' tile reason with zero new subsystem. That makes it an ADAPT onto L5 (richer observability / error-reason taxonomy / tile-reason), with a minor reinforcement of L4 (re-arm a mid-delivery queued task on dispatcher restart instead of dropping it). It is NOT a parallel team/run persistence system — Shepherd's existing per-projectKey file stores + ledger already cover persistence; there are no teams/runs/outcomes to persist. Net: ADAPT, reinforces L5, because the real value is a watch-console surfacing detail, not the team/executor plumbing.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold the surfacing into L5 as a new tile/error reason "interrupted (turn cut off before last Shepherd shutdown)". Mechanism, pure and off-by-default: on the first refresh after Hammerspoon/Mac boot, cc-core compares each status file against the last-persisted fleet snapshot (Shepherd already keeps per-tick prev snapshots in-memory and persists queues/labels/groups to disk; add one lightweight cc-state snapshot of {projectKey, status, updated} written on the existing refresh loop). A session that was 'working'/'approval' in the prior snapshot AND whose status file hasn't advanced since boot gets it.interrupted=true → a distinct tile reason in the L5 error-reason taxonomy and a one-line detail-panel note. No new action is taken: resume stays the operator's existing Continue button (claude-dashboard.lua:2437 ledgerFor continue) or the existing auto-respawn/drain paths — Shepherd flags, the human/Claude Code resumes. Sliver into L4: when the level-triggered routing dispatcher restarts, a queued task that was mid-delivery (the FX.writeQueue/queuePop commit.persist guard already exists ~claude-dashboard.lua:1816-1820) should be re-armed rather than dropped — a robustness note on L4's queue feed, not a new run-executor. Explicitly NOT building: any team/members/mailbox/mission-log/runs/outcomes object, per-team SQLite store, run re-dispatch, or buildRecoveredRunMessage-style auto-recovery prompt injection (those are the cline executor/server and stay DONT_ADD).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT(->L5) ruling holds. Verified against the actual code. WHAT REFUTES THE VERBATIM FEATURE (and the judge already concedes): the cline candidate is (a) a multi-agent-TEAM state store (members/mailbox/mission-log/runs/outcomes, per-team SQLite at ~/.cline/data/teams/) — a SERVER/multi-user surface — and (b) a run RE-EXECUTOR that re-dispatches queued runs and injects auto-recovery prompts. Both are squarely in Shepherd's no-go zone: Shepherd runs no agent loop, executes no tools, edits no workspace, has no server/users (context.md HARD SCOPE LIMITS). The candidate itself states "the run re-executor does not [transfer]." So the verbatim feature is DONT_ADD. Shepherd has no teams/runs/outcomes to persist — its persistence is already covered by per-projectKey file stores (cc-labels.json/cc-groups.json/cc-queue, claude-dashboard.lua:61-65,470-474) + the opt-in JSONL ledger. No parallel persistence system is warranted. WHY ADAPT NONETHELESS HOLDS — I tried two refutations to push it to DONT_ADD and both fail: (1) "Redundant with existing frozen/error heuristics." Refuted by the code. The watchdog/auto-respawn frozen detection (cc-core.lua:2173-2175, stepAutoRespawn) only fires after `updated` goes silent for respawnStaleSeconds (default 600s) and CANNOT distinguish a turn cut off by a Hammerspoon/Mac restart from a long-running build — the comment at 2166-2171 explicitly notes a healthy long Bash command freezes `updated` for minutes. transcriptError/frozen-on-API-error (claude-dashboard.lua:5425-5427) keys off a transcript api_error, a different cause. None of these surface "this session's turn was cut off by Shepherd's own shutdown." That residual signal is genuine and distinct — it is exactly L5's error-reason taxonomy / tile-reason charter (todos.md:512-516). (2) "It fights the documented invariant that prev is in-memory and the first post-reload tick is an observation, not an edge (claude-dashboard.lua:5583-5586, cc-core.lua:1972)." This is real but does NOT defeat ADAPT — it CONSTRAINS the impl and the judge's proposal already respects it. That invariant exists to stop AUTONOMOUS ACTIONS (feed/respawn/continue) from mass-firing on boot. The proposal takes NO action: resume stays the operator's existing Continue button (handleAction "continue", dispatchSerialized at 5616) / existing auto paths; it only sets a read-only it.interrupted=true display flag + a tile reason + a one-line detail note. A pure observation flag is fully compatible with "first refresh is an observation." MECHANISM is sound and cheap, with one correction to the judge: Shepherd does NOT currently persist any fleet snapshot (grep for cc-state/fleetSnapshot returns nothing; prev is in-memory only, 139). So this requires ADDING one lightweight off-by-default disk snapshot of {projectKey,status,updated} on the existing refresh loop, compared on the first post-boot tick (status files already carry `updated`/`since`, cc-status.sh:81,193-195). That is one small persisted artifact feeding a pure cc-core derivation — net new code is minimal and lives in the existing L5 surface, not a new subsystem. NET: reinforces L5 (richer observability: a distinct "interrupted (turn cut off before last Shepherd shutdown)" tile/error reason + detail note), with a minor L4 robustness sliver (re-arm a mid-delivery queued task on dispatcher restart rather than dropping it — the delivery-gated queuePop/commit.persist guard already exists at claude-dashboard.lua:1812-1819). Explicitly DONT_ADD: any team/members/mailbox/mission-log/runs/outcomes object, per-team SQLite store, run re-dispatch, or auto-recovery prompt injection (the cline server/executor — stays out).

#### Diff / 'View Changes' since a checkpoint or last completion  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** The core operator value — "show me what this session actually changed in the codebase" — is exactly what a WATCH console wants, and it's the one signal Shepherd deliberately omits today (cc-core.lua:1128: "Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth, so the honest signal... is the continue/auto_continue count, not a claimed outcome"). A real diff view closes that gap with ground truth instead of inference: it turns "the prompt said fix X" into "here are the files that changed," which directly strengthens the shift report, lineage ("files touched" count), and the human's approve/deny judgment. Shepherd can produce this honestly from purely LOCAL reads it's already comfortable with: `git -C <cwd> status --porcelain=v1 -z` + `git diff` per file, rendered as a collapsible unified-diff `<pre>` in a detail-panel 'Changes' tab — no executor, no snapshotting, no API call. This is already recognized as worth building: it's a queued OpenHands-corroborated item (todos.md:493) that explicitly shares the L4/L5 diff-view tab framework. The cline feature is strong prior art that the diff surface is a high-value operator pattern.
- **Against:** cline's specific design is built on the agent-execution plumbing Shepherd structurally lacks. The diff is taken against a CHECKPOINT — a snapshot of the workspace cline itself created because cline is the in-process executor writing those files; Shepherd never writes the files and keeps no per-edit snapshots, so "diff between two checkpoints" and "Restore" (revert workspace to a checkpoint) are uncopyable — they require the snapshotting executor and would mutate a workspace Shepherd is forbidden to edit. "View Changes since the previous completion" depends on cline's task/completion event boundaries inside its own loop; Shepherd only sees status-file transitions, not authoritative completion markers. And "Explain Changes" makes a fresh LLM call to stream per-hunk commentary — a flat scope violation: Shepherd "does NOT proxy/make API calls," stores no secrets, and runs no agent loop. Taken literally, most of the feature is DONT_ADD; only the read-only diff-view shell survives, so copying it "close to as-is" is impossible.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED (visualize what a session changed) is real and already queued, so this is ADAPT, not ADD or DONT_ADD. The decisive move is reshaping the substrate: cline diffs against agent-authored checkpoint snapshots because cline is the executor; Shepherd has no checkpoints and cannot make them (no agent loop, no workspace editing), so it must diff against git working-tree/HEAD state instead — which is exactly the queued 'Per-session git changes/diff view' (todos.md:493-497) that feeds the L5 detail-panel 'Changes' tab. Three cline sub-features are explicitly DONT_ADD slices to drop: (1) Restore-to-checkpoint (needs the snapshotting executor + mutates the workspace); (2) two-checkpoint compare (no checkpoints exist); (3) 'Explain Changes' streaming LLM commentary (Shepherd does not make API calls — hard scope limit). What transfers and improves Shepherd: the multifile collapsible unified-diff UI, the binary-file filter, the 'See New Changes' availability badge (Shepherd's analog: only show the tab/affordance when `parseGitStatus` reports >0 changed files, mirroring getDiffCount>0), and the "diff since a boundary" idea reshaped onto Shepherd's real boundaries (last spawn/clear/continue ledger event, or session start) rather than cline task-completions. Lazy-load on open + manual refresh, never on the 1s tick — consistent with Shepherd's discipline. I tie reinforces to L5 (the detail-panel tabs + the OpenHands diff-view sub-item both live in/under L5; L5:519 names the 'Changes' tab from the L4 diff view directly).
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the queued OpenHands diff-view sub-item (todos.md:493) and L5 detail-panel tabs (todos.md:517). Concretely: add a 'Changes' tab in the L5 detail-panel tab strip; pure `core.parseGitStatus(porcelain)` (A/D/M/R/?? — already unit-tested per the queued spec) drives an availability badge that only shows the tab when changed-files>0 (Shepherd's getDiffCount>0 analog); `FX.gitChanges(cwd)` = `git -C <q> status --porcelain=v1 -z`, `FX.gitDiff(cwd, path)` per file, rendered as collapsible unified-diff `<pre>` (not Monaco), binary files filtered out by git's own marker. Add an optional "since boundary" selector that diffs against `HEAD` or the commit/time of the session's last spawn/clear/continue ledger event (Shepherd's honest analog of "since last completion"), feeding a "files touched" count into the shift report / lineage to fill the deliberately-omitted "what changed" signal (cc-core.lua:1128). Lazy on open + manual refresh, never the 1s loop; off-by-default. DROP from cline: Restore-to-checkpoint, two-checkpoint compare, and the 'Explain Changes' LLM call (scope: no API calls, no executor, no snapshots).
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute and could not; the ADAPT ruling holds, verified against the code. Two refutation angles both fail. (1) "It secretly needs the executor / API." Confirmed it does NOT, and the judge already excised exactly those parts. cline diffs against agent-authored checkpoint SNAPSHOTS because cline runs the loop and edits files; Shepherd does neither — its only "snapshots" are in-memory tile/ledger status reads (claude-dashboard.lua:136, cc-core.lua:1356/1973), never file-content snapshots of an edited workspace. So Restore-to-checkpoint and two-checkpoint compare are correctly DROPPED (no snapshotting executor, mutates workspace). The 'Explain Changes' streaming LLM call is correctly DROPPED too: Shepherd "costs no tokens and makes no network call" (cc-core.lua:1613) and "calls the LLM" only by typing keystrokes into a live session (hs.eventtap.keyStrokes via dispatchSerialized, claude-dashboard.lua:1221/1451) — a hard scope limit. The three headline cline differentiators are all out, exactly as the executor/no-API mismatch dictates. (2) "What's left is redundant with the already-queued git diff view → DONT_ADD." This was my strongest refutation and it still fails, which is why this is ADAPT not DONT_ADD. The queued 'Per-session git changes/diff view' (todos.md:493-497) and L5 detail-panel tabs (todos.md:517) cover the multifile collapsible unified-diff <pre>, git's own binary filter, the unit-tested pure core.parseGitStatus (A/D/M/R/??), the changed-files count, and lazy-on-open discipline — and FX.gitRoot already exists (claude-dashboard.lua:954). But cline contributes two genuine reinforcements the bare queued item lacks: the "See New Changes" AVAILABILITY BADGE (show the tab/affordance only when changed-files>0 — the honest getDiffCount>0 analog), and the "diff SINCE A BOUNDARY" selector reshaped onto Shepherd's real boundaries (HEAD, or the last spawn/clear/continue ledger event) instead of cline task-completions. That second item directly fills the signal cc-core.lua:1128 deliberately omits: the shift report reports "OPERATIONS ONLY: there is NO 'what shipped' changelog, by design... Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth." A real git-diff Changes tab supplies that ground truth honestly (files touched, not claimed outcomes). That is reinforcement of L5, the textbook ADD→ADAPT case, not a parallel system and not redundancy. The judge mapped to the correct queued item (L5, todos.md:493/517), applied the executor mismatch and the no-API-calls limit precisely, dropped the right three sub-features, and kept only what transfers onto Shepherd's primitives with lazy/manual-refresh discipline and off-by-default. Verdict stands: ADAPT (high), reinforces L5. Refutation unsuccessful.

#### History browse: fuzzy search / sort / facet filters / favorite-pin  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** An operator running a fleet accumulates a long tail of past activity, and "find that one session/run and act on it" is a real recurring need. The candidate's verbs — fuzzy text search, sort by recency/size/relevance, toggle facets (favorites-only, current-workspace-only), and star-to-protect — are pure browse/filter/sort UX with no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing. That places them squarely in the transferable MANAGEMENT/UX lane the brief endorses. Shepherd already proves each primitive in isolation: filterTiles (token-AND search over sessions), fuzzyFilter (fd-backed), the rg-backed fleet-wide transcript+ledger search (searchArgv/parseSearchResults/annotateSearchHits), filterLedger (ts/session/type facets), and a pin/persist pattern (groups via cc-groups.json, relabels, detail-tab pin/unpin keyed by stable projectKey + localStorage). "Current workspace only" is just projectKey filtering Shepherd already does everywhere; "favorites only" maps directly onto that projectKey-keyed persistence. Auto-switching sort to most-relevant when a query is present is a cheap ergonomics win. Consolidating today's three scattered search surfaces (tile, fleet, audit) behind one query box with sort + facet chips would make the existing data far more usable — a genuine UX upgrade, not a new subsystem.
- **Against:** cline's HistoryView browses a first-class persisted entity Shepherd structurally does not have: a saved, resumable TASK (a full conversation the executor recorded, with finalized token/cost/size totals and a resume action). Shepherd persists only ephemeral live tiles (from cc-status/<id>.json) and an opt-in operations ledger that deliberately records "operations only... NO 'what shipped' changelog, by design" (cc-core.lua:1126) — there is no per-task object to list, sort, or star. Two of the candidate's sort keys lack an honest data source: "most-expensive" requires dollar cost, which Shepherd never computes (it tracks local tokens/context at zero cost and has no pricing tables — adding $ math is provider-dependent and out of scope), and "most-relevant" presumes a stored-document corpus to score. Favoriting a "task" implies a durable task record to protect from deletion, but Shepherd has no task store and no retention to protect against; favoriting would have to be redefined as pinning a projectKey — a different object than cline's isFavorited. So the feature as specified leans on the executor's persisted-conversation model; only its filter/sort verbs survive the mismatch, and most of those already exist.
- **Ruling:** The candidate is browse/filter/sort/pin UX with no executor dependency, so it clears the cline mismatch — not DONT_ADD. But it is not a clean ADD either: (1) the headline value (fuzzy history search) is already implemented across three surfaces, and (2) the feature's object — a resumable, cost-bearing, favoritable TASK — does not exist in Shepherd. What is genuinely new and worth pulling: unifying the scattered search surfaces behind one query box with sort options and facet chips (favorites/current-workspace), plus a star/pin facet. Reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, the corpus is the ledger + transcript records (not cline tasks), sort keys become newest/oldest/most-tokens/most-relevant (DROP most-expensive — no $ data), favorite becomes a projectKey-keyed pin reusing the cc-groups/relabel/detail-tab persistence pattern, and current-workspace-only is existing projectKey filtering. This is exactly L5's lane (richer session observability & detail; todos.md:514 already proposes a search/filter facet on the error-reason taxonomy, and todos.md:519-520 already proposes tab pin/unpin persisted to localStorage keyed by projectKey). Hence ADAPT reinforcing L5, high confidence.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L5. Build one search/history overlay (or extend the audit overlay) querying the ledger + transcript corpus Shepherd already reads: (a) a single fuzzy query box reusing filterTiles/searchArgv, auto-biased to relevance ordering when the query is non-empty; (b) sort chips newest/oldest/most-tokens/most-relevant fed by filterLedger ordering + the existing zero-cost transcript token aggregation — DROP most-expensive (no dollar cost in scope); (c) facet toggles "current workspace only" (projectKey filter, already used everywhere) and "pinned only"; (d) a star/pin persisted by stable projectKey in cc-groups/relabel-style JSON or webview localStorage (same pattern as detail-tab pin/unpin, todos.md:519-520), keeping the pure-core-selectors / fx-read split. Rows show tokens + size from existing token tracking; no cost column, no resumable-task object, no new persisted task entity.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L5) verdict holds. (1) No-mismatch confirmed: this is pure browse/filter/sort/pin UX — no agent loop, tool execution, workspace edit/snapshot, or server — so the cline-executor and server grounds for DONT_ADD do not apply. (2) Not a clean ADD: the headline value (fuzzy history search) is already implemented three times over — core.filterTiles + its JS twin tileMatches/visibleItems behind the #searchbar (cc-core.lua:322 / claude-dashboard.lua:3618,3011); the ripgrep fleet transcript+ledger search core.searchArgv/parseSearchResults/annotateSearchHits with its own overlay (cc-core.lua:830-894); and core.fuzzyFilter for folders (cc-core.lua:3502). And the cline 'resumable, cost-bearing TASK' object does not exist in Shepherd, so it can't be added verbatim. (3) Not DONT_ADD-redundant either: the genuinely-new residue is unifying those scattered surfaces behind one query box with sort chips + facet toggles plus a star/pin FACET over ledger/transcript records — pinning a history record doesn't exist today (pin exists only as the queued detail-tab pin, todos.md:519-520). The judge's Shepherd mapping is accurate and conservative: corpus = ledger (filterLedger, cc-core.lua:523, already sorts newest-first and filters by projectKey) + transcript token aggregation (sumUsage, cc-core.lua:1684); favorite/pin reuses the projectKey-keyed setLabel/setGroup persistence pattern (cc-core.lua:307/377); current-workspace-only is existing projectKey filtering. Critically it DROPS the most-expensive sort, which is correct — token usage is local zero-cost (cc-core.lua:1611, no network call) and there is NO dollar/price/cost field anywhere in the codebase. This is squarely L5's lane (todos.md:514 already proposes a search/filter facet; 517-520 the tab pin/unpin persisted by projectKey; L5 framed as richer observability over the transcript Shepherd already tails). Reshaped-onto-primitives with overlap and a small new facet = ADAPT reinforcing L5, not ADD and not DONT_ADD.

#### Card detail view: agent TUI + scoped diff + inline diff comments as steering  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** "Review the changes this session made, then tell it to fix one" is the single most valuable review-console action, and Shepherd is well-positioned for two of the three halves. (1) The scoped-diff half maps cleanly onto a real git diff Shepherd can own: it already has the L4 item specced — `FX.gitChanges(cwd)` runs `git status --porcelain=v1 -z`, `FX.gitDiff` per file, pure unit-tested `core.parseGitStatus`, collapsible unified-diff rendered as `<pre>` (deliberately not Monaco), lazy on open. That delivers "a diff of all changes in that card's worktree" without any executor. (2) The steering half is already live: the candidate itself concedes "the steer-via-free-text-message pattern transfers," and Shepherd's nudge textarea + `handleAction "nudge"` paste (cc-core.lua:138-146; todos.md:426 says the conversational half "is already the live nudge textarea") IS exactly send-free-text-back-to-the-running-agent. (3) A detail view to host both is queued as L5 "Detail-panel tabs" (Activity/Timeline/Decisions/Usage/Queue + Changes). So a "Changes" tab where each file's diff renders and a per-file (not per-line-anchored) comment becomes a nudge is a coherent, buildable ADAPT that materially improves the watch/review loop and feeds the "files touched" signal into the shift report.
- **Against:** The candidate's distinctive value is the three features Shepherd structurally cannot host. (1) "The agent's full TUI (conversation/actions)" — Shepherd runs no agent loop and owns no conversation buffer; it only tails the transcript for derived signals (status, error, plan/TODO) and reaches the real TUI via Jump-to-window. Rendering the live TUI is the editor's job, and L5 explicitly DROPs Code/Terminal tabs. (2) "Checkpoint system scoping diffs to message ranges" requires snapshotting the workspace the agent edits across turns — that is executor/workspace-management plumbing Shepherd has none of; it can only ever show working-tree-vs-HEAD via real git, not message-range checkpoints. (3) "Clicking any diff line lets the operator leave a comment sent back as steering" — anchoring a comment to a specific diff line and feeding it into the agent's in-context attention is in-process executor work (cline drives the loop and re-injects). Shepherd can paste free text into a window but cannot bind it to a line the agent will treat as a positioned annotation. So the parts that make this card more than "a diff tab + the existing nudge box" are precisely the cline-executor parts — building those is impossible, and building only the achievable parts means it collapses into work already queued, arguing for DONT_ADD-as-redundant rather than a new feature.
- **Ruling:** The card decomposes into three halves with sharply different fits. TUI rendering = DONT_ADD (no agent loop/conversation buffer; the real TUI is the editor window via Jump; L5 already drops Code/Terminal tabs). Checkpoint-scoped-diff and line-anchored-comment-as-steering = the cline mismatch (workspace snapshotting + in-process re-injection Shepherd cannot do). But the load-bearing operator NEED — review a session's changes in a detail view, then steer it with free text — is already on the roadmap and partly shipped: the L4 "Per-session git changes/diff view" item (todos.md:493-497, real-git `FX.gitChanges`/`FX.gitDiff` + pure `parseGitStatus`, `<pre>` not Monaco, lazy) is the achievable diff half, and the nudge textarea + `handleAction "nudge"` (cc-core.lua:138-146; todos.md:426) is the steering half the card admits transfers. L5 "Detail-panel tabs" is the home that hosts both. So this is not a new system — it ADAPTS onto and reinforces L5 (which itself pulls in the L4 diff item). The honest reshape: a Changes tab showing real working-tree diff with per-FILE (degrade from per-line) comments that fire the existing nudge paste, scoped vs HEAD (not message-range checkpoints). Confidence high — the mapping to existing queued items is exact.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reinforces L5 (Detail-panel tabs, todos.md:517-521) which already pulls in the L4 "Per-session git changes/diff view" (todos.md:493-497). Concretely: (a) Diff = the queued L4 path — `FX.gitChanges(cwd)` (`git -C <q> status --porcelain=v1 -z`) + `FX.gitDiff` per file, pure unit-tested `core.parseGitStatus` (A/D/M/R/??), collapsible unified-diff `<pre>` in a new "Changes" detail-panel tab, lazy on open + manual refresh (never on the 1s loop, per the refresh-discipline invariant); scope is working-tree-vs-HEAD via real git, NOT message-range checkpoints. (b) Comment-as-steering = a per-file (degraded from cline's per-line) "comment" textarea in the Changes tab whose submit routes through `handleAction "nudge"` → `FX.pasteIntoWindow` (cc-core.lua:138-146), gated on the delivery-status return per the keystroke-delivery contract, ledgered. (c) DROP entirely: rendering the agent TUI (Jump stays the editor affordance, consistent with L5 dropping Code/Terminal tabs), the checkpoint/message-range snapshotting, and true diff-line-anchored injection. Feeds a "files touched" count into the shift report/lineage (the deliberately-omitted "what changed" signal). Pure assembly in cc-core; render in panel JS.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L5) ruling is correct and its citations are verified in the codebase. The card cleanly decomposes into three halves with different fits, and the judge handles each correctly: (1) TUI rendering = DONT_ADD. Confirmed in cc-core.lua: there is no agent-conversation/agent-loop buffer. "TUI" appears only as the keystroke target (the permission TUI / kitty TUI that fx sends keys to, e.g. lines 21, 185, 2766), never a rendered agent conversation. The editor window via Jump is the real TUI, and L5 explicitly DROPS Code/Terminal/Browser tabs (todos.md:521). Shepherd renders no agent. (2) Checkpoint-scoped diffs + line-anchored comment re-injection = the cline mismatch. These need workspace snapshotting (checkpoints of files the agent EDITED) and in-process re-injection at a diff line — Shepherd edits nothing, runs no agent loop, manages no workspace (context.md:13 "No network, no multi-user, no secrets... reads session status off the local filesystem"). No checkpoint-of-edited-files concept exists in core. Correctly dropped. (3) The load-bearing operator NEED (review a session's changes, then steer with free text) is already queued/partly shipped, exactly as the judge maps: - Diff half = L4 "Per-session git changes/diff view" (todos.md:493-497): real-git FX.gitChanges(cwd) via `git -C <q> status --porcelain=v1 -z` + FX.gitDiff, pure unit-tested core.parseGitStatus (A/D/M/R/??), collapsible unified-diff <pre> (NOT Monaco), lazy on open + manual refresh, never on the 1s loop, scoped working-tree-vs-HEAD (NOT message-range checkpoints). Exact match, including the refresh-discipline invariant and the "files touched" signal into the shift report/lineage. - Steering half = the SHIPPED handleAction "nudge" (cc-core.lua:138-148) -> fx.pasteIntoWindow(tgt,{text}), gated on the `delivered()` keystroke-delivery contract (so an undelivered paste is never ledgered). Free-text paste into the running session — exactly the pattern the card itself admits transfers. - Home = L5 "Detail-panel tabs" (todos.md:517-521), which already lists "Changes from the L4 diff view" as a tab. This is not a new system: it maps onto L5 (host tab strip) + the L4 diff item with exact fidelity. Verdict ADAPT (not ADD) is right because there IS a net-new affordance — wiring a per-FILE comment (degraded from cline's per-line) in the Changes tab to fire the existing nudge paste — so it reinforces/extends rather than being pure redundancy (which would be DONT_ADD). I cannot push it to DONT_ADD (the comment-to-nudge wiring + files-touched count are genuinely new, not already present) and cannot push it to ADD (it lands inside already-queued L5/L4, not a parallel system). ADAPT-reinforces-L5 is the precise verdict. Ruling holds. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (L4 diff item 493-497, L5 detail-panel tabs 517-521); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (handleAction nudge 119-148); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/context.md (scope limits, line 13).

#### Ship a card: Commit or Open PR (agent-driven, with conflict handling) + trash/resume cleanup  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** "Ship it" is a real operator need at the end of a Claude Code session's lifecycle, and Shepherd already owns the rest of that lifecycle (spawn -> watch -> nudge -> drain -> respawn -> clear). A "Ship" affordance fits as the terminal step. Crucially, Shepherd has a working precedent for exactly this shape: the Improve button (cc-core.lua:466-495) parses `git remote get-url origin`, builds a review-first PROMPT, and lets the already-running session do all the git work — Shepherd never runs git itself. A "Commit"/"Open PR" tile button could follow that identical pattern: emit a parameterized prompt ("commit your worktree changes onto base; if base moved, resolve conflicts; push and open a PR") into the session's own window via the dispatchSerialized chokepoint; the session executes it under the human's gate. The conflict-handling intelligence the candidate wants lives in the agent, which Shepherd is already steering. The trash/resume-cleanup half maps even more directly: Shepherd already does drain/close, tracks projectKey as stable identity across close/reopen, caches FX.gitRoot, and is worktree-aware on spawn — a "remove this session's ephemeral worktree on trash, keep a resume handle" action is squarely lifecycle management, not agent execution. And L5 already queued PR/MR-status-per-tile + a git changes/diff view, so a Ship action gives those read-only surfaces something to react to (badge flips to "PR #123 open").
- **Against:** The candidate as written is "agent-driven... the agent runs git/PR ops against an owned workspace... intelligently resolve merge conflicts... merge worktree changes into a commit on base." That is the cline executor doing tool execution and workspace editing — the exact mismatch. Worse, it is the single most deliberately-rejected capability in Shepherd's codebase: cc-core.lua:1128 enshrines "Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth... a prompt is an instruction, not an outcome" as a load-bearing design stance, and todos.md:439 already ruled "DROP the branch/commit/push executor — the spawned session owns that," with L5 (todos.md:531) reiterating "STATUS ONLY... DROP create tools... any VCS API call by Shepherd." Shepherd manages no workspace and owns no worktree — the worktree is the executor's (cline/apps/cli/src/utils/worktree.ts), created and torn down by the agent runtime Shepherd doesn't host. "Auto-commit/Auto-PR" pushing code without a human in the loop contradicts Shepherd's reason to exist (human-in-the-loop approve/deny). And "moving a card to trash removes its ephemeral worktree" presumes Shepherd created/owns that worktree — it didn't; deleting another tool's git worktree from under it is destructive overreach Shepherd has no authority for.
- **Ruling:** The candidate bundles two things with opposite verdicts. (1) The git/PR EXECUTION + conflict-resolution + auto-commit/auto-PR is pure cline-executor / workspace-editing mismatch, and it is the most explicitly-defended-against feature in the repo (cc-core.lua:1128, todos.md:439/531). Shepherd cannot and by stated design will not run git, edit/snapshot a workspace, or own a worktree. (2) But the underlying NEED — a terminal "ship" step in a session's lifecycle, and cleanup-on-trash with a resume handle — is real and maps onto Shepherd's primitives. The resolution is the Improve-button pattern: Shepherd emits a parameterized SHIP PROMPT (commit-or-PR, with conflict-handling instructions) into the session's own window via dispatchSerialized; the session does the git work under the gate; L5's read-only PR-status badge reflects the result. That is steering + prompt emission, not execution — which Shepherd already does. The trash/resume-cleanup half folds into existing drain/clear lifecycle keyed by projectKey, with the caveat that Shepherd must NOT delete a worktree it doesn't own; at most a "clear + remove worktree" prompt the session runs, or a guarded cleanup only on a worktree Shepherd itself spawned. So: ADAPT, reshaped onto prompt-emission + L5 status, executor/auto-push/own-the-worktree parts dropped. Reinforces L5 rather than spawning a parallel ship-pipeline.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a "Ship" tile action (sibling to Improve) with two prompt variants from new pure cc-core builders: core.commitPrompt(item) and core.openPrPrompt(item), each rendering a review-first instruction ("commit/PR your worktree changes onto <base>; if base moved, resolve conflicts; push; open PR — present a plan first / honor the gate") using FX.gitRoot + core.repoFromRemote (already present).
