Delivery via the dispatchSerialized chokepoint into the session's own window — Shepherd emits the prompt, the session executes git under the human gate (no auto-push by default; opt-in time-boxed autopilot already exists for hands-off). Wire the OUTCOME into L5: core.parsePrStatus/prBadge flips the tile to "PR #123 open/merged" via the opt-in cached `gh pr view` poll; ledger a ship/pr-opened event into lineage + shift report (without claiming outcome beyond what gh reports). Trash/resume: extend the drain/clear lifecycle keyed by projectKey — a "Clear + remove worktree" action that (a) only deletes a worktree Shepherd itself created on spawn (track it on the spawnSpec), else (b) emits a `git worktree remove` prompt to the session rather than deleting another tool's workspace; resume handle = existing projectKey + respawnSpec. DROP: Shepherd running git/PR ops itself, auto-conflict-resolution logic, default auto-commit/auto-PR, and Shepherd-side deletion of worktrees it didn't create.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT down to DONT_ADD and could not — the verdict holds, reinforcing L5 (and L3). The feature splits into three asks; verifying each against the repo confirms the judge's reshaping. (1) Git/PR EXECUTION + auto-conflict-resolution + auto-commit/auto-PR against an "owned workspace" is the textbook cline executor / workspace-editing mismatch. The repo defends against it at cc-core.lua:1128-1129 ("Shepherd has no git/CI/diff ground truth ... a prompt is an instruction, not an outcome") and todos.md:438-439 ("DROP ... the branch/commit/push executor — the spawned session owns that"). Spawn is literally `cd <existing folder> && claude ...` (cc-core.lua:2974, spawnInner): Shepherd launches a session in a pre-existing dir, runs no agent loop, edits nothing. Correctly DROPPED. (2) The ship-prompt + PR-status residue genuinely fits Shepherd's primitives, which is why this is NOT DONT_ADD. It is a structural clone of the live Improve button: pure builder core.improvePrompt (cc-core.lua:481, review-first prompt) delivered via dispatchSerialized + FX.pasteIntoWindow into the session's own window (claude-dashboard.lua:1054-1055), with core.repoFromRemote / FX.gitRoot already present. A "Ship" tile action emitting core.commitPrompt/openPrPrompt is the prompt-emission + steering Shepherd already does, not execution. (3) Trash/resume-worktree: "worktree" appears NOWHERE in the repo (cc-core.lua, claude-dashboard.lua, context.md, todos.md, shell hooks). Shepherd never creates/owns a worktree, so the judge's "track-on-spawnSpec / cleanup a worktree Shepherd created" branch is machinery for a non-existent concept; the honest mapping is "emit a git worktree remove prompt the session runs" folded into the existing drain/clear lifecycle keyed by projectKey, resume = existing projectKey + respawnSpec. Why ADAPT not ADD: the candidate is framed as a full agent-driven commit/PR card (ADD), but after the executor DROP only the prompt-emission slice survives, and that slice is already queued. PR-status is verbatim L5 "PR/MR status per tile" (todos.md:530-533: core.parsePrStatus/prBadge, opt-in gh pr view poll, ledger PR-opened/merged into lineage + shift report, DROP create tools/token/VCS API). The ship-prompt is already the L3 work-feed "Fix this → render template → inject via the Improve/pasteIntoWindow path" + resolver-with-"ask before pushing" (todos.md:433/436-439) and the summaryPrompt sibling-to-improvePrompt pattern (todos.md:534). So it is a reinforcement/extension of queued primitives — not a new system, not nothing — exactly the ADD→ADAPT downgrade the rubric prescribes. One caveat the "high" confidence understates: the worktree-ownership sub-part deserves a harder drop and the net-new value is thinner than implied (mostly already queued), but neither flips the verdict.

#### Per-run Markdown report artifacts  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** A durable per session post run note fits a watch console and is mostly half built. Shepherd already tails each session transcript JSONL, parses token and cost usage from it via usageEvent and sumUsage near cc-core.lua line 1611, renders Markdown from pure core via standupMarkdown near 1201, builds per session timelines via sessionTimeline near 732, and keeps an audit ledger. The candidate reshaped framing, a durable summary note captured from a session final message and status, is exactly what L5 already queues twice as the Post run self summary callback, a summaryPrompt sibling to auditReviewPrompt that fires on a fresh done edge, and as Export session archive, which writes the transcript plus a meta file under cc-exports via the existing audit export side effect path. Writing a small Markdown file per completed or failed session, with frontmatter for sessionId, projectKey, label, provider, model, status, and lineage, plus a body of summary text, usage totals, decision and timeline counts, and an error reason, is a deterministic byproduct of signals Shepherd already derives. It needs only a pure cc core assembler plus one FX file write on the done or error edge, off by default and honoring ledger redaction. It serves the operator directly as a browsable folder of what each session ended up doing, dovetailing with the PR status and error taxonomy rollups already queued in L5.
- **Against:** The cline implementation is welded to the agent executor. Fields like runId and trigger info, and the body listing tool calls, exact cost, and trigger event details, all come from cline owning the run loop and the scheduler, because it executed the tools and therefore knows the true call list and cost. Shepherd runs no loop and executes no tools. It can only read the local transcript after the fact plus the ledger of decisions it mediated, so any report is a reconstruction rather than a record of execution. There is also a real redundancy and over build risk. The fleet window version already shipped as the Shift Report using fleetStandup and standupMarkdown with Copy, so a per run file artifact could duplicate that surface and tempt scope creep toward authoritative outcomes, which context.md and the L5 self summary item explicitly forbid. Finally the runId and cron reports framing is scheduler shaped, and Shepherd has no scheduled run concept, only live sessions, so the keying and trigger fields must change.
- **Ruling:** The cline mismatch test asks whether the feature value depends on running the agent, executing tools, editing the workspace, or serving users. The artifact writing and the operator value, a durable browsable per session note, do not. They are post hoc reads of the local transcript plus ledger Shepherd already performs. Only cline authoritative fields, the true tool call list, the exact cost of this run, and trigger plumbing, depend on owning the executor, and those are dropped or softened in the reshape. So this is not DONT_ADD, because the need is real and the data is local. It is not ADD as is either, because the cron, runId, and trigger framing and the execution derived fields do not exist in Shepherd, and the framing must shift from a record of a run to a captured summary of a session. That is the textbook ADAPT, reshaping onto Shepherd primitives. It reinforces L5 specifically, being the union of the existing Post run self summary callback, the done edge trigger plus summary capture, and Export session archive, the file write plus meta side effect path, plus the markdown serialization pattern from the shipped shift report. The right build is one pure core.runReportMarkdown assembler plus the existing audit export FX write fired on the fresh done or error edge, off by default, with no new authoritative claims. Effort is M, because the data sources, usage parse, timeline, ledger filter, standupMarkdown, and the audit export side effect, all exist, so the work is a new pure assembler, an edge detector reusing the remoteControlSweepTargets and shouldAutoContinue patterns, frontmatter and body templating, a config gate, redaction handling, and unit tests.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L5. Add core.runReportMarkdown taking the session item plus a context carrying usage, timeline, decisions, errorReason, and lineage, as a pure sibling of standupMarkdown near cc-core.lua line 1201. Frontmatter is keyed by projectKey plus session_id plus label, not a cron runId since Shepherd has no scheduled runs, plus provider, model, and status. The body comes from the final message summary, the usageEvent and sumUsage totals near 1611, the sessionTimeline counts near 732, and the classifyError reason from the L5 error taxonomy item. Fire on the fresh done or error edge using the same target filter pattern as remoteControlSweepTargets and shouldAutoContinue, writing via the existing audit export side effect path, which means mkdir a cc_reports folder beside cc-exports, write the file, and optionally alert or Reveal, reusing the L5 Export session archive plumbing. Optionally emit a report ledger event. Off by default. Honor ledger redaction so prompt bodies are not leaked. Frame strictly as a Shepherd captured session summary, never an authoritative run record. Reuse the fleetStandup and standupMarkdown rendering conventions so the per session note and the Shift report stay visually consistent.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation failed; ADAPT holds, reinforcing L5. I tried to push this to DONT_ADD on the cline-mismatch (executor/cron) grounds and on redundancy, but verified against the code that neither sticks. (1) Executor/scheduler parts ARE a real mismatch and are correctly dropped in the reshape: the feature's native frame writes per "scheduled run" to ~/.cline/cron/reports/<run-id>.md with frontmatter keyed by runId/specId/trigger info, and the authoritative tool-call list + exact run cost. Shepherd has no cron/scheduled-runs/runId — confirmed by grep over context.md and README (zero matches). The judge softens exactly these (no runId, no authoritative claims). (2) But the operator value — a durable, browsable per-session summary note — does NOT depend on running the agent, executing tools, editing/snapshotting the workspace, or serving users; it's a post-hoc read of the local transcript + ledger Shepherd already tails. Every data source and the side-effect path exist: M.standupMarkdown (cc-core.lua:1201, the markdown-serialization sibling already used for the shift report), M.sumUsage (1684), M.sessionTimeline (732), the L5 classifyError taxonomy, the last_prompt/summary fields (cc-status.sh:199; e.summary at cc-core.lua:678/689), the audit-export side effect (claude-dashboard.lua:2120: mkdir+FX.writeFile+UTC-dated filename+alert, redaction already enforced), and the fresh done/error edge + target-filter pattern (remoteControlSweepTargets:2250, stepAutoContinue/shouldAutoContinue:2200-2239). (3) Not redundant or a parallel system: it is a near-exact UNION of two already-QUEUED L5 items — 'Export session archive' [H/S] (file write + meta.json, reuse audit-export path, honor redaction) fired on the done/error edge of 'Post-run self-summary callback' [M/M] (target filter modeled on remoteControlSweepTargets/shouldAutoContinue, optional summary ledger event). So it reinforces L5 rather than adding a new capability. Not ADD-as-is (cron/runId/specId/trigger framing genuinely absent; framing must shift from 'record of a run' to 'captured session summary'); not DONT_ADD (need is real, data is local, no executor required). Build: one pure core.runReportMarkdown assembler + the existing audit-export FX write on the fresh done/error edge, off by default, no new authoritative claims. Effort M.

#### doctor — local health, stale-process inspection, fix, and log  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L5
- **For:** A consolidated fleet-health view is a genuine operator need that fits Shepherd exactly. cline's `doctor` is "is my control plane healthy and are there zombie processes to reap" — recast onto Shepherd that becomes "is my fleet healthy, which tiles are dead/orphaned/hung, and let me reap them in one click." Shepherd already computes every input signal but scatters them: the stuck-session watchdog (core.isHung / trackProgress / applyProgress, purple .hung ring), orphan/ghost prune (core.shouldPrune for stale+no-session_id tiles), ghost-duplicate detection (core.staleDuplicateKeys after /clear or restart), the auto-respawn death-evidence gate (core.shouldAutoRespawn: frozen-at-working past respawn.auto.staleSeconds), and a tooling `make doctor` already exists. None of this is surfaced as ONE health summary the operator can read at a glance and act on. The candidate's own mapping — "detect dead/orphaned sessions (status file present but window gone), one-click reap-stale" — is the one true gap: Shepherd has hs.window/FX.focusWindow and can verify a tile's window still exists, but it does NOT cross-check window liveness against the status file, so a session whose terminal/editor window was closed (without SessionEnd firing) lingers as a misleading tile. A 'Fleet Health' detail-panel/section that rolls up hung + orphan + ghost-duplicate + window-gone counts with a 'reap stale' button (already-built prune/removeStatus + the existing intentional-close path) is squarely watch/steer, all local-filesystem-sourced, and reinforces L5 observability.
- **Against:** Most of cline's `doctor` payload is its server architecture and has literally no referent in Shepherd: hub URL/pid/uptime, listening pids, stale hub/CLI/sidecar pids, startup locks, RPC listeners, active connectors. Shepherd has NO hub, NO daemon, NO sidecar, NO RPC server, NO connectors — context.md forbids a network server outright. `doctor fix` killing stale RPC listeners/old processes via SIGTERM is reaping cline's own orphaned daemons, which Shepherd doesn't run; and Shepherd deliberately does NOT kill Claude processes (it has no pid tracking — it reads status files, steers via keyboard/hooks, and 'closes' via the session's own finish-then-close path, not SIGTERM). Importing process-kill semantics would cross Shepherd's watch/steer boundary into managing OS processes it doesn't own. Worse, much of the value is already shipped: `make doctor` covers tooling health, the watchdog covers hung sessions with re-alert, shouldPrune/staleDuplicateKeys already prune orphans and ghosts every tick, and auto-respawn already detects death — so a large chunk is redundant. The genuinely-new slice (window-gone detection + a rollup card) is small and additive, not a new 'doctor' subsystem.
- **Ruling:** The cline mismatch cleanly splits this feature. The hub/sidecar/RPC-listener/connector/startup-lock health and the SIGTERM-based `doctor fix` are pure cline-server-process plumbing — Shepherd runs no server, no daemon, and tracks no pids, so those are DONT_ADD by structure (and `doctor log` is trivially covered by Hammerspoon's own console). But the candidate explicitly states the transferable need: a fleet-health view detecting dead/orphaned sessions (status-file-present-but-window-gone) with one-click reap-stale. That is a watch/steer operator pattern, fully local-filesystem-sourced, and Shepherd already owns nearly all the substrate (isHung watchdog, shouldPrune orphans, staleDuplicateKeys ghosts, shouldAutoRespawn death-evidence, make doctor tooling). The honest verdict is therefore ADAPT, not ADD (too much is server-shaped or already shipped) and not DONT_ADD (a real, fitting gap remains). The reshape: a 'Fleet Health' rollup in the detail panel / a ⚙ section that surfaces hung/orphan/ghost-duplicate counts already computed, PLUS the one genuinely-new detector — cross-check each non-remote tile's window liveness (hs.window) against its status file to flag 'window gone, status lingering', wired to the existing prune/removeStatus + intentional-close path for one-click reap (NOT SIGTERM of a tracked pid). This is L5's lane (richer session observability + detail: window-gone is a new error-reason, and a health rollup is a detail-panel surface). Effort is M: signals exist, the new work is the window-liveness cross-check (watch for false positives on minimized/other-Space windows and on editor sessions sharing one window across tabs), the rollup UI, and the reap button reusing prune. Confidence high on the verdict split; the only judgment call is whether the window-gone check is reliable enough not to false-reap, which argues for surfacing-then-confirm rather than auto-reap.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reinforces L5 (richer session observability & detail). Build a 'Fleet Health' rollup — a detail-panel tab / ⚙ section — that surfaces signals Shepherd ALREADY computes but scatters: hung count (core.isHung / watchdog .hung), orphan/ghost-duplicate count (core.shouldPrune + core.staleDuplicateKeys), and death-edge candidates (core.shouldAutoRespawn). Add the ONE genuinely-new detector the candidate names: 'window gone, status file lingering' — cross-check each LOCAL non-remote tile's window liveness via hs.window/FX.focusWindow target against the present status file, flagging tiles whose terminal/editor window closed without a SessionEnd. Wire a one-click 'reap stale' to the EXISTING prune/removeStatus + intentional-close path (surface-then-confirm, NOT SIGTERM of a tracked pid — Shepherd tracks no pids and must not kill processes it doesn't own). Tooling health and the runtime log are already covered by `make doctor` and the Hammerspoon console, so do not rebuild those. Explicitly DROP the cline hub/sidecar/RPC-listener/connector/startup-lock health and `doctor fix` SIGTERM semantics — no referent in a server-less, daemon-less, pid-less watcher. Add 'window-gone' as a new error-reason in L5's taxonomy. Guard against false positives on minimized / other-Space windows and on editor sessions sharing one window across tabs (prefer confirm over auto-reap).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted on three fronts; none unseats the ADAPT-reinforces-L5 ruling. (1) "It's secretly server/pid/executor plumbing": true ONLY for the cline half the judge already dropped to DONT_ADD — hub URL/pid/uptime, listening/stale pids, startup locks, connectors, sidecar/RPC health, and `doctor fix`'s SIGTERM. Verified Shepherd tracks no pids and runs no daemon: FX.removeStatus (claude-dashboard.lua:1089) is just os.remove of a status file, and the spawn layer holds no process handles. So the SIGTERM-of-a-tracked-pid semantics have no referent and `doctor log` is covered by `make doctor` (Makefile:28-29, confirmed) + the Hammerspoon console. That avenue refutes nothing in the held portion. (2) Redundancy — my strongest line, but it cuts toward ADAPT not DONT_ADD: Shepherd ALREADY computes every health signal the rollup would surface — core.isHung (cc-core.lua:2375), core.shouldPrune (2268), core.staleDuplicateKeys (2295), core.shouldAutoRespawn (2116) — and the refresh loop already AUTO-prunes orphans/ghosts each tick (claude-dashboard.lua:5219-5229). But surfacing-already-computed-signals in a detail-panel section is the literal charter of L5 ("reshape views Shepherd ALREADY produces"), so redundancy = "fold into L5," exactly the ruling. (3) Feasibility/reliability of the one genuinely-new piece, "window gone, status file lingering": this is where DONT_ADD had its best shot and still fails. A stable session→window binding exists only for kitty (kitty_listen_on + kitty_window_id, used in staleDuplicateKeys termId); for editor tiles Shepherd has only TITLE-SUBSTRING matching (focusProject, claude-dashboard.lua:207-251) which the code itself documents as unreliable (loose fallback, prefix-named-sibling issues) and cannot tell apart multiple Claude tabs in one VS Code window. So an AUTO window-gone reaper would false-reap editors. Yet the judge already named exactly these hazards (minimized / other-Space / shared-window) and prescribed surface-then-confirm + adding "window-gone" as a new error-reason in L5's taxonomy (todos.md:512 classifyError already has runtime_error/timeout/unknown slots) — a confirm-gated badge wired to the existing prune/removeStatus path is defensible even though a silent auto-reaper is not. Net: the cline-server half is correctly DONT_ADD; the transferable fleet-health rollup + confirm-gated window-gone detector is a real, local, watch-console gap that lands squarely in L5. ADD is wrong (most of the feature is server-shaped or already shipped); DONT_ADD is wrong (a genuine gap remains). ADAPT reinforcing L5 holds.

### Reinforces L4 — routing/orchestration  (10)

#### Team mode toggle + named team (lead/teammate roles with role-gated tools)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** Shepherd is explicitly a multi-session fleet console, and "designate a coordinator that may spawn/manage subordinates, group them under a name, label roles, and nest children under a parent" is a genuinely useful operator pattern that the user's own roadmap already wants. The three transferable bits the prompt names all map onto Shepherd primitives without any agent loop: (1) the spawn flag = a per-role allowSpawn bit that hides the spawn affordance on a teammate tile (a guardrail-bundle/L2 bit); (2) the coordinator/teammate tile label = an L5 tile badge keyed off an L1 role; (3) nesting teammates under a lead via parent ids = exactly the L4 "sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage" item (stamp parent_session/spawnedBy, draw parent->child edges, collapse children under parent). A "team" is just a named session GROUP, which Shepherd already has. It gives the fleet view a real org structure (who coordinates whom) that today's flat tile grid lacks, and it's the natural label layer on top of the already-queued hierarchical-manager + delegation routing.
- **Against:** The literal cline feature's value is the EXTRA TOOL SURFACE the flag unlocks: the lead AGENT gains a set of team-management TOOLS (spawn/shut-down/clean-up teammate) that it CALLS inside its in-process agent loop, with every tool checking getMemberRole==='lead' at execution time. Shepherd runs no agent loop and executes no tools, so there is no lead agent to grant tools to and no runtime to enforce a per-tool role check — the core mechanism cannot be hosted. cline's lead-only "shut-down/clean-up teammates" is also a workspace/process-lifecycle action over agents the lead orchestrates; Shepherd's stance is the opposite (never auto-kill a child the human is steering — cascade-delete was explicitly dropped). And the team/coordinator construct in cline is partly a server/multi-user surface (cline-hub teams), which is hard-out of scope. Copying the feature as-is would be re-skinning a tool-execution + serving concept onto an app that has neither.
- **Ruling:** The cline feature has two layers. The valuable, transferable layer is org/structure metadata: a named team, a lead/coordinator vs teammate ROLE per member, role-gated authority to spawn/shut-down/clean-up subordinates (allowSpawn), and nesting teammates under a lead via parent ids. The non-transferable layer is the "extra tool SURFACE" the flag unlocks — an in-process set of team-management TOOLS the lead agent CALLS during its own agent loop. That tool-execution surface is the canonical cline mismatch: Shepherd runs no agent loop and executes no tools, so "the lead agent gains tools to manage teammates" cannot exist — there is no lead AGENT, only a lead SESSION the human steers. So the literal feature is DONT_ADD as-is. But the underlying NEED — designate one session as the coordinator that can spawn/manage subordinate sessions, group them under a team name, and show coordinator/teammate roles on tiles with parent->child nesting — is real and already squarely queued. todos.md L4 (Declarative routing & orchestration) explicitly carries: (a) "Hierarchical manager = designate which profile the coordinator session spawns from — NO in-process manager/manager_llm" (line 459) — this IS the lead/coordinator-with-spawn-authority idea, correctly reshaped to "the coordinator session emits spawn flags" rather than "the lead agent calls a spawn tool"; (b) "Delegation / role-addressed handoff" with addressable role/name identity from L1's cc-agents.json (lines 463-467); (c) "Sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage: stamp parent_session/spawnedBy, show parent->child edges, collapse children under parent" (lines 487-492) — this IS nesting teammates under a lead via parent ids, and it even explicitly says DROP cascade-delete (never auto-kill a child the human is steering), which is the right Shepherd answer to cline's lead-only "shut-down/clean-up teammates." The three named transferable bits map cleanly: spawn-authority -> L4 hierarchical-manager + L2 policy (allowSpawn = a guardrail-bundle/role bit, not a runtime tool gate); coordinator/teammate tile label -> L5 tile badge + L1 role; parent-id nesting -> L4 parent/delegation lineage. A "team" itself = an existing session GROUP (Shepherd already has session groups + armed-project routing); "teamName" = the group/armed-project label. So nothing here is parallel-new; it reinforces and slightly enriches L4 (plus L1 identity, L2 allowSpawn bit, L5 role badge). Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L4. The reshape: a team = a session group with a name; one member flagged role:'lead'/coordinator (the others 'teammate'); a per-role allowSpawn bit (L2 bundle) that, when false, hides the New-Session/spawn affordance for that tile (UX gating of a human-facing button, NOT a runtime tool gate); coordinator/teammate badges on tiles (L5); parent_session stamping so teammates nest under the lead in the topology/lineage view (L4 sub-conversations). All off-by-default, dry-run-able, ledgered. The honest delta beyond what L4 already says is small: the explicit named-team-as-group binding + the per-member role badge + the allowSpawn UX bit. That makes it a low-cost enrichment of L4, not a new system — hence effort S.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L4 (reinforces L4), drawing identity from L1 and the role badge from L5. Concretely: (1) Team = a named session GROUP / armed project (already exists) — teamName binds to the group label; no new container. (2) Per-member role on the tile: role:'lead'|'teammate' stored beside the L1 agent identity (cc-agents.json) / session group membership; lead/coordinator + teammate badges rendered as an off-by-default tile badge (L5 badge framework). (3) allowSpawn = a bit in an L2 guardrail bundle (or the role) that, when false on a teammate tile, hides/disables the New-Session and respawn affordances in the panel UI — a human-facing button gate, NOT a runtime tool gate (Shepherd cannot enforce tool calls). The lead's "manage teammates" becomes: only the lead-flagged tile shows the spawn-into-team and route-to-team controls, which emit the existing spawn flags / enqueue onto the team's routing queue (L4 hierarchical-manager: "designate which profile the coordinator session spawns from"). (4) Nesting: when the lead spawns/delegates, stamp parent_session/spawnedBy on the spawn/route ledger event (the L4 "sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage" item) so teammates nest under the lead in the routing topology view and collapse under the parent in session groups. Keep everything off-by-default, dry-run-able, ledgered; DROP cascade-delete/clean-up. Honest delta over L4 as already written: the named-team<->group binding, the per-member lead/teammate role badge, and the allowSpawn UX bit — a thin enrichment, not a new subsystem.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT/reinforces-L4 ruling holds. I tried to break it both directions and neither sticks. Push to DONT_ADD (redundant) fails: the transferable kernel is MOSTLY but not FULLY in L4 already. Verified in todos.md: L4 line 458-459 explicitly carries "Hierarchical 'manager' = designate which profile the coordinator session spawns from — NO in-process manager/manager_llm" (= lead/coordinator-with-spawn-authority, already reshaped); lines 463-464 carry role-addressed delegation drawing role/name identity from L1's cc-agents.json; lines 487-492 carry "Sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage… stamp parent_session/spawnedBy… parent→child edges… collapse children under parent" WITH an explicit "DROP cascade-delete (never auto-kill a child the human is steering)" — the correct Shepherd answer to cline's lead-only shut-down/clean-up. session groups already exist (cc-core.lua line 341 applyGroups; cc-groups.json) and armed-project routing v1 ships. But three small deltas are genuinely NOT verbatim in L4: (1) the named-team↔group binding (teamName = group/armed-project label), (2) a per-member lead/teammate ROLE badge distinct from group membership (rendered via the existing tile-badge framework, L5), and (3) the allowSpawn UX bit (hide the New-Session/respawn affordance on a teammate tile — a human-facing BUTTON gate, NOT a runtime tool gate, reshaped onto an L2 bundle bit). These are real reinforcements/enrichments, so it is not pure redundancy. Push to ADD (literal feature) fails: the cline feature's value mechanism is "the flag unlocks an in-process tool SURFACE the lead AGENT calls during its agent loop" — every team tool checks getMemberRole==='lead'. Shepherd runs no agent loop and executes no tools (hard scope limit, no executor). There is no lead AGENT, only a lead SESSION the human steers, so "the lead agent gains tools to manage teammates" cannot exist. That is the canonical cline mismatch and makes the literal feature unbuildable as written. What survives the cline-mismatch filter is org/structure metadata + UX (named team = session group, lead/teammate role badge, allowSpawn button gate, parent_session nesting), which is a thin enrichment of L4 (identity from L1, badge from L5, allowSpawn bit from L2; DROP cascade-delete). That is exactly ADD→ADAPT (extension/reinforcement of an existing queued item), not a parallel new system and not a server/multi-user feature. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L4, holds.

#### Mission log — structured coordination journal (progress/handoff/blocked/decision/done/error)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** This is the one cline-team feature whose value is pure observability/coordination, not agent execution — so the cline mismatch does NOT bite. An append-only, typed, queryable activity journal is exactly Shepherd's reason to exist as a watch/steer console. The candidate's own framing is honest about this: the kinds + evidence[]/nextAction shape are "a useful taxonomy for an append-only event ledger derived from status transitions and human actions" — i.e. it explicitly invites the ADAPT, not a port of cline's in-process journal. The genuinely additive contribution over what Shepherd already logs is the COORDINATION taxonomy: today the ledger records operator/mechanical events (spawn, decision, nudge, mode_change, clear, respawn, escalation, hung, auto_continue, route_arm — see claude-dashboard.lua ledgerFor call sites ~1554-2437) but it does NOT have first-class handoff / blocked / decision-rationale / done-with-nextAction entries that read as a multi-session work narrative. Those four kinds slot cleanly onto the SHIPPED ledger (cc-core.lua parseLedger/filterLedger/narrateEvent/renderNarrative/sessionTimeline, lines 505-741) and onto queued L4 delegation/handoff (todos.md ~463-477, which already wants `by:"router"`/delegation ledger events and a `task_done` event carrying duration/handoff counts). A `nextAction`/`blocked-on` field also directly serves L5's "surface plan/TODO on a tile" and the shift report. Low marginal cost: the storage, parse, filter, retention, redact/purge, narrative, timeline, and shift-report rendering all already exist — adding kinds is mostly new `NARRATE[t]` entries plus emit sites at the handoff/done boundaries.
- **Against:** A large chunk of the candidate is simply REDUNDANT with what Shepherd already ships. Shepherd's audit/event ledger is already append-only JSONL, already typed with ~25 kinds, already throttled-by-design (events emit at action boundaries, not on the 1s tick), already persisted (cc-ledger/ daily files with retention/size GC), and already queryable as history (filterLedger by session/projectKey/time/types; capLedgerSlice; sessionTimeline; renderNarrative; the 📜 Audit overlay; fleet insights; shift report). "decision" is already a richer first-class kind than the candidate's (outcome/tool/by/pattern, gateDecisionSummary grouping). "done" is observable from the status file's `done` state and is already counted; "error" is the shipped magenta Error tile + transcriptError. So building `team_mission_log` as a NEW parallel store (mission-log.json, a new TeamMissionLogInputSchema, missionLogIntervalSteps/Ms) would duplicate the ledger and violate the project's single-source-of-truth instinct — todos.md L4 explicitly says "the dispatcher + the ledger ARE the engine; DROP a parallel system." Worse, the candidate's deepest premise — an AGENT authoring progress/handoff/blocked entries as it executes — has no host in Shepherd: there is no in-process agent loop to write self-narrated progress, so the most distinctive cline behavior (the agent journaling its own reasoning mid-run) cannot transfer. What's left after removing the redundant store and the agent-authored entries is a modest taxonomy extension, not a feature.
- **Ruling:** The cline mismatch (no executor/workspace/server) genuinely does not apply here — a coordination journal is observability, which is Shepherd's core competence. That rules out DONT_ADD. But ADD is wrong because the substance — append-only typed queryable persisted throttled history — is already SHIPPED as the audit/event ledger (cc-core.lua 496-796), with a broader, more accurate kind set derived from real status transitions and human actions. Building a separate mission-log.json + TeamMissionLogInputSchema would be a parallel ledger, which todos.md explicitly warns against. The real, non-redundant nugget is the coordination TAXONOMY (handoff, blocked, decision-rationale, done-with-nextAction) plus the evidence[]/nextAction shape. That reshapes onto the existing ledger and most strongly extends queued L4 (declarative routing & delegation/handoff), which already plans `by:"router"`/delegation events and a per-task `task_done` event — handoff/blocked/done-with-nextAction are exactly the human-readable companions to those router events. It also feeds L5 (surface plan/TODO, error-reason taxonomy) and the shift report. Note the agent-authored-progress dimension is structurally unhostable; only the operator/transition-derived subset transfers. Hence ADAPT, reinforces L4, high confidence.
- **How (Shepherd):** Do NOT add a new mission-log.json/TeamMissionLogInputSchema store. Instead extend the SHIPPED ledger: (1) Add coordination kinds to the ledger's event vocabulary and to `NARRATE` (cc-core.lua, the table behind narrateEvent ~670-692): `handoff` {from, to/role, taskId, summary, nextAction}, `blocked` {summary, blockedOn, nextAction}, and a `done` summary carrying `nextAction`/`evidence`. Emit `handoff` at the L4 delegation boundary (todos.md ~463-467: render an L3 task + enqueue onto a named L1 agent's queue; the router already wants a `by:"router"`/delegation ledger event — this is its human-readable twin) and at the existing cross-session nudge ("Ask coworker"). Emit `done`/`task_done` from the per-task timing follow-up (todos.md ~473-477) so it carries durationS + nextAction. (2) Reuse evidence[] as a small links array on the event (relative paths / transcript anchors / PR url) so narrateEvent and the shift report can show "what backs this." (3) No new throttle subsystem — events are already boundary-emitted, not tick-emitted; the gateDecisionSummary-style grouping already collapses bursts. (4) Render through the EXISTING surfaces: filterLedger by `types={handoff,blocked,done}` for a "coordination" filter in the 📜 Audit overlay, sessionTimeline for the per-tile drill-down, and roll handoff/blocked counts into fleetStandup (the Shift tab). (5) `nextAction`/`blockedOn` also surfaces on the tile as part of L5's "surface plan/TODO on a tile."
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT/reinforces-L4 ruling holds. WHY DONT_ADD (cline mismatch) FAILS: A coordination journal is pure observability — typed entries derived from status transitions and human actions. That is Shepherd's core competence, not the in-process executor. Verified the SHIPPED ledger does exactly this with no agent loop / tool execution / workspace editing / server: cc-core.lua 496-797 — append-only JSONL (parseLedger 505, filterLedger 523 with session/projectKey/types/time slicing, capLedgerSlice 553), retention/GC (expiredLedgerFiles 597, ledgerCapVictims 625), narration (NARRATE table 642-667, narrateEvent 670), per-session timeline (sessionTimeline 732), gate-burst grouping (gateDecisionSummary 752), notification rollup (notificationEvents 787). The mismatch does not bite. WHY DONT_ADD (redundancy) FAILS — but kills the new-store half: The mechanics (append-only/typed/queryable/persisted/throttled) are 100% already shipped, so a separate mission-log.json + TeamMissionLogInputSchema would be a parallel ledger — and todos.md explicitly forbids that ('superset, not a parallel system' L1 line 257; 'one event family… reuse JSONL' L6 line 562; gateDecisionSummary 752 already collapses bursts, so no new throttle subsystem). BUT the feature is NOT fully redundant: the NARRATE vocabulary (642-667) has no handoff, no blocked, and no nextAction/evidence fields. The coordination TAXONOMY (handoff{from,to,nextAction}, blocked{blockedOn,nextAction}, done/decision carrying nextAction + evidence[] links) is a genuine delta. WHY ADD FAILS: The non-redundant nugget reshapes onto the existing ledger; it does not justify a new system. The agent-authored-progress dimension is structurally unhostable (Shepherd watches status files; it does not run the agent and cannot author its progress entries) — only the operator/transition-derived subset transfers, i.e. an extension, not a new store. WHY ADAPT / REINFORCES L4 IS RIGHT: The taxonomy is the human-readable twin of L4's already-queued router/delegation plumbing — verified in todos.md L4 (lines 463-477): ledger by:'router'/delegation events emitted at the delegation boundary ('Delegate' = render an L3 task + enqueue onto the named L1 agent's queue, line 465-467), the 'Ask coworker' cross-session nudge (466), and the task_done ledger event {projectKey,sessionId,role,durationS,toolRequests,approvals,escalations,continues,routerHandoffs} (474-475) rolled into fleetStandup/Shift tab. fleetStandup (cc-core.lua 1137) already aggregates lifecycle/problem/routed counts and already special-cases e.by=='router' (1169), so handoff/blocked/done counts fold in naturally. Surfaces already exist: filterLedger by types for a 'coordination' filter in the 📜 Audit overlay, sessionTimeline for per-tile drill-down, fleetStandup for the Shift tab; nextAction/blockedOn also feeds L5's surface-plan/TODO-on-a-tile (todos.md 504). No existing team_mission_log/mission-log reference found anywhere in the repo (grep clean), confirming it isn't already built. Net: extend the shipped ledger's vocabulary with handoff/blocked/done-with-nextAction + an evidence[] links array; do NOT add a parallel store. Ruling holds at ADAPT, high confidence. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (496-797 ledger, 642-667 NARRATE, 1137-1195 fleetStandup); /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md (441-497 L4, 504-516 L5, 544-562 L6).

#### Concurrency / overlap control for scheduled spawns (don't start if previous still busy)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The core NEED — "don't start a new run for a target while its previous run is still busy" + "a global cap on concurrent automated spawns" — is a pure operator-side dispatch guard, not agent execution, so it transfers cleanly. Shepherd ALREADY implements this concept piecemeal and would benefit from formalizing it: routePendingDone (cc-core.lua ~2007) is already a per-target in-flight guard (stops double-feeding a session before its status flips done→working); sessionFree (~2020) refuses any non-done session; routeTask/routePick pick at most ONE target per project per tick (de-facto per-project concurrency=1); BULK_STAGGER/staggerSlot serialize bulk spawns; auto-respawn (canRespawn ~2131) and auto-continue (shouldAutoContinue ~2200) both carry per-folder budgets. What is MISSING is exactly the candidate's two transferable pieces: (1) an explicit per-schedule/project maxParallel "skip if a member is still working," and (2) a GLOBAL cap across all sessions — Shepherd has per-folder budgets but no fleet-wide concurrent-spawn ceiling. todos.md:202 already flags the deferred "auto-spawn on starvation" item as blocked precisely on needing "a cap + UX on when/where" — this candidate IS that cap. As a pure cc-core predicate (sibling to sessionFree/routeBarrierMet) plus a fleet active-spawn counter, off by default, it slots into L4 with near-zero new surface.
- **Against:** The candidate's literal substrate does not exist in Shepherd: there is NO cron/scheduled-spawn mechanism. Every automation Shepherd has is event-triggered (death→respawn, stall→continue, queue+free-session→route); the hs.timer.doEvery loops drive status refresh / usage / bridge-sync, not periodic spawns. cline's ResourceLimiter exists to stop a SCHEDULED EXECUTOR from running multiple concurrent agent LOOPS and exhausting host CPU/RAM — that resource-exhaustion framing is the agent-execution concern Shepherd structurally cannot have (it runs no loop, the OS + Claude Code own the processes). And the overlap-guard value Shepherd needs is ALREADY 90% delivered by routePendingDone + sessionFree + the one-target-per-tick dispatcher + per-folder respawn/continue budgets — so importing a standalone "ResourceLimiter with acquire/release + requeue on 'concurrency limit reached'" risks building a parallel concurrency subsystem next to the dispatcher that already enforces this, which is exactly the "no parallel system" trap todos.md warns against.
- **Ruling:** The cline ResourceLimiter is built to keep a cron-driven agent EXECUTOR from spawning overlapping agent loops — that resource-exhaustion + scheduler framing is the mismatch (Shepherd runs no loop, schedules no spawns today). But strip that away and the residual operator pattern — "don't fire while the prior run for this target is still working" + "a global cap on concurrent automated spawns" — is a pure dispatch predicate that fits Shepherd perfectly, and Shepherd already implements it partially (routePendingDone as the in-flight guard, sessionFree's done-only rule, one-target-per-tick as implicit per-project cap=1, per-folder respawn/continue budgets). The only genuinely missing pieces are an explicit per-project maxParallel knob and a FLEET-WIDE concurrent-spawn ceiling — and L4 already owns the dispatcher these extend, while todos.md:202's deferred auto-spawn-on-starvation item explicitly asks for exactly this "cap." So it is not ADD (no scheduler to bolt onto, and a standalone limiter would parallel the dispatcher) and not DONT_ADD (the need is real and on-brand); it is ADAPT reinforcing L4.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L4 (declarative routing & orchestration) as a small extension of the existing dispatcher, NOT a new ResourceLimiter subsystem. (1) Per-project/per-schedule cap: add an optional maxParallel to the queue file's routing config; in routePick/routeTask (cc-core.lua ~2041–2070) count how many members of that project are currently working/pending and refuse to feed once count >= maxParallel — generalizing today's implicit cap=1 and the existing routePendingDone in-flight guard. (2) Global cap: a pure core.spawnSlotAvailable(activeAutomatedSpawns, globalCap) predicate (sibling to sessionFree ~2020 / routeBarrierMet) that the dispatcher AND the deferred auto-spawn-on-starvation path (todos.md:202) consult before any automated spawn; the dashboard keeps the live active-spawn counter (like injectionTailAt for BULK_STAGGER). On cap-hit, the task stays in the projectKey-keyed queue (requeue = it simply isn't dispatched this tick — no new requeue machinery). Reuse the L6 / by:"router" ledger to emit a concurrency_skipped event so the operator can see why a feed was withheld. All behind the existing queue.routing.enabled + routing:true double opt-in, off by default. Explicitly DROP cline's acquire/release lock object, the cron scheduler, and the host-resource-exhaustion framing.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails — the ADAPT ruling is correct against the actual code. The cline-specific value (a ResourceLimiter with acquire/release locks guarding a cron-driven in-process EXECUTOR from spawning overlapping agent LOOPS, plus host-resource-exhaustion framing) is the part that does NOT transfer, and the judge correctly drops it. But the residual is a pure dispatch predicate — "count active members of a target; refuse to feed once count >= cap" — which is exactly what Shepherd's level-triggered dispatcher already does, and that needs no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing/snapshotting, and no server. Verified in the code: (1) the dispatcher already enforces an IMPLICIT per-project cap=1 — claude-dashboard.lua:5630-5635 loops per project queue (routeGroups) and core.routeTask (cc-core.lua:2062) returns exactly ONE pick per tick; tests at tests/core.test.lua:2360-2378 confirm a single deterministic feed. (2) The in-flight guard already exists — routePending[item.key] (dashboard:5644), the done-only core.sessionFree (cc-core.lua:2020, status~='done' -> false), and core.routePendingDone (2007) implement 'don't fire while the prior run for this target is still working.' So the per-project subset is a REINFORCEMENT, not new. (3) The genuinely-missing pieces are exactly the two the judge names: an EXPLICIT per-project maxParallel knob (generalizing the hardcoded cap=1) and a FLEET-WIDE concurrent-spawn ceiling — the dispatcher's loop over routeGroups (5630-5677) has NO global throttle, so N armed projects can each feed one session in a single tick with no fleet cap. (4) The deferred-item fit is the clincher: todos.md:202 defers auto-spawn-on-starvation and states VERBATIM it 'needs a cap + UX on when/where,' and the queueStarved/starvedSince clock already exists (cc-core.lua:2075; dashboard:165) — a global concurrent-spawn ceiling is literally that cap. The double opt-in (queue.routing.enabled + per-project routing:true) and the by:'router' ledger channel are already in place for the concurrency_skipped event. Conclusion: not DONT_ADD (the need is real, on-brand, and half-built), not ADD (no scheduler to bolt onto; a standalone ResourceLimiter would parallel the existing dispatcher and duplicate routePending/sessionFree). It is a small extension of L4's declarative routing & orchestration. ADAPT (reinforces L4) holds.

#### Kanban board of task cards (manual or agent-generated; pending/in_progress/blocked/completed lifecycle)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The status-columned view of work units is a genuine operator need and Shepherd is already 80% of the way there. It HAS the lifecycle data: projectKey-keyed queues (pending), the level-triggered Project Routing v1 dispatcher feeding a free member (in_progress), the gate/escalation/hung machinery (blocked), and done/idle status off the status files (completed) — plus a ledger that records every transition. What it LACKS is the rollup: a single read-only view that shows, per armed project, how many tasks are queued vs in-flight vs starved vs done, with a completion sense. That is exactly the 'routing topology view (plot, reshaped)' already queued in L4 — a pure cc-core aggregation (sibling to fleetStats/lineageByProject) over routing state + by:"router"/queueStarved/starvedSince ledger events, rendered in the panel JS twin. The card ACTIONS also each have a real Shepherd home: start = feed/dispatch a queued task; block-with-reason = the gate's deny-reason (L2) / escalation; complete-with-summary = L5's post-run self-summary; claim = the router's longest-free pick. So the NEED maps cleanly onto shipped + queued primitives, and the per-status counts / completion% are a small pure aggregation, not new infrastructure.
- **Against:** cline's actual implementation is its team/hub SERVER layer (team/types.ts, team/team-tools.ts, team/projections.ts, launchKanban as a separate launched app) — the multi-user surface the brief flags as the SERVER mismatch, which Shepherd structurally cannot host. The card lifecycle is also agent-execution-coupled: 'claim' and 'complete-with-summary' presuppose an in-process executor that RUNS the work and reports an outcome, whereas Shepherd runs no agent loop and never owns a work-result. Worse, a free-floating card store with its own pending/in_progress/blocked/completed lifecycle is a second source of truth for 'what work exists,' duplicating the queue + routing + ledger and competing with projectKey-as-stable-identity — exactly the 'task backlog' that was already DECLINED as domain creep in the June 2026 pad-mirror batch (a local-first agent-era task manager). And a 'separate launched app' for the board is redundant with the tile grid, which already IS a board/overview of every session. Building it close to as-is would graft a standalone task tracker onto a session watcher.
- **Ruling:** The crux is the cline mismatch plus a near-exact local precedent. cline's Kanban is a SERVER/multi-user surface (the team-hub) whose cards carry an execution-coupled claim/complete/block lifecycle — Shepherd has no server, no users, no agent loop, and never owns a work-result, so the feature as-built is DONT_ADD territory. But the underlying NEED — a status-columned rollup of work units (pending / in-flight / blocked / done) with counts and a completion sense — is real and ALREADY maps onto Shepherd's shipped Project Routing v1 (projectKey queues + dispatcher + starvation flags + ledger) and the queued L4 'routing topology view.' That makes this ADAPT, not DONT_ADD or ADD. I anchor it to L4 because L4 explicitly contains the legitimate reshape (a read-only in-panel topology/rollup of routing+queue state, gated to ledger/routing-on) and the per-task timing/outcome counters that supply the completion sense. I deliberately do NOT propose a new card system: a 'task backlog' from a near-identical local-first task manager (pad) was just declined as domain creep, and the card actions all already have homes (feed/gate/router-pick/L5 summary). So the answer is: extend L4's topology view with per-status counts and a completion read off the existing queue+ledger — no card store, no separate launched app, no claim/complete/block lifecycle object.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L4's queued 'routing topology view': a pure cc-core aggregation (sibling to fleetStats ~943 / lineageByProject ~1061) that walks the projectKey-keyed queues (queueDepth, ~1856) plus the routing/ledger signals (by:"router", queueStarved/starvedSince, task_feed, plus a new task_done counter from L4's per-task timing item) into per-status buckets: queued (queue tasks not yet fed) = pending, routed-and-working = in_progress, escalation/hung/gate-held = blocked, fresh done/idle = completed. Surface per-status counts + a completion read in the panel JS twin (insights/sparkline/timeline family), gated to appear only while routing+ledger are on — NOT a new card store, NOT a separate launched app (the tile grid is the board), NOT a claim/complete/block card object. The action verbs already exist: start=feed/dispatch, block-reason=L2 deny-reason/escalation, complete-with-summary=L5 post-run self-summary, claim=router longest-free pick. Keep projectKey as the single source of truth for 'what work exists' — read from the queue+ledger, never a parallel kanban store.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT (reinforces L4) ruling and could not. Both refutation paths fail against the verified code. REFUTE-TO-DONT_ADD fails. cline's Kanban is a server/multi-user team-hub with execution-coupled cards (claim/complete/block objects where the agent owns a work-result). Shepherd has no server/users/agent loop, owns no work-result, edits/snapshots no workspace — so the feature AS-BUILT (separate launched app + card store + lifecycle object) is indeed DONT_ADD, and the judge correctly refuses all three (no card store, no separate app, no claim/complete/block object). But the judge is not smuggling that in: the underlying need (a status-columned rollup of work units with counts + a completion sense) maps onto primitives I confirmed exist. projectKey-keyed queues + queueDepth (cc-core.lua:1856) = pending; the level-triggered dispatcher routeTask/routePick/sessionFree (~2041-2070) routing work to free sessions = in_progress; queueStarved/starvedSince (~2075) + escalation/hung ledger signals = blocked; fresh done = completed. fleetStats (~954) and lineageByProject (~1061) are existing PURE aggregations that already bucket ledger events per project with counts. So a read-only per-status rollup off queue+ledger is a genuine LOCAL single-user console improvement — not DONT_ADD. REFUTE-TO-ADD fails. The feature cannot be added wholesale — the separate launched app, the card store, and the execution-coupled lifecycle object all require either a server or the in-process executor Shepherd lacks. The reshape onto Shepherd's primitives is mandatory, so it is not a clean ADD. ANCHOR is correct. L4 (todos.md:468-472) ALREADY queues a 'routing topology view' described verbatim as a read-only in-panel topology of routing/queue state, pure cc-core aggregation sibling to fleetStats/lineageByProject, gated to routing/ledger-on; and L4's per-task timing item (~473-477) adds the task_done counter that supplies the completion sense. Extending that view with per-status counts is reinforcement of an existing queued item, not a new system. The action verbs have verified homes: start=feed/dispatch (shouldFeed/routeTask), block-reason=L2 deny-reason+escalation, complete-with-summary=L5 post-run self-summary (todos.md:534), claim=router longest-free pick (routePick). This is the textbook ADAPT shape (real need + near-exact local precedent + mandatory reshape). Ruling holds at ADAPT, reinforcing L4. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua, /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/todos.md.

#### Shared task board with dependency chains + auto-start of next ready task  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The core mechanic — tasks carry dependsOn[], the list derives isReady (all deps completed) and blockedBy, and completing/trashing a card auto-starts the next ready dependent — is a near-exact restatement of work already queued for L4 and partly shipped. L4's join barriers (`core.routeBarrierMet(members, opts)` → true only when all/any predecessors are done) ARE the isReady/blockedBy computation; the shipped dispatcher already does "auto-start next ready" on a fresh done edge (`shouldFeed` cc-core.lua:1975, `routeTask`/`routePick` ~2041-2070, level-triggered single-pick-per-tick). "Assignee → route to a specific member" is L4's role-addressed delegation + `routePick` filtered by a route label, with addressable identity from L1 agent profiles. "Completion summary" is L5's post-run self-summary; "status" is the tiles Shepherd exists to show. Persistence (task-board.json) maps onto queue files that already ride an arm flag (`queueSetRouted`) keyed by projectKey, plus cc-templates.json for the structured task record. Linking cards into a chain is a thin UX layer over dependsOn that resolves to the barrier/sequential-mode the dispatcher already honors — and it directly unblocks the 4c-E follow-ups deliberately deferred (the affinity/route-label source). Strong fit, low conceptual novelty, reuses pure unit-tested cc-core primitives.
- **Against:** The feature is framed as "team-SHARED" — its cline evidence is squarely the cline-hub/teams server surface: team/team-tools.ts createTask/claimTask, team/schema.ts, an assignee field implying multiple identities contending for a shared list. That is the multi-user-serving half of the cline MISMATCH Shepherd structurally cannot host (no network server, no multi-user — context.md HARD SCOPE). claimTask presumes concurrent claimants negotiating over a shared store; Shepherd is one operator on one Mac. Taken literally and whole, this is DONT_ADD. There is also real overlap risk: Shepherd already ships a board (the tile grid), a queue, Project Routing v1, and saved task templates — naively adding a parallel task-board.json plus its own dependency dispatcher would duplicate the level-triggered dispatcher and create a second source of truth fighting the queue files. todos.md is explicit that the dispatcher + ledger ARE the engine and no new firing/event bus should be introduced; the dependency-chain UX must not become a second orchestration engine.
- **Ruling:** Decompose the candidate: (1) dependency engine — isReady/blockedBy/dependsOn + auto-start next ready on completion; (2) assignment to a specific member; (3) completion summary + status; (4) shared/team semantics with claimTask. Items 1-3 are a real, valuable need that maps cleanly onto Shepherd's existing primitives, and three of them are ALREADY backlogged. Item 1's "auto-start next ready dependent" is exactly the shipped done-edge dispatcher (shouldFeed/routeTask) gated by L4's planned join barrier (routeBarrierMet): isReady = barrier met, blockedBy = the unmet members. Item 2 is L4 role-addressed delegation + routePick label filtering with identity from L1. Item 3 is L5 post-run self-summary + the tiles. L3's single-hop {{prev_output}} chaining is the natural handoff payload between linked tasks. Item 4 (team/server/claimTask) is the cline MISMATCH and must be dropped: in Shepherd the assignee is a local session or L1 agent, the board is the existing tile grid + queue files, persistence is the projectKey-keyed queue + cc-templates.json — no shared store, no claim contention, no server. So the underlying need is real but must be reshaped onto Shepherd's dispatcher and does not warrant a parallel system. That is ADAPT/reinforces L4, not ADD (the team layer is structurally impossible, so it isn't as-is) and not DONT_ADD (the dependency-sequencing need is genuine and already half-built). Confidence high: cc-core.lua's dispatcher and the L4 roadmap text corroborate the mapping almost line-for-line.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L4. (a) Dependency model: extend the queue task record (L3 structured {name, description, expected_output}) with an optional dependsOn[] of task ids/labels; persist inside the existing projectKey-keyed queue file (rides the arm flag like queueSetRouted) — NOT a new task-board.json. (b) isReady/blockedBy: L4's pure core.routeBarrierMet over depended-on tasks' done-state (flat AND/OR over done, same stale/remote/error exclusions as sessionFree); gate a dependent task in routeTask so the dispatcher only picks ready tasks — realizing "dispatch only ready tasks." (c) auto-start next ready: already shipped shouldFeed (cc-core.lua:1975) + level-triggered routeTask done-edge — "completing/trashing a card auto-starts the next ready dependent" = on the fresh done/trash transition, re-run routeTask which now sees the barrier satisfied. (d) assignee → specific member: L4 role-addressed delegation — a route label (@role: prefix parsed in queueSplitLines, or inherited from a session group) resolved by routePick filtering members before the longest-free tiebreak; identity from L1 cc-agents.json. (e) chain UX: a panel-JS "link these cards" affordance that writes dependsOn[] + an optional route label, rendered in the webview twin alongside L4's routing topology view (plot). (f) completion summary: L5 post-run self-summary + a task_done ledger event (L4 per-task timing) into the Shift tab. DROP the team/cline-hub server, multi-user claimTask contention, and any shared cross-machine store — the board stays the local tile grid, the assignee a local session/L1 agent. Keep behind the existing queue.routing.enabled + per-project routing:true double opt-in, ledgered by:"router".
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed; the ADAPT (reinforces L4) survives, verified line-for-line against the actual source. WHY NOT ADD (as-is): The feature's defining premise is TEAM-SHARED — assignee, claimTask, and a shared task-board.json store. That is exactly the multi-user/server surface context.md:13 forbids ("no network, no multi-user, no secrets"). The board cannot enter as specified; the sharing/claim-contention core is structurally impossible in a local single-user console. So it is not ADD. WHY NOT DONT_ADD (not pure-redundant, not pure-mismatch): The dependency-DAG kernel (per-task dependsOn[], isReady, blockedBy, auto-start next ready dependent) is a genuine need that is only HALF-built. Shipped routing v1 is project-level done-edge feeding with NO inter-task dependency graph: shouldFeed (cc-core.lua:1975) fires on cur=="done" && prev!="done"; routeTask/routePick/sessionFree (cc-core.lua:2062/2041/2020) pick the longest-free member of a project — there is no dependsOn, no per-task readiness gate. The barrier primitive that would realize isReady/blockedBy is only PLANNED, not shipped: todos.md:460-462 specifies "pure core.routeBarrierMet(members, opts) -> true only when all (or any) members ... are done ... gate a designated 'join' task on it before routePick." So the kernel is neither shipped-redundant nor pure-mismatch — DONT_ADD is wrong. WHY ADAPT/reinforces L4 (mapping confirmed against source): 1) Dependency engine + auto-start next ready dependent = L4 join barrier routeBarrierMet (todos.md:460) gating a task before routePick, on top of the SHIPPED level-triggered done-edge dispatcher (shouldFeed cc-core.lua:1975 + routeTask cc-core.lua:2062). isReady = barrier met; blockedBy = the unmet depended-on tasks (same stale/remote/error exclusions as sessionFree). "Completing/trashing a card auto-starts the next ready dependent" = the existing done/trash transition re-running routeTask, which now sees the barrier satisfied. Sequential declared-order feeding is already specced as L4 process modes (queueRouteMode "sequential", todos.md:456-458). 2) Assignee -> specific member = L4 role-addressed delegation (todos.md:463-467) + routePick label filtering (the @role: prefix parsed in queueSplitLines), identity from L1 cc-agents.json. Not a shared assignee — a LOCAL session / L1 agent. 3) Completion summary + status = L5 post-run self-summary (core.summaryPrompt, todos.md:534) + L4 per-task task_done ledger event (todos.md:473-477) into the Shift tab. 4) Chain handoff payload = L3 {{prev_output}} single-hop context-chaining (todos.md:412). Persistence rides the existing projectKey-keyed queue file with the arm flag (queueSetRouted, cc-core.lua:1997) — NOT a new task-board.json. The board is the already-shipped tile grid + queue files. DROP (the cline mismatch, correctly excluded by the judge): the team/cline-hub server, multi-user claimTask contention, and any shared cross-machine task-board.json store. Those are the in-process/multi-user surfaces Shepherd is NEITHER. Keep behind the shipped queue.routing.enabled + per-project routing:true double opt-in, ledgered by:"router". Confidence high: cc-core.lua's dispatcher and the L4 roadmap text (todos.md:441-498) corroborate the judge's decomposition almost line-for-line. No refutation found.

#### Route a delegated task to a teammate (sync/async) + handoff/submit-and-exit  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The core need — address a task to a NAMED agent/role (not just "the project"), enqueue it, and watch its status — is exactly the gap L4's "Delegation / role-addressed handoff" was queued to close, and it directly embodies the user's stated priority ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory"). Shepherd already ships the engine: Project Routing v1 (routeTask/routePick/sessionFree) is a level-triggered dispatcher feeding an armed project's queue to a free member; pointing it at an agent/role target (identity from L1 cc-agents.json, payload from an L3 rendered task) is an incremental reshape, not a new system. The candidate's dedup guard is ALREADY BUILT — routePendingDone + the in-memory routePending in-flight marker (cc-core.lua 2007-2033) is precisely a "don't double-feed in parallel" guard. continueConversation maps to existing primitives (continue = live nudge into a running session; fresh = spawn-from-agent + seed queue). The async/fire-and-forget run, the runId handle (the queueKey/tile), and handoff_to_agent all ride the existing dispatcher + tile-status watch. So the majority of the feature is in-scope and largely pre-built.
- **Against:** Two sub-features hit the cline mismatch and cannot transfer. runMode 'sync' that "blocks and returns the result" requires an in-process agent loop to block on and a captured return value — Shepherd never blocks; it polls status files on a ~1s loop and has no executor to harvest a result from. submit_and_exit's "report the result to the parent" is the executor's return-value/output object, which L4's own design notes EXPLICITLY say to DROP ("NO in-process output object", "DROP ... return-value event bus / in-memory flow state — Shepherd has no firing engine"). Read literally, team_run_task is an SDK/team-server call into an agent runtime; that runtime layer is the part Shepherd structurally cannot host, so a naive copy would be DONT_ADD.
- **Ruling:** The candidate decomposes into five parts, and the split lands almost perfectly on the cline mismatch line. ROUTE-A-TASK-TO-A-NAMED-TEAMMATE (team_run_task): This IS Shepherd's shipped Project Routing v1 (routeTask/routePick/sessionFree, cc-core.lua ~2041-2070), just keyed on agent/role instead of project. todos.md L4 already spells this exact reshape out: "Delegation / role-addressed handoff: extend the dispatcher from done-only/by-project toward AGENT/ROLE-addressed targets... addressable identity from L1 (cc-agents.json role/name); handoff payload from L3 (rendered {{var}} task). 'Delegate' = render an L3 task + enqueue onto the named L1 agent's queue, delivered by the router (ledger by:'router'/delegation)." The candidate's "addressed delegation = routing a task into a specific session's queue and watching its status" is a one-line restatement of Shepherd's level-triggered dispatcher + tile-status watch. This is the clean ADAPT core. runMode ASYNC vs SYNC: The async half maps directly — enqueue, return a handle (the queueKey/tile is the "runId"), watch status on the 1s loop. The sync half ("blocks and returns the result") is the cline MISMATCH: blocking on a session and harvesting its return value requires the in-process executor Shepherd structurally lacks. Shepherd never blocks; it polls ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json. So only the async/fire-and-forget semantics transfer. continueConversation (continue prior context vs fresh): No new mechanism needed — "continue" = the live nudge into a running session (handleAction "nudge"); "fresh" = spawn-from-agent (L1) seeding a new session's queue. Both already exist as primitives; this is a flag on the Delegate action, not a feature. DEDUP GUARD (pendingSyncRuns prevents duplicate parallel calls): ALREADY EXISTS. routePendingDone + the in-memory routePending marker consulted by routePick/sessionFree (cc-core.lua 2007-2033) is exactly an in-flight guard that stops the next tick double-feeding a session whose status file hasn't flipped yet. Nothing to add. handoff_to_agent / submit_and_exit (report results to parent): submit_and_exit's "report the result" is the executor's return-value/output object — and L4 EXPLICITLY says to DROP it ("NO in-process output object"; "DROP ... return-value event bus / in-memory flow state — Shepherd has no firing engine"). The honest, in-scope stand-in is the done→ transition plus the L4 per-task timing "task_done" ledger event {durationS, approvals, routerHandoffs...} and the parent/delegation lineage edge — completion signal without a captured result. handoff_to_agent (move the whole task to another agent) is just a Delegate with the current task as payload — same dispatcher path. NET: the underlying NEED (address a task to a named agent, fire-and-forget queue + watch, continue-vs-fresh, dedup) is real and is precisely L4 (plus the already-shipped routing v1 and the existing in-flight guard). The executor-bound surfaces (sync blocking-with-return, submit_and_exit result object) are out of scope by the mismatch and are already pre-excluded in the L4 design notes. That is a textbook ADAPT-reinforces-L4, not a new system and not DONT_ADD (the in-scope majority clearly transfers). It also leans on L1 (agent identity) and L3 (rendered task payload), but its center of gravity is the routing/delegation dispatcher = L4. Weighs favorably with the user's stated priority: "pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory" — addressed handoff to a saved agent is the literal embodiment of that ask. Effort M, matching L4's own estimate, since routing v1 + the in-flight guard + nudge + spawn-seed already exist; the new work is the label→agent/role resolver, the Delegate action wiring across L1/L3, and the task_done/lineage ledger — not a from-scratch engine.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L4's role-addressed delegation: add a Delegate action that (1) resolves a target by agent/role name via L1's cc-agents.json identity, (2) renders the task payload via L3 ({{var}}/{{prev_output}}), and (3) enqueues onto the named agent's projectKey-keyed queue (reuse the pending-message queue so it delivers even with zero live members yet), delivered by the existing routeTask/routePick dispatcher with by:"router"/delegation ledger events. Map runMode: 'async' = today's fire-and-forget enqueue, the queueKey/tile is the "runId" you watch on the tile grid; DROP 'sync' blocking-with-return (no executor). continueConversation: 'continue' = handleAction "nudge" into the running session; 'fresh' = spawn-from-L1-agent then seed its queue. The dedup guard already exists (routePendingDone/routePending in-flight marker). For submit_and_exit, substitute the in-scope completion signal: the done→ transition + the L4 per-task "task_done" ledger event {durationS, approvals, routerHandoffs} + a parent/delegation lineage edge in the routing topology view — completion + handoff visibility without a captured result object. All behind the existing queue.routing.enabled + routing:true double opt-in, dry-run-able, ledgered, off by default.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not; verified against the source it holds. The judge's L4 mapping is verbatim-accurate to todos.md (lines 441-497): the "Delegation / role-addressed handoff" bullet literally defines "Delegate = render an L3 task + enqueue onto the named L1 agent's queue, delivered by the router (ledger by:'router'/delegation)" (463-467), and explicitly says "NO in-process output object" (477) and "DROP all... return-value event bus / in-memory flow state — Shepherd has no firing engine" (447). The feature decomposes exactly as the judge claims. Refutation angle B (executor-bound → DONT_ADD) is the strongest skeptical line, and the carve is correct, not a fudge: I grepped cc-core.lua + claude-dashboard.lua for any runId/blockUntil/waitFor-result/harvest/sync-run primitive and found NOTHING. Shepherd's delivery model is FX.pasteIntoWindow (clipboard/keystrokes into a GUI window) + a ~1s loop polling ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json (context.md confirms: no network, no executor, reads status off the filesystem). So the cline sync half ("blocks and returns the result") and submit_and_exit's result object genuinely require the in-process executor Shepherd structurally lacks — and both are already pre-excluded by L4's own design notes. Only the async/fire-and-forget surfaces transfer, which is exactly the verdict. The in-scope majority does transfer onto SHIPPED primitives, which I verified exist: Project Routing v1 dispatcher routeTask/routePick/sessionFree (cc-core.lua 2041-2070), the in-flight dedup guard routePendingDone + the in-memory routePending marker consulted by sessionFree (2007-2033) — so the "dedup guard" is already built, nothing to add; nudge (dispatchSerialized "nudge" in claude-dashboard.lua) = continueConversation 'continue'; spawn-from-agent (L1) = 'fresh'. L1 (cc-agents.json role/name identity, todos.md 247) and L3 ({{var}}/{{prev_output}} rendered task, 387/412) both exist as the judge says. Refutation angle A (redundant → DONT_ADD) fails: v1 is by-project; the feature's distinguishing axis is AGENT/ROLE-addressed targeting, which L4 explicitly says is NOT yet built ("extend the dispatcher from done-only/by-project toward AGENT/ROLE-addressed targets"). Angle C (net-new → ADD) fails: the dispatcher + guard + nudge + spawn-seed already exist, so the new work (label→agent resolver, Delegate wiring across L1/L3, task_done/lineage ledger) is an extension of an existing backlog item with a shipped v1 = ADAPT-reinforces, not from-scratch ADD. The underlying need (address a task to a named saved agent, fire-and-forget queue + watch, continue-vs-fresh, dedup) is the literal embodiment of the user's stated priority ("pre-configured agents we hand work off to, saved into a directory"). Center of gravity = L4. ADAPT (high) reinforces L4 stands.

#### Async run queue: concurrency cap, priority, retry+backoff, leases, heartbeats  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** Two pieces of this map directly onto the routing dispatcher Shepherd already shipped (4c-E / L4). (1) A global concurrency cap: maxConcurrentRuns is exactly the bound a watch/spawn console wants — "don't have more than N active sessions feeding work at once" — and routePick/routeTask already gate feeds on sessionFree per project; a fleet-wide active-session cap is a thin widening. (2) Per-task priority: routePick currently picks only the oldest-free MEMBER and has no notion of which queued TASK outranks another; adding a priority field that reorders selectNextDispatchableQueuedRun is a clean, useful upgrade for an operator who queues many tasks. And the "is it stuck" heartbeat has a real, already-loved analog: the stuck-session watchdog (isHung/trackProgress) — so the operator-facing value (cap, priority, stuck-detection) is genuinely Shepherd-shaped.
- **Against:** Most of the package presumes owning the run lifecycle — which is the cline mismatch. retryCount/maxRetries + exponential backoff (nextAttemptAt) require knowing a task FAILED as a unit and being able to re-dispatch that same work; Shepherd executes nothing and gets no terminal success/failure signal — its only "retry" primitives (per-folder auto-respawn budget, bounded Auto-Continue) re-poke an EXISTING session, they do not re-enqueue a task. leaseOwner is a multi-worker ownership lock to stop two executors double-running a job; Shepherd is single-user, single dispatcher, in-process — there is nothing to lease. heartbeatAt/lastProgressAt/currentActivity duplicate the shipped watchdog (isHung) rather than add anything. team_await_runs is a programmatic SDK barrier for a calling agent; Shepherd is a human console with a 1s refresh loop, not an awaitable API. list/cancel are already the tile grid + stop/drain. So ~70% of this is executor residue that is DONT_ADD or redundant.
- **Ruling:** Shepherd already has the dispatcher (level-triggered, done-only, feeds an armed project's queue to a sessionFree member), the queue with manual reordering (queueMove/queueRemoveAt), and the stuck-session watchdog (isHung/trackProgress — the heartbeat analog). The transferable kernel of this candidate is therefore narrow: (a) a fleet-wide concurrency cap on how many sessions the dispatcher keeps active, and (b) per-task priority so the queue dispatches the most important task first rather than FIFO/oldest-free. Both extend, rather than parallel, what exists — which is why this is ADAPT into L4 (declarative routing & orchestration; L4 already lists join barriers / process modes), with a tie to routing v1. The remaining machinery — retry/backoff, leases, heartbeats, await — either needs the in-process executor Shepherd structurally lacks (retry/backoff/lease/await) or duplicates the watchdog (heartbeats). Those parts are DONT_ADD. Pulling priority+cap into routing is worth it; importing the run-lifecycle scaffolding is not.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into the L4 routing follow-ups (currently deferred pending a UX call). Concretely: (1) add an optional per-task `priority` to queue items (cc-templates/queue rows already support reorder via queueMove) and have routeTask/routePick consult it when selecting which queued task to feed first — the dispatcher loop at claude-dashboard.lua ~5623 (routeGroups) is the wiring point. (2) Add a fleet-wide `queue.routing.maxConcurrent` setting (default e.g. 2) that the post-loop dispatcher honors by counting active (working/feeding) sessions before issuing a feed; pure check lives next to routeTask in cc-core.lua. Reuse the shipped watchdog (isHung) for "is it stuck" — do NOT add heartbeatAt fields. Explicitly DROP: retryCount/maxRetries/nextAttemptAt (Shepherd's per-folder respawn + Auto-Continue budgets already cover the only retry it can do), leaseOwner (no second executor exists), and team_await_runs (no awaitable API; the human watches tiles). list/cancel already exist as the tile grid + stop/drain.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute this down to DONT_ADD and could not. The judge's dissection is correct and verified against the code. The feature is mostly executor-bound — retry/backoff (nextAttemptAt), leaseOwner, and team_await_runs all presume owning the run lifecycle, which Shepherd structurally lacks (it runs no agent loop, executes no tools) — and the judge already DROPS exactly those parts. Heartbeats/lastProgress/currentActivity duplicate the shipped stuck-session watchdog (cc-core.lua:2367-2415: isHung/trackProgress/applyProgress, wired at claude-dashboard.lua:5549-5568), and list/cancel already exist as the tile grid + stop/drain. So the executor and redundancy objections only kill the parts the judge already kills. What survives is a narrow, genuinely-transferable, local-console kernel: (a) a fleet-wide concurrency cap and (b) per-task priority. I verified both are NOT already present and NOT redundant: grep for maxConcurrent/concurrencyCap/maxActive across cc-core.lua, claude-dashboard.lua, cc-config.example.json, README found nothing — the dispatcher only does "ONE feed per armed project per tick" (claude-dashboard.lua:5623, 5631), which is a per-project rate limit, not a fleet-wide cap on simultaneously-active sessions. And the queue is strictly FIFO (queuePop pops the head, cc-core.lua:1866) with only MANUAL reorder (queueMove/queueRemoveAt, 1890/1906) — there is no automatic per-task priority. The queue today is a flat list of plain strings ({tasks={"t1","t2"}}, 1843-1850), so adding priority means promoting items to objects, but that is a feasible extension, not a new system. Both surviving pieces EXTEND rather than parallel L4. L4 (todos.md:441-480, "Declarative routing & orchestration") is already explicitly about attaching per-task metadata to queue lines (route labels like @review:, parsed in queueSplitLines) and about dispatcher behavior (routeTask/routePick at cc-core.lua:2041-2070); a per-task priority is a direct sibling of the route label, and a fleet-wide maxConcurrent is a direct extension of the per-project dispatcher loop (routeGroups at claude-dashboard.lua:5631). Neither requires running the agent, editing/snapshotting the workspace, or any server — they are pure local scheduling knobs for a single-user console that watches/steers/spawns Claude Code sessions.
