The judge's mapping (priority+cap into L4 routing, reuse isHung for "is it stuck", DROP retry/lease/await) is exactly right. ADAPT/reinforces-L4 holds.

#### Inter-agent mailbox: direct messages + broadcast (steering nudge into next turn)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The candidate's load-bearing transferable mechanism is "inject a steer-message into a session's next turn," and the candidate itself reframes that as "a 'send a nudge to session X' / 'broadcast a nudge to a group' button" — which is precisely Shepherd's reason to exist. Shepherd already delivers this exact primitive: handleAction "nudge" pastes text into one session's window (cc-core.lua:138-148), and the fleet-wide bulk path broadcasts a nudge to every non-approval session via selectActionable + BULK_RULES.nudge with staggered dispatch (cc-core.lua:395-431). So the steer/broadcast half is a direct fit — and the persistence/addressing half maps cleanly onto queued work: L4 explicitly plans role-addressed delegation/handoff ("Ask coworker = the existing cross-session nudge, fire-and-forget") and the "Pending-message queue (queue-before-ready)" keyed by projectKey that a session drains on first ready (todos.md:463-486). A direct-message-to-a-named-agent that lands in that agent's queue, plus a broadcast-to-a-group that fans the same staggered nudge, is a natural enrichment of L4 onto Shepherd's existing nudge + per-session queue + group primitives. The subject/threaded/unread metadata could ride the queue line as L4's handoff payload already does, with the "[MAILBOX] read your mailbox" framing collapsing into the nudge text itself.
- **Against:** The full feature as specified is a persistent inter-agent coordination BUS, and its value depends on the part Shepherd structurally cannot host. The candidate's own note concedes "the full inter-agent coordination bus needs the loop." Delivery works by injecting a pendingSteerMessage that tells the recipient to autonomously call team_read_mailbox, and the recipient consumes it via consumePendingUserMessage inside its agent loop — that is in-process tool execution driving an autonomous turn, which Shepherd has none of (no executor, no tool calls, no event loop). Shepherd can paste "[MAILBOX] read your mailbox" into a window, but it cannot make a session execute team_read_mailbox, mark messages read, thread a reply, or run the back-and-forth that makes a mailbox a bus rather than a one-shot poke. The mailbox.json read/unread state machine and subject-threaded DTOs are bookkeeping for an autonomous reader Shepherd doesn't have. Building the literal team_send_message/team_broadcast/team_read_mailbox tools is DONT_ADD territory — they are agent-side tools, not operator-console affordances — and the operator-visible half is already shipped (nudge + bulk nudge) or queued (L4 handoff + pending-message queue), so building it standalone would be a parallel system to L4.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature: (1) the operator UX primitive "steer a message into a session's next turn, to one or to a group" is ALREADY SHIPPED in Shepherd as the per-session nudge and the staggered fleet/group bulk nudge — re-adding it is redundant. (2) The genuinely new ask — addressing a message to a NAMED agent/role and having it persist until that session is ready to receive it — is real and ALREADY QUEUED as L4 (role-addressed delegation/handoff + the projectKey-keyed pending-message queue). (3) The remaining slice — autonomous mailbox draining via team_read_mailbox, read/unread state, threaded replies, the coordination bus — depends on an in-process agent loop and tool execution Shepherd cannot host (the cline mismatch), so it's DONT_ADD. Net: the need is real and reshapes onto existing primitives, so ADAPT reinforcing L4. Do NOT build a standalone mailbox.json bus or the team_* tools; instead extend L4's handoff so a "message to agent <name>" renders into that agent's queue (delivered by the router as a nudge, fire-and-forget) and a "broadcast to group" fans the existing staggered group nudge. The nudge as a coordination signal stays human-mediated and one-directional, matching Shepherd's WATCH/STEER posture.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L4 (todos.md:463-486), not a new subsystem. "Send to one agent" = L4 role-addressed handoff: resolve the L1 agent name -> its projectKey queue, enqueue the message body as a queue item (queuePush, cc-core.lua:1858), delivered by the existing router/nudge path (handleAction "nudge", cc-core.lua:138) when that session reaches done/ready; ledger by:"router". "Send to a session not yet live" = L4's pending-message queue (projectKey-keyed, drained on first ready). "Broadcast to a group" = the existing fleet/group bulk nudge: selectActionable + BULK_RULES.nudge + the staggered headless/focus dispatcher (cc-core.lua:395-431) scoped to a session group (applyGroups). Subject/threading = optional prefix on the queue line (sibling to @route labels parsed in queueSplitLines ~1921), human-visible before send. DROP: mailbox.json read/unread state machine, team_read_mailbox autonomous-drain, threaded replies, pendingSteerMessage/consumePendingUserMessage (no agent loop to consume them), and the team_* tools (agent-side, not console-side).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted from both directions; both fail, so the ADAPT (reinforces L4) ruling holds. REFUTE-toward-DONT_ADD fails: the feature's genuinely-new kernel — addressing a message to a NAMED agent/role and persisting it until that session is ready to receive — is NOT redundant with anything shipped. Verified it is exactly L4's still-QUEUED work: "Delegation / role-addressed handoff... toward AGENT/ROLE-addressed targets" (todos.md:463-464) and the "Pending-message queue (queue-before-ready)... projectKey-keyed queue hold ordered tasks even with ZERO live members" (todos.md:482). So a real, unbuilt need exists; it can't be dismissed as redundant. REFUTE-toward-ADD fails: the feature as proposed cannot be built into Shepherd as-is. Its delivery mechanism is "inject a 'pendingSteerMessage' nudge into the recipient's next turn" plus team_read_mailbox autonomous drain and read/unread state — these are AGENT-SIDE tools consumed by an in-process agent loop. Shepherd runs NO loop: context.md confirms it "reads session status off the local filesystem" (~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json written by hooks) and steers via keystrokes/decision-files; Claude Code itself drives the session. Nothing in Shepherd can consume a pendingSteerMessage or call team_read_mailbox. Grep confirms ZERO mailbox/team_*/pendingSteer/unread primitives anywhere in cc-core.lua or claude-dashboard.lua. So the coordination-bus slice is the classic cline executor mismatch -> that slice is DONT_ADD. What survives is precisely the human-mediated operator slice the judge mapped, all verified present: handleAction \"nudge\" (cc-core.lua:138, clipboard paste+submit) as the per-session steer; BULK_RULES.nudge + selectActionable (cc-core.lua:395-431) + the staggered bulk dispatcher (claude-dashboard.lua:1206-1260, \"Nudge all\" button :3696) as the broadcast-to-group; queuePush (cc-core.lua:1858) + the router (routePick/routeTask, by:\"router\" cc-core.lua:2041-2069) as the deliver-when-ready path; queueSplitLines (cc-core.lua:1921) for subject/prefix on a queue line. The split is correct: operator UX primitive already shipped (redundant), named-agent/persist-until-ready is real and reshapes onto L4 (ADAPT), autonomous drain/team_* tools need a loop Shepherd lacks (DROP). Build it as an L4 extension (resolve agent name -> projectKey queue -> router-delivered nudge; broadcast = staggered group nudge), NOT a standalone mailbox.json bus. Net: ADAPT (high), reinforces L4. Ruling holds.

#### Parent-child session lineage / delegation graph  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The SESSION-GRAPH bookkeeping is the cleanly-transferable half and it lands squarely in Shepherd's wheelhouse: Shepherd already tracks per-session identity, already has session GROUPS with collapse, already has a "lineage" read (cc-core.lua:1061 lineageByProject / 1085 projectLineage, today keyed on respawn/clear/continue churn per project), and already has a routing dispatcher whose hand-offs are ledgered with by:"router". Stamping a spawn/delegate ledger event with parent_session/spawnedBy and rendering parent->child edges as nested tiles costs no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing — it is pure status/relationship bookkeeping over data Shepherd already owns. It directly fulfills the L4 routing topology view and gives the operator a real watch-surface for "which session spawned which," improving a watch/steer/spawn console. The user's stated interest in delegation/hand-off (agents we hand work off to) makes the parent->child edge genuinely useful, not cosmetic.
- **Against:** As framed in cline (team-child-session-manager.ts: upsertSubagentSession, makeSubSessionId, parentAgentId, "derived status" of sub-sessions reporting to a parent) this is fundamentally a SERVER/multi-user + in-process-executor construct: cline's sub-agents are agent loops it spawns and runs in-process, the parent/child ids are minted by cline's own executor, and the team/hub layer is a multi-user serving surface. Shepherd mints no agent ids, runs no loop, and has no parent process that "owns" children — its sessions are independently human-driven Claude Code GUIs. There is no parentAgentId to read unless Claude Code itself exposes one on the status file, so the literal data model (sub-session derived status, supervisor rollup, cascade) cannot be lifted. Cascade-delete of children is explicitly dangerous in a human-in-the-loop console.
- **Ruling:** Near-exact, already-queued item. todos.md L4 (lines 487-492) carries it verbatim as the OpenHands-corroborated "Sub-conversations = parent/delegation lineage" enrichment: stamp the route/spawn ledger event with parent_session/spawnedBy, show parent->child edges in the routing topology view, reuse session GROUPS + collapse for "collapse children under parent." The candidate's own evidence concedes the executor half is n/a and only the SESSION-GRAPH bookkeeping transfers "if parent/child agent ids are exposed" — exactly the reshaping L4 specifies (Shepherd derives the edge from its OWN spawn/route event, not from a cline-style parentAgentId on a sub-agent it runs). #### Live team progress projection + lifecycle event stream + parallel per-agent cards  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L4
- **For:** The WATCH/AGGREGATE half is a near-perfect fit and the candidate itself concedes it ("a per-team dashboard aggregated from member session statuses transfers"). team_status (member statuses, task counts, unread, active/queued runs, outcome counts) is exactly the read Shepherd already produces: a "team" = a session GROUP; member statuses = the tiles' idle/working/approval/done/error; active/queued runs = the per-session queue + Project Routing v1 dispatcher; outcome counts = the audit ledger; fleet rollup = fleetStats/fleetStandup. The TeamProgressSummary projection (completionPct, readyTaskIds, blockedTaskIds, latestRunId) is pure deterministic aggregation off already-local data — precisely what cc-core.lua exists to do (sibling to fleetStats ~943 / lineageByProject ~1061), and it sharpens L4's routing-topology view (queue-depth/starvation nodes/edges) plus L5's plan/TODO-on-tile read with a real per-group "how far along is this team" number. The team_progress lifecycle event maps onto the existing append-only JSONL ledger + the ~1s refresh that pushes state to the webview. A per-group progress strip is a genuine gap: today GROUPS organize tiles but don't summarize collective completion/blocked, so this adds operator value without any new plumbing.
- **Against:** The headline framing ("N specialists streaming in parallel per-agent cards + a synthesized brief") is the cline-mismatch trap. The synthesizer is an in-process agent that reads member outputs and emits a combined brief — that is LLM/tool execution Shepherd structurally cannot host (the candidate admits "the synthesizer needs the loop"). The "streaming" cards depend on the executor pushing live token streams per agent; Shepherd only reads discrete status JSON off disk on a ~1s tick, so it can never render token-level streaming — at best it shows tiles that refresh. readyTaskIds/blockedTaskIds/missingRequiredSections also presume an executor-owned structured task graph with declared required output sections; Shepherd has no task object model, no run object, and no notion of an output's "sections" — todos.md explicitly says "NO in-process output object" and "NO in-process manager/manager_llm." Taken as-is this is a server/executor feature (cline-hub teams), and Shepherd has already DROPPED "multi-tenant access-groups/teams/keys" and "A2A agent cards." So the verbatim feature is DONT_ADD; only a reshaped slice survives.
- **Ruling:** Split the bundle. The aggregation/projection/lifecycle-event side is real, fits the local single-user model, and reinforces queued work — so ADAPT, not DONT_ADD. The in-process synthesizer and token-stream "streaming per-agent cards" cannot transfer and are dropped. Concretely: treat a session GROUP as the "team"; add a pure core.teamProgress(group, tiles, ledger, routingState) that returns completionPct (done members / total), blockedTaskIds (queued tasks gated by routeBarrierMet or awaiting approval), readyTaskIds (queued+dispatchable per routePick), and latestRunId (most recent ledger run for the group) — a sibling to fleetStats/fleetStandup, unit-tested, zero hs.*. Surface it as a per-group progress strip in the tile grid and as a node-summary in L4's routing-topology view; the team_progress "stream" is just a new ledger event type the existing 1s loop already renders. DROP: the synthesizer/combined brief, missingRequiredSections (no output-section model), and any token-level streaming — "cards" are Shepherd's existing tiles, refreshed off status files, never live streams. This is the WATCH/AGGREGATE side the candidate says transfers, mapped onto primitives Shepherd already has, with no executor/server/output-object.
- **How (Shepherd):** L4 (primary): extend the routing-topology aggregation with a per-group/per-project progress projection. New pure core.teamProgress(group, tiles, routingState, ledgerEvents) -> {completionPct, readyTaskIds, blockedTaskIds, latestRunId, memberStatusCounts} as a sibling to core.fleetStats (cc-core.lua ~943) and core.lineageByProject (~1061); reuse core.routeBarrierMet (and_/or_ done-gate) for blockedTaskIds and routePick eligibility for readyTaskIds. "Team" = an existing session GROUP. Render: a per-group progress strip on the tile grid and a node annotation in the L4 topology view (gated to appear only while routing/ledger on); rolled into fleetStandup / the Shift tab. team_progress lifecycle = a new ledger event type emitted on the existing ~1s refresh, no new transport. Assists L5 (plan/TODO-on-tile gives per-member completion signal). DROP from cline: the in-process synthesizer/combined brief, missingRequiredSections (no output-section/run object model), and token-stream "streaming cards" (cards stay status-file-backed tiles).
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute the ADAPT-reinforces-L4 ruling and could not. The judge correctly split the bundle and the kept half genuinely transfers. WHY THE SYNTHESIZER/STREAMING HALF IS RIGHTLY DROPPED (refutation of "this is a full ADD"): The feature itself admits "the synthesizer needs the loop." Shepherd runs no agent loop and edits/produces nothing (context.md:12-13,22-24: pure cc-core + an fx effects table + a ~1s refresh; "it reads session status off the local filesystem"). So the in-process synthesizer/combined brief and any TOKEN-STREAM "streaming per-agent cards" cannot transfer — Shepherd's "cards" are status-file-backed TILES refreshed off ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json on the 1s tick, never live token streams. missingRequiredSections also dies: it presupposes an output-section/run-OBJECT model, and there is none (the ledger is flat {emoji,verb} event types — cc-core.lua:642-667 NARRATE; no run object anywhere). The judge dropped exactly these three. WHY IT IS NOT PURE DONT_ADD (refutation of downgrading further): The WATCH/AGGREGATE half maps cleanly onto primitives Shepherd already has, single-user and local. "Team" = an existing session GROUP (applyGroups/groupNames/setGroup, cc-core.lua:341-379). A pure core.teamProgress(group, tiles, routingState, ledger) -> {completionPct, readyTaskIds, blockedTaskIds, latestRunId, memberStatusCounts} is a legitimate sibling to the SHIPPED, unit-tested aggregators core.fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954, tested in tests/core.test.lua) and core.lineageByProject (1061). blockedTaskIds reuses core.routeBarrierMet — already QUEUED in L4 (todos.md:460, the and_/or_ done-gate); readyTaskIds reuses routePick/sessionFree eligibility (SHIPPED, cc-core.lua:2041/2020); latestRunId is a most-recent ledger read. The team_progress "lifecycle stream" is just a new ledger event type the existing 1s loop already renders (a one-line add to NARRATE), no new transport/server. WHY IT'S ADAPT-REINFORCES-L4 specifically: every kept piece lands on L4 (Declarative routing & orchestration), which already queues the routing-topology view with per-node annotations (todos.md:468), routeBarrierMet barriers (460), and per-task task_done counters rolled into fleetStandup (473). The projection becomes a per-group progress strip on the tile grid + a node-summary in the L4 topology view, gated to appear only while routing/ledger are on. It reinforces queued work rather than spawning a parallel system, so ADAPT (not a fresh ADD) is correct, and it does improve the local single-user console (so not DONT_ADD). The judge's file/line anchors, reuse mapping (routeBarrierMet/routePick), the "team = group" reframe, and the three DROPs are all accurate. Ruling holds at ADAPT.

### Reinforces L2 — policy bundles  (5)

#### Enterprise/remote org-pushed Rules/Skills/Workflows/policy (always-enabled, locked)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** The transferable kernel under this feature is "named rules/skills/workflows/policy + model-provider settings, attachable to sessions, with toggles and a clear precedence ladder so a fleet-wide default can be overridden in a controlled way." That is genuinely useful to a fleet console and maps almost 1:1 onto what Shepherd already has queued: L1 saves agent profiles carrying skills[]/mcpServers[]/policyBundle, and L2 turns the flat policies.patterns into named bundles + attachments + a resolver whose precedence is "session override > attached bundle > fleet default." The candidate's precedence model (org-managed wins highest) is exactly a precedence tier on that same resolver. Its "remote skill resolved by frontmatter name with no on-disk file" corroborates L1's frontmatter-name skills enumerator (Shepherd's live on disk in ~/.claude/skills, which is the right local shape). The locked/alwaysEnabled tier is precisely L2's already-listed "enforced/locked fields (force permMode / a gate list the session can't loosen / force provider)" sub-bullet — strong third-party corroboration that a force-on/force-off tier belongs on the bundle resolver. And feature-locks like "disable YOLO" / "allowed MCP servers" map to bundle fields (lockedPermMode, autoDeny set, an MCP allow subset) layered over L1's MCP registry. So the operator pattern transfers; it reshapes onto L1+L2 primitives without new infrastructure.
- **Against:** The defining value of THIS feature is the part Shepherd structurally cannot and should not host: a central org server that PUSHES config over the network to a FLEET OF CLIENT MACHINES, enforced because the admin is a DIFFERENT party from the operator. Shepherd's hard scope (context.md line 13) is "no network, no multi-user, no secrets." The remote-config transport (fetch from a server, transformRemoteConfigToStateShape, org tenancy) is a direct scope violation — there is no server to fetch from and no second party. Worse, the locking semantics are inherently meaningless on a single-user console: a lock exists so admin A can stop user B from loosening a setting. With one operator who OWNS the config file, "locked, managed by your organization" is theater — they can edit the JSON. L2 already saw this and explicitly marked enforced/locked fields "the weakest piece; defer unless needed — a single-user console rarely needs to defend a preset against itself." So the one mechanic that distinguishes this candidate from plain L1/L2 (the lock + remote push) is exactly the mechanic Shepherd has reasoned itself out of needing. Building the remote/lock layer as specced would add network+enforcement complexity for a non-existent threat model.
- **Ruling:** The feature splits into three separable pieces against Shepherd's scope boundary. (1) The remote-fetch/org-push TRANSPORT and multi-tenant model = hard DON'T-ADD: no network server, no multi-user, no secrets (context.md:13); there is no admin-vs-operator party split on a single-user Mac. (2) The lock semantics (alwaysEnabled force-on / locked force-off / "managed by your organization") = redundant-with-defer: this is verbatim L2's existing "enforced/locked fields" sub-bullet, which the project already chose to DEFER because "a single-user console rarely needs to defend a preset against itself." Third-party corroboration of an already-deferred item does not make it newly worth building. (3) The real kernel — rules/skills/workflows/policy/provider as named, attachable, toggleable config with a precedence ladder — is ALREADY fully captured by L1 (profiles carry skills[]/mcpServers[]/policyBundle; frontmatter-name skills enumerator) + L2 (named bundles + attachments + session>bundle>default resolver) + L3 (local definition-source directory). The only arguably-new contribution is "fleet-wide default as a precedence tier with an optional force tier," which is just L2's resolver plus its already-deferred lock field. Net: this is corroboration, not a new system. Verdict ADAPT, reinforcing L2 (precedence + the deferred lock tier) and corroborating L1 (named-config-as-attachable, frontmatter skills). Strip the network transport and the multi-user enforcement model entirely; if anything is upgraded, it is promoting L2's locked-fields from "defer" to "ship a single 'fleet-default bundle' tier with optional locked rows that simply can't be toggled in the panel UI" — a UX affordance, not org enforcement.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L2 (and corroborate L1), build NO remote/network/lock-enforcement infrastructure. Concretely: (a) L2 already plans policies.bundles + policies.attachments + a resolver mirroring resolveGateTools with precedence "session override > attached bundle > fleet default" — keep that, and treat the candidate as the prompt to add ONE explicit "fleet-default bundle" entry at the bottom of that ladder (the local analog of the org default). (b) Promote L2's deferred "enforced/locked fields" sub-bullet to a thin UX-only affordance: a bundle may mark fields (permMode, gateTools, provider) as locked = the panel renders them read-only with a lock glyph + "set by fleet default" tooltip (mirrors FeatureSettingsSection.tsx isRemoteLocked codicon), but it is honestly just a guardrail-against-fat-finger, NOT enforcement — the operator can still edit cc-config.json. Document it as such. (c) "disable YOLO / allowed MCP servers" map to existing primitives: a bundle field that force-clears autopilot/headless toggles (lock the autopilot control) and an MCP allow-subset over L1's cc-mcp.json registry — no new schema axis. (d) The "remote skill, no on-disk file, resolved by frontmatter name" stays L1's on-disk ~/.claude/skills frontmatter enumerator (FX.listSkills) — the local-disk equivalent. DROP entirely: the remote-config fetch client, transformRemoteConfigToStateShape, GlobalInstructionsFileSchema network delivery, org/tenant identity, ProviderSettings push, and any genuine cross-machine enforcement (all = no-network/no-multi-user/no-secrets scope).
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT toward DONT_ADD and failed — the ruling holds. Verified against the codebase: (1) The feature's NOVEL value is org enforcement over a remote transport + multi-tenant identity + inline remote delivery — all three violate hard scope (context.md:13 "no network, no multi-user, no secrets"; no admin-vs-operator party split exists on a single-user Mac). Those pieces are correctly DROPPED, not adapted. (2) The lock semantics (alwaysEnabled force-on / locked force-off / "managed by your organization") are VERBATIM L2's existing "enforced/locked fields" sub-bullet (todos.md:360-361), which the project already chose to DEFER ("a single-user console rarely needs to defend a preset against itself"). Third-party corroboration of an already-deferred item is the textbook ADAPT-reinforces case. (3) The real in-scope kernel is already captured: the precedence resolver the feature implies a "fleet-default" tier for ALREADY EXISTS as cc-core.lua:2537 resolveGateTools(override, providerDefault, fleetDefault) — i.e. L2's planned "session override > attached bundle > fleet default" ladder. The only arguably-new contribution is one explicit fleet-default bundle row at the bottom of that existing ladder — which is exactly what L2 plans. (4) "Remote skill, no on-disk file, resolved by frontmatter name" maps to L1's on-disk ~/.claude/skills frontmatter enumerator (todos.md:266-267, 307-310; FX.listSkills) — the local analog. A full DONT_ADD would require the LOCAL fleet-default tier to be redundant-with-something-SHIPPED; it is not shipped, it is queued in L2 — and the project's stated protocol for a queued match is "verdict ADAPT and set reinforces to the Lx." My strongest refutation (it "secretly needs the server/multi-user surface") is true only of the transport, which the judge already strips; the residual local affordance is legitimately in-scope. So the refutation does not overturn the ruling. The judge's mapping is correct: reinforce L2 (add one fleet-default tier at the bottom of the resolver; promote the deferred locked-fields to a UX-only read-only affordance with a lock glyph + "set by fleet default" tooltip — an honest fat-finger guardrail, NOT enforcement, since the operator can still edit cc-config.json), corroborate L1 (frontmatter-name skills), and DROP the remote-config fetch client, org/tenant identity, inline remote delivery, ProviderSettings push, and all cross-machine enforcement. ADAPT (high), reinforces L2.

#### Max-requests check-in cap (count-box safety rail for Autopilot)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** A count-based "pause after N auto-approved actions" rail is genuinely useful and fits Shepherd's reason to exist (human-in-the-loop steering), and it costs almost nothing because the plumbing already exists. Shepherd's Autopilot (cc-approve.sh:169-179) is purely time-boxed: cc-autopilot/<key> holds an expiry epoch and the hot path auto-allows while cc_now < EXP. A clock is a blunt instrument — 15 quiet minutes might be 2 actions or 200. A count cap is the orthogonal, often-better bound: "trust this session for the next N approvals, then make me look again." The data to enforce it is already captured — every autopilot auto-allow writes ledger_decision allow autopilot (cc-approve.sh:175), and cc-core already tallies per-session decisions/tool_requests in sessionRisk (cc-core.lua:1392-1399). So enforcement is: count by:"autopilot" allow-decisions since the window opened; at the ceiling, clear the autopilot file so the next request falls through to the human panel. No agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace edits — it only changes WHEN the existing auto-approve stops. It pairs naturally with the time-box (whichever fires first) and is the whole-session twin of L2's already-queued per-tool toolLimits counter (todos.md:376).
- **Against:** The count-cap is not a new feature so much as a second termination condition on a primitive Shepherd already ships, so building it standalone risks redundancy. Shepherd already has time-boxed Autopilot, a per-session risk score, a gate decision log, and L2 already queues a ledger-counted usage limit (toolLimits) that becomes an autoDeny at a ceiling — a count cap is largely that mechanism with whole-session scope, so it should fold INTO that work rather than spawn a parallel knob. The "favorites quick-toggle set" half is dead weight: it is a removed no-op in cline (favorites:[] legacy) and is fully covered in Shepherd by spawn presets, saved templates, session groups, and tile search — adding it would be pure redundancy. There is also a real semantic gap: cline's max-requests counted the executor's own agent turns; Shepherd cannot see turns, only the gate decisions it brokers, so a "max-requests" cap here can only count auto-approvals (autopilot/autoAllow/approveRepeats), a coarser, soft (next-request) bound — Claude Code still owns true mid-action enforcement. Sold as a faithful port it would over-promise.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED (a cheap, count-based forced-check-in safety rail) is real and fits a watch/steer console, but it must be reshaped onto Shepherd's existing primitives rather than built as-is — hence ADAPT, not ADD. It is NOT DONT_ADD: nothing here needs the in-process agent loop, tool execution, workspace editing/snapshotting, or a server. It only adds a second stop condition to the already-shipped, time-boxed Autopilot, which already lives in a per-session file the approve hot path reads and already ledgers each auto-allow with by:"autopilot". The cleanest home is L2 (Named policy/guardrail bundles): todos.md:376 already specifies a ledger-counted Per-tool usage limit (toolLimits={Bash=5}) that autoDenies at a ceiling — the check-in cap is the whole-session generalization of exactly that counter, so it should ride L2's resolver/bundle field and its ledger-tally pattern, not a parallel system. The favorites half is a removed cline no-op and redundant with presets/templates/groups — drop it. The "max-requests" framing must be honestly narrowed to "max auto-approved actions" since Shepherd sees gate decisions, not executor turns, making it a soft next-request bound. Effort is S: reuse the cc-autopilot file + hot path + the existing sessionRisk ledger tally; add one pure cc-core counting fn, a config/UI count field beside the autopilot minutes, and a clear-autopilot-at-cap check, honoring the cc-core.lua <-> cc-approve.sh keep-in-sync contract.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend Autopilot with a count ceiling, surfaced as an L2 bundle field. Config: add policies.autopilot.maxActions (default 0 = off, time-box-only) beside the existing policies.autopilot.minutes, and mirror it as an L2 bundle field {autopilot:{minutes,maxActions}} so a saved agent/template can carry it. cc-core: one new pure fn autopilotActionsUsed(events, key, windowStartTs) counting by:"autopilot" allow-decisions in the ledger since the current window opened (reuse the sessionRisk tally loop at cc-core.lua:1392-1399); honor the cc-core.lua <-> cc-approve.sh keep-in-sync contract. cc-approve.sh: in the autopilot block (169-179), after the cc_now<EXP time check, if maxActions>0 and used>=maxActions, do NOT auto-allow — fall through to the panel; the existing ledger_decision allow autopilot is the counter (no new write). claude-dashboard.lua setAutopilot (~935) stamps the window-start ts so the count resets each arm; auto-clear cc-autopilot/<key> when used>=maxActions so the badge flips off. UI: a count input beside the existing "window of N min" row (~3221) and an "N/cap" suffix on the autopilot tile badge (~5440/4375). DROP the favorites quick-toggle set (redundant with presets/templates/groups/tile-search). Keep off by default; never default_on.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails on every axis; ADAPT holds and reinforces L2. (1) No in-process executor: capping equals counting by:"autopilot" allow-decisions in the existing JSONL ledger and refusing the auto-allow at a ceiling — pure decision/status logic, exactly what Shepherd already does for the time-box. Verified the hot path at cc-approve.sh:170-179 (EXP time-box check then `ledger_decision allow autopilot` + emit_allow at lines 175-176) and the ledger write {outcome:allow, by:autopilot} (lines 134,175), so the counter is already on disk — no new write, no tool execution, no file editing, no workspace snapshot. The reusable tally loop is at cc-core.lua:1392-1399 (riskScore iterating events by e.type/e.by). (2) No server/multi-user: per-session local file (FX.setAutopilot, claude-dashboard.lua:935) + local ledger. (3) Not redundant/DONT_ADD: the existing time-box is wall-clock only and L2's toolLimit (todos.md:376-379) is per-tool — neither caps whole-session auto-approved action COUNT, so a burst of many cheap auto-allows inside the window is a real gap a count-box closes; genuine increment. (4) Not a pure ADD: cline's literal feature counted EXECUTOR turns (which Shepherd never sees) and shipped a 'favorites' quick-toggle. It must be reshaped — narrowed to 'max auto-approved gate actions' and ridden onto L2's existing ledger-counted toolLimit resolver/badge pattern as a whole-session generalization {autopilot:{minutes,maxActions}}, not a parallel system; and the favorites half is a removed cline no-op redundant with presets/templates/groups/tile-search → DROP. Reshaping required + reinforces L2 = ADAPT, not ADD, not DONT_ADD. Effort S; keep maxActions default 0/off, honor the cc-core.lua<->cc-approve.sh keep-in-sync contract.

#### Plan / Act dual-mode switch with strict-plan enforcement  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** cline is the closest analog to Claude Code, so its operator-facing patterns transfer well, and the candidate has three distinct claims worth weighing. (1) Plan/Act switching that carries context forward — directly maps to a watch/steer/spawn console: the operator wants to flip a running session between "discuss/research only" and "execute" without losing the conversation. (2) "Approve plan -> Act" in one keystroke — a real ergonomic win that fits Shepherd's keystroke-injection model exactly: submit the held response and cycle the mode in a single serialized chain, the same primitive nudge/clear/compact already use. (3) Strict Plan Mode — enforcing the no-edit constraint at the harness level rather than trusting the model is genuinely valuable for an unattended fleet, and it is precisely the kind of guardrail an operator console should own rather than delegate to the prompt. The user's stated priority (saved agents with attached policy) is reinforced: a "Planner" agent profile (L1) that pairs permMode=plan with a read-only policy bundle (L2) is exactly this candidate, made durable and reusable.
- **Against:** Most of the candidate is already shipped and the rest reduces to existing primitives, so building it "as-is" would be re-adding what exists. Plan/Act mode switching ALREADY EXISTS: MODE_CYCLE includes "plan", spawnFlags emits --permission-mode plan (so -p/--plan is covered), and there is a detail-panel Mode dropdown plus a New-session permission-mode picker — the live switch already carries context forward because it just Shift+Tabs a running session via modeCycleSteps. Strict Plan Mode (a hard-block list of mutating tools) is not a new mechanism: Shepherd's gate + autoDeny of the DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS mutating set (Bash/Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit) already produces a read-only session, and L2 already queues that exact thing as the "read-only" / "no-Bash" starter bundle. Crucially, cline's enforcement lives INSIDE the agent loop (ToolExecutor.isToolRestrictedInPlanMode intercepts tool calls in-process) — Shepherd has no executor and cannot intercept a tool call; it can only autoDeny at the approval gate, which is a different (and already-built) mechanism. So the only net-new sliver is the one-keystroke "approve plan -> Act", a minor convenience, not a feature warranting its own system.
- **Ruling:** Decompose the candidate into its claims and test each against "already have / structurally can't / transferable need." Claim A (Plan/Act switch carrying context): ALREADY DONE — M.MODE_CYCLE contains "plan", modeCycleSteps + the Shift+Tab dispatch flip a live session in place (context is inherently preserved), and the detail-panel + New-session pickers expose it. Claim B (-p/--plan CLI): ALREADY DONE — spawnFlags emits "--permission-mode plan". Claim C (Strict Plan Mode hard-block): this is cline's in-process ToolExecutor restriction, which Shepherd structurally cannot host (no agent loop, no tool interception) — BUT the operator NEED behind it (don't trust the model to honor plan-mode; enforce it) already has a Shepherd-native realization: the approval gate + autoDeny of the mutating set, which L2 is formalizing as a named read-only/no-Bash bundle attachable per-session. So Strict Plan = L2, reshaped onto the gate, not cline's executor hook. Claim D (one-keystroke approve-plan->Act): genuinely new and small — fits the keystroke chokepoint (submit held response, then cycle to acceptEdits/default), worth an XS add but not its own system. Net verdict: ADAPT, reinforcing L2 (and adjacent to L1's "Planner" agent profile = permMode:plan + read-only policyBundle). The bulk is redundant; the residual need (gate-enforced plan-time read-only + a quick approve->Act chain) folds cleanly into queued work. Effort is small because the heavy lifting (mode cycle, gate, autoDeny) exists; only the approve->Act chain and the named bundle wiring remain, the latter being L2's scope anyway.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L2 (named policy/guardrail bundles) plus a thin UX add, NOT a new mode system. (1) Strict Plan Mode = L2's "read-only" starter bundle: autoDeny the DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS mutating set (Bash Write Edit MultiEdit NotebookEdit) via the gate — NOT an allow-list (mind the gate semantics: tools not in gate.tools run freely). Make this bundle auto-attach when a session/profile sets permMode=plan, so the no-edit constraint is gate-enforced instead of model-trusted. (2) L1 "Planner" agent profile = {permMode:"plan", policyBundle:"read-only"} spawned via existing spawnFlags --permission-mode plan + the L2 resolver. (3) New, small: a one-keystroke "Approve plan -> Act" detail-panel action that, through the dispatchSerialized chokepoint, submits the held response (Enter) then runs modeCycleSteps(plan -> acceptEdits/default) — reuse the existing mode-cycle + paste primitives; ledger it as a mode_change. No new switch, no executor, no tool interception (Shepherd can't intercept a tool call — it only autoDenies at the gate).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt failed — every load-bearing claim is verified in-tree and the ADAPT/reinforces-L2 ruling is correct. (A) Plan/Act switch carrying context = ALREADY DONE: M.MODE_CYCLE includes "plan" (cc-core.lua:3013) and modeCycleSteps (cc-core.lua:3019) flips a LIVE in-place session via Shift+Tab, so conversation context is inherently preserved; the detail panel + New-session pickers expose it (claude-dashboard.lua:3086, 4244). (B) -p/--plan CLI = ALREADY DONE: spawnFlags emits "--permission-mode plan" (cc-core.lua:3001-3008; tests core.test.lua:791,880,918). (C) Strict Plan hard-block is cline's in-process ToolExecutor interception, which Shepherd STRUCTURALLY CANNOT host (no agent loop, no tool execution) — but the operator need (enforce no-edit, don't trust the model) is already Shepherd-native via the gate + autoDeny of the mutating set DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS="Bash Write Edit MultiEdit NotebookEdit" (cc-core.lua:2525), and L2 explicitly formalizes a named "read-only" starter bundle doing exactly this (todos.md:357-359). Key correctness point the judge nailed: Shepherd's gate is an ESCALATION list, not an allow-list (tools not in it run freely, resolveGateTools cc-core.lua:2537), so it must be autoDeny — NOT an allow-list — and auto-attach when permMode=plan. (D) One-keystroke approve-plan->Act is genuinely new but SMALL and feasible with no executor: the answer/Enter submit (cc-core.lua:183-190, KEY_APPROVE return) and set-mode via the dispatchSerialized chokepoint with mode_change ledgering (claude-dashboard.lua:2430-2466) already exist; the new action just chains submit-held-response then modeCycleSteps(plan->acceptEdits/default). Net: bulk is redundant (A,B), C's executor-hook form is impossible but its need = L2 on the gate, D is a thin Shepherd-native UX add (adjacent to L1's Planner profile = {permMode:plan, policyBundle:read-only}). Not DONT_ADD (the residual approve->Act chain genuinely improves a local single-user console steering a Claude Code session) and not a clean ADD (heavy lifting already exists). ADAPT holds.

#### Per-category auto-approve toggles with inline policy summary (Read/Edit/Safe Commands/Browser/MCP)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** Auto-approve policy is the single most direct operator-pattern analog cline shares with Shepherd: deciding which tool classes skip a per-call human prompt is exactly Shepherd's gate/autopilot job, and human-in-the-loop approval is Shepherd's reason to exist. The valuable kernel is the SHAPE: a small, named, scannable set of auto-approve dimensions with an inline "what's currently auto-approved" summary, instead of today's flat anonymous autoAllow/autoDeny glob textareas plus a separate per-session gate-tools dropdown. That readable summary maps cleanly onto core.gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752), and the category set generalizes Shepherd's existing Default/All/None/Custom gate split into the named, attachable policy bundles already queued as L2 (policies.bundles = {autoAllow[], autoDeny[], gateTools, autopilot, lockedPermMode}). The Read/Edit workspace-vs-external sub-toggle is a genuinely useful refinement (path-scoped policy) that fits L2's per-rule model and ties to L1 (a saved agent carries a default bundle — read-only / no-Bash / MCP-only starter bundles are already in the L2 plan). It improves a watch/steer console because clearer auto-approve policy = fewer surprise unattended actions and a more legible gate.
- **Against:** The feature's headline value depends on cline RUNNING the agent loop and EXECUTING tools, which Shepherd structurally cannot do. "Execute safe commands" hinges on the model tagging each command requires_approval at generation time and the executor gating on it — there is no fixed allowlist, so without the in-process loop emitting that flag and a tool runner honoring it, the toggle has nothing to act on. "Use the browser" is meaningless: Shepherd has no browser tool. Auto-approving Read/Edit/MCP only matters to the process that performs those reads/writes/MCP calls; Shepherd performs none — Claude Code itself owns tool execution and its own permission prompts. Worse, cline's semantics are a positive allow-list ("toggle ON to grant + skip the prompt"), which is the EXACT inversion of Shepherd's gate (todos.md:236-239: gate.tools is a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL list; tools not in it run freely; read-only is built by autoDeny of the mutating set, never by an allow-list). Copying the checkbox semantics literally would be backwards and dangerous. And the operator need it serves is already substantially met: Plan/Act-style perm-mode switch, per-session gate dropdown, autoAllow/autoDeny with syntax hints, autopilot, and the gate decision log all ship today.
- **Ruling:** The crux: separate the operator pattern (a compact, named, summarized auto-approve policy) from the execution plumbing (model-tagged requires_approval + a tool runner + a browser). The plumbing is pure cline-mismatch — no agent loop, no executor, no browser — so the literal feature is DONT_ADD-shaped. But the pattern is real, partially shipped, and squarely in Shepherd's wheelhouse (the gate IS the auto-approve surface). It does not justify a fresh ADD because Shepherd already has the autoAllow/autoDeny + gate-tools + autopilot machinery and a queued plan (L2) to turn it into named bundles; this candidate is best read as design pressure on L2 — specifically (a) the inline policy SUMMARY ("Auto-approve: Read, Safe Commands") as a first-class readout, and (b) the path-scoped workspace-vs-external Read/Edit dimension. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L2. The one hard constraint to carry into L2: keep Shepherd's hold-for-approval inversion (autoDeny-the-mutating-set), never reintroduce cline's positive-allow-list framing, and drop Browser entirely + drop the requires_approval safe/unsafe split (no executor to consume it; the closest legible substitute is autoDeny globs like Bash(rm*) that already exist).
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L2 (Named policy/guardrail bundles), not a parallel system. (1) Replace today's flat policies.patterns {autoAllow[],autoDeny[]} + per-session gate.tools with named policies.bundles {autoAllow[], autoDeny[], gateTools, autopilot, lockedPermMode}; ship the candidate's categories as STARTER BUNDLES already planned (read-only = autoDeny DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS, no-Bash, MCP-only) rather than literal checkboxes — honoring the gate-semantics inversion (todos.md:236-239). (2) The inline policy summary becomes a Settings/detail-panel readout built from core.gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752) + the resolved bundle name (the L2 transparency thread into ledger_decision by/pattern). (3) The Read/Edit workspace-vs-external sub-toggle = a path-scoped autoDeny dimension in a bundle rule (Edit outside the launch folder gets gated/denied), resolved by the one pure resolver mirroring resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537) — session override > attached bundle > fleet default. (4) DROP: the Browser toggle (no browser tool), the model-tagged requires_approval safe/unsafe command split (no executor to emit/consume it), and the positive allow-list framing.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails; the ADAPT (reinforces L2) ruling holds. I verified the codebase and the judge's citations are all accurate: gateDecisionSummary (cc-core.lua:752), resolveGateTools (cc-core.lua:2537), the gate-semantics inversion note (todos.md:236-239), and the L2 plan (todos.md:336-385). Two refutation paths exist and both fail. (1) DONT_ADD ("it's pure cline-executor plumbing"): only PART of the feature is — the model-tagged requires_approval safe/unsafe split needs an executor to emit/consume the tag (Shepherd runs no agent loop, executes no tools), and the Browser toggle needs a browser tool Shepherd lacks and explicitly DROPs (todos.md:521). The judge already surgically dropped both. The surviving pattern — a compact, named, summarized auto-approve policy — is NOT execution; it is config/policy management, which is the transferable class. Shepherd's gate IS the auto-approve surface: it already ships autoAllow/autoDeny globs, autopilot (time-boxed auto-approve-all), and the gate.tools hold-list. So DONT_ADD overreaches. (2) Fresh ADD: also wrong, because L2 (todos.md:336-385) is a near-complete blueprint for exactly this — named policies.bundles {autoAllow[], autoDeny[], gateTools, autopilot, lockedPermMode}, starter bundles (read-only/no-Bash/MCP-only = the candidate's category checkboxes), a resolver mirroring resolveGateTools, and transparency via gateDecisionSummary. A parallel system would duplicate it. That leaves ADAPT (reinforce L2) as correct: the candidate contributes two genuinely-additive nuances as design pressure on L2 — (a) the inline policy summary ('Auto-approve: Read, Safe Commands') as a first-class always-visible readout (sharper than L2's current 'thread bundle name into ledger' framing, buildable on gateDecisionSummary at cc-core.lua:752), and (b) the Read/Edit workspace-vs-external path-scoped dimension, which maps cleanly onto the existing stable projectKey/launch-folder primitive (cc-core.lua:52) as a path-scoped autoDeny rule. The hard constraints the judge carries are exactly right and verified in-repo: keep the autoDeny hold-for-approval inversion, never a positive allow-list (todos.md:236-239), and drop Browser + the requires_approval split (no executor). Ruling holds at ADAPT.

#### Declarative per-tool policies (autoApprove/enabled, tiered, safe-baseline, conditional)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L2
- **For:** This is the closest cline analog to Shepherd's own reason to exist (human-in-the-loop permission control), and it maps onto operator-facing CONFIG, not agent execution. Shepherd already ships every primitive this feature needs: an ordered auto-policy pipeline in cc-approve.sh (autoDeny -> autopilot -> autoAllow -> approveRepeats -> panel), declarative policies.patterns.{autoAllow,autoDeny} globs, a per-session gate.tools override resolved by core.resolveGateTools, autopilot (time-boxed auto-approve-all), and a single combined off-switch (arming the gate also disables auto-policies). The candidate's best ideas are genuine upgrades to that substrate: (a) keying policy by TOOL NAME with explicit allow/ask intent is cleaner than two parallel glob lists; (b) a curated SAFE baseline that stays approved even when global auto-approve is off is exactly the kind of named starter bundle L2 already calls for (read-only / no-Bash / MCP-only); (c) tiered policies (reads auto, writes ask) match L2's per-rule {action: deny|allow|flag, tier: pre|observe}; (d) the deny-with-reason / steering hook overlaps the crewAI-corroborated deny-reason enrichment already under L2. It directly serves the user's stated priority of saved agents carrying a default policy bundle (L1 profile -> attached L2 bundle). All of it can be expressed declaratively in cc-config.json and resolved by one new pure cc-core function, with zero new execution surface.
- **Against:** Two of the candidate's pillars assume an EXECUTOR that Shepherd structurally is not, and adopting cline's model literally would invert Shepherd's gate semantics. First, {enabled:false} (hide/disable a tool so it never appears to the agent) has no Shepherd analog: Shepherd runs no agent loop and does not enumerate or gate tool availability — the best it can do is deny at the gate (the tool still gets requested first) or pass --disallowed-tools at spawn (native Claude Code owns that, not Shepherd). Second, the file-hook overrideInput (rewrite a tool's arguments mid-call) requires editing the in-flight tool invocation; Shepherd's PreToolUse hook (cc-approve.sh) only ever emits allow/deny — it cannot mutate tool input, so cancel/review/overrideInput collapses to just allow/deny+reason. Third and most dangerous: cline's policy is an ALLOW-LIST keyed by tool with a SAFE_AUTO_APPROVE baseline, but Shepherd's gate.tools is the OPPOSITE — a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL/escalation list where tools NOT listed run freely (cc-approve.sh `*) exit 0`). Porting "auto-approve read_files/search, leave them enabled" verbatim would be a backwards no-op (those tools already run free) or, worse, invite someone to phrase a tool list as "grant," which silently weakens the gate. So the literal cline shape cannot be copied; only the declarative tiered-bundle IDEA transfers, and that idea is already fully scoped as L2.
- **Ruling:** The candidate is essentially a restatement of L2 (Named policy/guardrail bundles) framed in cline's vocabulary. The underlying NEED — declarative, tiered, reusable per-tool approval policy with a safe baseline and one global toggle — is real, fits a watch/steer console, and aligns with the user's saved-agent priority. But it cannot be built as-is for three reasons documented at todos.md:236 and in the cline mismatch: (1) gate.tools is a hold-for-approval list, NOT an allow-list, so cline's allow-list-with-safe-baseline model must be re-expressed as autoDeny of the mutating set, not auto-approve of reads; (2) {enabled:false} tool-hiding needs the executor Shepherd lacks (closest legitimate mapping is --disallowed-tools at spawn, owned by native Claude Code); (3) overrideInput needs in-flight tool-call editing that the PreToolUse hook cannot do. Everything that DOES transfer — named tiered bundles, a curated safe-baseline starter bundle, per-rule allow/deny/flag tiers, deny-with-reason, the single global enable — is already enumerated under L2 with the resolver, the KEEP-IN-SYNC contract, and the off-by-default constraint. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L2; not ADD because the literal feature would invert the gate model and assumes execution surface, not DONT_ADD because the operator pattern is squarely Shepherd's domain and already queued.
- **How (Shepherd):** Folds into L2 (todos.md:336). Concretely: (1) Add cline's per-tool keyed intent as the bundle shape — policies.bundles[name].rules = {Tool -> action: allow|deny|flag} — but RESOLVE it through the existing hold-for-approval semantics: 'ask' = the tool stays in the effective gate.tools list; 'allow' = an autoAllow entry (or autopilot-equivalent); 'deny' = autoDeny. Reuse the autoDeny->autopilot->autoAllow->approveRepeats pipeline in cc-approve.sh (~166-196) unchanged; it just reads the resolved bundle. (2) SAFE_AUTO_APPROVE baseline -> ship as L2's read-only/no-Bash/MCP-only named starter bundles (todos.md:357); since reads already run free under the gate, the 'safe baseline that survives global-off' is naturally expressed as 'these stay OUT of gate.tools even when the gate is armed.' (3) Tiered (reads auto / writes ask) = autoDeny/ask the DEFAULT_GATE_TOOLS mutating set, leave reads ungated — already the default shape. (4) Single global toggle = the existing cc-gate.enabled flag plus policies.patterns.enabled. (5) Conditional/hook steering -> only the deny+deny-reason path (crewAI enrichment at todos.md:368); DROP overrideInput (no in-flight input mutation) and {enabled:false} tool-hiding (map to an optional --disallowed-tools spawn flag on the L1 agent profile, native Claude Code enforces). New code: ONE pure cc-core resolver mirroring resolveGateTools precedence (session override > attached bundle > fleet default), honoring the cc-core.lua <-> cc-approve.sh KEEP-IN-SYNC contract; bundle CRUD + attach UI in the Settings panel.
- **Skeptic:** Tried to refute ADAPT; could not. All three of the judge's structural claims are verified in the code. (1) The gate is a HOLD-FOR-APPROVAL list, not an allow-list: cc-approve.sh:96-99 sends any tool NOT in GATE_TOOLS to `*) exit 0` (runs free, ungated), so cline's SAFE_AUTO_APPROVE "reads auto / everything-else asks" model is the literal inverse and must be re-expressed as autoDeny of the mutating set, not auto-approve of reads (confirmed at todos.md:236-238). (2) {enabled:false} tool-hiding needs an executor Shepherd lacks: spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:3001) emits only --permission-mode/--remote-control; no runtime tool-disable exists, the only legit map is a --disallowed-tools launch flag enforced by native Claude Code (todos.md:374). (3) overrideInput is impossible: the PreToolUse hook emits ONLY permissionDecision allow|deny+reason via emit_allow/emit_deny (cc-approve.sh:37-44), it cannot mutate a tool call's input in flight. Everything that DOES transfer is already enumerated under L2 with the resolver, the cc-core.lua<->cc-approve.sh KEEP-IN-SYNC contract, and off-by-default: named tiered bundles + per-rule action deny|allow|flag (todos.md:340,380), read-only/no-Bash/MCP-only safe-baseline starter bundles (todos.md:357), tiered reads-auto/writes-ask = Shepherd's DEFAULT gate shape, single global toggle = existing cc-gate.enabled + policies.patterns.enabled flags (cc-approve.sh:66,139), and deny-reason (todos.md:368). The need is real, local, single-user, watch/steer-shaped, and matches the saved-agent priority, so not DONT_ADD; but it is a restatement of L2 in cline's vocabulary (reinforcing, not new) with overrideInput and runtime {enabled:false} correctly dropped, so not ADD. Verdict ADAPT, reinforces L2, holds.

### Reinforces L6 — rule engine  (5)

#### Automatic context condensing vs programmatic truncation (with threshold)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The explicitly-stated transferable takeaway — "a per-session context-% THRESHOLD that fires a /compact keystroke" — is something Shepherd is uniquely positioned to do, and every prerequisite already ships. Shepherd reads per-session context tokens from the local transcript (contextTokens), divides by window×context.autoCompactFraction to track Claude Code's own "% until auto-compact", and renders it as a 7-band bar (contextFractionFor/contextBand). It already types /compact as a manual action from the tile button, context menu, and detail panel via the dispatchSerialized chokepoint. And it already has the exact bounded-auto-action template this needs: stepAutoContinue (opt-in, off by default, grace clock, per-target attempt cap, ledger event). An auto-compact stepper is a near-clone of that — fire /compact when a quiescent (idle/done) tile crosses a configured %, on a cooldown, capped per session. The operator NEED is real for a watch console: long-running sessions silently hit the context wall and degrade; a watcher that proactively compacts at, say, 85% is squarely "steer the session," not "run the agent." It folds cleanly into L6 as a new `compact` processor on a `context-threshold` trigger, alongside the existing feed/continue/nudge/relabel/log catalog.
- **Against:** The bulk of the candidate is pure in-process executor plumbing that Shepherd structurally cannot host: auto-condense asks the model to summarize and REPLACES the conversation history; programmatic truncation drops older messages from the agent's message array (getNextTruncationRange keeps the first pair, drops half/quarter, writes a [NOTE] marker); the file-read de-dup rewrites prior tool results in-place. Shepherd has no conversation array, makes no API calls, and cannot read/splice/rewrite a session's messages — Claude Code owns and condenses its own history. None of that transfers. What's left after stripping it is thin: Claude Code ALREADY auto-compacts on its own at its own threshold, so Shepherd firing /compact earlier is a marginal nicety, and a mistimed auto-/compact mid-turn or over a pending prompt could disrupt a session (mitigated only by gating to quiescent tiles). Auto-typing destructive-ish slash commands also cuts against Shepherd's human-in-the-loop ethos.
- **Ruling:** The candidate splits cleanly on the cline mismatch. Half of it — auto-condense (model-summarize + replace history), programmatic truncation of the message array, file-read de-dup, [NOTE] markers, getNextTruncationRange — is in-process agent-loop work on a conversation array Shepherd does not own and cannot touch. That half is DONT_ADD by structure. But the brief itself isolates the transferable kernel: a per-session context-% threshold that triggers a /compact keystroke. That kernel lands on primitives Shepherd already has end-to-end — per-tile context-% (contextFractionFor/contextBand, calibrated against the editor's "% until auto-compact"), the /compact keystroke through dispatchSerialized, and a proven off-by-default bounded-auto-action stepper (shouldAutoContinue/stepAutoContinue with grace clock, per-target cap, and ledger event). So this is not ADD-as-is (the engine doesn't transfer) and not DONT_ADD (the watcher-side trigger genuinely fits and is wanted). It is ADAPT, and it belongs to the same automation family L6 generalizes: L6's processor catalog (feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log) gains a `compact` processor and its trigger kinds gain a `context-threshold` predicate, sitting beside shouldAutoContinue. The interim hard-coded form mirrors autoContinue.* config exactly. Effort is S: bar/%/keystroke/ledger/quiescent-gate/off-by-default scaffolding all exist; net-new is one pure gate + stepper modeled on stepAutoContinue, a config block, and a settings toggle. Confidence high — the mapping is direct and the prior-art template is already in the codebase.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reshape to "Auto-Compact at a context-% threshold," modeled 1:1 on Auto-Continue. (1) New pure gates in cc-core.lua beside shouldAutoContinue (~2200): `shouldAutoCompact({frac, thresholdPct, status, cooldownElapsed})` — fire only on a quiescent tile (status idle/done, never working/approval/error, reusing the remoteControlSweepTargets quiescence guard) whose context_frac ≥ threshold; and `stepAutoCompact(state, item, opts)` owning a per-session cooldown clock + per-session cap so it can't loop. (2) Threshold source already exists: it.context_frac / contextFractionFor (the "% until auto-compact" reading). (3) Effect already exists: type /compact via dispatchSerialized (same path as the b-compact button / context-menu Compact), emit a `compact`/`auto_compact` ledger event (ledgerFor) and an 🔔 Alert like auto_continue. (4) Config block `autoCompact.{enabled=false, thresholdPct=85, cooldownSeconds, maxPerSession}` mirroring autoContinue.*, with a settings-panel toggle next to "Auto-Continue (API-error recovery)" (~3162) and the s-cont-* wiring. OFF BY DEFAULT. (5) Final home: a L6 rule — trigger kind `context-threshold` (scope sessionKey) + processor kind `compact` (map to handleAction "compact") in cc-rules.json, retiring the hard-coded block once L6 lands. Skip remote tiles and non-Anthropic/gateway tiles where /compact behavior differs (reuse isAnthropicSession).
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT holds. The feature SPLITS on the cline mismatch and the judge handled the split correctly. The engine half — auto-condense (model-summarize + replace history), programmatic truncation of the message array, file-read de-dup, [NOTE] markers, getNextTruncationRange — is in-process agent-loop work on a conversation array Shepherd does not own and cannot touch (no executor, edits nothing). That half is structurally DONT_ADD. But the brief itself isolates the transferable kernel: a per-session context-% THRESHOLD that fires a /compact keystroke. That is pure watcher-side steering, and every primitive it needs is confirmed in the actual code, not assumed: (1) stepper template — shouldAutoContinue (cc-core.lua:2200) + stepAutoContinue (2220) are a pure 'is-it-time' gate plus a stepper that owns a grace clock, per-target cap, and off-by-default budget; a shouldAutoCompact/stepAutoCompact sits beside them 1:1. (2) Threshold source — contextFractionFor (1806) already yields the editor's '% until auto-compact' reading; contextBand (1817) drives the per-tile bar. (3) Quiescence guard — remoteControlSweepTargets (2250) already fires slash commands only on idle/done tiles (skipping working/approval/error) and gates on isAnthropicSession to skip gateway/third-party tiles where /compact differs. (4) Effect — /compact already runs end-to-end through dispatchSerialized (claude-dashboard.lua:2304) with a ledgerFor({type='compact'}) event and the b-compact button (3070); handleAction treats compact as a keystroke action (cc-core.lua:2927). (5) Config + UI prior art — autoContinue.{enabled=false,delaySeconds,maxAttempts} (cc-config.example.json:136) plus its settings toggle (s-cont-auto, dashboard 3758) and tick loop (5609-5616) are the exact off-by-default mirror to clone. This is NOT redundant: no existing trigger fires /compact on a context-% predicate (autopilot=auto-approve, auto-continue=API-error recovery, the gate=permissions). It is NOT server/multi-user, NOT executor-dependent. ADD-as-is is wrong (the condensing/truncation engine does not transfer to a watcher that owns no array); DONT_ADD is wrong (the threshold-trigger kernel directly improves the local single-user console and rides primitives that already ship — context-% tracking is already live). Final home is L6: the processor catalog (feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log) gains a 'compact' processor and trigger kinds (done|error|hung|approval|starved) gain a 'context-threshold' predicate (todos.md:544), retiring the interim hard-coded block. Effort S. Cannot refute.

#### Cron scheduling engine (5-field expressions + timezone + presets + next-run-at + upcoming view)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** Shepherd already SPAWNS Claude Code GUI sessions on demand (spawnSpec/FX.spawnSession, presets, a present local claude CLI) and already FEEDS queues on a level-triggered dispatcher — so "fire a spawn/feed at a wall-clock time" needs NO new structural capability, only a new trigger. The cline cron engine cleanly decomposes into the two things Shepherd is built for: (1) a pure, deterministic parser + minute-scan next-occurrence finder (parseCron/getNextCronTime/validateTimezone) is textbook cc-core.lua material — zero hs.*, unit-testable beside the existing os.date/civil-day helpers (~1626) and runSequence scheduler — and (2) an 'upcoming' read-only list sorted by nextRunAt maps onto the existing read-only overlay family (insights/sparklines/timeline/audit). It fills a real gap: every Shepherd automation today is event/level-triggered (done/error/hung/approval/starved) or short relative-delay (delaySeconds grace); there is NO time-of-day trigger anywhere, and the queued L6 rule engine's trigger taxonomy explicitly lacks one. The ~1s refresh loop is a ready evaluation tick for "is any schedule due now?". Presets (weekday 9am, hourly, first-of-month) + a weekday/time picker fit the operator-config UX Shepherd favors (Settings tabs, preset chips). Nothing here touches an agent loop, tool execution, workspace editing/snapshotting, secrets, or multi-user.
- **Against:** Shepherd's reason-to-exist is HUMAN-IN-THE-LOOP (approve/deny/nudge); a cron engine that spawns sessions while the operator is away pushes toward unattended autonomy, the opposite pole, and a scheduled spawn that lands in an approval-gated session just sits at 'Needs you' with no human present — value leaks unless paired with autopilot/headless gate (the riskiest config). Persistence is a structural weakness: Hammerspoon timers only fire while Shepherd is running and the Mac is awake — there is no launchd/OS-level durability, so a 'daily 9am' that the app missed (asleep/quit) either silently skips or misfires, which real cron users expect not to happen; catch-up/coalescing semantics are fiddly. Full IANA timezone correctness (DST transitions, validateTimezone) is non-trivial to hand-roll zero-dependency in Lua and is mostly wasted for a single-user single-machine console already living in the user's local tz. And cline's cron is the front-half of its cloud/routine SCHEDULED-AGENT product (runs the agent unattended in the cloud) — the part with teeth is the executor, which Shepherd structurally cannot host; copying the scheduler without it risks a half-feature whose only honest job is 'spawn a GUI window at a time'.
- **Ruling:** The cline MISMATCH does NOT bite here: the schedule does not run an agent loop, execute tools, or edit/snapshot a workspace — it triggers a SPAWN (or queue-feed), which Shepherd already does on demand. So the value does not depend on the executor; it depends on a trigger Shepherd lacks, which makes it transferable. But it should NOT be built as cline's standalone cron product (separate scheduler.ts/schedule-service/routine-view); it should be reshaped as a new trigger.kind on the queued L6 event-callback rule engine, whose taxonomy is currently done|error|hung|approval|starved — adding time|cron there yields 'when <time> -> feed|spawn|nudge|log' reusing L6's per-tick pure predicate evaluation, the by:"rule" ledger event, fx-persisted status mutation, and the resolver/validator family (with L1 agent profiles as the spawn target). The pure parser/next-occurrence finder goes in cc-core; the 'upcoming' view is a read-only overlay twin. Hence ADAPT/reinforces L6, not ADD (avoid a parallel scheduler) and not DONT_ADD (no executor needed). Scope-clean: no server, no users, no secrets. The against-side concerns (persistence, unattended HITL tension, TZ hand-rolling) are real but off-by-default/config matters, not structural blockers — they argue for a thin slice (local tz, presets, skip-if-missed semantics, off by default) rather than rejection. High confidence because the parser is pure cc-core work and the trigger slots into an already-designed queued primitive.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend L6's rule registry (cc-rules.json) with a new trigger kind: trigger:{kind:"cron", expr:"0 9 * * MON-FRI", tz?:IANA} alongside done|error|hung|approval|starved. Pure cc-core.lua: parseCron + nextCronOccurrence(now) (minute-scan, days-from-civil style like the os.time helper ~1626) + a small CRON_PRESETS table; evaluate "is any cron rule due since last tick?" on the existing ~1s refresh loop (coalesce missed fires; skip-if-app-was-down). Processor reuses the L6 catalog mapped onto present effects: spawn (FX.spawnSession via an L1 agent profile / spawn preset as target), feed (routeTask into a queue), nudge, log->appendLedger; lifecycle emits a by:"rule" ledger event. The 'upcoming' view is a read-only overlay twin in the insights/timeline/audit family listing nextRunAt across enabled rules. A weekday/time picker + preset chips live in the Settings/rules UI. Off by default; ship local-tz-only first (defer full IANA/DST). Spawn flags ride the existing spawnSpec (--agent/--mcp-config) — native Claude Code owns execution; Shepherd only emits the spawn at the scheduled minute.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute ADAPT and could not. The standard refutation lever — the cline EXECUTOR mismatch — does not bite here, which I verified in the code: spawnInner/spawnAppleScript/spawnFlags (cc-core.lua:2970-3001) are pure command builders that emit `cd <dir> && claude [flags] [prompt]` into a terminal; the spawned claude is native and owns its own agent loop. Shepherd runs no loop, edits/snapshots nothing. A cron fire is the same emit-a-command Shepherd already does on demand, just at minute T — so the value does NOT depend on an executor. It is also not server/multi-user/secrets work: cron evaluation is local pure logic on an injected `now`. It is not redundant with existing automation either. Every current trigger (shouldFeed ~1975, isHung ~2375, shouldAutoContinue ~2200, shouldAutoRespawn ~2116) is level-triggered on SESSION STATE; none is wall-clock/time-triggered. So a time|cron trigger is a genuinely new axis Shepherd lacks. And the ~1s refresh loop already evaluates pure predicates against an injected `now` (isHung at 2378), so a pure nextCronOccurrence(now) predicate slots into the same machinery — reusing the days-from-civil epoch helper at cc-core.lua:1626 (the helper the judge cited, confirmed present). The judge is right to reshape it as a new trigger.kind on the QUEUED L6 rule engine (cc-rules.json — verified L6 is queued in todos.md ~544, NOT built; grep for cron/parseCron/cc-rules in cc-core.lua and claude-dashboard.lua returns nothing) rather than a standalone scheduler.ts/routine-view. That is precisely why this is ADD->ADAPT (extends a queued primitive, avoids a parallel system), not DONT_ADD. One real inaccuracy in the judge's MAPPING, but it strengthens the downgrade rather than refuting it: L6's processor catalog in todos.md is feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log — it does NOT list spawn; the judge silently adds spawn as the headline action. And unattended cron->SPAWN is exactly the automation Shepherd already deferred as too aggressive — the deferred 'auto-spawn on starvation' item (todos.md ~202) says spawn 'is an aggressive automation; needs a cap + UX.' So cron->spawn has an open UX gate, while cron->feed/nudge/log is clean and already in L6's catalog. That argues for a thinner slice (local-tz, presets, skip-if-missed, off by default, feed/log first) — which is an ADAPT scoping note, not a structural blocker. ADAPT/reinforces-L6 holds.

#### Event-triggered agent runs (non-cron triggers) with throttling  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The transferable core of this feature — a declarative when-event->action rule with a normalized event envelope and a throttling vocabulary — is precisely the operator pattern Shepherd already wants. The candidate itself concedes the split: the HTTP/webhook/connector ingress "needs a server," but "the when-event->action rule shape + throttling vocabulary + normalized event envelope transfer to a local status-file-driven rule engine." That local engine is L6 (Event-callback rule engine), already queued from the OpenHands mining: opt-in cc-rules.json rules {trigger:{scope, kind: done|error|hung|approval|starved}, processor:{feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log}, once?, retryUntil?} evaluated each refresh tick by pure cc-core predicates beside shouldFeed/shouldAutoContinue/isHung. Two pieces are genuine enrichments L6 doesn't yet name: (1) the throttling vocabulary — debounceSeconds/dedupeWindowSeconds/cooldownSeconds/maxParallel — which formalizes the ad-hoc rate limits Shepherd's autonomy loops already carry (auto-respawn per-folder budget, auto-continue per-folder cooldown spacing, watchdog re-alert suppression); a shared throttle struct on the rule shape unifies these. (2) The normalized event envelope concept lets Shepherd's status-file-derived signals (done/error/hung/approval/starved edges) AND a couple of cheap pollable local events (PR opened/merged via the L5 gh-poll badge; collision detected; a manual local.manual_test trigger from a tile button) be matched by the same dot-path JSON filters. This widens an existing primitive, fits the watch/steer/spawn console, and is off-by-default like everything that automates.
- **Against:** The headline of the feature — events arriving via GitHub webhooks, connector adapters, and an SDK/HTTP ingestEvent() endpoint — requires an inbound network server, which Shepherd's hard scope forbids (NO network server, NO multi-user, NO secrets to authenticate a webhook). plugin-emitted events via registerAutomationEventType + a contribution registry presuppose cline's in-process plugin host and SDK runtime that Shepherd does not have. So roughly half the feature's value (real external triggers like github.pull_request.opened pushed in) is structurally impossible here without violating context.md. There's also a duplication risk: Shepherd already has hard-coded event->action loops (auto-respawn on a frozen working file, auto-continue on an Error tile, escalation/watchdog on hung, the level-triggered project-routing dispatcher feeding an armed queue on a done edge). One could argue the marginal win of a declarative rule layer over these working, tuned, off-by-default loops is small and adds a config surface and a new failure mode (a misfiring rule typing into sessions) — i.e. the need is already met well enough by the bespoke loops plus L6 as already scoped, and the throttling vocabulary alone isn't worth a verdict bump.
- **Ruling:** This is the cline analog of an already-queued backlog item, not a new system, so the verdict is ADAPT reinforcing L6 (not ADD, and not a parallel proposal). The cline MISMATCH cleanly bisects the feature: the event INGRESS half (webhooks/connectors/SDK ingestEvent/plugin registry) depends on a server + in-process plugin/SDK host Shepherd structurally lacks — that half is DONT_ADD and must be dropped. The event SHAPE half (normalized envelope, dot-path filters, when-event->action rule, debounce/dedupe/cooldown/maxParallel throttles) is exactly the local, status-file-driven rule engine the candidate text says transfers — and that is L6. L6 today enumerates triggers (done|error|hung|approval|starved) and processors (feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log) but does NOT specify a throttling vocabulary or a normalized-envelope/filter abstraction; this candidate supplies both, and Shepherd's existing autonomy loops already prove the throttles are needed (per-folder budgets, cooldown spacing, re-alert suppression are scattered, un-unified versions of debounce/cooldown/dedupe). So the right action is to enrich L6's spec with (a) a shared throttle struct {debounceSeconds, dedupeWindowSeconds, cooldownSeconds, maxParallel} applied uniformly across rule firings, and (b) a thin normalized event envelope so the existing status-derived edges and a few cheap local pollable events (PR status from the L5 gh badge, collision, a manual tile-button local trigger) feed one matcher. GitHub PR events specifically can be honored WITHOUT a webhook by Shepherd polling gh (already proposed in L5's PR-status badge) and synthesizing a github.pull_request.opened envelope edge locally — pull, not push. Effort M, consistent with L6's existing M estimate, since it's pure cc-core predicates + a throttle struct + envelope normalizer layered on the refresh tick, no new ingress.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L6 (Event-callback rule engine), don't build separately. Concretely: (1) Extend the queued cc-rules.json rule struct with an optional throttle block {debounceSeconds, dedupeWindowSeconds, cooldownSeconds, maxParallel}; implement as a pure cc-core helper (e.g. core.throttleAllows(ruleState, now)) evaluated alongside shouldFeed (~cc-core 1975)/shouldAutoContinue (~2200)/isHung (~2375), with per-rule fire-state persisted through fx like routePending/alerted markers — and refactor the existing scattered budgets/cooldowns (auto-respawn per-folder budget ~5595, auto-continue spacing ~2200, watchdog re-alert) to share this struct where natural. (2) Add a pure core.normalizeEvent(signal) producing {type, scope:projectKey|sessionKey, payload} from the status-file-derived edges (done/error/hung/approval/starved) plus optional local pollables — PR status from L5's core.parsePrStatus gh poll synthesized as github.pull_request.opened/merged envelopes (pull not push, no webhook), core.collisions as a collision event, and a manual local.manual_test envelope emitted by a tile right-click button. (3) core.eventMatchesFilters(envelope, dotPathFilters) mirrors the candidate's attribute/payload matching, reusing L1/L3's name->resolver + validator family. (4) Processors stay the present-effect catalog (log->appendLedger, relabel->setLabel, nudge->handleAction, feed->routeTask, continue->dispatchSerialized type), and every firing/suppression emits a by:"rule" ledger event into the existing automation result ledger (the second L6 bullet). EXPLICITLY DROP from the candidate: the HTTP/webhook server ingress, connector adapters, the SDK ingestEvent() endpoint, and registerAutomationEventType/plugin-emitted events — those need a server / in-process plugin+SDK host that context.md forbids.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempt fails; the ADAPT-reinforces-L6 ruling holds. I tried all four skeptic paths against the codebase and each only kills the already-dropped half. (a) In-process executor? No. The transferable half (normalized event envelope, dot-path filters, when-event->action rule, debounce/dedupe/cooldown/maxParallel throttle struct) is pure decision logic over status-file edges. I confirmed the cited predicates exist exactly where the judge says — shouldFeed (cc-core.lua:1975), shouldAutoContinue (:2200), isHung (:2375), routeTask (:2062), setLabel (:307), collisions (:2334). A throttleAllows/normalizeEvent/eventMatchesFilters helper sits alongside these and edits/snapshots/runs nothing. Fails. (b) Server/multi-user? This is the feature's only real server dependence, and the judge already AMPUTATED it: the candidate's HTTP webhook ingress, connector adapters, SDK ingestEvent(), and registerAutomationEventType/plugin-emitted events are EXPLICITLY DROPPED. I grepped the whole codebase — no hs.httpserver/listener exists (the only 'socket' hits are kitty remote-control terminal sockets, cc-core.lua:2297+). The residual draws events from local status edges plus a gh poll (pull, not push), and that PR path is already independently proposed in L5 as core.parsePrStatus via a cached 'gh pr view' poll (todos.md:530-535, L5:32-35). So server-dependence cannot kill the residual. Fails. (c) Redundant / already queued? It IS the cline analog of L6 (todos.md:544-562) — but NOT redundant with L6 as written.
