I verified L6 today enumerates triggers (done|error|hung|approval|starved) and processors (feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log) but contains ZERO throttle vocabulary (no debounce/dedupe/cooldown/maxParallel anywhere) and no normalized-envelope/dot-path-filter abstraction. The throttles exist only as SCATTERED, un-unified versions — per-folder respawn budget (cc-core.lua:2131), auto-continue spacing/budget (:2206), watchdog re-alert. So the candidate genuinely enriches L6 (shared throttle struct + envelope normalizer + filter matcher) rather than duplicating it — the definition of ADAPT-reinforces. Fails as a DONT_ADD-via-redundancy. (d) SDK plumbing mistaken for a console feature? The ingestEvent()/registerAutomationEventType parts ARE plumbing — and they're dropped. The rule-shape/throttle/envelope are operator-facing config layered on the existing ~1s refresh tick. Fails. No path downgrades to ADD (it reinforces an existing backlog item, not a new system) or to DONT_ADD (a real, non-redundant local enrichment survives the amputation). Effort M is consistent with L6's existing M estimate — pure cc-core predicates + a throttle struct + an envelope normalizer on the refresh tick, no new ingress. ADAPT (reinforces L6) stands.

#### Connector task-update relay (proactive deduped progress pushed back to chat)  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The transferable kernel — "watch for a state change, format a human-readable deduped status digest, and push it outbound" — is exactly what an operator console for a Claude Code fleet wants, and Shepherd already has 90% of it. It detects every session state transition (idle/working/approval/done/error), raises escalation/hung/auto_respawn/auto_continue events into a notification-history feed (core.notificationEvents/NOTIFY_TYPES) with an unseen counter (core.unseenNotificationCount), and already builds the exact "N sessions, M approvals, escalations, time blocked on you, per-project rollup" digest the candidate calls buildProgressSummary — that's core.fleetStandup + core.standupMarkdown (the 📋 shift report). The candidate's createTaskUpdateFingerprint dedup maps cleanly onto Shepherd's existing per-event identity (it already dedups the 🔔 feed by lastSeen timestamp and groups gate decisions). What's genuinely missing is only the proactive PUSH: today Shepherd's digests are pull (you open the overlay) and its alerts are local hs.alert.show. Adding a "notify on state-change" outbound channel (system notification / file digest, later a configured webhook) closes a real operator gap — leave the desk, get told when a session needs you or the fleet finishes — and it rides Shepherd's own primitives, not the agent loop.
- **Against:** The feature's actual VALUE in cline is the transport it explicitly puts out of scope: streamTeamProgress(TeamProgressProjectionEvent) off the cline-hub progress stream, posted back into a bound multi-user CHAT THREAD on a connector. That is precisely the server/multi-user/connector surface Shepherd's hard scope forbids (NO network server, NO multi-user, NO bound external chat). Strip the transport and what remains — detect a state change, build a deduped fleet digest — Shepherd already HAS (notificationEvents + fleetStandup + unseenNotificationCount + the gate-decision/notification ledger). So the candidate is partly redundant (the digest builder exists) and partly out-of-scope (the chat relay). There is also a real risk of building a parallel notification system instead of extending the one fefeature set already shipped; the "push" sliver alone doesn't justify a new subsystem.
- **Ruling:** Apply the cline mismatch: the candidate's transport (chat connector + hub progress stream) is the multi-user SERVER surface Shepherd structurally cannot host — DON'T_ADD that. But the prompt instructs me to judge the OUTBOUND notify-on-state-change PATTERN reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives, and there the need is real and the building blocks already exist. The digest-builder half (buildProgressSummary → fleetStandup/standupMarkdown) and the fingerprint-dedup half (createTaskUpdateFingerprint → notificationEvents/unseenNotificationCount) are already shipped, so this is not a fresh system — it's an extension. The one missing capability is a proactive outbound push triggered by a state-change rule, which is exactly the shape of the queued L6 event-callback rule engine ("declarative when-event → action automation rules + automation result ledger"). A deduped fleet-status digest pushed to a local channel (system notification, then optionally a user-configured local webhook respecting the no-secrets rule) is a clean 'notify' ACTION for L6, fed by the existing standup/notification builders. Hence ADAPT/reinforces L6 rather than ADD (the as-is chat-relay can't live here) or DONT_ADD (the operator need and most of the machinery do fit). Off-by-default per Shepherd's automation convention.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a 'notify/push-digest' action to the L6 event-callback rule engine. Trigger: existing state-change events Shepherd already raises (NOTIFY_TYPES: escalation/hung/auto_respawn/auto_continue, plus approval-needed and done transitions from the refresh-edge detector in claude-dashboard.lua's prev[key] diff). Payload: reuse core.fleetStandup + core.standupMarkdown for the "N runs active, M in progress, X/Y complete" fleet digest and the existing per-event narrative for the per-session "[project] started/completed/failed" line — both already pure in cc-core. Dedup: reuse the lastSeen-timestamp + per-event identity already backing core.notificationEvents/unseenNotificationCount (generalize to a per-session fingerprint key if needed). Channel: a new FX.notify effect routed through the single fx table — local first (hs.notify system notification + an optional appended JSONL digest file under cc-ledger/), no network server, no chat binding, no secrets; a user-supplied local webhook URL could come later behind the same off-by-default flag, with $ENV resolved by the spawned shell per scope. All pure aggregation stays in cc-core; only the delivery is an fx effect.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation attempted and failed; the ADAPT-reinforces-L6 ruling holds. The feature's chat-connector + hub-progress-stream transport is the multi-user SERVER surface Shepherd structurally cannot host, and the judge correctly excludes it (out of scope). What remains — the OUTBOUND notify-on-state-change PATTERN, which the prompt explicitly instructs me to judge — maps cleanly onto Shepherd's existing primitives, all verified in code: (1) the deduped fleet digest payload is core.fleetStandup (cc-core.lua:1137) + core.standupMarkdown (cc-core.lua:1201), already pure and shipping the exact "Sessions active: N · allow/deny · Escalations · Stalls + by-project" rollup; (2) the per-session fingerprint dedup is M.notificationEvents/M.unseenNotificationCount (cc-core.lua:787/801) over M.NOTIFY_TYPES (cc-core.lua:786 = escalation/hung/auto_respawn/auto_continue), which is the started/update/completed/failed event set; (3) an outbound push channel ALREADY exists — FX.push async-POSTs to ntfy.sh (claude-dashboard.lua:973) and is already fired on state-change at lines 5541/5561, off by default behind escalation.push. The one new piece is a RULE-TRIGGERED 'notify/push-digest' processor that pushes an AGGREGATED, deduped fleet digest (not today's single-session per-event alert), which slots directly into L6's declared processor catalog (todos.md:544-556 — feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log) evaluated by the same per-refresh predicate engine. Strongest refutation considered: redundancy with the existing FX.push/escalation.push pipeline pushing toward DONT_ADD. Rejected because today's push is a per-session per-event nag, whereas the candidate's value is the deduped FLEET-STATUS digest as a rule action — fleetStandup is currently pull-only (Shift tab, claude-dashboard.lua:1999) and never auto-pushed. That makes it a genuine extension of shipped machinery, not a duplicate, and not the agent-loop/tool-execution/workspace-editing/server work that would force DONT_ADD. Off-by-default per Shepherd's automation convention. ADAPT/reinforces L6 is correct.

#### Scheduled agent runs: create + full lifecycle (list/get/pause/resume/delete/update/trigger-now) + one-off  `[H/L]`  · reinforces L6
- **For:** The thing cline schedules is exactly the thing Shepherd already does on demand: launch a configured agent run. Shepherd's spawn path (M.spawnSpec/FX.spawnSession) already takes folder + provider/model + permission-mode + effort + seed-prompt and can seed a session queue, and Shepherd already runs a ~1s hs.timer refresh loop. So "fire a spawn at a cron/clock time" is not executor plumbing — it's a thin new TRIGGER on a primitive Shepherd owns. It directly fills two things already on the board: the deferred "auto-spawn on starvation" item (which lacks a when/where UX) and the user's stated priority of pre-configured agents you hand work to — a nightly "spawn the review agent on repo X with this task" is the canonical use. cline's create/list/get/pause/resume/delete/update CRUD + trigger-now + one-off-at-deadline + persist-across-restart + catch-up-one-overdue is a clean, well-scoped operator surface that maps onto Shepherd's existing JSON-config + pure-predicate + ledger pattern; it's a recurrence/time KIND that L6's event→action engine doesn't yet have (L6 triggers are done/error/hung/approval/starved — all level-triggered off status, none clock-driven).
- **Against:** Most of cline's scheduler ships as a SERVER surface: ensureSchedulerHub / hub address / a separate scheduler process, with LocalScheduleClient as the fallback. That hub/multi-user half is squarely DONT_ADD for a single-user, no-network, no-server panel. cline's "workspace" field is the editable repo the agent loops over and snapshots — Shepherd has no workspace, so that field doesn't transfer (only the spawn folder/cwd does). Full CRUD with update-any-field-in-place, revision-guarded re-queue, and a persistent catch-up daemon is a lot of state machinery for what, on Shepherd, is "spawn one GUI session at a time"; Hammerspoon timers GC unretained closures (per project memory) and the panel may not even be running at the cron moment, so "catch up at most one overdue run on startup" is the ONLY honest semantic — true cron reliability needs launchd, which is out of scope. There's also a real safety smell: unattended timed SPAWNS are the most aggressive automation in the app (the board already flagged auto-spawn as needing a cap + UX), and a fully-featured scheduler invites people to treat Shepherd as a headless agent runner it structurally is not.
- **Ruling:** The candidate splits cleanly along the cline mismatch. The hub/LocalScheduleClient/multi-user serving half is DONT_ADD — Shepherd has no server. But the core NEED (fire a configured agent run on a cron/clock trigger, with lifecycle controls) reshapes onto primitives Shepherd already owns: spawn (M.spawnSpec/FX.spawnSession), a refresh-tick loop, JSON config files (cc-rules.json/cc-agents.json), pure predicates (shouldFeed/shouldAutoContinue/isHung), and the ledger. That is precisely L6's event-callback rule engine, which today only has level-triggered status KINDs (done/error/hung/approval/starved) and no clock/cron trigger and no "spawn" processor. So this is ADD-a-trigger-kind-and-a-processor to L6, not a parallel system — hence ADAPT reinforcing L6. The scheduled run should target an L1 saved-agent profile (so the agent's skills/MCP/policy config rides along, matching the user's priority) rather than re-specifying provider/model/mode inline. Drop the hub; keep only the in-process local scheduler with one-overdue-catch-up; keep it off by default with a per-trigger cap, since unattended spawn is the app's most aggressive automation.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add to L6 a new trigger kind trigger:{kind:"cron"|"oneoff", cron|atEpoch, scope: projectKey} plus a processor:{kind:"spawn", agent: L1-profile-name, taskRef: L3-template} — evaluated each refresh tick by a pure cc-core predicate (sibling to shouldFeed ~1975 / shouldAutoContinue ~2200 / isHung ~2375) that returns the due fire(s) given an injected clock; the dashboard executes via the existing FX.spawnSession, retained on M.timer (not a bare doAfter — GC). Persist schedules in cc-schedules.json (sibling to cc-rules.json), with status self-mutation through fx like routePending/alerted markers (nextRunTs, lastRunTs, revision guard for one-off). Lifecycle = CRUD over that file in the Settings overlay (enable/disable = pause/resume; trigger-now = call FX.spawnSession immediately; one-off = atEpoch with revision-guarded single fire). On panel start, catch up at most one overdue run per schedule (the only reliable semantic, since the panel may be closed). Emit by:"rule"/by:"schedule" ledger events into the L6 automation result ledger. Off by default; reuse the auto-spawn cap discussed in the deferred 4c-E "auto-spawn on starvation" note. Drop cline's scheduler hub / LocalScheduleClient split — keep only the in-process local path.
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails — the ADAPT(high)/reinforces-L6 ruling is correct, verified against the codebase. Walking the four common errors: (a) NOT the in-process executor. A cron-triggered spawn is identical to what FX.spawnSession (claude-dashboard.lua:1523) already does on a button press — focus a window / run the local `claude` CLI; native Claude Code still runs the agent loop. No file editing, no checkpoints/snapshots, no tool execution. The only delta is WHEN the spawn fires (clock-due vs click). Executor mismatch does not apply. (b) The server/multi-user half IS present in the cline feature (the scheduler hub + LocalScheduleClient split) and is correctly DROPPED to DONT_ADD; the surviving in-process local path needs no server because Shepherd already runs a persistent ~1s refresh tick that can evaluate due schedules. Keep-operator-pattern/drop-server-plumbing is the textbook ADAPT shape, not grounds for DONT_ADD. (c) NOT redundant and NOT already queued. I confirmed no existing feature gives a wall-clock/cron trigger that spawns a configured run: autopilot = time-boxed auto-approve; auto-respawn = reactive respawn of a dead/drained EXISTING session (no clock); auto-continue = reactive error-resume; project routing 4c-E = level-triggered done dispatcher (no clock); watchdog/drain = stall-threshold timers, not cron. Crucially, L6 as specified in todos.md (line 544+) has trigger kinds strictly done|error|hung|approval|starved — all level-triggered status KINDs — with NO clock/cron trigger and NO 'spawn' processor (its processor catalog is feed|continue|nudge|relabel|log). So this is genuinely an ADD-a-trigger-kind-and-a-processor EXTENSION of L6, i.e. ADD->ADAPT, exactly the ruling. (d) The CRUD-over-cc-schedules.json + Settings-overlay lifecycle is real operator UX (same shape as presets/templates); only the hub/LocalScheduleClient abstraction was SDK plumbing, and it is dropped. Every primitive the judge's mapping leans on exists and the line refs are accurate: pure injected-clock predicates sibling to shouldFeed (cc-core.lua:1975), shouldAutoContinue (2200), isHung (2375), and routePendingDone(now)/isHung(now) already take an injected clock; spawnSpec (3061)/spawnFlags (3001)/respawnSpec; fx-mediated status self-mutation precedent in routePending/alerted markers (for nextRunTs/lastRunTs/revision guard); the GC-safe after()/M.timer wrapper (claude-dashboard.lua:1189, and the timer-GC memory note) for retaining the spawn timer instead of a bare doAfter; JSON sibling-file persistence via M.json.encode/decode (cc-templates.json/cc-config.json precedent); and an L1 profile target so skills/MCP/policy ride along (L1 schema at todos.md:247, the user's #1 priority) rather than re-specifying provider/model/mode inline. The aggressive-automation caveat is already on record — the deferred 4c-E 'auto-spawn on starvation' note (todos.md:202) literally says it 'needs a cap + UX' — so off-by-default with a per-trigger cap is the right guard, which the mapping includes. Net: genuinely improves the local single-user console (fire a configured run on a schedule while away), reshapes onto owned primitives, and the only mismatched part is excised. Holds as ADAPT reinforcing L6.

### Reinforces L3 — templates  (2)

#### Built-in context/planning slash commands (/newtask, /smol|/compact, /deep-planning, /reportbug, /explain-changes)  `[H/S]`  · reinforces L3
- **For:** These commands are about the conversation lifecycle — exactly Shepherd's domain. Shepherd already proves the pattern works: /clear and /compact are first-class keystroke actions today (cc-core.lua ~1897-1910, dashboard ~2290-2305, with the destructive /clear behind a native-confirm submenu) and the panel already injects /effort, /model, mode-cycle, and slash-aware text (the isSlash double-Return path in claude-dashboard.lua ~1290-1326). So /smol is literally already shipped, and the other four are just more curated prompt-strings typed into a focused session through the SAME proven injection path — zero new architecture. /newtask is the standout: "hand off a distilled summary into a fresh clean-context task" is the operator move Shepherd is built around — it already has spawn + seedPrompt (L1) + done-only project routing + the queued L4 role-addressed handoff ("render an L3 task → enqueue onto a target"). A "New task from this session" button that types Claude Code's own /newtask, then spawns a fresh tile seeded with the handoff text and links lineage, is squarely a watch/steer/spawn-console feature and directly serves the user's stated priority of clean handoff between saved agents. /reportbug and /explain-changes are high-value canned templates that fit the existing saved-task-templates store (cc-templates.json, L3) verbatim. Curating these five as named, parameterized templates with slash-command autocomplete (already a queued L1 sub-item) is cheap and makes the panel's "Compact/Clear" pair into a fuller context-lifecycle toolbar.
- **Against:** Four of the five derive their actual value from the in-process agent executing, which is the cline mismatch. /deep-planning's payoff is the agent investigating the codebase and WRITING implementation_plan.md into the workspace — file authoring Shepherd structurally cannot do. /reportbug and /explain-changes only matter because the agent reads the repo, runs git, and synthesizes; Shepherd typing the prompt is just delegating to Claude Code, which already ships these as built-in slash commands the user can type directly. /newtask's core trick — distilling the live conversation into a clean summary — is an LLM job; Shepherd has transcriptSnippet but cannot produce the distillation itself, so it can only type Claude Code's own /newtask and hope to scrape the result, which it has no reliable mechanism to capture and re-thread. /smol is fully redundant: /compact and /clear are already shipped buttons. So building a "commands system" risks re-implementing, as a parallel registry, prompt templates that (a) Claude Code already owns natively (the user can just type /deep-planning in the session) and (b) Shepherd already covers via saved task templates. The honest net-new surface here is thin, and a dedicated command framework would duplicate L1's slash-command autocomplete and L3's template store rather than extend them.
- **Ruling:** Split the bundle by where the value lives. /smol(=/compact) and /clear: already shipped — DONT add, it's redundant. /deep-planning, /reportbug, /explain-changes: their worth is agent execution + workspace file-writing (the cline mismatch), and Claude Code already exposes them natively, so Shepherd should NOT reimplement them — at most seed them as canned entries in the existing L3 template store with slash autocomplete (queued under L1). /newtask is the one genuine operator pattern: spawning a fresh, clean-context session seeded from a handoff payload is exactly watch/steer/spawn, and it reinforces the queued L1 seedPrompt + L4 role-addressed handoff. Because the net-new, in-scope contribution is "curate these as parameterized templates and add a 'new task from this session' handoff spawn," this is ADAPT onto L3 (templates are the carrier) — not a new commands subsystem, and not ADD (Shepherd shouldn't host a prompt-expansion engine that mostly duplicates Claude Code's own built-ins). The agent-execution and file-writing halves are firmly DONT_ADD and must be dropped.
- **How (Shepherd):** Drop /smol|/compact (already shipped as the compact action, cc-core.lua ~1897). Drop the agent-execution/file-writing cores of /deep-planning (writes implementation_plan.md), /reportbug, /explain-changes — those stay native Claude Code slash commands the operator types into the session. KEEP two things: (1) Seed the existing saved-task-templates store (cc-templates.json / L3, core.templatePush ~3604) with a small curated set — a planning-prompt template and an explain-changes template — surfaced with the slash-command autocomplete that's already a queued L1 sub-item, so the operator one-clicks them into a focused session via the existing pasteIntoWindow/isSlash injection path (claude-dashboard.lua ~1290). (2) Add a 'New task from this session' handoff: type Claude Code's own /newtask into the session, then spawn a fresh tile via spawnSpec seeded with seedPrompt (L1) and link lineage — realizing the L4 'render a task → enqueue/handoff to a fresh session' delegation move. No new command engine; extend L3 (templates as carrier) and lean on L1 seedPrompt + L4 handoff.
- **Skeptic:** I tried to refute it and could not — the ADAPT(high) reinforcing L3 survives skeptical verification against the actual codebase. The judge correctly split the bundle by where value lives: REDUNDANT half (DROP, confirmed): /smol(=/compact) and /clear are already shipped as first-class actions — cc-core.lua NARRATE map ~652, the b-compact button (claude-dashboard.lua ~3070), confirm dialogs ~1897-1905, and ledgerFor compact ~2303-2305. Re-adding them is pure redundancy. CLINE-MISMATCH half (DROP, confirmed): /deep-planning writes implementation_plan.md, /reportbug and /explain-changes inspect/narrate the workspace — their worth is agent execution + workspace file-writing, the exact mismatch (Shepherd runs no loop, edits no files). They are also native Claude Code slash commands the operator types into the session. Grep confirms zero presence of deep-planning/newtask/reportbug/explain-changes/implementation_plan in cc-core.lua or claude-dashboard.lua, so these are net-new (not redundant) but firmly DONT_ADD. IN-SCOPE half (KEEP via ADAPT, confirmed): (1) Seed the existing L3 template store (templatePush/templateGet/templateList at cc-core.lua ~3604-3638; cc-templates.json) with a small curated set, surfaced via slash-command autocomplete that is GENUINELY QUEUED under L1 (todos.md ~311-314, '/-typeahead ... /model, /effort, clear, compact'), one-clicked into a session via the existing isSlash injection path (claude-dashboard.lua ~1292, second-Return-past-autocomplete ~1326). (2) A 'New task from this session' handoff: type Claude Code's own /newtask, then spawn a fresh clean-context tile via spawnSpec (cc-core.lua ~3061, accepts a task/[prompt]) seeded with seedPrompt and lineage-linked (lineageByProject/lineageSummary, claude-dashboard.lua ~5371-5392). seedPrompt exists ONLY as a queued L1 field (todos.md ~256-260) — not yet in code — so the judge correctly treats it as the queued carrier, not something already built. This is correctly ADAPT, not ADD or DONT_ADD: it builds no new prompt-expansion/command engine (which would duplicate Claude Code's own built-ins). It rides the existing template store (L3 = carrier) and reinforces queued L1 (seedPrompt + slash autocomplete) and L4 (render-a-task -> handoff to a fresh session). /newtask is the one genuine operator spawn pattern — spawning a fresh clean-context session seeded from a handoff payload is exactly watch/steer/SPAWN. The agent-execution and file-writing halves are properly dropped. Refutation fails; ADAPT holds.

#### Workflows: reusable step-by-step runbooks invoked by slash command  `[H/M]`  · reinforces L3
- **For:** This is the cleanest config/UX pattern in cline for Shepherd to absorb, and it directly answers the user's stated priority ("pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs saved into a directory"). A workflow is just a named runbook on local disk that gets injected into a session's input — no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace editing. Shepherd already has every primitive needed: cc-templates.json + templateList/templatePush as the runbook store, the no-auto-send templateInsert paste path (claude-dashboard.lua:3476), and the #tpl-menu/#n-suggest/onPathKey slash-typeahead UI the L1 enrichment already calls for. Adding a "definition source" directory (the configurable local dir L3 explicitly specifies) scanned with the existing fd/rg accelerators, surfaced as /<filename> in the slash autocomplete, and pasted into the chat input (human submits) is a small, in-scope addition that makes multi-step procedures like release/pr-review repeatable across the fleet. The local>global>remote precedence and toggle/discover/autocomplete behavior map onto Shepherd's existing skills-card + preset-source patterns. Native Claude Code already owns its own /command and skill execution, so Shepherd emitting a runbook into the input is honest and non-overlapping.
- **Against:** cline's actual mechanism is structurally a mismatch: it wraps the file in <explicit_instructions type=...> tags and INJECTS it into the in-process agent's message so the executor follows the procedure step-by-step — that injection-into-the-loop is exactly the agent-execution Shepherd cannot host. Shepherd can only paste text into a GUI input and let the human submit; it cannot enforce that the procedure is followed, cannot wrap-and-steer the loop, and cannot read back step results. Worse, native Claude Code ALREADY supports its own slash-commands, skills, and project .clinerules-style files that the running session reads directly — so a "runbook" is most naturally authored as a native skill/command in ~/.claude that the session invokes itself, making a Shepherd-side parallel runbook store partly redundant and risking two competing command namespaces. And the core of it (variable interpolation, definition-source directory, render-before-spawn, slash autocomplete) is already specced under L3 + the L1 enrichment, so building it as a separate "workflows" system would duplicate queued work rather than extend it.
- **Ruling:** The need is real and already recognized — L3 ("Parameterized + versioned prompt templates with a definition source") plus the L1 OpenHands-corroborated "slash-command autocomplete" enrichment together ARE this feature, minus the cline-specific <explicit_instructions> injection that depends on an agent loop. Verdict is ADAPT, not ADD, because the worthwhile part must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives: a runbook is not injected into a loop, it is pasted into a live session's input via the existing no-auto-send templateInsert path, and the human (or Claude Code's own native slash/skill mechanism) drives execution. Not DONT_ADD, because unlike cline's executor-bound injection, the operator pattern — named procedures on local disk, discovered/toggled/autocompleted with local>global>remote precedence, pasted on demand — transfers cleanly and the user explicitly wants exactly this directory-of-saved-definitions model. The honest framing on the tile/card is "Shepherd pasted this runbook; the session/human runs it," matching how Shepherd already labels skills as "what Shepherd attached at spawn." This reinforces L3 and should be folded into it (definition-source dir + /<name> autocomplete) rather than built as a competing subsystem.
- **How (Shepherd):** Fold into L3 (reinforces) + the L1 slash-command-autocomplete enrichment. Concretely: (1) Extend L3's "Definition SOURCE" — add a configurable local workflows/runbooks dir (default e.g. ~/.claude/workflows + project .clinerules/workflows; scanned via folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin fd/rg, grep/find fallback) producing {name, text} runbook records with local>global>remote precedence, alongside the existing cc-templates.json store (templateList/templatePush, cc-core.lua:3590/3604). (2) Slash invocation: extend the L1 #n-suggest/onPathKey typeahead (claude-dashboard.lua:4083) + #tpl-menu render to match /<filename> against runbook names AND Shepherd-known commands; selecting one routes through the existing no-auto-send templateInsert (claude-dashboard.lua:3476) so it lands in the nudge/spawn textarea for the human to submit — NEVER auto-sent, never wrapped to steer a loop Shepherd doesn't run. (3) Apply {{var}}/{{date}}/{{prev_output}} interpolation from L3's renderTemplate/templateVars so runbooks can be parameterized. (4) Optionally surface a runbook as a spawn-time --append-system-prompt block via L1's spawnFlags when picked from the New Session modal. Strictly local-disk; reuse the L1/L2 safe-path resolver ([A-Za-z0-9._-], reject escapes). Toggling/discovery mirror the L1 skills card (FX.listSkills sibling reader). Honest label: "runbook pasted by Shepherd; session/human executes."
- **Skeptic:** Refutation fails — the ADAPT (reinforces L3) ruling is correct and code-grounded. (1) The feature's transferable core is ALREADY QUEUED. L3 (todos.md:387-426) already specifies a "Definition SOURCE": a configurable local dir scanned with the existing accelerators (folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin fd/rg, grep/find fallback) producing named {name, prompt} records (todos.md:400-404), plus {{var}}/{{date}}/{{prev_output}} interpolation (todos.md:393-395, 411-415), pasted via the existing NO-AUTO-SEND templateInsert path (todos.md:397-398; verified at claude-dashboard.lua:3476-3485 — it writes template text into the #nudge textarea, no submit). The L1 enrichment (todos.md:311-314) already specifies /<filename> slash-command autocomplete reusing the #n-suggest/onPathKey typeahead (verified claude-dashboard.lua:4083) + #tpl-menu render (3462). That pair IS cline Workflows, primitive-for-primitive: a directory of named runbooks, discovered/toggled/autocompleted, local>global>remote precedence, /<name> to insert. (2) The ONE cline-specific element — wrapping the file in <explicit_instructions type=...> and injecting it into the agent loop — is exactly the EXECUTOR-bound part Shepherd cannot host. Confirmed at claude-dashboard.lua:1290-1327: when Shepherd pastes a slash command it relies on Claude Code's OWN native autocomplete popup (the 1st Return accepts the suggestion, a 2nd submits); Shepherd runs no loop, so there is nothing to inject instructions into. A runbook here is pasted text the human/session executes, not a procedure steering a loop. So this is NOT a clean ADD. (3) It is genuinely worthwhile (not DONT_ADD): named procedures on local disk, autocompleted and pasted on demand, directly matches the user's stated 'saved into a directory' priority and improves the local single-user console. The only ADD-over-ADAPT argument — that /<name> slash invocation is a new surface — is already neutralized: the slash typeahead is the queued L1 enrichment and invocation routes through the existing templateInsert no-auto-send path, so it folds into L3 rather than forming a parallel subsystem. Net: ADAPT (high), reinforces L3 + the L1 slash-autocomplete enrichment. Honest tile label: 'runbook pasted by Shepherd; session/human executes.'

---

## ❌ DON'T-ADD — 22 items

### Model/Provider Config (4)
- **Multi-provider catalog with per-provider config forms (~45 providers)** — The core mechanism (per-provider API-client auth + capability metadata) is squarely the cline mismatch: it exists to configure an in-process executor that Shepherd does not have. Shepherd already collapses "all providers" into one generic env-injection bundle (cc-core.lua:2618-2746: providerById, providerEnv, providerByModel, respawnSpec; kind=anthropic|gateway) because it only emits a `claude` spawn, and Claude Code owns the actual call. So the literal 45-form catalog is DONT_ADD territory. But the underlying NEED — operators not having to hand-craft the env-bundle JSON for a known backend — is real and already half-built. The right shape is a small curated-preset layer on the EXISTING provider system: a picker of common backends (OpenRouter/Groq/Together/Ollama/LM Studio/Bedrock-via-gateway/etc.) that pre-fills the ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL + model + headers scaffold and asks only for the env-var NAME (never a key — secrets scope is hard). That is exactly L1's {provider, requiredEnv[]} territory, hence ADAPT/reinforces L1, not a parallel subsystem. Hard constraints kept: no key fields (env-ref only), no Shepherd-side client, no capability metadata Shepherd can't use.
- **Live model / reasoning-effort / thinking-budget switching on a running session** — Apply the cline mismatch: cline's value here is split. The "switch model/effort live from the surface" operator pattern is steering, fits Shepherd, and is ALREADY SHIPPED — so it is not an ADD. The "rebuild the API handler live / numeric thinking-budget" half is executor plumbing (Shepherd makes no API calls and cannot host a buildApiHandler), so that half is structurally DONT_ADD. The honest verdict is ADAPT, not DONT_ADD, because a thin, real residue transfers onto Shepherd's existing /model + /effort controls: (1) add the `minimal` effort level to EFFORT_LEVELS + the dropdown; (2) make the Effort/Model controls capability-aware — only offer reasoning levels a model actually supports, sourced from L1's per-model metadata, so Shepherd never types a no-op /effort. The numeric token-budget slider is correctly dropped (no slash command backs it). This reinforces L1 (Agent Profiles registry) because the model→supported-effort-levels mapping is exactly the kind of model metadata L1's saved-agent {model, ...} records should carry; do not stand up a parallel capability system. Effort is small: extend an existing enum and gate two existing dropdowns by a lookup table.
- **Dynamic model-list fetching/refresh per provider** — The underlying NEED (an accurate, low-maintenance model menu per provider) is real and maps onto L1/the existing providers tab — but the MECHANISM cline ships (Shepherd's process making authenticated, sometimes-subscribed API calls to provider catalog endpoints) violates the network-call ban and the no-secrets-storage invariant simultaneously, and is the canonical executor/proxy pattern Shepherd cannot adopt. The only honest adaptation is to NOT have Shepherd fetch anything: let the spawned Claude Code session own the catalog (it already does, via in-session /model), and at most let L1 record a free-text or paste-in model list per profile that the operator curates. That degenerate, network-free version is just "edit the providers list" which Shepherd already supports, so there is nothing net-new worth building. The structural blocker dominates; ruling DONT_ADD rather than ADAPT because every part that adds value over today's hardcoded list requires the forbidden network+key client.
- **Cross-runtime agent backend selection per card/session (Cline CLI, Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode)** — The candidate is a multi-EXECUTOR feature wearing config clothing. Shepherd already validates "card carries model+provider" — but only WITHIN Claude Code, because its observability contract is Claude Code's hooks. Cline/Codex/OpenCode are separate agent loops with their own (incompatible or absent) status/approval instrumentation. To make their tiles real, Shepherd would need a per-runtime status adapter, a per-runtime approval IPC, a per-runtime context/model probe, and a per-runtime keystroke/headless-drive contract — i.e. re-implement the cc-status/cc-approve/dispatchSerialized stack N times for runtimes Shepherd doesn't own and can't instrument. That is precisely the agent-loop/tool-execution surface context.md forbids. Without it, selecting a non-Claude backend yields a launched-but-unwatched process: it breaks the gate, the context bar, /model, autopilot, auto-continue, the risk score, AskUserQuestion surfacing, and the Error-state Continue — every primitive that makes a tile a managed tile rather than a launcher icon. The user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory) is real and already captured by L1's Agent Profiles registry, but that is config FOR Claude Code (--agent/--mcp-config/--plugin-dir flags Shepherd emits and Claude Code executes); it is not a cross-runtime backend axis. There's no L-item to reinforce because none proposes driving non-Claude runtimes — they all assume the Claude Code hook contract. Verdict: DONT_ADD. The honest narrow ADAPT (let providers also point at a Claude-Code-compatible gateway model) already EXISTS as the kind=gateway provider path, so even the salvageable kernel is redundant.

### Connectors & Notifications (4)
- **Chat-platform connectors (Telegram/Slack/Discord/WhatsApp/Google Chat/Linear) bridged to agent sessions** — Weighing both: the FOR case reduces to two separable wishes, and Shepherd already covers the load-bearing one. The "drive/approve from my phone" need is served end-to-end by the existing `remoteControl.*` flag wiring (claude.ai / mobile Remote Control) plus the headless nonce-bound gate for local approvals — without Shepherd ever running a listener, holding a token, or executing a turn. Building six adapters to re-implement that transport would force Shepherd to become exactly the network-server / secrets-store / agent-executor / multi-user surface that all three governing scope rules forbid, and these traits are the feature's substance, not removable plumbing — strip them and nothing of value remains that isn't redundant. The wizard-schema pattern is mildly attractive but is a thin generic UX idea (typed fields + conditional visibility + secret redaction) that L1/L2 can adopt on their own terms without importing connectors; it doesn't justify ADAPT because there is no connector NEED to reshape — the need is already met. The Linear→work-feed sliver is genuinely interesting but is fully subsumed by the already-queued L3 work feed, which deliberately polls on-demand (no inbound webhook, no server). So this is not an ADAPT that reinforces an Lx — it is a clean DONT_ADD whose only real residue (remote control) Shepherd already ships and whose work-feed residue L3 already owns. The June-2026 review's standing precedent (drop the webhook server / OAuth / executor) makes this a high-confidence ruling.
- **Connector thread-to-session binding + lifecycle/status management + in-chat controls** — Strip the cline mismatch in two passes and almost nothing survives. Pass 1 (server): thread bindings, participant identity/mute, the hub URL, listActiveConnectors over a hub, spawnDetachedConnector/stopAll daemons, and the Channels add/remove view are all the cline-hub multi-user network surface — they require ingesting external chat over the wire, holding a bot credential (forbidden: no secrets storage), and serving non-local participants. Shepherd is single-user, reads status off the LOCAL filesystem, makes no API/network calls, and runs no daemons it must supervise. Pass 2 (executor): serializedThread + sessionId binding presumes Shepherd OWNS resumable conversation/session state; it does not — Claude Code owns the transcript, and Shepherd only watches ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json and injects keystrokes. So the binding has no in-process referent to attach to. The in-chat command vocabulary is the only seductive part, but its worth is the chat DELIVERY channel (the server), and the operator CAPABILITY it would deliver already exists three ways in Shepherd: (a) native panel actions for clear/compact/stop/mode/model/tools/autopilot, (b) the per-session queue for "schedule a task," and most directly (c) Claude Code's own Remote Control, which Shepherd already auto-arms (remoteControl.* + /rc sweep) specifically so you can drive a LOCAL session from claude.ai/mobile chat. Adding a Shepherd-hosted chat hub to re-deliver those verbs is both out of scope and redundant with native RC. The lifecycle board pattern superficially resembles the tile grid, but the tiles it would show are background protocol daemons Shepherd would have to run — not Claude Code sessions read off disk — so even the board reduces to "supervise the server we're not allowed to run." This does not reinforce any L1-L6 item: it is not an agent profile, policy bundle, prompt template, routing rule, observability read, or event-rule; it is a new networked transport. Comparable to how the OpenHands event-streaming and crewAI in-process-engine candidates were nuked on the same axes, here BOTH the server and executor mismatches fire, leaving no pure, deterministic, local residue worth building. The honest verdict is DONT_ADD.
- **Notification lifecycle hook (observation-only shell hook on attention/completion)** — The candidate describes Shepherd's existing core integration contract, not a new capability. Evidence: settings-hooks.json wires a "Notification" hook (plus Stop/PermissionRequest) into cc-status.sh + cc-popup.sh; cc-status.sh's `notification` case already parses notification_type/type/message and derives status approval (user_attention/requiresUserAction) vs done (task_complete via *idle*) into the status file ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json that the panel reads; cc-popup.sh is exactly an observation-only hook (output ignored, default-off); and escalation.push→ntfy.sh already forwards events to an external system. So the hook, the installer wiring (mergeHooks/make setup), the structured-JSON status file, the observation-only semantics, and an external-notify channel all exist and are deployed. The candidate doesn't trip the cline mismatch (it is genuinely a watch/steer pattern, not executor/server) — the reason it's DONT_ADD is the OTHER gate: "redundant with what Shepherd already has." The single non-redundant residue is a vocabulary upgrade: cline's explicit requiresUserAction boolean + severity scale + clean user_attention/task_complete split is sharper than cc-status.sh's loose field-sniffing. That residue is an enrichment of existing status/error fields and belongs to the already-queued L5 error-reason taxonomy (classifyError → reason; add severity; feed shift report + 🔔 history), so I tag reinforces=L5 rather than spinning up a parallel feature. Net: DONT_ADD as a feature; fold the severity/taxonomy idea into L5.
- **Outbound enterprise webhook + auto-installed lifecycle hooks (HTTP, stored token)** — Split the candidate in two. HALF A (its reason to exist): outbound HTTP POST of lifecycle/tool events to a configured enterprise endpoint, authenticated by a token persisted to disk (Cline/webhook_config.json), armed by a cline:// deep link. Every load-bearing element of Half A is a direct hard-scope violation — outbound network calls (Shepherd makes none; it reads ~/.claude/cc-status/*.json locally and never proxies), a stored secret token (Shepherd stores no secrets by design), and an enterprise/multi-user serving intent. This is the cline MISMATCH in its server/enterprise form, and not a close call: the repo already evaluated an isomorphic webhook+OAuth+signature pattern and wrote "DROP the webhook server, signature verification, OAuth" (todos.md:438), with the audit-ledger memory reaffirming the local-only stance. HALF B (the stated transferable nugget): auto-install local lifecycle hook scripts that emit events. This is sound AND already done — install.sh installs cc-status.sh across the full hook lifecycle, events flow into the opt-in audit ledger (session_start/end, tool_request), and L6 layers a declarative when-event→action rule engine (log/relabel/nudge/feed, by:"rule" ledger events). So Half B adds nothing not shipped/queued, and Half A is forbidden. No ADAPT path preserves the candidate's identity: strip network + token + enterprise endpoint and you are left with "have local hooks emit lifecycle events into a local store," which is literally Shepherd's existing architecture. Hence DONT_ADD rather than ADAPT-onto-L6 — crediting it as an L6/ledger reinforcement would mis-attribute a feature whose entire delta is the disallowed transport. High confidence: the violation is explicit in context.md's three NOs and the prior written DROP decision.

### Auto-approve & Permissions (3)
- **YOLO mode (auto-approve everything; auto Plan->Act; suppress ask-question)** — YOLO decomposes into three needs, and Shepherd already satisfies all three through better-scoped primitives. Auto-approve-everything = per-session time-boxed AUTOPILOT (off by default, opt-in, expiry-bounded). Plan->Act and full-bypass = the permission-MODE switch (MODE_CYCLE already contains plan, acceptEdits, default, and bypassPermissions, wired both at spawn via --permission-mode and live via Shift+Tab cycling). The only net-new element — globally and permanently disabling the ask-followup-question tool so there are zero prompts — directly negates Shepherd's stated reason to exist (human-in-the-loop: surfacing AskUserQuestion, approve/deny, nudge). cline's YOLO is shaped for an UNATTENDED IN-PROCESS EXECUTOR that runs the agent loop with nobody watching; Shepherd is the opposite stance — a human watching tiles and steering. Adopting a global/permanent/all-categories switch would replace Shepherd's deliberately bounded autopilot with something that erases its value. The truly-unattended path is also already handled: native Claude Code owns bypassPermissions, and Shepherd already emits it as a launch flag, so the operator can already spawn a no-prompt session without Shepherd hosting a YOLO concept of its own. Nothing here reinforces a queued Lx item; it's redundant with shipped autopilot + mode switching. Verdict DONT_ADD on redundancy grounds (per the rubric: "redundant with what Shepherd already has"), reinforced by the human-in-the-loop conflict.
- **Interactive per-call approval dialog (allow-once / allow-always / reject, with formatted preview)** — Mapping the candidate's three parts onto Shepherd: (1) the per-call approve/deny dialog and (2) the formatted preview of files/commands/URLs/the-question are already shipped — cc-approve.sh + cc-status.sh's summarize_tool extract the identical tool_input fields, and the panel's d-pending/renderAsk render them with Approve/Deny. (3) The "desktop file-IPC mode (request JSON written, decision JSON read by an external UI, decoupled from the agent)" is not a new idea to import — it is a verbatim description of Shepherd's own architecture: the gate is the CLI-side writer/poller and the Hammerspoon panel is the decoupled external UI, already nonce-bound for the headless/SSH case. So the bulk of the feature is redundant, and the "switch to act mode" / in-loop interception piece is the cline executor mismatch (Shepherd runs no agent loop). The only genuine gap is a one-click "Allow always" promote-to-standing-auto-approval ON the approval prompt itself; today that promotion exists only as approveRepeats (auto-on-by-SIG) and as hand-typed autoAllow globs in Settings, not as a button at the moment of decision. That sliver does not justify a new feature — it is a small enhancement to the existing autoAllow/policy-bundle surface, hence reinforces L2 (named attachable policy bundles, the home for per-tool allow rules). Verdict DONT_ADD because the feature as specified is overwhelmingly redundant with the shipped gate; the worthwhile residue is captured by L2.
- **Connector access control / per-event authorization hook (allow-list + {allow|deny,reason})** — This is the cline MISMATCH applied at the level of WHO the gate is for. Shepherd's gate authorizes a TOOL CALL made by the local human's own running session (the human is the only actor, and is trusted by construction). The candidate authorizes an INBOUND ACTOR over a connector — it presupposes a server receiving messages from arbitrary remote users and an agent loop those users remotely steer. Strip the connector/server/multi-user layer and nothing remains that Shepherd doesn't already have: the per-event allow/deny+reason hook is the existing nonce-bound gate, and the deny-reason enrichment is already queued under L2. There is no actor-to-allow-list in a single-user local console, so the feature's distinguishing capability has no surface to attach to. I deliberately did NOT set reinforces=L2: the deny-reason/policy work is already independently queued, and marking this as reinforcing would smuggle a connector-authorization framing onto L2 that the L2 scope notes explicitly DROP (fingerprint/identity, credentials/impersonation/delegation-token, no-auth scope). The honest ruling is that the transferable mechanism is already present/queued and the novel part is out of scope — DONT_ADD, not ADAPT.

### Checkpoints (3)
- **Per-step automatic workspace snapshots (shadow git)** — The feature's worth depends entirely on (a) hooking per-tool-step events from an in-process agent loop and (b) editing/restoring the workspace the agent changed — the two things the cline mismatch rules as DONT_ADD. Shepherd is a watcher/steerer with no executor, no tool events, and no workspace mutation; the snapshot ("write whole tree after each step") and restore ("overwrite live files") halves are both structurally outside its model, and even the commit cadence violates the no-heavy-work-on-the-1s-loop invariant. The only watch-console-appropriate slice — surfacing WHAT files a session touched — is already on the backlog as the L4/L5 read-only Changes/git-diff overlay (todos.md 493-497: FX.gitChanges + core.parseGitStatus, status-only, never on the loop, no commit/restore). Since the transferable need is already captured by an existing queued item and the snapshot/restore engine itself cannot be hosted, this is DONT_ADD rather than ADAPT — adding it as a reinforcement would over-claim; the existing diff view deliberately does NOT do snapshot/restore and shouldn't be expanded to.
- **Checkpoint restore (task-and-workspace / workspace-only / task-only)** — This is a clean DONT_ADD on the Cline mismatch. Checkpoint restore is the most executor-bound feature in Cline's surface: it composes three things Shepherd has none of — ownership of the agent loop (to cancel/resume the task), a shadow git snapshot of the workspace Cline edited (to git reset --hard), and ownership of the conversation transcript and API-request metrics (to truncate/rewrite/preserve). Shepherd watches status files and steers via keystrokes/hooks; it does not run the loop, edit or snapshot files, or own the transcript. There is no faithful way to host this: a git reset on the user's real working tree would be a destructive operation a watch console must never perform, and there is no message history for Shepherd to rewind because Claude Code owns it. Shepherd's existing /clear+compact+auto-respawn cover the only adjacent thing it CAN do (coarse reset-and-restart of a session it doesn't snapshot), and the queued L5 git-diff view is read-only observability, deliberately not a mutate/restore tool. The candidate doesn't reinforce any L1-L6 backlog item because all six are management/config/observability/routing patterns, none is a workspace-or-transcript restore engine. Verdict: DONT_ADD, high confidence.
- **Checkpoints on/off setting with graceful degradation + slow-init warning** — Splitting the candidate into payload vs. wrapper resolves it cleanly. PAYLOAD = workspace git-snapshot for rollback: textbook cline-mismatch (needs the agent loop + workspace ownership Shepherd structurally lacks; redundant with Claude Code's own native checkpointing). On its own that is DONT_ADD. WRAPPER = the off-by-default + capability-detect + actionable-error-with-one-click-disable + bounded-init-self-disable convention: this is real, it is operator-facing config/UX (not agent execution), and Shepherd only PARTIALLY has it. The question explicitly instructs to "judge the operator pattern, not the agent-execution/file-editing/sandbox plumbing," and weights config/UX conventions favorably when they fit the local model. The convention's three increments (actionable error + one-click disable + bounded self-disabling init) plug directly into the queued L1 pre-flight validator and the existing make-doctor / config-drift surfaces — and the slow-init guard echoes a lesson Shepherd already paid for twice (the 64KB pipe-buffer and folder-scan deadlocks). That makes it ADAPT reinforcing L1, not a fresh primitive. I rejected ADD (the literal checkpoints feature cannot exist here) and rejected pure DONT_ADD (that would discard a genuine, cheap UX-convention upgrade that the roadmap's own validator wants). Effort is S: no new subsystem — extend the L1 validator's refusal path with a disable/override affordance, and add a timeout+self-disable wrapper to FX capability probes. Confidence high: scope limits make the payload verdict unambiguous and the convention's landing spot (L1) is already named in todos.md.

### Task Lifecycle & Context (2)
- **Stale-file context tracking (detect external edits)** — This is the cline mismatch in its purest form. FileContextTracker (fileWatchers, recentlyModifiedFiles, recentlyEditedByCline, trackFileContext) exists to correct THE AGENT'S EDIT FLOW from inside the agent loop: detect that workspace content drifted from what the model last saw, mark the in-context copy stale, and force a re-read before the next diff. Every load-bearing part — owning the agent's context window, distinguishing self-edits from external edits at tool-call granularity, gating/steering the next tool call — requires the in-process executor Shepherd structurally is not. Shepherd watches ~/.claude/cc-status/ status files and steers GUI sessions via keyboard/hooks; it does not proxy the model, does not run Edit, and cannot make a session re-read a file. The candidate is also redundant: native Claude Code already fails an Edit on stale content and re-reads, so the operator value Shepherd could add (a read-only "this edited file changed externally" badge) is a different, much smaller feature that is already covered by the L5 detail-panel "Changes"/git-diff observability tab — not a reason to clone cline's executor-side tracker. Verdict DONT_ADD: the worth depends on running the agent and editing the workspace, which Shepherd cannot host. (No reinforces set: the genuinely transferable sliver is already inside L5's read-only observability scope and does not need a new backlog item.)
- **Per-card / per-tile live progress (latest message + tool call + status color)** — All three pieces of the candidate (status-color dot, latest assistant message, latest tool-call line) are already implemented in Shepherd and verified in source: STATUS_COLORS/SD_LABELS + `.dot` themes (cc-core.lua:28-33, claude-dashboard.lua:2715-2752), transcriptSnippet activity peek on by default (cc-core.lua:1544, claude-dashboard.lua:107-110 + 5407-5412), and the pending tool summary rendered on the tile (cc-status.sh:41/136-137/228-229; claude-dashboard.lua:4951-4952). The candidate's TeamRunRecord.currentActivity/lastProgressMessage are functionally identical to it.activity + it.pending.summary, both derived from the same local-file watch model with no agent loop or API calls. Verdict is DONT_ADD on the redundancy rule. The one genuine gap — the activity SNIPPET currently shows only in the detail panel, not as a per-tile subtitle line — is a cosmetic enhancement that belongs to L5 (richer session observability: "surface plan/TODO on a tile"), not a new system; hence reinforces L5 rather than ADAPT, since the underlying need is already met fleet-wide.

### CLI & Headless (2)
- **Non-interactive one-shot / piped / headless run with NDJSON stream + exit-code contract** — The candidate's value is entirely in running a one-shot agent turn headlessly and reporting its outcome via a streamed event union plus an exit code — i.e. being the executor. That is precisely what Shepherd structurally is not (no agent loop, no tool execution, no workspace, no server, no run to exit). The pitch itself retreats to a single transferable idea — the typed event vocabulary as a ledger-export model — and that already exists in Shepherd: cc-core.parseLedger/filterLedger over a ts+type-stamped JSONL union, the NARRATE map of ~25 disjoint event types with provenance, and the audit-export action (cc-core line ~2120 / dashboard auditExport) that writes the full uncapped event set as a real .jsonl file for jq piping. There is no new primitive here that fits Shepherd: no queued Lx item is about a headless CLI entrypoint, and the event-taxonomy angle is the already-built audit ledger plus the queued L6 automation-result ledger, not a parallel run-and-exit surface. Adding a Shepherd CLI that "runs a turn and exits" would require importing the agent loop wholesale — the explicit DONT_ADD trigger. Verdict DONT_ADD with high confidence; no reinforces, because the genuinely-adjacent capability is already shipped rather than queued.
- **Zen / background fire-and-forget dispatch (daemon)** — Split the feature: (1) the daemon executor and (2) the operator gesture. Part 1 — a background hub server submitting ChatStartSessionRequest and running the agent loop unattended — is the canonical cline mismatch (server + executor + tool execution + no human). Shepherd hosts none of that: no server, no loop, no tools; it reads status files and types keystrokes into already-running GUI sessions. Part 2 — auto-approve, walk away, retrieve via history, notify on completion — is not novel here: autopilot already provides time-boxed auto-approve-all; auto-continue and graceful drain already make unattended forward progress and closure (bounded, opt-in, ledgered per the L6/automation ledger); the activity peek already snaps to the final assistant line on the done edge; the ledger/timeline/export already provide later retrieval; and notificationEvents + the 🐑 menubar already give a completion-ping channel. So the residual value of --zen on top of what Shepherd ships is just "package autopilot+auto-continue into one click and fire an hs.notify on done" — a trivial UX convenience, not a feature, and not worth a schema entry. Critically, the thing that makes --zen worth building in cline (you can exit the CLI because a daemon keeps driving) is the thing Shepherd cannot and must not host. This also does not advance the user's stated priority (saved agents/skills/MCP in a directory → L1) at all. Verdict DONT_ADD: the load-bearing part is structurally impossible, and the rest is redundant.

### MCP & Plugins (1)
- **Plugin sandboxing (subprocess isolation)** — This is the cline mismatch in its purest form: a pure agent-execution-plumbing feature. SubprocessSandbox / loadSandboxedPlugins / the bootstrap shim exist only to safely RUN untrusted plugin code inside cline's in-process agent. Shepherd never loads or runs plugins — native Claude Code does, in its own process, when Shepherd spawns a session with `--plugin-dir`/`claude plugin install`. There is no Shepherd-resident plugin host to wrap in a subprocess boundary, and building one would require Shepherd to start executing plugin code, which violates its NO-executor / NO-tool-execution / NO-in-process-agent-loop scope (context.md: "does NOT execute/run an agent loop"). The legitimate adjacent need — managing which plugins a saved agent spawns with, and validating their names safely — is already covered by L1 (per-profile plugins[], gated by spawn.live, degrade-if-CLI-absent) plus the L1 safe-path hardening that validates skill/MCP/plugin names against `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` and rejects out-of-root resolution before any FX write. That name-validation is Shepherd's correct, in-scope analog of "plugin safety"; subprocess isolation of running plugin code is not. Note it does not even reinforce an Lx as an extension — it would be a parallel executor capability, so no reinforces is set.

### Rules/Skills/Workflows (1)
- **File hooks: lifecycle scripts in a config directory keyed by event name** — Strip the feature to its core: a runtime that finds an event-named script and runs it with a JSON payload on stdin, as part of executing the agent. That core is the in-process agent loop / tool execution that Shepherd structurally cannot host — Shepherd has no PreToolUse/PostToolUse to fire because it executes no tools; it only WATCHES the events Claude Code's own runtime fires. So as a thing-to-build, it is DONT_ADD per the MISMATCH. Crucially, the residual value once you remove the executor is not net-new: (1) consuming/auditing hook payloads is the existing opt-in audit ledger; (2) declarative "on this lifecycle edge, do X" is precisely L6's event-callback rule engine (cc-rules.json with a feed/continue/nudge/relabel/log processor catalog, evaluated each refresh tick), which already generalizes Shepherd's hard-coded auto-respawn/auto-continue; (3) a managed, toggleable inventory of which scripts bind which events is L5's queued Hooks inspector. The user's directory-of-config priority is real, but it's already served by L1 (agent profiles) + L6 + L5, not by importing cline's runtime-executed hooks. I weighted the FOR case generously because Shepherd genuinely sits on the same event vocabulary and the config UX is appealing — but every piece that fits is redundant with existing/queued work, and the one piece that's new (a Shepherd-owned execution lifecycle) is exactly what scope forbids. Hence DONT_ADD, flagging L6 as where the legitimate slice already lives so we don't spin up a parallel hooks subsystem.

### Multi-Agent Teams (1)
- **Convergent team outcome with required-section gate + fragment review** — The feature has two layers. The literal layer — a titled outcome holding required sections, agents attaching document fragments, a reviewer approving/rejecting fragment text, and a finalize that assembles the deliverable — is a cline-hub TEAM (server + multi-user + shared document store + workspace content). Shepherd has no server, no users, no shared document store, and edits/assembles no files; the fragment text lives in each Claude Code session's own workspace that Shepherd merely watches. That layer is DONT_ADD. But the prompt itself isolates the genuinely transferable sliver: the completion gate — "don't mark done until N required conditions each have an approval." That sliver is real and currently absent. Shepherd's "done" is a single status edge per tile; it has no multi-condition, human-signed-off definition-of-done for a multi-session project. That maps cleanly onto a primitive Shepherd has ALREADY QUEUED — L4's join barrier (core.routeBarrierMet, AND/OR over members done) — which the candidate usefully enriches with (a) named required conditions rather than a flat all-done count, and (b) a human approve/reject step per condition, reusing the existing gate's approve/deny UX and the audit ledger. Because the NEED is real but must be reshaped onto Shepherd's primitives AND it extends a queued item, the rule is ADAPT with reinforces=L4. I withhold high confidence because the adapted version is a thin checklist over status (Shepherd can't judge section CONTENT, only record a human checkbox), so it risks overlapping the existing gate + L4 barrier + L4 per-task outcome counters without much added watch value; the win is the human-sign-off-per-condition framing, not the document machinery.

### Scheduled Agents (1)
- **Schedule execution history + per-schedule statistics + active executions** — Decompose the candidate into (a) a scheduler that fires runs, (b) per-run telemetry status/duration/tokens/cost, and (c) read/aggregate views (history, stats tiles, active list). (a) doesn't exist in Shepherd and isn't queued; (b) is pure executor output — Shepherd runs no agent loop, makes no API calls, and meters no tokens/cost, so it structurally cannot produce running/done/failed-with-duration-and-cost records; (c) is the one transferable pattern, and Shepherd ALREADY ships it as fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954, approval/denial rates + per-session rollups + blocked-time) and fleetStandup/standupMarkdown (cc-core.lua:1136-1245, lifecycle + auto-action + problem tallies by project) plus sparkline buckets. So every part is either impossible without the executor or already present. There is no per-schedule axis to hang anything on. This is the cline mismatch (no executor, no API calls, no cost metering) compounded by redundancy with the existing insights/shift-report aggregates. The candidate's own note that token/cost 'depend on data from running the agent' confirms the load-bearing half is out of scope. DONT_ADD; if anything, the appetite for run-level success/duration signal is already satisfied by the shift report, and any future enrichment belongs to L5 observability, not a new scheduled-execution subsystem.
